- `shared/ui/weather-chip.ts` — `<vision-weather-chip>`, the reusable presentational piece (docs/OPS-CORE-PLAN.md §W: "Command header + Fly OSD" — built shared from the start, knowing upfront it had two consumers): injects whichever `WeatherStore` instance its host page provided (no data inputs beyond the optional `limitMps`, mirrors `TelemetryOsd`'s own DI-sharing idiom), renders nothing at all while `reading()` is `undefined`. `.chip.ok`/`.chip.warn`/`.chip.danger` (the existing global severity classes, `src/styles.css`) carry the color — no new chip CSS needed. **Not the same chip as `features/fly/fly-osd.ts`'s pre-existing "Wind" chip** (docs/FC-INTEGRATIONS-PLAN.md F-e, the drone's own onboard MAVLink `WIND` reading) — see that component's own doc comment for the telemetry-instrument-vs-ambient-forecast distinction; the two intentionally coexist without colliding.

### `src/app/shared/ui/events-rail.ts`/`.html`/`.css` — the detection-events rail (docs/MVP2-PLAN.md §E, E-b bullet 1; moved here in docs/MVP3-PLAN.md §C-c)

**`EventsRail`** (`<vision-events-rail>`) — label/asset filters over `EventsStore`'s shared feed, a newest-first list capped at 20, each row naming its source/confidence/relative time and, when resolvable, clickable. Moved here from `features/wall/wall.ts`/`.html` when the Command dashboard needed the identical rail as its own "events feed" section — this codebase's established "move to a shared home once a second page needs it" precedent (`core/fleet/device-logic.ts`'s doc comment, the same reason `shared/map/fleet-map.ts` moved out of `features/map/` this same cycle — see the `/map` fleet overview section below). Behavior — filters, the 20-row cap, row content, click target — is byte-for-byte unchanged from `WallPage`'s old inline markup; only the internal `.event-list`/`.event-row` styling and the label/asset-filter/1s-clock state moved with it into this component. Output: `open: output<DetectionEvent>()` — the host resolves navigation itself (`resolveEventTarget` + `FleetStore.streams()`), identical logic in `WallPage.openEvent`. **Sizing/positioning (width, max-height, sticky, overflow) stays each host's own concern**, applied directly to this component's own tag — mirrors `shared/map/fleet-map.ts`'s `:host{height:100%}` + the host's own sizing class working together; this component only owns what's visually *inside* its own card. **Does not manage `EventsStore.activate()`/`release()` itself** — like `shared/map/fleet-map.ts` injecting `EventsStore` directly for its own event markers, that lifecycle stays the host's job, so a host rendering both this rail and the fleet map only owns the refcount once, not per consumer.

**Third host, docs/UX-REWORK-PLAN.md §U-c: `shared/ui/notification-bell.ts`'s dropdown** — `CommandPage` no longer renders `<vision-events-rail>` at all (`command.css`'s old `.events-rail-embed` class is gone with it); the header bell's own dropdown embeds it instead (`class="bell-dropdown"`, the same "host applies its own sizing class to this component's tag" convention, just a popover's worth of extra `position`/`box-shadow` on top). `WallPage` is the component's only *page* host left (`wall.css`'s `.events-rail`, unchanged). See `notification-bell.ts`'s own doc comment for why this component's behavior itself needed **zero changes** to move host — filters/cap/row content/click target are exactly as `WallPage` already had them, the bell just resolves navigation itself instead of forwarding to a page.

### `src/app/features/devices/**` — asset-first inventory + the single "+ Add source" flow (docs/CYCLES-PLAN.md §4, §8, §9, §11)

- `devices-page-logic.ts` (renamed from `warehouse-logic.ts` per vision-web/docs/UI-STRUCTURE-PLAN.md §3, B1 — this page's own folder already holds `features/devices/simulate-logic.ts`, and the identical basename collided with the generic `core/fleet/warehouse-logic.ts` below) — pure, Angular-free logic behind the Devices page's own view models, unit-tested. The **generic** lifecycle-action-menu state machine and edit-request builders moved to `core/fleet/warehouse-logic.ts` in docs/CYCLES-PLAN.md §11 (CD-b — the asset detail page needs them too; re-exported here so this page's own imports keep working verbatim, see that file's entry above). What's left, specific to this page's rendering:
  - `mapDeviceOwners(assets: AssetDetails[])` — deviceId → owning asset, generalizing `simulate-logic.ts#mapSimulatedDevices` from just the `simulated` category to every asset the page loads (ownership isn't on `Device` itself).
  - `buildWarehouseRows(devices, owners, liveDeviceIds)` → `WarehouseRow[]` (device + lifecycle + owner? + streaming + archived) — the **Advanced/raw-devices table**'s view model (below), and `buildAssetRows(assets)` → `AssetRow[]` (defaults a missing `lifecycle` to `'ACTIVE'`) — a leftover type from CW-b's card-grid, kept only because `filterAssetRowsByArchived` still has spec coverage worth keeping green; the page itself no longer renders it (see "Asset-first list" below).
  - **`buildAssetListRows(assets: AssetDetails[], liveDeviceIds)` → `AssetListRow[]`** (docs/CYCLES-PLAN.md §11, CD-b item 1) — the **primary** asset-first list's view model: `deviceCount` (`asset.devices.length`), `streaming` (any of the asset's devices in `liveDeviceIds`), `watchDeviceId` (`findVideoDevice(asset.devices)?.id`, `undefined` when the asset has no VIDEO-capable device — the list hides its Watch button in that case), plus the same `lifecycle`/`archived` defaulting as `buildAssetRows`. Needs `AssetDetails` (not just `AssetSummary`) for `deviceCount`/`watchDeviceId`, which is why `devices.ts`'s own `assets` signal now holds the full `AssetDetails[]` the page already fetches for the Advanced table's "owned by" column (see below) — one fetch, three consumers.
  - `filterRowsByArchived`/`filterAssetRowsByArchived`/`filterAssetListRowsByArchived` — the client-side half of the "show archived" toggle, one per row-shape.
- `simulate-logic.ts` — pure, Angular-free logic behind the Simulate step, unit-tested (mirrors `core/telemetry/telemetry-logic.ts`'s split of pure derivation from the injectable/component that drives it):
  - `SimulateMode = 'direct' | 'rtsp' | 'synthetic' | 'testDrone'` and `FileSimulateForm` (the `direct`/`rtsp` form shape — no `mode: 'synthetic'`/`'testDrone'` possible, by type).
  - `buildSimulationRequest(form: FileSimulateForm): StartSimulationRequest` — trims `videoPath`/`name`, maps `mode` straight onto `transport` (`'direct'`/`'rtsp'` are literally the same strings), omits `displayName`/`latitude`/`longitude` when blank/`null` rather than sending them as empty/`null` (the `@JsonInclude(NON_NULL)` convention every request DTO here follows).
  - `buildSyntheticRegisterRequest(name: string): RegisterDeviceRequest` — the `synthetic` mode *is* the Devices page's pre-existing "Add the simulated source" quick-add (`protocol: 'sim'`, `uri: 'sim://demo'`), just reachable from the flow too; blank/whitespace name falls back to `'sim-demo'`. `DevicesPage.registerSimulator()` (the quick-add button in the empty state) now calls this same function with `''` — one implementation, two entry points, per the plan's "all zero-hardware entry points live in one place."
  - `SIMULATED_CATEGORY = 'simulated'` / `isSimulatedAsset(asset)` — narrows `listAssets()` results to ones the flow created.
  - `mapSimulatedDevices(simulatedAssets: AssetDetails[]): ReadonlyMap<string, SimulatedDeviceInfo>` — deviceId → `{assetId, displayName}`, built from `AssetDetails` already fetched for *only* the simulated-category assets (see below) — not an N+1 `getAsset` per device in the table.
  - The watch-target resolution (first `VIDEO`-capable device on an asset) used to live here as `findVideoDevice` but moved to `core/fleet/device-logic.ts` in docs/CYCLES-PLAN.md §6 — see that file's entry above. **`TestDroneForm`/`buildTestDroneRequest`** (docs/CYCLES-PLAN.md §9, CU-b item 7) similarly used to live here but moved to **`core/fleet/simulation-logic.ts`** (vision-web/docs/UI-STRUCTURE-PLAN.md §3, B1) once `features/map/map.ts` needed the same "moving test drone, no file" request builder its own Devices-page-local module used to hold exclusively — same cross-feature rule as `findVideoDevice`'s own move. See that file's own entry above (API surface, `../fleet/warehouse-logic.ts` bullet's sibling) for the full shape: `{name, latitude, longitude, autoStart}` in, a `StartSimulationRequest` with **`videoPath` omitted entirely** out (not even blank — omitting the field is what tells the backend to register a moving VIDEO+TELEMETRY device with no file), never a `transport`. Distinct from `synthetic`: that mode registers a still, telemetry-free `sim`-protocol device via the plain register endpoint; `testDrone` posts to `/api/simulations` and comes back moving.
- **`devices.ts`/`.html`/`.css` — `DevicesPage`.** Two surfaces stack top to bottom (docs/CYCLES-PLAN.md §11, CD-b item 1): **Assets** (the tab's primary surface, asset-first) and a collapsed **Advanced** disclosure holding the pre-CD-b raw-devices table — nothing lost, the low-level view just stopped being the front door. The "+ Add source" progressive flow (docs/CYCLES-PLAN.md §9, CU-b item 1) is unchanged by this cycle: a page-head button opens a single card (`#add-source-card`); with no method chosen yet it shows a `.method-grid` of three `.method-tile` buttons (Register manually / Discover on network / Simulate — each with a small geometric glyph echoing the header's aperture brand mark, `app.css`), deliberately **not** numbered steps — alternative doors, not a sequence. `addSourceMethod: signal<'register'|'discover'|'simulate'|null>` narrows the card to one method's fields (a `‹` back button returns to the picker):
  - **Register** — the exact `name`/`protocol`/`uri` form + the advanced-mode stream-options editor that used to be its own `#register-card`.
  - **Discover** — the exact scan-timeout/Scan button/results table that used to be its own "Scan for devices" card; a result's **Use** button switches the flow to `register` with the fields pre-filled and the card highlighted (`useCandidate()`), same UX as before.
  - **Simulate** — the mode `<select>` offers four options: `direct`/`rtsp` (file path required), `testDrone` (docs/CYCLES-PLAN.md §9, CU-b item 7 — home lat/lon optional, no file), `synthetic` (name only, no telemetry). `simNeedsVideoPath`/`simNeedsHomePoint` computeds gate which fields render per mode. **Submit** (`submitSimulate()`) branches on mode: `synthetic` calls `fleet.register(buildSyntheticRegisterRequest(...))`; `testDrone` calls `fleet.simulate(buildTestDroneRequest(...))`; `direct`/`rtsp` calls `fleet.simulate(buildSimulationRequest(...))` — the latter two share `startFileOrSyntheticSimulation()`'s watch-resolution/toast shape (a `null`/falsy result means `FleetStore.run()` already toasted the failure and the form stays open for a retry; on success, if `autoStart` produced no `streamId`, one toast ("created — start it from the device list when ready"); if it did, `resolveWatchTarget(assetId)` resolves the device id and the success toast carries a **Watch** action that navigates to `/live/:deviceId`). Any successful register/simulate submission closes the whole Add-source flow (`closeAddSource()`).

- **Assets — the asset-first primary list (docs/CYCLES-PLAN.md §11, CD-b item 1, item 4).** One row per **asset**, exactly `assetListRows()` (`buildAssetListRows` + `filterAssetListRowsByArchived`) drives it, rendered via **CDK virtual scroll** (`@angular/cdk/scrolling`'s `ScrollingModule`, `<cdk-virtual-scroll-viewport itemSize="60">` + `*cdkVirtualFor` — this app's first use of `@angular/cdk` beyond the `leaflet`-adjacent modules; a fixed-height, self-scrolling `.asset-viewport` is what CDK virtual scroll requires, so this one list breaks from the page's usual full-document scroll on purpose) toward the "thousands of assets" posture item 4 asks for — rendering cost stays `O(visible rows)` regardless of how many assets exist. Each row carries exactly **Watch · Open · Archive**:
  - **Watch** (`watchAsset(row)`, hidden when `row.watchDeviceId` is `undefined`) navigates straight to `/live/:deviceId` for the asset's first VIDEO-capable device — no intermediate page.
  - **Open** (`openAsset(row)`) navigates to `/assets/:assetId` — the new asset detail page (below).
  - **Archive** (`archiveAssetNow`) fires immediately, no confirm panel (docs/UX-REWORK-PLAN.md §U-a2 item 3b — "Undo over confirm"; the older `archiveConfirmAssetId` inline panel this bullet used to describe is gone) — calls `VisionApi.deleteAsset` directly (not `fleet.deleteAsset`, whose own `run()`-wrapped toast has no Undo slot) and shows an `UndoToastService.showUndo` toast naming the `AssetDeletionResponse` counts (devices archived / usages retained / streams stopped), Undo calling `undoArchiveAsset` (restores the asset, then cascades to every device its own archive took down — see this file's own Status entry on the cascade). See the Q2/Q3 Status entry (bottom of this file) for the 10s Undo-toast primitive itself.
  - Rename/category-edit/activate/deactivate/restore — CW-b's asset-card actions — moved to the asset detail page's own header (below); the list intentionally does not offer them (item 1: "exactly Watch · Open · Archive at list level").
  - **Honest scalability ceiling, documented not solved (item 4):** list *rendering* is `O(visible)`, but the *fetch* behind it (`refreshWarehouseAssets()` below) is `O(total assets)` — one `getAsset()` per asset, needed for `deviceCount`/`watchDeviceId`/the Advanced table's "owned by" column alike. This cycle deliberately does **not** add a new automatic poll for this list (unlike `FleetStore`'s 5s device/stream poll) — an O(total) fetch firing every 5s regardless of tab activity would be a *worse* posture for "thousands of assets" than today's load-once/mutate/manual-Refresh cadence, given the backend's in-memory repositories (P-a/P-b, MVP2-PLAN) are the real ceiling here, not this cycle's job to raise.

- **Advanced — raw devices, collapsed by default (docs/CYCLES-PLAN.md §11 item 1).** `advancedDevicesOpen` signal, default `false`, toggled by a small button; when open, renders the **exact** pre-CD-b warehouse table (docs/CYCLES-PLAN.md §8, CW-b), byte-for-byte the same markup/logic, just demoted:
  - **"Show archived" toggle** (`showArchived` signal, page-head checkbox — shared by both the Assets list and this table) drives which device/asset list is shown. Off (default): the table reads `fleet.devices()` (FleetStore's own signal — unaffected, still archived-free, so Wall/Live never see an archived device regardless of this page's toggle). On: a page-local `allDevicesIncludingArchived` signal, populated by `fleet.listDevicesIncludingArchived()`, feeds the table instead.
  - **Device row action menu** (`deviceActionsFor(row)` → `availableDeviceActions`, now imported from `core/fleet/warehouse-logic.ts` — see above); the flat button row this bullet originally described collapsed into a per-row kebab menu in the U-a2 pass (see that Status entry). `onDeviceAction(row, action)` dispatches: `activate`/`restore`/`unassign` fire immediately, busy-guarded by `busyDeviceId`, with a plain confirmation toast (`FleetStore`'s own); **`archive`/`deactivate`** (`archiveDeviceNow`/`deactivateDeviceNow`) also fire immediately but bypass `FleetStore` to show an `UndoToastService.showUndo` toast instead (Undo restores/reactivates — see the Q2/Q3 Status entry); `rename`/`assign` open **one inline extra `<tr>`** below the row (`rowAction`/`rowActionMode(deviceId)` — at most one open at a time) with a small form (rename) or an asset `<select>` (assign, options from `assignableAssets()` — every non-archived asset).
  - **Simulated devices are recognizable and stoppable**: the State column adds a "Simulated" chip (`.chip.accent`, title shows the owning asset's name) and the actions column adds a **Stop simulation** button calling `fleet.stopSimulation(assetId)` — separate from the ordinary per-device Start/Stop (which only starts/stops the RX-visible stream): stopping *the simulation* also tears down an RTSP-transport asset's transmitted feed, which the ordinary stream-stop endpoint has no way to reach (`DefaultSimulationService` tracks the `FeedId` privately).
  - **One shared asset fetch, now feeding three consumers**: `refreshWarehouseAssets()` does one `listAssets()`/`listAssetsIncludingArchived()` call, then one `getAsset()` per asset returned, and now stores the full `AssetDetails[]` in the page's own `assets` signal (previously just the lighter `AssetSummary[]`) — this feeds `deviceOwners` (`mapDeviceOwners`, the "owned by" column + unassign action), `simulatedDevices` (`mapSimulatedDevices`, filtered), **and** `assetListRows` (`buildAssetListRows`, the primary list above) from one round rather than a fourth, list-specific fetch. Same silent-degrade convention as before (enrichment, not a user-initiated action). Every lifecycle **mutation**, by contrast, funnels through a `FleetStore` method and refreshes the whole warehouse view afterward (`refreshWarehouse()`), so a 404 before CW-a ships still surfaces as exactly one toast (from `FleetStore`) and the page keeps working.

### `src/app/features/asset-detail/**` — one asset's full picture (docs/CYCLES-PLAN.md §11, CD-b item 2–3)

`/assets/:assetId`, the Devices page's asset-first list "Open" target: characteristics, live video when streaming, a map with position + trail, telemetry grouped per source device, usage history, and a "Hardware" section carrying the full warehouse actions the list demoted.

- `asset-detail-logic.ts` — pure, unit-tested, mirrors `core/telemetry/telemetry-logic.ts`'s split:
  - `groupTelemetryByDevice(samples: TelemetrySample[])` → `ReadonlyMap<deviceId, TelemetrySample[]>` — regroups one usage's mixed-source samples (CD-a's `deviceId` field is what makes this possible) back apart, preserving each device's own chronological order.
  - `telemetryDevices(devices: Device[])` → every `TELEMETRY`-capable device on the asset — the panels the grouped view renders one-per, including a device that has reported no sample yet (its panel just says so).
  - `freshestSample(byDevice)` — the single freshest sample across every source device (docs/CYCLES-PLAN.md §11 item 3: "the map marker uses the freshest source"), `O(devices)` since each group's own samples are chronological. Used for the page's "source: <device name>" caption next to the map, not for the map's own position/trail — see below for why those reuse `TelemetryStore.latest()`/`trail()` directly instead.
- **`asset-detail.ts`/`.html`/`.css` — `AssetDetailPage`.** Lists `TelemetryStore`/`DetectionsStore` in its own `providers` (one poller-set per route activation, same idiom as `LivePage`) and drives them from `effect()`s exactly like `LivePage` does. **`TelemetryStore.track()` is called with *any* device on the asset**, not necessarily a TELEMETRY-capable one specifically — the store resolves the *owning asset*'s open usage regardless of which of the asset's devices it's given (`findOwningAsset` matches on any device id), so `latest()`/`trail()` already aggregate every source device's samples into one position/trail exactly like item 3 asks; `groupTelemetryByDevice` reruns that same poll's raw `samples()` through per-device grouping for the page's own labeled panels. This is why `<vision-live-map>` (below) can be reused **completely unmodified** for this page's map — feed it the same DI-shared `TelemetryStore` instance and it just works.
  - **Reuses rather than rebuilds**: `<vision-player>` (self-recovering, shared with Wall/Live/the map dock — this page benefits from item 5 for free), `<vision-stream-info>` (docs/MVP2-PLAN.md §U-info's info panel), `<vision-live-map>` (moved to `shared/map/` specifically for this reuse — see its entry below), and the `core/fleet/warehouse-logic.ts` lifecycle logic (rename/lifecycle for both the asset header and the Hardware section's devices).
  - **Data orchestration lives in the component**, not a dedicated store class (mirrors `features/devices/devices.ts`'s own precedent — pure-logic extraction is for testable *derivation*, not HTTP orchestration): `load(assetId)` fetches `getAsset`, polled every 5s via `PollScheduler` (`ASSET_POLL_INTERVAL_MS`) so the page stays live without a manual refresh; a 1s `PollScheduler` clock backs per-device sample-age/staleness and the live-ticking usage durations (`formatDuration`/`sessionDurationSeconds`, `core/stream-info-logic.ts`).
  - **Header**: name, category chip, lifecycle chip, streaming chip; rename/category edit inline (`editingAsset`/`nameDraft`/`categoryDraft`, `buildAssetEdit`); the one lifecycle button is now **Archive/Restore only** (`operatorAssetActions` — U-a2 item 2 dropped activate/deactivate from every asset-level surface), Archive firing immediately with an `UndoToastService` toast, no confirm card (U-a2 item 3b, closed out by the Q2/Q3 Status entry's 10s primitive) — all the asset-level mutation UI CW-b's card grid used to have, relocated here per item 1/2.
  - **Video**: `<vision-player>` bound to the first VIDEO-capable device's active stream, with the same overlay/burned/off boxes toggle as Live (below) — omitted entirely (with an explanatory empty state) when the asset has no VIDEO-capable device. This page has its own Stop/Start (same header actions as Live) and, since docs/MVP2-PLAN.md §S, S-b, the identical `stopped`/`explicitlyStopped`/`hasBeenLive` wiring `LivePage` has — see that page's own MODULE.md entry (or `shared/player/player.ts`'s `stopped` input doc) for the full reasoning.
  - **Map**: `<vision-live-map>`, shown whenever the asset has ≥1 TELEMETRY-capable device, with a "source: <device name>" caption from `freshestSourceName` (`asset-detail-logic.ts#freshestSample`).
  - **Telemetry, per source device (item 3)**: one panel per `telemetryDevices(asset.devices)` entry, each showing that device's own latest position/altitude/heading/battery/sample-age (independently stale-highlighted) — `latestSampleFor(deviceId)`/`deviceSampleAgeSeconds(deviceId)`/`deviceStale(deviceId)` read `telemetryByDevice` (the `groupTelemetryByDevice` computed) from the template, the same "plain method reading a signal, called from `@for`" idiom `features/devices/devices.ts#simulatedInfo` already established.
  - **Usage history**: `asset.recentUsages`, a table of started/duration (`usageDuration`, live-ticking for the still-open one)/status/sample count, plus (docs/MVP2-PLAN.md §R, R-b) a per-row action: a finished usage (`endedAt` present) gets a **Replay** link to `/assets/:assetId/replay/:usageId`; the still-open row gets **Watch live** (`/live/:deviceId`, when the asset has a video device) instead — replaying a usage that's still growing isn't meaningful, see `features/replay/**` below.
  - **Hardware**: `asset.devices`, full lifecycle actions (`deviceActionsFor` = `availableDeviceActions(device.state, true)` minus `assign` — every device shown here is already owned by this asset, so `assign` never applies; `unassign` does) via the same per-row kebab menu as the Advanced devices table (U-a2 item 7); `archive`/`deactivate` (`archiveDeviceNow`/`deactivateDeviceNow`) fire immediately with an `UndoToastService` toast, mirroring `features/devices/devices.ts`'s own identical pair — see the Q2/Q3 Status entry; plus an on-demand **"Attach a device"** picker (`openAssign()` — fetches every device + every asset's `AssetDetails` once, on click, not kept warm on every page load, to compute the unowned set; `confirmAssign()` calls `fleet.assignDevice`).

### `src/app/features/replay/**` — flight-replay cockpit (docs/MVP2-PLAN.md §R, R-b; video/clip export + event deep link, docs/OPS-CORE-PLAN.md §R, R-c/Q1)

`/assets/:assetId/replay/:usageId`, the asset detail page's usage-history "Replay" target for one **finished** usage — a referee's use case: "verify where the machine was at minute 7." **`/replay?asset=…&usage=…&t=…`** (docs/OPS-CORE-PLAN.md §Q1) is a second, flat route to the same `ReplayPage` — the event → replay deep link's own target, see `replay.ts`'s own class doc comment and the bullet below for how the two routes are told apart.

- `replay-logic.ts` — pure, unit-tested (mirrors `shared/player/player-recovery.ts`/`shared/map/flight-plan-logic.ts`'s split of pure state/derivation from the component that drives it); every function here re-derives its answer from an already-fetched `UsageTimeline` plus a scrub-time signal, so scrubbing never re-fetches:
  - `nearestSample(samples, atMs)` — the telemetry sample nearest `atMs` (binary search, no interpolation — "the closest recorded reading, not a synthesized one"). Backs the map marker and the per-device telemetry panels alike.
  - `trailPrefix(samples, atMs)` — the flight trail drawn "up to" the scrub position: every positioned sample with `at <= atMs`, in `core/telemetry/telemetry-logic.ts#deriveTrail`'s same gap-skipping shape.
  - `nearestDetectionResult(detections, atMs)` / `isDetectionNear(result, atMs, toleranceMs = DETECTION_MATCH_TOLERANCE_MS = 2000)` — the detection result nearest the scrub time, and whether it's close enough to count as "right now" rather than a leftover from a distant part of the flight (a legacy/streamless usage's empty `detections[]`, or a flight where CV only ran intermittently, both read honestly as "no detection data near this moment", never a fabricated reading).
  - `bucketDetections(detections, fromMs, toMs, bucketCount = DEFAULT_DETECTION_BUCKETS = 120)` → `DetectionDensityBucket[]` (`{atMs, count}`) — buckets only the *positive* frames (`detections.length > 0`) into equal-width slots, returning only the non-empty ones, ascending by time; backs the scrub bar's density strip (one element per bucket, not per detection, so a flight with hundreds of positive frames stays cheap to render) and the click-to-jump target (each bucket's `atMs` is its earliest result's `capturedAt`).
  - **`capDetectionBuckets(buckets, cap = DETECTION_STRIP_CAP = 200)` → `{buckets, totalCount}`** (docs/OPS-CORE-PLAN.md §Q3a, new) — a pure slice on top of `bucketDetections`'s own ascending output: keeps only the latest `cap` buckets (the tail) plus the pre-cap `totalCount`, so `ReplayPage.detectionStripCapNote` can render a quiet "Showing latest N of totalCount markers" only when something was actually trimmed. Rarely engages today (`DEFAULT_DETECTION_BUCKETS`=120 is already under the 200 cap) — see the Q2/Q3 Status entry for why it exists as a backstop regardless.
  - `clampToRange(atMs, fromMs, toMs)` / `advancePlaybackClock(atMs, deltaRealMs, speed, fromMs, toMs)` — the playback clock: advances by `deltaRealMs * speed` (wall-clock ms since the last `requestAnimationFrame` tick), clamped to `toMs`; reaching the end **stops** playback (`playing: false`) rather than looping. Stateless per call — a speed change between ticks (the 1×/4×/16× buttons) just changes the next tick's multiplier, nothing to reconcile.
  - **Recording video pane (docs/OPS-CORE-PLAN.md §R, R-c, new)** — anchored off the recording's own `start` (`ReplayService#recordingFor`'s own contract: "start is the usage's own `startedAt`" — "clip t=0 aligns with usage start"), never `timeline.from` directly (the two coincide by the endpoint's own promise, not this page's window logic): `videoOffsetSeconds(atMs, recordingStartMs)` (scrub → `<video>.currentTime`, seconds, never negative) and `videoTimeToAtMs(currentSeconds, recordingStartMs, fromMs, toMs)` (the reverse, clamped into the replay window). `shouldSeekVideo(currentSeconds, targetSeconds, thresholdSeconds = VIDEO_SEEK_THRESHOLD_SECONDS = 0.25)` is the guarded-effect's own threshold check — see `ReplayPage`'s own doc comment for the full "who's driving `atMs`, and how the loop is broken" writeup.
  - **Clip export (docs/OPS-CORE-PLAN.md §R, R-c, new)** — `ClipWindow {startOffsetMs, durationMs}`; `wholeFlightClipWindow(recordingStartMs, fromMs, toMs)` (the whole replay window, anchored off the recording's own start) and `selectedClipWindow(recordingStartMs, selectionStartMs?, selectionEndMs?, fromMs, toMs)` (the current clip-mark selection when both marks are set and well-ordered, else the whole flight — "current selected window (or whole flight if no selection)"). `buildClipDownloadUrl(recordingUrl, window)` — the "Download clip" `<a download>`'s own href builder: given the recording's own base `/get` URL (whose `start`/`duration` params already describe the *whole* recorded window) and a `window` of offsets relative to that same `start`, returns a new URL with only `start`/`duration` replaced — `path` and every other part of the URL (origin included) untouched; `undefined` for an unparseable URL or one carrying no `start` param to anchor against, never a broken href.
  - **Event → replay deep link (docs/OPS-CORE-PLAN.md §Q1, new)** — `parseDeepLinkOffsetMs(raw)`, a small defensive parse of the `?t=` query param (a plain offset-ms string; `undefined` for anything absent/non-numeric, never `NaN`).
- `replay-map.ts`/`.html`/`.css` — `ReplayMap` (`<vision-replay-map>`), purely presentational (no store/DI — driven by `[trail]`/`[markerPosition]`/`[markerHeadingDegrees]`/`[fullTrail]` inputs from `ReplayPage`'s own scrub-time `computed`s). A fourth home for the shared Leaflet bootstrap (`shared/map/leaflet-loader.ts`: `importLeaflet`/`ensureLeafletStylesheet`/`MAP_LAYERS`/`mapLayerTileLayer`/`droneDivIcon`), alongside `shared/map/live-map.ts`/`shared/map/fleet-map.ts`/`shared/map/flight-plan-dialog.ts`. Lives in `features/replay/` (not `shared/map/`) since only this page uses it — mirrors `shared/map/live-map.ts`'s own original home before a second page needed it (see that module's doc comment). Unlike `LiveMap`'s always-on auto-follow, this map fits the view to the **whole** route once on load (`[fullTrail]`, independent of the scrub-bounded `[trail]`) — replay is reviewed, not chased; `autoFollow` (default off) optionally recenters on the marker as the scrub position moves, and "Fit route" re-runs the fit on demand.
- `replay.ts`/`.html`/`.css` — `ReplayPage`. Route inputs `assetId`/`usageId` match their path params (`assets/:assetId/replay/:usageId`) exactly — see Routes above; `features/asset-detail/asset-detail.ts`'s own `assetId` input now follows the identical pattern too, since docs/MVP2-PLAN.md §V, V-b (see Gotchas). **Fetches once, scrubs in memory** (docs/MVP2-PLAN.md §R, R-b's own "no re-fetch per drag frame" requirement): `load()` calls `getAsset(assetId)` (display name/devices, for panel labels) and `usageTimeline(usageId, {maxPoints: 2000})` (the server's clamp ceiling) exactly once per route activation via one `effect()` (no poller); every panel below is a `computed()` over the cached `timeline` signal and the `atMs` scrub signal.
  - **Two routes, one component (docs/OPS-CORE-PLAN.md §Q1, new)**: `assetId`/`usageId` are now `input<string | undefined>` (were `input.required<string>()`) — **unaliased**, so the nested route's path params keep binding by name exactly as before; a second pair, `assetIdParam`/`usageIdParam`/`deepLinkOffsetParam` (aliased `{alias: 'asset'|'usage'|'t'}`), binds the flat `/replay` route's own query params. `effectiveAssetId`/`effectiveUsageId` (`computed`) resolve whichever route actually supplied a value; `assetId` is genuinely optional on the flat route (not in the deep link's own pinned URL shape), but every deep-link call site this batch adds (`shared/ui/notification-bell.ts`, `features/wall/wall.ts`) already has it in hand at click time and includes it anyway — the "no asset id" branch (skips `getAsset`, degrades the per-device telemetry panels to whatever `groupTelemetryByDevice` can do from the raw `TelemetrySample.deviceId` alone) is a defensive fallback, not the common case. `backLink` (a `computed`) is the header's "back" target — the asset detail page when known, else `/command`.
  - **Open usages redirect rather than render** (docs/MVP2-PLAN.md §R, R-b: "open one = watch live instead"): if the fetched usage's `endedAt` is absent, the page shows a notice + a **Watch live** link instead of the cockpit — covers a stray direct link to an open usage's replay URL, not just the history row (which already only offers Replay for finished ones).
  - **Recording video pane (docs/OPS-CORE-PLAN.md §R, R-c, new)** — `load()` also fires `loadRecording(usageId)` (`VisionApi.usageRecording`, independent of the timeline fetch — a recording lookup failure never blocks the rest of the cockpit) into a `recording` signal; `videoAvailable` gates a plain `<video controls [src]="recording()!.url">` beside the scrub bar vs. a compact empty state ("No recording for this flight" + a mediamtx-enablement hint) — the same empty state also covers the `<video>` firing its own `error` event (`onVideoError`, a genuinely-missing recording segment, e.g. recording enabled only after the flight happened), never a broken player.
    - **Guarded two-way sync, the `videoDrivenUpdate` flag** (docs/OPS-CORE-PLAN.md §R, R-c: "scrubbing the timeline seeks the video and video timeupdate moves the timeline cursor, guard against feedback loops with the codebase's established guarded-effect idiom") — mirrors `shared/map/fleet-map/fleet-map.ts#suppressAutoFitDisable`'s own "flag set right before a programmatic write, checked (and cleared) by the listener that write would otherwise re-trigger" idiom. One `effect()` seeks `<video>.currentTime` whenever `atMs` changes (skipped when `videoDrivenUpdate` is set — that change originated from the video itself — and otherwise only when `shouldSeekVideo`'s drift threshold is crossed, so ordinary 1× playback doesn't reseek on every single frame); `onVideoTimeUpdate` (the `<video>`'s own `timeupdate`) sets the flag and writes `atMs` back, but **only while this page's own RAF playback clock (`playing()`) isn't already the one driving the cursor** — the two halves never fight because exactly one side is "driving" at any moment, by construction, not by timing luck.
  - **Clip export ("Download clip", docs/OPS-CORE-PLAN.md §R, R-c, new)** — `markClipStart()`/`markClipEnd()` capture the current scrub position into `clipSelectionStartMs`/`clipSelectionEndMs`; `downloadClipUrl` (a `computed`) resolves `selectedClipWindow(...)` against those marks (or the whole flight with none) and builds the `<a download>` href via `buildClipDownloadUrl` — hidden entirely whenever no recording/window is resolvable, never a dead link.
  - **Playback** drives `atMs` via `requestAnimationFrame`, not `PollScheduler` — mirrors `shared/player/player.ts`'s own precedent of a raw browser timer for a playback clock tied to one view's lifecycle rather than a "re-fetch while visible" poll (see Gotchas' `PollScheduler` heartbeat note). `togglePlay()`/`pause()`/`setSpeed()` plus the pure `advancePlaybackClock` (`replay-logic.ts`) drive each frame; reaching the end auto-pauses; the Play button relabels **Replay** once at the end, restarting from `fromMs` on the next press.
  - **Scrubbing** is a plain `<input type="range">` (`onScrub`, `[min]`/`[max]` = the window bounds in epoch ms, `[step]` ≈ 1/1000th of the window's span so it feels continuous regardless of flight length) — dragging pauses playback and takes over; no HTTP call in the handler, just `atMs.set(...)` against the already-cached `timeline`. The detection density strip (`detectionBuckets`, `capDetectionBuckets` applied over `bucketDetections` — docs/OPS-CORE-PLAN.md §Q3a) overlays the same track, each bucket a small `--density`-scaled marker (`bucket.count / maxBucketCount()`, one hue — `var(--warn)` — opacity-ramped, not a rainbow) whose click calls `jumpTo(bucket.atMs)`; `detectionStripCapNote` renders a quiet "Showing latest N of totalCount markers" line under the strip, `null` (nothing rendered) unless the 200-bucket cap actually trimmed something.
  - **Telemetry-at-scrub, per source device**: mirrors `asset-detail.html`'s own per-device panels almost verbatim, except every value comes from `nearestSample(telemetryByDevice.get(deviceId), atMs)` (the scrub-time reading) rather than "latest" — each panel also names the exact recorded sample's own timestamp (`sampleAtScrubFor`/`sampleTimeMs`) so the reading's precision (or gap) is never hidden.
  - **Detections-at-scrub** lists `nearestDetectionResult(...)`'s own `label · confidence` chips only when `isDetectionNear` — otherwise one of three honest empty states, all distinct: **no detections recorded for this flight** (`timeline.detections` is empty outright — a legacy usage, or one from a streamless asset, per R-a's own documented gap before R-a2), **no detection data near this moment** (detections exist elsewhere in the flight but not near the current scrub time), or **nothing detected at this instant** (the nearest inference frame ran and simply saw nothing) — three different facts, never collapsed into one generic "—".

### `src/app/features/live/**` — video-first cockpit: player, collapsible rail, docs/CYCLES-PLAN.md §2, §9 (CU-b item 4), docs/MVP1-PLAN.md §C8 bullet 4

`LivePage` lists `TelemetryStore` and `DetectionsStore` in its own `providers` (one poller per route activation each, stopped on route leave) and drives them from `effect()`s: `TelemetryStore` keyed on `deviceId()`/`hasTelemetry()` (the existing device-capability computed, unchanged — only a `TELEMETRY`-capable device is worth tracking at all); `DetectionsStore` keyed on `stream()?.streamId` (detections only make sense while a stream is actually running — there is no streamId to poll otherwise).

- **Video-first layout (docs/CYCLES-PLAN.md §9, CU-b item 4).** `live.html`'s `.stage` now holds only the player (and the no-publisher notice) — nothing overlays the video any more. Everything else that used to sit above/below it (telemetry OSD, detections strip) or beside it (Source/Detection-settings/Map/PTZ cards) now lives in `.side`, a **collapsible right rail**: `LivePage.railOpen` (signal, default `true`) toggled by a slim `.rail-toggle` tab between stage and rail — a narrow drawer-handle-style button rather than a full-width "hide details" bar, so it reads as part of the rail's edge. Collapsing it drops `.side` from the DOM entirely and `.layout` reflows to just the player + the toggle tab (`grid-template-columns: minmax(0,1fr) auto` via `.rail-collapsed`).
- **Map inset toggle + keyboard shortcut** (`LivePage.mapInsetVisible`, independent of `railOpen` — the map can be hidden even while the rail stays open). The Map card's header carries a Hide/Show button; `M` (case-insensitive, ignored while a form field has focus, and only wired while `hasTelemetry()`) toggles it too — a `document`-level `keydown` listener added in the constructor and removed via `DestroyRef.onDestroy`, the same manual-listener idiom `FleetMap` already uses for its own delegated click handler, not a `@HostListener` (this codebase has no precedent for that decorator).
- `telemetry-osd.ts` — `TelemetryOsd`, now the rail's "Telemetry" card content (previously a strip above the player). Purely presentational: injects the same `TelemetryStore` instance from `LivePage`'s DI (no inputs), every value a `computed` over it. Coordinates, altitude, a CSS-rotated compass needle (`transform: rotate(headingDeg)`), a color-thresholded battery bar (ok/low/critical), and sample age (highlighted via `store.stale()`, >5s). Rendered only when `telemetry.hasTelemetry()`; otherwise the card shows a plain "no telemetry yet" line — the silent-degrade state, never an error.
- **The Map card** now renders `<vision-live-map>` from `shared/map/live-map.ts` — the component itself moved there in docs/CYCLES-PLAN.md §11 (CD-b) so the asset detail page could reuse it unmodified; see that file's own entry below for its (unchanged) behavior. `live.ts`'s import path is the only thing that changed here.
- `shared/player/detections-strip.ts` (moved from `features/live/` in docs/MVP3-PLAN.md §C-b when the Fly cockpit needed the identical strip — same "no page imports another page's module" precedent as `shared/map/live-map.ts`'s own earlier move; `live.ts`'s import path is the only thing that changed, behavior is byte-for-byte the same) — `DetectionsStrip` (docs/MVP1-PLAN.md §C8 bullet 4), the rail's "Detections" card content (previously a strip below the player) whenever `live()` is true. Purely presentational, DI-shares `LivePage`'s `DetectionsStore` (no inputs), mirroring `TelemetryOsd`'s shape exactly: a subtle "CV" status dot (`store.status()`, green/`on` or grey/`off`, `title`/`aria-label` spell out which) plus up to 8 `.chip`s (`store.chips()`, e.g. "person · 0.87") — the strip itself only *lists* what is currently being seen; the boxes themselves now render two ways (docs/CYCLES-PLAN.md §11 item 6): the server's burned-in pixels (`Java2DOverlayRenderer`, adapter-overlay, unchanged) and/or `shared/player/player.ts`'s own client-side vector overlay (below), toggled by the card header's **Overlay/Burned/Off** button group (`LivePage.boxesMode`, defaults to `'overlay'`, passed straight into `<vision-player [boxesMode]>`). "No detections yet." shown in place of chips when the list is empty, never an error state.
- **The old "Source" card is now `<vision-stream-info>`** (docs/MVP2-PLAN.md §U-info, folded into CD-b) — see `shared/player/stream-info-panel.ts`'s own entry below. `LivePage.latencySeconds` receives `<vision-player>`'s `(latencyChanged)` output and feeds it straight through as an input; the advanced-mode raw stream-options block (unchanged) now sits below the panel inside the same "Stream" card rather than "Source".
- **`stopped` wiring** (docs/MVP2-PLAN.md §S, S-b, new): `LivePage.stopped = computed(() => explicitlyStopped() || (hasBeenLive() && !live()))`, piped into `<vision-player [stopped]>`. `stop()` sets `explicitlyStopped` true (after `fleet.stop()` resolves); `start()` sets it false again (a fresh attach). `hasBeenLive` latches once `live()` is ever observed true for this page instance (an `effect()` in the constructor) so a stream that later disappears from the streams list — this page's own Stop, or someone else's — reads as stopped too, without needing a fictional "was this ever live in some past session" signal. See `shared/player/player.ts`'s `stopped` input and the S-b Status entry below for the full incident this closes.

### `src/app/shared/player/player.ts`, `player-recovery.ts`, `live-edge-logic.ts`, `detection-overlay-logic.ts`, `stream-info-panel.ts`, `live-map.ts` — the shared video surface (docs/CYCLES-PLAN.md §11, CD-b items 5–6; docs/MVP2-PLAN.md §U-info, §L/§U3, §V/V-b)

- **`player.ts` — `Player` (`<vision-player>`), WHEP-first with HLS fallback, self-recovering (docs/MVP2-PLAN.md §L/§U3, item 5).** Reused as-is by `wall-tile.ts`, `live.html`, `features/asset-detail/asset-detail.html`, and `features/command/asset-panel.ts` (the now-deleted `shared/map/live-dock.ts` used to be a fifth, before Command's §U-c rework retired it) — every one of those surfaces gets everything below for free. Inputs: `src` (HLS playlist URL — also the WHEP fallback target), `whepUrl` (mediamtx's own absolute WHEP origin URL, `null`/absent for HLS-only — **never rewritten/proxied**, POST straight to it), `suspended`, **`stopped`** (docs/MVP2-PLAN.md §S, S-b, new — see below), `compact`, `detections`, `boxesMode`. Outputs: `latencyChanged`, `transportChanged: output<Transport>()` (`Transport = 'webrtc'|'hls'`).
  - **WHEP-first, HLS-fallback** (docs/MVP2-PLAN.md §L/§U3): when `whepUrl` is set, POSTs an SDP offer to it (`Content-Type: application/sdp`, non-trickle — gathers ICE, bounded to `ICE_GATHERING_TIMEOUT_MS`=3s, before sending), applies the SDP answer via `RTCPeerConnection`, waits for `ontrack`. Any failure *before a track ever arrives* (POST error, `connectionState` `failed`/`disconnected`, or no track within `WHEP_NO_TRACK_TIMEOUT_MS`=6s) falls back to the exact same HLS `beginAttach` path `src` has always used — **load-bearing**, not an edge case, see Gotchas. **The no-track timeout is only ever armed while genuinely still waiting** (`Player#whepTrackArrived`/`player-recovery.ts#isWhepStillWaitingForTrack`, docs/REALTIME-PLAN.md Phase R-b follow-up — a live soak test caught `ontrack` racing ahead of the arm call site 100% of the time on fast/loopback connections; see the dedicated R-b-follow-up Gotchas entry and Status subsection for the full incident). `behindLive` is pinned to `0` the instant a track arrives (a real number, not `null` — the detection-overlay matcher needs one) though the visible badge reads `'live · WebRTC'` rather than `'0.0s behind'`. `RTCPeerConnection`/`fetch` are browser built-ins — no dynamic import, no extra chunk; the WHEP code still only ships in whichever *lazy* chunk imports `Player` (never the initial bundle, since `Player` is never `providedIn: 'root'`).
  - **Connect once: ICE-restart-first recovery, session lifecycle, a stable certificate** (docs/REALTIME-PLAN.md Phase R-b — full writeup in the dedicated R-b Status entry below; summarized here since it's now this player's default post-play recovery path). Once a WHEP transport has ever reached `playing`:
    - **item 1 — ICE restart before teardown.** `connectionState==='disconnected'` starts a 5s grace timer (`player-recovery.ts#ICE_RESTART_GRACE_MS`/`WhepIceState`/`reduceWhepIce`, a further independent pure state machine sibling to `PacingState` — see that module's own entry below); `'failed'` (or the grace timer expiring without self-healing) calls `pc.restartIce()`, creates a fresh offer, and sends it as a WHEP **PATCH** to the session URL (`Player#onWhepConnectionStateChange`/`attemptIceRestart`) — `application/trickle-ice-sdpfrag`, `If-Match: "*"`, verified byte-for-byte against mediamtx `v1.19.2`'s own source (`shared/player/webrtc-ice-restart.ts`'s module doc has the full mapping). The **same** `RTCPeerConnection` is reused throughout — media keeps flowing, the visible chip never leaves `'playing'` for an in-place restart. The R-a stall watchdog (`getStats()`-confirmed no progress) triggers the identical flow, not an immediate teardown, once played before. A rejected PATCH (`shared/player/webrtc-ice-restart.ts#isPatchFallbackStatus` — non-2xx, `404`/`412` named explicitly) is the **only** "last resort" that falls through to the pre-existing full teardown+re-POST/HLS-fallback path, completely unchanged from R-a. A pre-play failure still skips straight to that same fallback, exactly as before this cycle — restarting a connection that was never actually live isn't meaningful.
    - **item 2 — the session resource is honored.** The POST's `Location` header (`whepSessionUrl`) is captured and resolved to an absolute URL; `teardownWhep()` (called on every genuine teardown — a fresh `src`/`whepUrl`/`stopped` value, component destroy, or the new `pagehide` listener for a real tab close) fire-and-forgets a `DELETE` to it (`keepalive: true`, not `navigator.sendBeacon` — `sendBeacon` can only ever issue a `POST`, and mediamtx's session resource only accepts a real `DELETE` verb). **Never** sent from the ICE-restart path, which deliberately never calls `teardownWhep()` at all.
    - **item 3 — a stable DTLS fingerprint.** Every `RTCPeerConnection` this player creates passes the browser's one persisted ECDSA certificate (`shared/player/webrtc-certificate.ts#WebrtcCertificateService`, `providedIn: 'root'`, IndexedDB-backed — see its own API-surface entry below) via the `certificates` option — the same fingerprint across every tile and every reconnect, for server-side viewer correlation, without needing an application-level session/cookie.
    - **item 4 — still one `RTCPeerConnection` per tile.** No multi-track/SFU work; unchanged.
  - **Indefinite self-recovery**, driven by `player-recovery.ts`'s pure state machine (`reduceRecovery`, wrapped by `reduceTransportRecovery` for the transport decision — see that module's own entry below) rather than ad hoc component state: before ever having played, a missing playlist (a fresh stream's normal 404-until-muxed) stays a gentle `waiting` retried on a fixed `COLD_START_RETRY_DELAY_MS` (1.5s) cadence — "no error flash on fresh streams". Once attached, a **stall watchdog** (`isStalled`, no fragment/segment progress for `STALL_WATCHDOG_MS` = 8s — reset on every hls.js `FRAG_BUFFERED`/native `progress` event, or, for WHEP, real `getStats()` decode/receive progress — see below) or any other fatal hls.js/native/WHEP error moves to `reconnecting`: destroy the current attachment (HLS or WHEP, whichever is active) and reattach after a **capped exponential backoff** (`reconnectDelayMs`: 1s, 2s, 4s, …, capped at 30s, held there indefinitely — never gives up while `src`/`whepUrl` stay set, including across a backend restart that outlasts any single attempt window). `error` is now reserved for the one truly unrecoverable case (this browser can play neither transport at all) — a fatal error/stall never lands there. The visible state chip (`phase()`, one of `idle`/`connecting`/`waiting`/`playing`/`reconnecting`/`error`) replaces the old plain `state`; its "playing" badge additionally names the live transport and, for HLS once it's noticeably behind, quantifies it (`'live · WebRTC'` / `'live · HLS'` / `'live · HLS · 4.2s behind'` — see `live-edge-logic.ts#behindLiveChipLabel` below, docs/MVP2-PLAN.md §V, V-b). A **"Retry now"** button during `reconnecting` cancels the pending backoff and reattaches immediately, on whichever transport is currently active.
  - **HLS live-edge tuning** (docs/MVP2-PLAN.md §V, V-b — `HLS_LOW_LATENCY_MODE`/`HLS_LIVE_SYNC_DURATION_COUNT`/`HLS_LIVE_MAX_LATENCY_DURATION_COUNT`/`HLS_MAX_LIVE_SYNC_PLAYBACK_RATE`/`HLS_MAX_BUFFER_LENGTH_SECONDS`/`HLS_BACK_BUFFER_LENGTH_SECONDS`, named constants with their own doc-comment block right above `beginAttach`): `lowLatencyMode: true` (unchanged) plus **segment-count**, not duration-based, sync/catch-up tuning (`liveSyncDurationCount: 2`, `liveMaxLatencyDurationCount: 4` — hls.js's own default for the latter is `Infinity`, i.e. it does not forcibly resync a drifted playhead *at all* unless this is set) — segment-count rather than a fixed-seconds target specifically because V-a shrinks segment/part duration server-side as part of this same latency push, and a duration-based target would need re-tuning every time that number changes; a count-based one scales automatically either side of that landing, and works identically against a stock (non-LL) playlist that never advertises `PART-HOLD-BACK` at all. `maxLiveSyncPlaybackRate: 1.5` additionally turns on hls.js's own gentle rate-based catch-up (nudges `video.playbackRate` above `1`, capped at `1.5`, while behind-but-in-range) — this player already had `lowLatencyMode: true` before this cycle but never raised this off its inert default of `1`, so the catch-up mechanism has been silently off until now. `maxBufferLength: 10` (down from hls.js's own `30`s, VOD-sized default) bounds the forward-buffer target sensibly for a player that is never seeked into a past buffer; `backBufferLength: 30` is unchanged, just promoted to a named constant.
  - **Snap-to-live** (docs/MVP2-PLAN.md §V, V-b — `shared/player/live-edge-logic.ts#shouldSnapToLive`/`SnapToLiveReason`): after a recovery reattach reaches `playing` again (`'recovered'` — read directly off `player-recovery.ts`'s own state, `transportState().recovery.phase === 'reconnecting'`, checked *before* the `firstSegment` event is applied — not a parallel flag) or the tab regains visibility while still `playing` (`'visibilityRestored'`, detected by diffing `document.hidden` on the existing 1s latency-sampling timer rather than a dedicated `visibilitychange` listener — mirrors `core/poll-scheduler.ts`'s own "check `document.hidden` from an existing heartbeat" precedent), the player forward-seeks the `<video>` to `hls.liveSyncPosition` (falling back to the end of the native `seekable` range for Safari's native-HLS path, same fallback shape as `measureBehindLive`) instead of leaving playback wherever its buffer already sits. `'recovered'` always snaps (a reattach's buffer is freshly built from nothing — see the function's own doc comment for why trusting the still-`null` `behindLive` measurement at that exact moment would be pointless); `'visibilityRestored'` only snaps once the current measurement clears `SNAP_TO_LIVE_THRESHOLD_SECONDS` (3s) — hysteresis against reseeking a tab glanced away from for under a second. Never called for a `webrtc` transport (WHEP has no seekable buffer to fall behind — `behindLive` is pinned to `0`).
  - **Behind-live chip, honestly, with hysteresis** (docs/MVP2-PLAN.md §V, V-b — `shared/player/live-edge-logic.ts#shouldShowBehindLive`/`behindLiveChipLabel`): the HLS badge only quantifies "…s behind" once `behindLive` clears `BEHIND_LIVE_SHOW_THRESHOLD_SECONDS` (4s), and only stops once it drops back below the *lower* `BEHIND_LIVE_HIDE_THRESHOLD_SECONDS` (2s) — the two-threshold dead band is what stops the chip flickering between `'live · HLS'` and `'live · HLS · 4.1s behind'` as the per-second measurement jitters across one single cutoff. The hysteresis flag (`showBehindLive`, a signal) updates once per latency sample, not on every render, and resets to hidden on every fresh attach (`teardownMedia`) — a new attach starts the judgment over, it doesn't inherit the previous one's verdict.
  - **Deliberately-stopped state** (docs/MVP2-PLAN.md §S, S-b, new): `stopped: input(false)`, checked first in `reattach()` ahead of `src`/`whepUrl` — once `true`, nothing attaches, ever, regardless of what `src`/`whepUrl` say (even a stray/late/flapping value from a host still catching up with the backend). Drives the new absorbing `'stopped'` `PlayerPhase`/`RecoveryEvent` in `player-recovery.ts` (see its own entry below) and a calm "Stream stopped" status line — no spinner, no reconnect hint, no Retry-now button. `LivePage`/`AssetDetailPage` are the two callers that wire it (their own Stop action plus "this page watched it go live, then it disappeared from the streams list" — see each page's own `stopped` computed for the exact rule); `WallTile`/`LiveDock` deliberately don't (see the S-b Status entry below for why both are already safe without it).
  - **Client-side detection overlay** (item 6): optional `detections: input<DetectionResult[]>([])` / `boxesMode: input<BoxesMode>('overlay')` draw a `<canvas>` positioned over the `<video>` (`shouldDrawOverlay`/`selectDetectionResult`, `detection-overlay-logic.ts`) — boxes stay pixel-perfect at any video bitrate (unlike the server's burned-in pixels) and are hoverable (a small label+confidence tooltip, hit-tested against the last-drawn box rects on `mousemove`). **Sync**: `selectDetectionResult(results, nowMs, latencySeconds, slackBatches=1)` matches by `capturedAt` against the player's own measured `behindLive` (`0` for a live WHEP track — near-zero latency still picks the freshest batch correctly) — the newest result whose `capturedAt` isn't *ahead* of the estimated on-screen instant (`nowMs - latencySeconds*1000`), plus one observed batch-interval of slack for scheduling jitter, falling back to the oldest available result if every one looks "future" (e.g. right after attach, before latency has settled). `'burned'`/`'off'` both suppress the canvas — there is no server-side toggle to disable burn-in itself (out of scope this cycle, "no server change" per the plan), so the two states differ only in what they claim about intent, not in rendering; see the module's own doc comment. Callers that never pass `detections` simply see the canvas draw nothing — fully additive. **Per-model box hues (docs/OPS-CORE-PLAN.md §Q3b, new)**: `drawBox` colors both the stroke and the label background via `modelHue(detectionModelKey(detection), alphaPercent?)` instead of the old hardcoded `'#4f8cff'`/`'rgb(79 140 255 / 85%)'` literals — see `detection-overlay-logic.ts`'s own bullet below for the color rule. A small **model legend** (`overlayModelKeys`/`showModelLegend`, top-right pill stack, `modelSwatch(key)`) renders only once the batch currently on screen (`overlayResult`, the same `selectDetectionResult` pick mirrored as a `computed`) actually mixes ≥2 distinct model keys — a single-model stream never shows it.
  - `latencyChanged = output<number | null>()` — emits `behindLive` on every sample so a host's info panel (below) can echo the same number rather than re-measuring. `transportChanged = output<Transport>()` — likewise for the live transport.
  - **Real WHEP stall detection via `getStats()`** (docs/REALTIME-PLAN.md Phase R-a item 1 — see §0 there for the measured ~10-11s false-stall churn this closes): before this, `lastFrameAt` was only ever set at attach and in the one-shot `ontrack` handler, so `STALL_WATCHDOG_MS` declared a perfectly healthy WHEP stream stalled ~8-10s after connecting, every time — WHEP tore down and reattached (a fresh `OPTIONS`+`POST`) roughly every 10-11s forever, on every `<vision-player>` instance, primary and secondary tiles alike. Fixed: the existing 2s WHEP watchdog tick (`startWhepWatchdog`/`tickWhepWatchdog`) now also calls `refreshWhepStatsProgress`, which awaits `RTCPeerConnection.getStats()` and refreshes `lastFrameAt` whenever the inbound video track's `framesDecoded`/`bytesReceived` (`player-recovery.ts#extractWhepStatsSnapshot`/`didWhepStatsAdvance`) moved since the previous tick — real evidence of ongoing decode. A genuinely dead feed's counters stop moving, so the watchdog still fires for it exactly as before. Logs (`console.debug`) only on an advancing/stalled *transition* (`Player#whepStatsAdvancing`), never every 2s tick, to avoid console spam on a healthy stream.
- `player-recovery.ts` — the pure state machine above. `PlayerPhase`, `RecoveryState`, `reduceRecovery`, `reconnectDelayMs`, `isStalled`, `COLD_START_RETRY_DELAY_MS`/`STALL_WATCHDOG_MS` are **unchanged** from before U3 — every existing caller/spec keeps working verbatim. **New (docs/MVP2-PLAN.md §L/§U3):** `Transport = 'webrtc'|'hls'`, `TransportRecoveryState {transport, recovery: RecoveryState}`, `initialTransportState(hasWhepUrl)` (starts `webrtc` when a WHEP URL exists, `hls` otherwise), `reduceTransportRecovery(state, event)` — a thin wrapper, not a parallel machine: every event still funnels through `reduceRecovery` for phase/attempt bookkeeping; the one added rule is that a `webrtc` transport failing (`fatalError`/`stalled`/`unsupported`) *before it has ever reached `playing`* falls over to `hls` permanently for this attach lifetime (restarting `hls`'s own recovery at a clean `connecting`), while a `webrtc` transport that fails *after* `playing` at least once instead retries `webrtc` itself with the identical capped backoff — see the function's own doc comment for the exact "never played yet" vs. "played before" check (mirrors `reduceRecovery`'s own `playlistNotReady` precedent). Unit-tested without hls.js/`RTCPeerConnection`/timers/a `<video>` element. **Untouched by V-b** (docs/MVP2-PLAN.md §V) — no new `RecoveryEvent`s were needed; the snap-to-live/behind-live-chip logic V-b adds only *reads* this module's existing state (`TransportRecoveryState.recovery.phase`), it never feeds new events back into the reducer, so this file and its own spec file are exactly as they were after U3, and V-b's own logic + specs live entirely in the new `live-edge-logic.ts`/`live-edge-logic.spec.ts` sibling instead. **New in S-b (docs/MVP2-PLAN.md §S)**: `PlayerPhase`/`RecoveryEvent` gain `'stopped'` — always wins from any phase (`reduceRecovery`'s first check), landing on `{phase: 'stopped', attempt: 0}`, and is then **absorbing**: every other event (`stalled`/`fatalError`/`firstSegment`/`playlistNotReady`/`unsupported`) is a no-op (returns the exact same `state` reference) while `phase === 'stopped'`, except `'attachStarted'`/`'reset'` — "a fresh attach is actually wanted now" — which still escape it. This is the fix for the diagnosed stop-freeze mechanism; see the S-b Status entry below for the full incident writeup and the regression test encoding it. **New in S-c (docs/MVP2-PLAN.md §S) — `PacingState`/`advancePacing`/`cyclePacingDelayMs`/`shouldAttemptWhep`, a further, independent pure state machine bolted on top, closing S-b's own flagged-open gap.** `RecoveryState`/`reduceRecovery`/`TransportRecoveryState`/`reduceTransportRecovery` (including their own `attempt` field/`firstSegment`-resets-it-immediately behavior) are **completely unchanged** by S-c too — every one of the pre-existing specs above still passes verbatim, `attempt` still drives the chip's *local* per-cycle bookkeeping. `PacingState {cycleAttempt, playingSinceMs, lastWhepAttemptAtMs}` is the separate, saga-wide counter `shared/player/player.ts` actually uses for delay/WHEP-retry decisions now — see the dedicated S-c Status entry below for the full three-rule contract and the request-rate math. **New in R-a (docs/REALTIME-PLAN.md Phase R-a):** `WhepStatsSnapshot {framesDecoded, bytesReceived}`, `WhepInboundRtpStatLike` (a small structural type matching the bits of a `getStats()` report entry this module reads — deliberately not the full DOM `RTCStats` union, so it's testable with plain object literals, no jsdom WebRTC shim), `extractWhepStatsSnapshot(stats)` (finds the inbound video RTP entry), `didWhepStatsAdvance(previous, current)` (item 1's stall-detection comparison — see the Player entry above); `attachKey(src, whepUrl, suspended, stopped)` (item 4 — the reattach effect's identity-key builder, extracted from `shared/player/player.ts` so the "same primitive values → same key, regardless of the enclosing object's identity" guarantee secondary tiles lean on is directly unit-tested). **New in R-b (docs/REALTIME-PLAN.md Phase R-b item 1) — `WhepIcePhase`/`WhepIceState`/`INITIAL_WHEP_ICE_STATE`/`WhepIceEvent`/`reduceWhepIce`/`ICE_RESTART_GRACE_MS`, a further, independent pure state machine, sibling to `PacingState` for the identical reason: kept separate from `RecoveryState`/`TransportRecoveryState` rather than bolted on as ad-hoc flags, since an in-place ICE restart must never flip the visible chip out of `'playing'` the way every other failure this file models does.** `'stable'|'grace'|'restarting'` — `'disconnected'` starts the grace phase (a no-op unless `'stable'`), `'graceExpired'` (a no-op unless `'grace'`) or `'failed'` (always, from any phase) moves to `'restarting'` and bumps `restartAttempt`, `'reconnected'` self-heals `'grace'` back to `'stable'` before ever restarting, `'restartSucceeded'` closes `'restarting'` back to `'stable'`. `RecoveryState`/`reduceRecovery`/`TransportRecoveryState`/`reduceTransportRecovery`/`PacingState`/`advancePacing` are **completely unchanged** by this — every existing spec still passes verbatim; `shared/player/player.ts` only ever reaches into that pre-existing machinery as R-b's own explicitly-named "last resort" (a rejected ICE-restart PATCH), unchanged from before this cycle.
- **`webrtc-ice-restart.ts`** (docs/REALTIME-PLAN.md Phase R-b item 1, new) — the pure SDP-fragment logic behind the ICE-restart PATCH, split out the same way `live-edge-logic.ts` was (a sibling to `player-recovery.ts`, aware of it, not grown inside it): `parseSdp(sdp)` (extracts `iceUfrag`/`icePwd` plus per-media `mid`/gathered `a=candidate:` lines — reads `a=mid:` directly rather than assuming array position, since a transceiver's mid is fixed at first negotiation and never changes across a later ICE restart on the same PC), `buildIceRestartFragment(localSdp)` (the `application/trickle-ice-sdpfrag` PATCH body — mirrors mediamtx's own bundled `reader.js#generateSdpFragment`, generalized from one trickled candidate to every candidate already sitting in a completed non-trickle-gathered offer), `applyIceRestartAnswer(currentRemoteSdp, answerFragment)` (splices the server's new answer-side ICE credentials into the current remote SDP for `setRemoteDescription` — mirrors mediamtx's own server-side `replaceICECredentials`, client side; throws on a fragment with no usable credentials, never a silent no-op), `candidatesFromFragment(fragment)` (any candidates mediamtx's own answer fragment carries, ready for `addIceCandidate`), `isPatchFallbackStatus(status)` (200/204 succeed, everything else — 404/412 named explicitly — falls back). **Verified byte-for-byte against mediamtx `v1.19.2`'s own source** (`internal/servers/webrtc/session.go`/`http_server.go`, fetched and read directly, not guessed at from the WHIP/WHEP drafts) — see the module's own top doc comment for the full mapping. mediamtx's own bundled reference WHEP client (`reader.js`) does **not** exercise this path at all (it only ever trickles candidates under the original ufrag and falls back to a full teardown+re-POST on any failure, no grace period) — this player is deliberately more capable, which is exactly why there was no reference client code to copy this flow from; every function here had to be derived from the server's own request-handling logic instead. Unit-tested (19 cases) entirely with plain strings — no `RTCPeerConnection`/jsdom WebRTC shim needed at all.
- **`live-edge-logic.ts`** (docs/MVP2-PLAN.md §V, V-b, new) — the pure logic behind the two behaviors above, unit-tested (16 cases), split out the same way `detection-overlay-logic.ts` was: `SnapToLiveReason`, `SNAP_TO_LIVE_THRESHOLD_SECONDS`, `shouldSnapToLive(reason, behindLiveSeconds, thresholdSeconds?)`; `BEHIND_LIVE_SHOW_THRESHOLD_SECONDS`/`BEHIND_LIVE_HIDE_THRESHOLD_SECONDS`, `shouldShowBehindLive(behindLiveSeconds, currentlyShowing, showThresholdSeconds?, hideThresholdSeconds?)`; `behindLiveChipLabel(transport, behindLiveSeconds, showBehindLive)`. Deliberately *aware* of `player-recovery.ts`'s `Transport` type (imported, not redefined) and of the reducer's phase concept (`'recovered'` as a reason) without becoming a second reducer itself — see the module's own top doc comment for why it stays a sibling rather than growing inside `player-recovery.ts`.
- `detection-overlay-logic.ts` — the pure sync matcher above (`BoxesMode`, `selectDetectionResult`, `shouldDrawOverlay`, `DEFAULT_SLACK_BATCHES`), unit-tested. Untouched by U3 — `latencySeconds=0` (WHEP) was already handled correctly by the existing `latencySeconds !== null && latencySeconds >= 0` check. **Composite-model box hues (docs/OPS-CORE-PLAN.md §Q3b, new)**: `detectionModelKey(detection)` resolves a `Detection`'s color key from its own `label` prefix (`"orion12l:tank"` → `"orion12l"` — cv-service's own composite-mode tagging, `registry.py#detect_composite`), never from `Detection.modelId` (every detection in one composite batch shares the *same* `modelId`, the request's whole comma-joined composite id — proto's `Detection` message has no per-detection model-tag field, so `modelId` can't distinguish members within a batch even though it's on the wire); an unprefixed label (any single-model stream, whichever model) resolves to `DEFAULT_MODEL_KEY`. `modelHue(modelKey, alphaPercent = 100)` is the pure hash→color function: `DEFAULT_MODEL_KEY` always returns the exact original box color (`DEFAULT_BOX_COLOR = '#4f8cff'`, or the byte-identical `'rgb(79 140 255 / 85%)'` at `alphaPercent=85` — a single-model stream sees zero visual change); any other key hashes deterministically (a plain djb2-ish string hash → hue `[0,360)`) into `hsl(hue 90% 65%)` (fixed saturation/lightness, same vivid band as `--accent`), stable across calls/reconnects/reloads with no stateful registry. `distinctModelKeys(detections)` — every distinct key in a batch, first-seen order; `shared/player/player.ts`'s legend gate shows a chip only once this reaches ≥2 (the frame *currently* on screen actually mixes models, not merely "composite mode is configured" — a frame where only one member model detected anything still reads as single-model, matching `DEFAULT_MODEL_KEY`'s own zero-change guarantee). Tests: stability (same key → same color), pairwise distinctness across a sample of keys, the exact default-color/default-fill literals, and `distinctModelKeys`' dedupe/order/single-vs-multi cases.
- `stream-info-panel.ts` — `StreamInfoPanel` (`<vision-stream-info>`, docs/MVP2-PLAN.md §U-info): user-meaning-first replacement for a bare "Source URI / Device id / Stream id / Started" plumbing block, used by `features/live/live.html`'s "Stream" card and `features/asset-detail/asset-detail.html`'s "Stream" card. DI-shares the host's `FleetStore` (root) and `DetectionsStore` (page-provided — every host must list it in its own `providers`, exactly like `TelemetryOsd`/`DetectionsStrip`); takes `deviceId`/`latencySeconds`/**`transport: input<Transport>('hls')`** (docs/MVP2-PLAN.md §L/§U3, new) as inputs, the latter two piped from `<vision-player>`'s `latencyChanged`/`transportChanged` outputs, never re-measured. Renders: a protocol chip + human source description (`core/stream-info-logic.ts#describeSource` — "MJPEG · 192.168.0.107:8080" / "File · flight.mp4 (looped)" / "Simulated · Pattern generator, no camera", never a bare URI, credentials never surfaced here even when the raw `uri` carries them), a live-ticking "Streaming for" duration (`formatDuration`/`sessionDurationSeconds`), **`Transport: WebRTC (WHEP)` / `HLS`** (`core/stream-info-logic.ts#transportLabel`, now honest about which is actually live — no longer a hardcoded `"HLS"`), a latency line (`formatLatency`), a CV status line (`DetectionsStore.status()` on/off + last-detection age — the same two-state derivation `detections-logic.ts#cvStatus` already uses, never a fabricated third "degraded" state, per the explicit "surface honestly, don't invent" directive), a **Copy view link** button (`navigator.clipboard.writeText`, toasts on success/failure), and a collapsed **`<details>` "Technical details"** disclosure holding every raw identifier (device id, URI, stream id, raw `startedAt`) — reachable, never deleted, just not the front door. **Gap, not invented**: resolution/FPS are not shown — no current API DTO (`ActiveStreamResponse`, `DetectionResultResponse`) exposes frame dimensions or a video FPS figure (verified against `vision-api`'s DTOs before writing this), so per docs/MVP2-PLAN.md §U-info's own scoping ("implements what current APIs already serve"), that field is omitted rather than guessed; a future cycle that adds it server-side is where this panel would grow that line. The now-deleted `shared/map/live-dock.ts`'s compact preview never rendered this panel at all (docs/CYCLES-PLAN.md §11's own deviation) but did bind `<vision-player>`'s `whepUrl` input, so that docked preview got WHEP-first playback even without the info panel — its successor, `features/command/asset-panel.ts`'s Video tab, inherits the same omission.
- `live-map.ts`/`.html`/`.css` — `LiveMap` (`<vision-live-map>`), moved here from `features/live/` in docs/CYCLES-PLAN.md §11 (CD-b) when the asset detail page needed the identical single-asset map inset — this codebase has no precedent for one page importing another page's module (see `core/fleet/device-logic.ts`'s doc comment for the precedent this follows), so a shared `shared/map/` home was used instead of a cross-page import, exactly as `core/fleet/device-logic.ts`/`core/fleet/warehouse-logic.ts` did for pure logic. Behavior is otherwise **completely unchanged** from before the move (verified by the unchanged `leaflet-src` chunk size and near-identical `live` chunk size in Status below) — DI-shares whichever `TelemetryStore` instance its host page provides; Leaflet loads via a **dynamic** `import('leaflet')` inside `initMap()` (via `shared/map/leaflet-loader.ts#importLeaflet`), landing in its own lazy chunk (`leaflet-src`, ~38 kB gz); a divIcon drone marker rotated to heading, a breadcrumb polyline of the current usage's trail, a start-point flag, an auto-follow toggle, a switchable base layer (`shared/map/leaflet-loader.ts#MAP_LAYERS`, bound to `SettingsStore.mapLayer`), expand-to-full-pane, and the offline-tile fallback (`tilesOk`).

### `src/app/shared/map/leaflet-loader.ts` — shared Leaflet bootstrap + the switchable map-layer catalogue + the IndexedDB tile cache (docs/CYCLES-PLAN.md §6, §9; docs/MVP3-PLAN.md §C-b)

Extracted from `live-map.ts` when `features/map/fleet-map.ts` needed the identical setup — intra-app DRY (unlike the cross-adapter rule in the Java side of this repo, nothing stops two Angular pages sharing a plain module). `importLeaflet()` (the dynamic `import('leaflet')` + UMD default/namespace unwrap) and `ensureLeafletStylesheet()` (the runtime `<link>` injection, see Gotchas) are shared as before; each host component still does its **own** dynamic `import('leaflet')` call site (inside `initMap()`, called from `afterNextRender`) so the chunking behavior is unchanged — this module only shares the JS logic around that call, not the call site itself, and definitely not the CSS (`::ng-deep` rules targeting Leaflet-rendered DOM are still per-component, duplicated by design; `live-map.css`/`fleet-map.css` each carry their own `.drone-arrow` rule).

**Map-tile cache (docs/MVP3-PLAN.md's Build rules).** Every map view used to re-fetch raster tiles from the network on every load, with only the browser's own ephemeral HTTP cache in between; `mapLayerTileLayer` now builds every tile layer as a small `L.TileLayer` subclass (`cachedTileLayerClass`, via `L.TileLayer.extend(...)` — Leaflet's own subclassing helper, since `@types/leaflet` marks `createTile` `protected` and a plain object literal sidesteps that) whose only override is `createTile`: resolve the tile's bytes through `resolveTileSrc` (an IndexedDB hit serves a stored blob immediately; a miss `fetch`es, renders from the fetched blob, and stores it in the background) instead of a bare `img.src = url`. Landing here — not in each host component — is what makes every map inherit it for free: `LiveMap`, `FleetMap`, `ReplayMap`, and `FlightPlanDialog` all build their tile layer through this one function already (grep-verified, all four call `mapLayerTileLayer` directly), so the Fly cockpit's map inset (which reuses `LiveMap` unmodified) gets the cache without `features/fly/**` knowing this exists at all.

- **No service worker, works over plain `http://`** — the entire reason for this shape (per the plan's own Build rule): a service worker requires a secure context, and a LAN viewer at `http://<ip>:8080` (exactly the field-ops user this feature is for) would silently get no caching at all from an `ngsw`/SW-based answer. IndexedDB and `fetch` both work without a secure context, so this cache works identically for a `https://` demo host and a bare-`http://` LAN one.
- **Key**: `shared/map/tile-cache-logic.ts#tileCacheKey(layerId, z, x, y)` → `"{layerId}/{z}/{x}/{y}"` — per-layer, not just per-coordinate (two layers can tile the identical `z/x/y` slot with completely different imagery, e.g. Standard vs. Satellite over the same location).
- **Cap and eviction**: `TILE_CACHE_MAX_BYTES` = **300 MB**, a byte-size cap rather than an entry-count cap (the plan's own "pick, justify, document") — tile byte size varies a lot by layer (satellite JPEGs vs. dark-mode PNGs can differ 5-10×), so an entry cap would let one layer's actual disk usage balloon for the same "tile count" as another's; a byte cap bounds what field-ops devices actually pay in storage, directly, and 300 MB comfortably covers a multi-layer, multi-zoom session (~8,000-15,000 tiles at a rough 20-40 kB/tile average) without approaching typical browser-imposed IndexedDB origin quotas. `shared/map/tile-cache-logic.ts#planEviction(existing, incomingKey, incomingSize, capBytes)` — pure, unit-tested (7 cases): sorts existing entries oldest-`lastAccessedAt`-first and evicts from that end until back under the cap: **LRU**, not FIFO — reading a cached tile touches its timestamp (`getCachedTile` in `shared/map/tile-cache-db.ts`), so a tile still being panned across stays in even if it was first fetched long ago.
- **IndexedDB glue** (`shared/map/tile-cache-db.ts`, minimal, documented, no dedicated spec — this codebase's standing precedent for imperative browser-API wiring, and jsdom has no IndexedDB to test against regardless): two object stores, `tileMeta` (key/size/lastAccessedAt, no blob) and `tileBlobs` (key/blob) — eviction planning only ever cursors the lightweight `tileMeta` store, never deserializing tile pixel data to decide what to keep; only a confirmed get/put of one specific tile ever touches `tileBlobs`. `getCachedTile`/`putCachedTile` both degrade to `undefined`/a no-op (never throw) when IndexedDB is unavailable or a request errors — a broken cache must never break a map.
- **Offline-grid fallback preserved**: any failure in `resolveTileSrc` (offline, a tile host that refuses a CORS-readable `fetch`, IndexedDB unavailable) falls back to the plain `<img src>` request every map used before this cache existed, so `onStatus(false)` still fires via the image's own error event exactly as before — the pre-existing "tiles unavailable" badges (`LiveMap`/`FleetMap`/`ReplayMap`) are unaffected.
- **Cache on demand only, never bulk-prefetch**: tiles are only ever resolved here in response to Leaflet's own `createTile` calls — i.e. panning/zooming the map already on screen — there is no prefetch loop anywhere in this change, respecting the plan's own tile-usage-policy note.
- **CORS dependency, verified not assumed** (see Gotchas): `resolveTileSrc`'s `fetch(url, {mode: 'cors'})` needs the tile host to answer with `Access-Control-Allow-Origin`; a bare `<img src>` never did. Checked directly against all four current providers (`curl -I`) before relying on this — every one already sends `access-control-allow-origin: *` today. A future fifth layer that doesn't would still render correctly (the `<img>` still loads via `<img src>`'s own no-CORS-needed path once `resolveTileSrc` falls back) — it just wouldn't cache, silently, not a broken map.
- **Tests**: `shared/map/tile-cache-logic.spec.ts` — 9 cases (`tileCacheKey`: 2; `planEviction`: 7, incl. under-cap no-op, multi-entry oldest-first eviction, overwrite-not-double-counted, a single incoming tile alone exceeding the cap, empty cache, the default cap).

**Map layers (docs/CYCLES-PLAN.md §9, CU-b item 6).** `MAP_LAYERS: readonly MapLayerDef[]` — four entries (`MapLayerId` from `core/settings/settings-store.ts`), each `{id, label, url, attribution, maxZoom}`: **Standard** (plain OSM raster), **Night** (CARTO Dark Matter — the app's default, and the real-dark-tiles replacement for what used to be an always-on CSS `invert()` filter over OSM; that filter and its `.vision-tiles`/`DARK_TILE_CLASS` are gone), **Relief** (OpenTopoMap, contour shading), **Satellite** (Esri World Imagery). `mapLayerDef(id)` looks one up, falling back to `standard` for anything unrecognized (a stale persisted value, say). `mapLayerTileLayer(L, layerId, onStatus)` (replacing the old `darkTileLayer`) builds a fresh `Leaflet.TileLayer` for one layer, wiring `tileerror`/`load` into `onStatus` same as before. Both `LiveMap` and `FleetMap` own a `tileLayer` field, swap it (`remove()` old, `addTo(map)` new) in an `applyLayer(layerId)` method driven by an `effect()` on `SettingsStore.mapLayer` — Leaflet's attribution control follows whichever instance is currently added, so switching layers updates the shown attribution automatically, no manual wiring needed. None of the four tile hosts need an API key; they're public tile servers with their own (generous but real) rate limits — fine for this app's traffic, worth knowing if a demo hammers one hard enough to get temporarily throttled.

### `src/app/shared/map/flight-plan-dialog.ts`/`.html`/`.css`, `flight-plan-logic.ts` — the flight-plan map editor (docs/CYCLES-PLAN.md §7, CT-b)
