- `flight-plan-logic.ts` — pure, unit-tested (mirrors `player-recovery.ts`/`detection-overlay-logic.ts`'s split): `EditorWaypoint {latitude, longitude, altitudeMeters: number|null}`, `FlightPlanForm {waypoints, speedMps, routeMode}`. `seedTriangle(home?)` — the demo triangle's own shape/scale (`scripts/demo.sh`'s `TELEMETRY_ROUTE`), computed once as offsets from that triangle's centroid and reapplied near any home point (or `FALLBACK_HOME_POINT`, San Francisco, when none is given) — "sensible defaults prefilled". `addWaypoint`/`moveWaypoint`/`updateWaypointAltitude`/`removeWaypoint` — pure array ops, one per editor action. `parseManualWaypoints(text)`/`formatManualWaypoints(waypoints)` — the `lat,lon[,altM]`-per-line manual fallback, lenient (an unparsable/out-of-range line is skipped, not rejected). `canSavePlan(waypoints)` (≥2, mirrors the wire shape's own minimum) and `buildTelemetryRequest(form): TelemetryPlanRequest | undefined` — the draft → wire-shape serializer (`undefined` below the minimum; `speedMps`/per-waypoint `altitudeMeters` omitted, not `null`, when unset).
- `flight-plan-dialog.ts` — `FlightPlanDialog` (`<vision-flight-plan-dialog>`), a **modal dialog**, not embedded inline — reused verbatim by two unrelated pages (`features/devices/devices.ts`'s Simulate step and `features/map/map.ts`'s "Add a test drone" empty-state action), following the same "no page imports another page's module" precedent as `shared/map/live-map.ts`/`core/fleet/device-logic.ts` (shared `shared/map/` home instead of a cross-page import). Inputs: `homeLatitude`/`homeLongitude` (seed the demo triangle near an already-entered home point), `initialPlan` (re-opening via "Edit flight plan" seeds from the caller's saved draft instead of a fresh seed). Outputs: `saved: output<FlightPlanForm>()`, `cancelled: output<void>()`. Click the embedded mini-map (`shared/map/leaflet-loader.ts#importLeaflet`/`ensureLeafletStylesheet`/`MAP_LAYERS` — the same shared Leaflet bootstrap and layer switcher every other map here uses, bound to the same `SettingsStore.mapLayer`) to append a numbered waypoint marker + dashed polyline preview; drag a marker to reposition (only the Leaflet `dragend` event writes back to the waypoint signal — a live `drag` tick would otherwise fight the drag by rebuilding every marker mid-gesture). Waypoint list below the map: per-waypoint altitude input + Remove button. A collapsed `<details>` "Enter coordinates manually" holds the `lat,lon[,altM]` text fallback (`parseManualWaypoints`/`formatManualWaypoints`). Speed (default `DEFAULT_SPEED_MPS`=12) + route mode select — **loop/bounce/once**, the actual `RouteMode` contract (CT-a), not just loop/once. Save disabled until `canSavePlan`.
- **Devices page wiring** (`features/devices/devices.ts`/`.html`): the Simulate step's telemetry-accepting modes (`direct`/`rtsp`/`testDrone` — gated by the pre-existing `simNeedsHomePoint()`) show a "Flight plan" block (Draw/Edit/Clear, or a summary chip once a plan exists) instead of raw fields; the bare home-lat/lon fields **remain**, demoted into a `<details>` "Home point (used only without a flight plan)" fallback disclosure (CT-b's own spec text: "bare lat/lon home-position fields remain when no route is drawn"). `flightPlan: signal<FlightPlanForm | undefined>` holds the draft; `buildTelemetryRequest(plan)` serializes it into the `telemetry` field of the request only at submit time. `FileSimulateForm` (`features/devices/simulate-logic.ts`) and `TestDroneForm` (`core/fleet/simulation-logic.ts`) each gained an optional `telemetry?: TelemetryPlanRequest` field, threaded straight into `StartSimulationRequest.telemetry` by `buildSimulationRequest`/`buildTestDroneRequest` respectively.
- **Map page wiring** (`features/map/map.ts`/`.html`): the empty-state's original one-click **"Add a test drone"** button is **unchanged** (still the true one-click no-fields path, CU-b item 7) — a **second** button, **"Draw a flight plan…"**, opens the same `<vision-flight-plan-dialog>`; on save it calls `fleet.simulate(buildTestDroneRequest({..., telemetry}))` then `mapStore.refresh()`, mirroring `addTestDrone()`'s own shape. This is the "there it can open the same dialog" half of the task — deliberately additive rather than replacing the fast path, see Status below.

### The former `/map` fleet overview tab (docs/CYCLES-PLAN.md §6, §9; folded into Command by docs/UX-REWORK-PLAN.md §U-c) — `core/map/map-logic.ts`, `core/map/map-store.ts`, `shared/map/fleet-map.ts`

> **docs/UX-REWORK-PLAN.md §U-c: `features/map/**` no longer holds a page.** `MapPage`/`map.ts`/`.html`/`.css` are **deleted** — `features/map/map.routes.ts` is now a bare `{path: 'map', redirectTo: 'command', pathMatch: 'full'}`. Every job this page did (the fleet map, "every asset" rail, docked live preview, the empty-state "Add a test drone") lives in `features/command/**` now — see that section's own entry above for the current shape. `core/map/map-logic.ts`/`core/map/map-store.ts`/`shared/map/fleet-map.ts` are all **unchanged and still exactly as described below** — `CommandPage` is simply their one remaining consumer, in place of `MapPage`. **`shared/map/live-dock.ts` (`LiveDock`) — the one piece this blockquote used to flag as a zero-consumer orphan left in place — is now deleted** (infra+cleanup batch, 2026-07-24, docs/REALTIME-PLAN.md's WHEP re-soak task): confirmed zero remaining references anywhere in `src/` (grep-verified, not assumed) before removal — Command's docked-preview role had already moved to `AssetPanel`'s Video tab, and `MapPage`, `LiveDock`'s only other host, was already gone. The rest of this section is kept as an accurate **reference** for `fleet-map.ts`/`map-store.ts`/`map-logic.ts`'s own current behavior, even though the "written when `MapPage` was still the only page composing them" framing below is now historical.

The referee's tournament overview and the crew's "where is it" view: every asset with a known position on one map, rather than one device at a time like `/live/:deviceId`.

**File layout, since docs/MVP3-PLAN.md §C-c:** `map-logic.ts` (pure logic) lives in `core/map/`, `map-store.ts` (`FleetMapStore`) lives in `core/map/`, and `fleet-map.ts` (the one remaining reusable presentational piece — `live-dock.ts` was its sibling here until the 2026-07-24 cleanup deleted it, see the blockquote above) lives in `shared/map/` — all moved there when the Command dashboard needed to embed this feature's fleet map as a second consumer (before eventually becoming its *only* consumer, docs/UX-REWORK-PLAN.md §U-c) — this codebase's own "move to a shared home once a second page needs it" precedent (see `core/fleet/device-logic.ts`'s doc comment). Every bullet below describes their current behavior, unchanged by that consumer count going 1 → 2 → 1 again.

- `core/map/map-logic.ts` — pure, unit-tested (mirrors `core/telemetry/telemetry-logic.ts`'s split of pure derivation from the injectable that drives it):
  - `bucketForAsset`/`bucketAssets` — derive which of three buckets an asset falls into **from `AssetSummary` alone**: `streaming` (has `lastKnownPosition`, `status === 'STREAMING'`), `offline` (has `lastKnownPosition`, otherwise), `noPosition` (no `lastKnownPosition` at all — including a streaming asset in the brief window before its first positioned sample, see `DefaultStreamService#start`). This is safe because `AssetSummaryResponse#lastKnownPosition` is recomputed server-side from the freshest usage row on **every** request and updates on every telemetry sample, even mid-usage (`DefaultAssetService#toSummary`/`#lastKnownPosition`) — it is already live enough to bucket a streaming asset as plottable before this page's own telemetry poll for it has landed.
  - `windowTrail(trail, max = TRAIL_WINDOW)` / `TRAIL_WINDOW = 60` — keeps only the most recent `max` trail points, so a long-running flight doesn't fill the map with old track.
  - `snapshotFromSamples(samples)` → `AssetTelemetrySnapshot { latest, trail }` — the per-asset analog of `TelemetryStore`'s own `latest`/`trail`, windowed.
  - `buildMarker(asset, telemetry, nowMs)` / `buildMarkers(...)` → `FleetMarker | undefined` — combines a bucketed asset with its (possibly not-yet-arrived) telemetry snapshot into a plottable marker (`undefined` for `noPosition`). A `streaming` asset with no snapshot yet, or whose latest sample carries no lat/lon, falls back to `lastKnownPosition` for *where* to plot it, but still surfaces whatever the latest sample does carry (heading, battery, age) independently.
  - `fingerprintMarkers(markers)` — a cheap string key of asset ids + positions, so `FleetMap` can tell "the plotted set moved" apart from `markers()` merely re-deriving every second (it embeds `sampleAgeSeconds`, ticking on the same 1s clock as `TelemetryStore`'s own age readout) — auto-fit only re-runs `fitBounds()` when this actually changes.
  - `nextAutoFitEnabled(current, event)` — the auto-fit reducer: `'userInteraction'` always disables it, `'recenterClicked'` always re-enables it; idempotent either way.
- `core/map/map-store.ts` — `FleetMapStore` (`@Injectable()`, **not** `providedIn: 'root'` — page-provided like `TelemetryStore`; `CommandPage` lists it in its own `providers` (the former `MapPage` did too, before docs/UX-REWORK-PLAN.md §U-c deleted it), so its pollers start/stop with whichever route currently owns them). Polls `GET /api/assets` every 5s (paused while `document.hidden`, via `PollScheduler`) into `assets`/`buckets`/`markers` signals. **Concurrency cap** (the risk docs/CYCLES-PLAN.md §6 called out): after every asset refresh, `reconcileTrackers` starts a per-asset 2s telemetry poller (one `getAsset()` to find the open usage via `selectOpenUsage`, then `usageTelemetry` polling, also via `PollScheduler`) for every asset newly bucketed `streaming`, and tears one down the moment its asset is no longer `streaming` — `offline`/`noPosition` assets never get a telemetry poller at all. Doesn't reuse `TelemetryStore` itself: that class starts from a *device* id and re-derives the owning asset via `listAssets()`+`getAsset()`; this store already knows the asset id from its own 5s poll, so it resolves the open usage directly with one `getAsset()` per newly-streaming asset instead of repeating that lookup. Also exposes `resolveWatchDevice(assetId)` (one `getAsset()` + `findVideoDevice`, best-effort) and a public `refresh()` (like `FleetStore`'s, mainly so tests don't need to wait on the internal interval).
- `shared/map/fleet-map.ts`/`.html`/`.css` — `FleetMap`, the actual Leaflet rendering (injects `FleetMapStore` from its host's DI — `CommandPage` today, `MapPage` before its docs/UX-REWORK-PLAN.md §U-c deletion — same DI-sharing pattern as `LiveMap`/`TelemetryStore`). Leaflet loads the same way as `LiveMap` (`shared/map/leaflet-loader.ts#importLeaflet`, its own dynamic import, landing in the same shared-but-still-lazy `leaflet-src` chunk); the same switchable base layer as `LiveMap` (`shared/map/leaflet-loader.ts#MAP_LAYERS`, a `.controls.layers` button row bound to the same `SettingsStore.mapLayer`, so choosing a layer on one map is reflected on the other the next time it's visited). Renders one marker per `FleetMarker`: a rotated divIcon arrow + breadcrumb polyline for `live` ones, a small dimmed dot for offline ones, added/updated/removed by diffing against the previous marker set on every `effect()` tick. Popups are raw HTML (Leaflet popups aren't Angular templates) with name, category, a status chip, battery/altitude/age when live, an **Open full cockpit** button (renamed from "Watch"), and — for `live` markers only — a **Preview** button; a single delegated `click` listener on the map container (matching `.watch-btn`/`.preview-btn[data-asset-id]`) drives the `watch`/`preview` outputs. **Docked preview** (docs/CYCLES-PLAN.md §9, CU-b item 5): clicking a `live` marker directly (not just its popup) also fires `preview` — a `leafletMarker.on('click', …)` listener attached once per marker, re-reading `live` from `store.markers()` at click time rather than trusting the closure (a marker can go offline long after the listener was attached). Resolving *which* device to preview is still `FleetMapStore.resolveWatchDevice`'s job (docking, same split as before); the `watch` output itself is just an assetId — `CommandPage`, its sole host today, routes it straight to `/fly?asset=<id>&watch=1` with no device lookup at all (docs/MVP3-PLAN.md §C-c — see that page's own doc comment; the now-deleted `MapPage` used to resolve the same output to `/live/:deviceId` instead — same component, host-specific meaning, unchanged idea). **Zoom control vs. layer switcher (fixed 2026-07-24, infra+cleanup batch):** Leaflet's default zoom control lands `topleft` by construction — the same corner `.controls.layers` (this component's own base-layer segmented control) occupies, so the two used to render stacked on top of each other. `initMap()` now passes `zoomControl: false` to `L.map(...)` and adds one back explicitly via `L.control.zoom({position: 'bottomright'})` — the one corner nothing else in this template claims (Recenter sits `topright`, the offline badge sits `bottomleft`). `shared/map/live-map.ts` had the identical bug (visually confirmed on `/fly`'s cockpit inset) and got the identical fix — any future map added under `shared/map/**` should default its own zoom control to `bottomright` too, or explicitly justify a different corner. **Auto-fit**: fits bounds to every marker on load and whenever `fingerprintMarkers` changes while `autoFit()` is true; every programmatic `map.fitBounds()` call is wrapped in a `suppressAutoFitDisable` flag so the `movestart`/`zoomstart` listener (registered once, in `initMap()`) can reliably tell an actual drag/scroll/zoom-button/keyboard interaction apart from the component's own recentering — only the former calls `nextAutoFitEnabled(current, 'userInteraction')`. The Recenter control (visible/labelled via `autoFit()`) calls `nextAutoFitEnabled(current, 'recenterClicked')` and force-refits immediately, bypassing the fingerprint gate.
- **`shared/map/live-dock.ts`/`.html`/`.css` — `LiveDock` — deleted (infra+cleanup batch, 2026-07-24).** Historical shape, for context on the reuse this section's other bullets still reference: the docked live preview panel (docs/CYCLES-PLAN.md §9, CU-b item 5) — inline `<vision-player>` + a small OSD-style battery/altitude/heading/age summary (from an already-known `FleetMarker`, no second telemetry poller) + a "Watch live"/"Open full cockpit" link to `/live/:deviceId`. Its docked-preview role moved to `AssetPanel`'s own Video tab when Command's §U-c rework shipped (see that section's own doc comment), leaving it a zero-consumer orphan; this cleanup batch grep-verified zero remaining references anywhere in `src/` and deleted all three files rather than leaving it flagged again.
- **The following two bullets describe the now-deleted `MapPage`'s own template/behavior — kept as historical record of what docs/CYCLES-PLAN.md §11/CU-b shipped, not current code.** `features/command/**`'s own MODULE.md section above describes the *current* equivalents (the entity rail, `AssetPanel`'s Video tab, the ported empty state).
  - *Historical*: the right rail listed every asset, not only unpositioned ones (docs/CYCLES-PLAN.md §11, CD-b); `MapPage.railAssets` was `mapStore.assets()`, each row's chip switched on `bucketFor(asset)`, with Watch/Preview/Open actions.
  - *Historical*: `features/map/map.ts`/`.html`/`.css` — `MapPage`. Its own `providers`-scoped `FleetMapStore`; an empty state offering "Add a test drone" (`buildTestDroneRequest`, ported verbatim into `CommandPage`, docs/UX-REWORK-PLAN.md §U-c); `<vision-fleet-map>` + a docked `<vision-live-dock>` + a "No position yet" rail; at most one docked preview at a time; responsive stacking under 900px (`CommandPage`'s own `command.css` has its own, similar breakpoint).

### Detection events — rail, per-asset section, map markers, notifications (docs/MVP2-PLAN.md §E, E-b)

The final MVP2 UI cycle: "a person was seen at 14:03 — where?" E-a's own scoping note carries forward unchanged here — **this feed is detection events only**. `EventPublisherPort`'s generic `Event`s (`PIPELINE_ERROR` etc., the docs/MVP2-PLAN.md §U-info ask) are not exposed by `GET /api/events` (a fire-and-forget/write-only port with no read side, per E-a's own MODULE.md) — no page here fakes an errors feed; the player state chip (docs/MVP2-PLAN.md §V, V-b) remains the connectivity surface, and every events surface below is labeled "detection events" rather than implying it covers pipeline health too.

- **`core/events/events-logic.ts`** — pure, unit-tested (40 spec cases; 61 total across this feature — see the tests bullet below), mirrors `core/telemetry/telemetry-logic.ts`'s split of derivation from the injectable that drives it:
  - **Merge/dedupe/cursor** — `mergeEvents(existing, incoming, maxRetained = MAX_RETAINED_EVENTS = 200)` upserts by id (`incoming` always wins — `EventController`'s `sinceMs` cursor guarantees a repeated id is always a fresher read, never a stale duplicate), re-sorts newest-first by `lastSeen`, trims oldest-first. `advanceCursor(currentSinceMs, incoming)` adopts the newest batch's `lastSeen` (`incoming[0]`, since `EventController` returns newest-first), never regresses; `DetectionEventRepositoryPort#findRecent`'s `sinceInclusive` is genuinely inclusive, so re-requesting the exact cursor instant on the next poll is correct, not an off-by-one risk.
  - **Filtering** — `filterEvents(events, {label?, assetId?})`, `distinctLabels(events)` (alphabetical, backs the Wall rail's label `<select>`).
  - **Human-facing** — `capitalizeLabel`, `formatConfidence` (0..1 → whole-percent string), `relativeTimeLabel(atIso, nowMs)` (reuses `stream-info-logic.ts#formatDuration` rather than a second duration renderer, suffixed " ago"), `describeEventSource(event, devices, streams)` (the streaming device's own name when resolvable via `FleetStore`'s snapshots, else a short id fragment — `DetectionEvent` itself carries no display name).
  - **Navigation** — `resolveEventTarget(event, streams)` → `{kind: 'asset'|'live', id}` or `undefined`: prefers the asset detail page when `assetId` resolved, falls back to the live cockpit when the event's `streamId` is still an active stream (a device with no asset yet can still be watched live), else nothing to navigate to — the Wall rail renders an unresolvable event as a disabled row rather than guessing.
  - **Map markers** — `selectEventMarkers(events, max = MAX_EVENT_MARKERS = 30)`: only events carrying a `position` (most never get one — E-a stamps it only when telemetry was available at open time), capped to the most recent `max` (assumes `events` is already newest-first, true for everything `EventsStore.events()` hands out).
  - **Notification decision** — `shouldNotify({event, alreadySeen, notificationsEnabled, permission, documentHidden})`: `!alreadySeen && event.state === 'OPEN' && notificationsEnabled && permission === 'granted' && documentHidden` — every gate independently necessary, see the function's own doc comment for why each exists. `eventNotificationText(event)` → `{title, body}` for the native `Notification` (e.g. `"Person detected"` / `"87% confidence"`).
- **`core/events/events-store.ts` — `EventsStore`.** The one deliberately-shared singleton this cycle's plan explicitly permitted ("a small shared events-store singleton is acceptable if justified — document cost"), unlike `TelemetryStore`/`DetectionsStore`/`FleetMapStore`'s page-provided precedent:
  - **Why shared, not page-scoped**: three unrelated pages (Wall's rail, the fleet map's event markers, the asset detail page's assetId-filtered fallback) all want the *same* `GET /api/events` feed. A page-scoped instance per consumer (mirroring `TelemetryStore`) would mean re-fetching the full recent-events window on every tab switch (no continuous `sinceMs` cursor) and — worse — would let an already-seen event re-fire a browser notification just because the user happened to navigate between pages in between polls (no continuous "seen id" dedupe set). One long-lived `providedIn: 'root'` instance keeps both continuous across navigation.
  - **Cost, documented per the plan's own ask**: only ever *injected* from lazy route components (`WallPage`/`MapPage`/`AssetDetailPage`, plus `FleetMap` reading the already-activated singleton), never from `app.ts` or any other eager path — so it tree-shakes into whichever chunk the bundler shares across those lazy consumers, not the initial bundle (confirmed: the initial-bundle delta this cycle is +0.27 kB raw, matching only the two new eager `VisionApi` method bodies — see Status below).
  - **`activate()`/`release()` — "O(visible) discipline" (bullet 5)**: a refcounted pair, not `providedIn: 'root'`'s usual "poll from app boot forever" (`FleetStore`'s own posture). `WallPage`/`MapPage`/`AssetDetailPage` each call `activate()` in their constructor and `release()` on `DestroyRef.onDestroy` — the first `activate()` since the last full `release()` triggers an immediate poll and starts the shared 5s cadence; the poll stops entirely the moment the refcount returns to zero. No events poll ever runs while the user is on Devices/Settings/Debug/Live/Replay, however long that lasts.
  - **`ignoreHidden: true` — the one deliberate exception to "pause while hidden" in this app.** Every other poller (`FleetStore`, `TelemetryStore`, `DetectionsStore`, `FleetMapStore`) pauses while `document.hidden` to save battery/network — the right default when the only cost of pausing is "the display is a few seconds stale until the tab returns". This store cannot follow that default: bullet 4's entire premise is alerting the user *while they are not looking*, and a poll that itself paused the instant the tab backgrounds could, by construction, only ever discover new events while already visible — making `shouldNotify`'s own `documentHidden` gate permanently unsatisfiable. So `EventsStore`'s poll registration passes `{ignoreHidden: true}` to `PollScheduler.schedule` (a new opt-in option, see that file's own entry above) and keeps running at the same 5s cadence for as long as ≥1 of the three pages above is mounted underneath — the SPA route doesn't unmount just because the OS-level window/tab loses focus, which is exactly the scenario (a second monitor, another tab, a backgrounded browser) this exists for.
  - **Dedupe** (`seenIds`, a plain `Set<string>` — every id ever observed in a poll response, whether or not a notification fired for it) is what makes `shouldNotify`'s `alreadySeen` gate work across the whole session, not per-poll: an event already returned once (and therefore already shown in the rail, if the tab was visible then) can never fire a delayed notification later just because the tab happened to go background afterward.
  - Errors silently degrade (background enrichment, not a user-initiated action — same convention as every other poller here); a `new Notification(...)` construction failure (some environments accept the permission grant but still throw) is caught and swallowed rather than breaking the poll loop.
- **Wall rail (bullet 1) — `features/wall/wall.ts`/`.html`/`.css`.** A 300px sticky right-hand rail (responsive: moves below the grid under 1000px, mirroring `features/map/map.css`'s own `.rail` breakpoint), newest-first, capped to the 20 most recent (post-filter) via `WALL_EVENTS_DISPLAY_LIMIT` — the full retained 200 live in `EventsStore`, the rail only ever shows a "recent activity" slice. An OPEN event's row gets a pulsing `.dot.live` (the same class/animation `styles.css` already defines for every other "this is happening right now" indicator in the app — no new visual language invented) and an accent left-border (`.event-row.open`); CLOSED rows are plain. Two `<select>` filters (label, asset — options derived from whatever's currently in the feed via `distinctLabels`/a page-local `availableAssets` computed keyed by `describeEventSource`) drive `filterEvents`; empty states are honest and distinct: "no events yet" (feed is genuinely empty) vs. "no events match this filter" (feed has data, the filter excluded all of it). Each row is a `<button>`, disabled when `resolveEventTarget` can't resolve anything (an event with neither a live `assetId` nor a still-active stream) — `(click)="openEvent(event)"` navigates to the asset detail page or the live cockpit per that function's own kind. `WallPage`'s constructor calls `events.activate()`/registers `events.release()` on destroy (bullet 5) plus its own 1s `PollScheduler` clock so `relativeTime()` labels keep ticking between the 5s events poll itself.
- **Asset detail Events section (bullet 2) — `features/asset-detail/asset-detail.ts`/`.html`/`.css`.** A `span-2` card between Telemetry and Usage history, `displayedEvents = computed(() => live() ? streamEventsSignal() : filterEvents(events.events(), {assetId: assetId()}))` — exactly the plan's own two-branch spec: while the asset is actively streaming, a **dedicated per-stream poll** (`pollStreamEvents`, `STREAM_EVENTS_POLL_INTERVAL_MS = 5_000`, calling `VisionApi.streamEvents(streamId)` directly — mirrors `DetectionsStore`'s own per-stream precedent, but inlined as a page-local `PollScheduler` registration rather than a new injectable class, since this page already inlines its asset-poll/clock registrations the same way) gives the precise, complete history for *that* stream; otherwise, the page filters the shared global feed (`EventsStore`, activated/released exactly like `WallPage`) by `assetId` — strictly less precise (bounded by the global feed's own 200-event retention and 50-per-poll limit) but the only option once there's no live `streamId` to ask about directly. Rows reuse the identical `.event-list`/`.event-row` markup/CSS as the Wall rail, minus the click affordance (informational here, not a navigation target — the page is already the asset's own detail view).
- **Fleet map event markers (bullet 3) — `shared/map/fleet-map.ts`/`.css` (moved from `features/map/` in docs/MVP3-PLAN.md §C-c — see that section's own entry below), `features/map/map.ts`/`.html`.** A second, independent Leaflet marker layer (`eventMarkerHandles`, keyed by event id, `zIndexOffset: -100` so they sit visually behind the asset/drone markers) driven by `computed(() => selectEventMarkers(events.events()))` — `FleetMap` injects `EventsStore` directly (root singleton, trivial anywhere) rather than duplicating the activate/release lifecycle; `MapPage`'s own constructor is the one call site that activates/releases it for this page, exactly like `WallPage`/`AssetDetailPage`. **Deliberately excluded from auto-fit** — `fitToMarkers`/the fingerprint-gated re-fit effect still only ever look at `store.markers()` (asset markers), unchanged; a stray old event elsewhere on the map must never yank the fleet view away from where the assets actually are. A small pulsing amber/red dot (`.event-marker-dot`, OPEN pulses via the same `@keyframes` idiom as `styles.css`'s `.dot.live`, CLOSED is static) with a popup: label, state chip, peak confidence, first/last-seen (both `relativeTimeLabel`, computed at popup-build time — not independently ticking between the 5s events poll like the Wall rail's clock-backed one, a deliberate small simplification since a map popup is glanced at, not stared at), and an **Open asset** button when `assetId` resolved (`.event-asset-btn[data-asset-id]`, wired through the same single delegated map-container click listener `watch-btn`/`preview-btn` already use, emitting a new `openEventAsset = output<string>()` that `MapPage.openEventAsset()` forwards straight to its existing `openAsset()`/`router.navigate(['/assets', id])`).
  - **Scoped gap, not a shortcut, updated by docs/OPS-CORE-PLAN.md §Q1**: clicking an event marker still links to the asset detail page, **not** a replay moment — but this is now a deliberate scoping choice, not an unbuildable one. §Q1 gave `core/events/events-logic.ts#resolveReplayDeepLink` enough to resolve event→usage (`AssetDetails.recentUsages` carries the usage window an event's `firstSeen` falls in) — the original "no current API exposes that link" gap this bullet used to record is closed, and `shared/ui/notification-bell.ts`/`features/wall/wall.ts` (below) both use it. This component's own popups stay asset-only regardless: they're raw HTML driven by one delegated map-container click listener (see `fleet-map.ts`'s own class doc comment), not a natural place to host an async `VisionApi` lookup + router navigation the way an Angular component template already is.
- **Notification opt-in (bullet 4) — `features/settings/settings.ts`/`.html`.** A "Notifications" card, third from the top (before "Detection profile"): one checkbox, `SettingsStore.eventNotifications` persisted. `toggleEventNotifications(checked)` is the **only** place `Notification.requestPermission()` is ever called — turning the toggle **off** never touches the browser permission at all (just this app's own opt-in flag); turning it **on** calls `requestPermission()` when the current grant is `'default'` (most browsers require a direct user gesture for the prompt to appear at all, which a checkbox click is and a background poll is not), and only actually sets `eventNotifications` to `true` if the result is `'granted'` — a `'denied'` browser-level refusal leaves the setting off with an explanatory toast rather than a checkbox that silently reverts with no stated reason. `notificationPermission()` (a plain method, not a signal — this app has no precedent for polling `Notification.permission` for external changes, and a mid-session revoke from browser chrome is a rare, refresh-recoverable edge case not worth a new polling concern) reads `'unsupported'` when the global doesn't exist at all, driving a small explanatory line under the toggle for `'denied'`/`'unsupported'`.
- **Poll cadence summary (bullet 5)**: events poll at 5s while ≥1 of Wall/Map/asset-detail is mounted (`EventsStore.activate`/`release`, `ignoreHidden: true` so it also keeps running while the tab itself is backgrounded — see that class's own doc comment above for why); the asset detail page's *additional* per-stream poll (also 5s) runs only while that specific asset is actively streaming, on top of — not instead of — the shared global poll (bullet 2's page still activates `EventsStore` too, since its own header/other pages/future features may want the global feed's dedupe state warm regardless of which branch `displayedEvents` is currently reading). **Superseded by docs/UX-REWORK-PLAN.md §U-c — see the update paragraph immediately below**: this bullet's own "O(visible) discipline"/"no events poll ever runs while the user is on Devices/Settings/Debug/Live/Replay" claim is no longer true of the *whole app*, only of these three pages' own marginal contribution to an already-running poll.

**Update, docs/UX-REWORK-PLAN.md §U-c — events become notifications, and `EventsStore` becomes effectively always-on.** `CommandPage` no longer embeds `<vision-events-rail>` and no longer calls `EventsStore.activate()`/`release()` (see the Command section above) — its old bullet-5 slot in "which pages keep this poll alive" is gone. In its place: **`shared/ui/notification-bell.ts`** (`<vision-notification-bell>`, mounted in `app.html`'s header, next to the live-count/online status chips) is a **fourth consumer that never releases** — `App` is the root component and is only ever destroyed on a full page unload, so this component's own `activate()` (constructor) effectively never pairs with a real `release()` during normal use, matching `FleetStore`'s own "starts at boot, never stops" posture rather than `EventsStore`'s old "O(visible) discipline" one. **This is the intended, necessary consequence of a persistent header bell**, not a regression: an unread-count badge and toast notifications that only worked while the operator happened to be on Wall/Command/an asset page would defeat the entire point. `WallPage`/`AssetDetailPage` still call `activate()`/`release()` too (harmless — the refcount just never returns to zero while the bell is mounted, i.e. always). `FleetMap`'s own event markers (unchanged — it injects `EventsStore` directly, never activates it itself) now simply read a feed the bell keeps warm everywhere, rather than one a page used to keep warm only on its own route.
- **The bell itself** (`shared/ui/notification-bell.ts`) has two independent id-tracking sets, deliberately not one: `readIds` (a signal, replaced wholesale with every currently-listed event id whenever the dropdown opens — drives the unread badge, `shared/ui/notification-logic.ts#unreadEvents`) and `toastedIds` (a plain mutable `Set`, mirrors `EventsStore`'s own private `seenIds` precedent — marks an id "already toasted" so a still-OPEN event's `lastSeen` advancing on a later poll doesn't re-toast it, `notification-logic.ts#newlyOpenedEvents`). The very first read after mount seeds `toastedIds` with whatever's already in the feed **without** toasting any of it (history, not news) — only genuinely new `OPEN` arrivals on a *later* poll toast. Opening the dropdown does not suppress future toasts, and a toast firing does not mark its event read — independent concerns.
- **Dropdown = `<vision-events-rail>`, unmodified, third host** (see that component's own section above) — "reuse events-rail's row rendering by moving it into the dropdown", read literally: the whole component moves in as `class="bell-dropdown"` (mirrors `wall.css`'s `.events-rail`/the old `command.css`'s `.events-rail-embed` "host sizes the tag directly" convention, plus `position: absolute`/`box-shadow` since this host is a popover). A native `<details>`/`<summary>` disclosure (`app.css`'s `.tab-more` idiom, this app's third consumer of that exact shape after `.tab-more-menu`/`.kebab-menu`) — `(toggle)="onToggle(bell.open)"` is what marks everything read; the row click (`(open)`) sets `details.open = false` then resolves/navigates.
- **Event → replay deep link (docs/OPS-CORE-PLAN.md §Q1, new)** — both the bell's row click (`NotificationBell.navigate`) and `WallPage.openEvent` now try `core/events/events-logic.ts#resolveReplayDeepLink` first: a **lazy, click-time-only** `VisionApi.getAsset(event.assetId)` (never done per-row on render — this feed can hold up to 200 events, an eager lookup per row would be its own O(N) burst) resolves the covering, **finished** usage (`findCoveringUsage`, over `AssetDetails.recentUsages`) and navigates to `/replay?asset=…&usage=…&t=…`, scrubbed to the event's own `firstSeen` offset from that usage's start; a `resolveReplayDeepLink` miss (no covering usage, or it's still open — see that function's own doc comment for why an open usage falls through rather than linking) or a failed lookup both fall back to the pre-existing `resolveEventTarget` (asset detail / live cockpit) target, unchanged. `shared/map/fleet-map/fleet-map.ts`'s own event-marker popup "Details" button is **deliberately not wired to this** — it stays asset-only, a scoping call documented on that component's own class doc comment (threading `VisionApi`/router resolution into imperative popup HTML was judged disproportionate for this batch).
- **Geofence breach toasts (docs/OPS-CORE-PLAN.md §G-c, new)** — a *second*, independent feed and dedup set inside this same component: `LiveStore.liveEvents()` (the generic `event` SSE topic, **not** this bell's own `DetectionEvent` list — see `core/geofence/**`'s own section above for why) is scanned by a second `effect()`, seeded silently on first read (mirrors the detection-event toast effect's own "history, not news" rule) and then toasting `core/geofence/geofence-logic.ts#geofenceBreachToastMessage(event)` via `ToastService.error(...)` (red, matching the plan's own "icon/severity red" ask) for every genuinely new `enter` breach — an `exit` never toasts (relief isn't alert-worthy). Deliberately **not** folded into the unread-badge count or the dropdown list itself (both are typed to `DetectionEvent`, and a breach isn't one) — a future cycle wanting breaches counted there too would need to widen that typing.
- **Toasts, not native `Notification`s** — a **new**, always-on affordance, independent of `SettingsStore.eventNotifications`'s existing opt-in native-browser-notification toggle (unchanged, still gated on permission + `document.hidden`, see the "Notification opt-in" bullet above): every genuinely new `OPEN` event calls `ToastService.notify(...)` (new method + `'notification'` kind, `core/toast.service.ts` — see its own entry in the API surface section above) with a `{label: 'Watch live' | 'Details', onClick}` action built from the same `resolveEventTarget` every other events consumer uses. No permission/hidden gating on the toast itself — it's this app's own chrome, inert unless the tab is open, so there's no "user isn't looking" case left to gate on (unlike the native `Notification` path, which exists specifically for that case).
- **Cost, measured**: `EventsRail`/`EventsStore`/`core/events/events-logic.ts` are now reachable from `App`'s own eager import graph (`NotificationBell` → `EventsRail`), so that whole chain moved from a shared **lazy** chunk into the **initial** bundle — confirmed via `ng build --configuration production`: initial bundle **309.26 kB → 325.88 kB raw** (+16.62 kB), pushing the pre-existing 300 kB *warning* budget overage from 9.26 kB to 25.88 kB over (still the warning threshold, not the 360 kB error one). This is the direct, unavoidable cost of "a bell in the header" — a header is eager by definition. Documented, not fixed; a future cycle chasing initial-bundle size should know this is where the recent growth came from, not re-diagnose it.

**Wire type / API client additions** (`core/api/models.ts`, `core/api/vision-api.ts`): `DetectionEventState = 'OPEN' | 'CLOSED'`, `DetectionEvent` (mirrors `dto.DetectionEventResponse`: `id`, `streamId`, `assetId?`, `label`, `peakConfidence`, `firstSeen`, `lastSeen`, `state`, `position?` — the latter two doc comments spelled out in `models.ts` itself: `position` is stamped once at open time and never updated, and this DTO is explicitly *not* where pipeline-error events would ever appear). `VisionApi.events(sinceMs?, limit = 50)` → `GET /api/events` (`sinceMs` only added to the query string when defined — the first poll of a session omits it entirely, matching `EventController`'s own "null means no lower bound"); `VisionApi.streamEvents(streamId, limit = 50)` → `GET /api/streams/{streamId}/events`.

<a id="events-tests"></a>Tests: `core/events/events-logic.spec.ts` (40 cases), `core/events/events-store.spec.ts` (14 cases, `VisionApi`/`PollScheduler`/`SettingsStore` all stubbed via `TestBed` provider overrides — a scheduler stub that captures the registered callback lets a test trigger a second poll deterministically without reaching into a private method or juggling fake timers), `core/poll-scheduler.spec.ts` (+2, `ignoreHidden`), `core/api/vision-api.spec.ts` (+3), `core/settings/settings-store.spec.ts` (+2) — 336 → 397 (61 new). Full test-count/bundle-delta table: Status below.

### `src/app/features/debug/**` — the raw API console (WEB-PLAN W5)

Deliberately outside the "no component calls `fetch` directly" rule: this page's entire purpose is issuing raw, possibly-malformed requests and showing the raw failure, so `VisionApi`/`FleetStore`'s typed shapes and single-toast error handling would work against it.

- `debug-api.service.ts` — `DebugApiService` (`@Injectable providedIn: 'root'`), the only thing in this module that touches `HttpClient` outside `core/api/`. `send({method, path, body?})` issues the request with `observe: 'response'`, `responseType: 'text'` (so any body — JSON or not — parses manually downstream) and returns `RawResponse { status, statusText, ms, headers, bodyText, reached }` for both success and error paths uniformly (a non-2xx is not a thrown exception here, it's data). `reached` is `false` only when the request never got a response at all (network failure / status 0). Request body text is sent as parsed JSON when it parses, verbatim otherwise — a malformed body is something this console must let you send, not reject client-side. Not used by any other page.
- `debug-endpoints.ts` — pure, Angular-free: `DEBUG_ENDPOINTS`, one entry per route in vision-api/MODULE.md's controller table (14 total), each with `method`/`path`/`label` and (POSTs only) a pretty-printed `exampleBody`. `prefillForEndpoint(id)` is what the endpoint `<select>` uses to fill method/path/body; path templates carry `{placeholder}` segments through untouched for the user to hand-edit. `methodHasBody(method)` gates the body textarea (POST/PUT/PATCH).
- `debug-response.ts` — pure: `formatResponseBody(text)` pretty-prints JSON with a raw-text fallback (never throws) plus an explicit `(empty body)` case; `isSuccessStatus(status)` for the 2xx badge.
- `debug-history.ts` — pure: `pushHistoryEntry(history, entry, limit = 20)`, newest-first, capped, non-mutating. Backs the last-~20-requests log; clicking a row re-fills the form (`DebugPage.replay()`) but does not re-send.
- `debug.ts`/`.html`/`.css` — `DebugPage`, three cards: the console above; Health (`GET /actuator/health` parsed for `status`/`components[].status`, up/down chips, raw JSON in a `<details>`); Last scan (`POST /api/discovery/scan` with `{}`, raw `ScanResultResponse` including `failedMethods`). All three reuse `DebugApiService.send()`.

## Conventions

- Standalone components only, `changeDetection: ChangeDetectionStrategy.OnPush` everywhere, signals for all component state, zoneless (`provideBrowserGlobalErrorListeners()`, no `zone.js`).
- `api/models.ts` is the **only** place wire shapes are declared — when a Java DTO changes, fix this file first, then chase usages (`grep -rn` the changed field name under `src/`).
- HTTP errors are reported once, by the layer that knows what the user attempted (`FleetStore.run()`), never by an interceptor.
- **Diagnostic logging: plain `console.*`, stable per-file prefix, no logging service — plus an NgRx/Redux-flavored action log for `shared/player/player.ts`'s two reducers** (added investigating the "Fly shows only the asset name and an empty box" freeze — this codebase had zero `console.*` calls before that, grep-verified; see the Gotchas entry below for the incident itself). `shared/player/player.ts` logs as `[player]`, `core/fleet/fleet-store.ts` as `[fleet]`, `features/fly/fly.ts` as `[fly]` — each file declares its own `const LOG_PREFIX` near the top rather than a shared logging service (no such service exists, and this app's own "thin, no ceremony" bias argues against inventing one for three call sites). `info` for a normal state transition worth seeing in sequence (source selected, stream start request/response, transport changed), `warn` for a recoverable failure about to retry (HLS/WHEP fatal error, stall watchdog, WHEP ICE failure), `error` for something a person should specifically go looking for (HTML instead of an HLS playlist, WHEP/HLS totally unsupported). Deliberately one line per transition, not a trace of every tick (no per-`OVERLAY_REDRAW_MS`/per-`WATCHDOG_TICK_MS` spam) — a real incident's console should read as a short story, not a wall. **`player-recovery.ts`'s `reduceRecovery`/`reduceTransportRecovery`/`advancePacing` are already pure `(state, action) => state` reducers** (predate this logging, unchanged by it) — `shared/player/player.ts#dispatchRecovery(event)`/`dispatchPacing(event, now)` are now the *only* call sites that feed a reducer result into `transportState`/`pacingState`, and each logs `[action] <event> → phase X→Y[, transport A→B]` / `[action] pacing/<event> → cycleAttempt N→M` at `info` whenever the state actually changes (a no-op dispatch logs nothing) — the console reads like an NgRx DevTools action log (action name, before/after state) without adding `@ngrx/store` as a dependency for three reducers this file already had.

## Gotchas

- **`AssetDetailPage.assetId`'s route-input binding — flagged by R-b as suspicious-but-not-chased-further, fixed by docs/MVP2-PLAN.md §V, V-b.** R-b's own writeup (kept below, for the record) correctly identified the mechanism but stopped short of checking the actual runtime effect; V-b did, with a throwaway `RouterTestingHarness` spec (`navigateByUrl('/assets/probe-…', AssetDetailPage)`, then asserted on `instance.assetId()`, deleted again once confirmed — not part of this app's permanent suite, since this app has no other page-component-level specs to fold it into): **the mismatch was not inert.** `withComponentInputBinding()` called `setInput('assetId', undefined)` on every activation exactly as predicted, `assetId()` read back as `undefined`, and the page's own `effect()` called `this.api.getAsset(undefined)` — which Angular's `HttpClient` happily serializes to a `GET /api/assets/undefined` request. Concretely, **every real navigation to the asset detail page (Devices list "Open", Map rail "Open") hit the "Unknown asset" empty state instead of the actual asset**, unconditionally, regardless of whether the asset existed — the page was not silently degraded, it was **fully broken** for its one real entry point. The fix: `app.routes.ts`'s path segment renamed `assets/:id` → `assets/:assetId`, matching `AssetDetailPage.assetId`'s own public input name exactly (the same fix the replay route already modeled, see the Routes entry above) — the alternative option (alias the input to `id`) was rejected as the less-consistent one, since it would leave `assets/:assetId/replay/:usageId` and `assets/:assetId` naming the same path segment two different ways. No call site needed touching: every `router.navigate([...])` to this route is positional (`['/assets', assetId]`, `features/devices/devices.ts`/`features/map/map.ts`), not param-name-based. Re-ran the same probe spec after the fix to confirm `assetId()` now reads the real value before deleting it. **Original R-b finding, for the record**: "`app.routes.ts`'s `assets/:id` route produces `params = {id: '…'}`, but `AssetDetailPage` declares `readonly assetId = input.required<string>()` (public name `assetId`, no alias) — the two names don't match, so … the binder calls `setInput('assetId', undefined)` on every activation rather than leaving it unset. Whether this is actually inert in practice … wasn't chased further."
- **`proxy.conf.json` must carry a `/hls` entry for `ng serve` playback — its absence looks exactly like a broken player, not a broken proxy.** Before this was added, `ng serve`'s proxy only forwarded `/api`, `/actuator`, `/legacy` — a request for `viewUrl` (`/hls/<streamId>/index.m3u8`, always app-relative, see `api/models.ts#ActiveStream.viewUrl`) fell through to Angular's own dev-server, which answered with the SPA's `index.html` (HTTP 200, `content-type: text/html`) instead of a 404 or an error — indistinguishable from "the app is broken" at a glance, and a plausible-looking 200 response, not an obvious proxy miss. Confirmed live with `curl`: `http://localhost:4200/hls/<id>/index.m3u8` returned the SPA shell before this fix, the real playlist (`content-type: application/vnd.apple.mpegurl`) after. **This alone does not explain a stuck/frozen page** — WHEP is tried first (see the next Gotcha below) and `viewUrl` is only ever the *fallback* target; a working WHEP path plays fine even with this proxy entry missing, and a broken HLS fallback alone degrades to the ordinary `reconnecting`/cold-start-retry UI, not a hang. `shared/player/player.ts` now specifically detects this shape (hls.js's own `Hls.ErrorDetails.MANIFEST_PARSING_ERROR` — a 200 response whose body doesn't start with `#EXTM3U`) and logs `"got HTML instead of HLS playlist — is the /hls proxy configured?"` at `error`, so this doesn't need re-diagnosing from a network tab next time. **`ng serve` must be restarted after editing `proxy.conf.json`** — it's read once at dev-server startup, not hot-reloaded.
- **`/api/live`'s SSE stream needs no `proxy.conf.json` change — verified live against the running dev server, not just assumed** (docs/REALTIME-PLAN.md §4, Phase R-c): the existing `/api` entry already covers it (it's just another path under `/api`), and `ng serve`'s dev-server proxy (`http-proxy`, via `@angular/build`) pipes a proxied response through rather than buffering it whole before forwarding — confirmed with `curl -D - http://localhost:4200/api/live` against the *current* (pre-R-c) backend on `:8080`: the response streamed back immediately as `Transfer-Encoding: chunked` (a prompt 404, in this case, since that backend predates `/api/live` entirely — see the Status entry below for why that's exactly the fallback path this cycle needs), with no proxy-side hang or delay. No `server.compression.enabled` is set in `vision-app`'s `application.properties` either, so there is no gzip-buffering concern to guard against server-side once a real `/api/live` backend is running. Nothing to change here; documented so a future cycle doesn't re-litigate it.
- **A genuinely frozen tab (not just a black video box) — two real bugs, only the second one was the actual cause; recorded in the order they were found so a future reader doesn't stop at the first plausible-looking one.** Reported as "`/fly` shows only the asset name and an empty box"; escalated once a user hit `/live/:deviceId` directly on the jar-served app (`/hls` proxy irrelevant there — no dev server involved at all) and the *whole tab* went unresponsive (Chrome's own "Page Unresponsive" prompt / DevTools Network couldn't even paint), not just the player.
  1. **First finding (real, but not sufficient on its own): an unbounded `hls.recoverMediaError()` retry loop.** `beginAttach`'s `Hls.Events.ERROR` handler called `hls.recoverMediaError()` on every `MEDIA_ERROR` with no counter — hls.js's own docs are explicit that a fatal `MEDIA_ERROR` can re-fire immediately after `recoverMediaError()` if the underlying source is persistently broken, and calling it again unconditionally forever is a tight, allocating cycle. **Fixed** (still in, still correct to keep): `MEDIA_ERROR_RECOVERY_LIMIT` (3) caps consecutive `recoverMediaError()` calls per attach lifetime (reset on real `FRAG_BUFFERED` progress, or `teardownMedia()`'s own reset ahead of the next attach); once exhausted, it falls through to the same timer-based, capped-backoff `scheduleReconnect` path every other fatal error already takes. This is a genuine, worthwhile hardening (hls.js's own guidance says exactly this is required) — but shipping *only* this did **not** close the report: the user reproduced the identical hard freeze immediately afterward, against `/live/:deviceId`, with a plain, healthy, real-camera stream that never hit `MEDIA_ERROR` at all.
  2. **Second finding (the actual root cause): `shared/player/player.ts`'s reattach `effect()` could be re-notified by Angular's own change-detection/effect-flush machinery with every one of its tracked input signals (`src`, `whepUrl`, `suspended`, `stopped`) reading back byte-for-byte identical to the previous run — confirmed with instance/run counters logged live (CDP-profiled against `/live/:deviceId`, a real webcam stream, no dev proxy, no broken source): the same `Player` instance, the same four values every time, hundreds of runs a second, indefinitely.** `reattach()` had no reason to distrust its own inputs and redid the *entire* attach from scratch each notification (`teardown()` — destroying any in-flight `RTCPeerConnection`/`Hls` instance — then a brand-new WHEP POST or HLS load): each cycle alone is cheap, but at hundreds of cycles a second the tab's main thread never got a spare tick for anything else, including its own DevTools/CDP protocol handling (`Runtime.evaluate` and even `Profiler.start`/`Profiler.stop` timed out against the live repro — a genuine main-thread starvation, not merely "slow"). Reproduced identically against both a healthy stream and a deliberately dead one (an RTSP device pointed at a mediamtx path with no publisher) — **not source-health-dependent at all**, and reproduced on both `/fly` and `/live/:deviceId` (both compose `<vision-player>` the same way), ruling out anything Fly-specific. **Root Angular-internals question left open**: exactly *why* Angular re-notifies an effect whose tracked signals didn't change value was not chased further (would need instrumenting Angular's own change-detection scheduler, out of this module's scope) — what's fixed and verified is the *effect*, robustly, regardless of cause. **Fixed**: the effect now computes a plain string key from the four tracked values and skips calling `reattach()` again if the key is unchanged from the last time it actually acted (`Player#lastAttachKey`) — logged at `info` when a re-notification is skipped, so this is visible rather than silently absorbed. Verified via CDP: 30+ second sessions against both a healthy real-camera stream and a deliberately dead RTSP feed, both ending with `Runtime.evaluate` still answering promptly (previously: unresponsive within 1-2 seconds every time, on both cases). **This is the one to remember if "the whole tab froze" resurfaces**: check first whether an `effect()` reading `input()`s is being re-run with unchanged values before assuming the retry/backoff logic downstream of it is the culprit — the backoff logic can be perfectly correct and still not matter if the thing driving it restarts from zero hundreds of times a second.
  - **Transparency added alongside the fix, not just for this incident** (this codebase now roughly follows NgRx/Redux's "log every action and the state transition it caused" idiom, without adding the dependency — `player-recovery.ts`'s `reduceRecovery`/`reduceTransportRecovery`/`advancePacing` were already pure `(state, action) => state` reducers; they just had no visibility into what was actually being dispatched). `Player#dispatchRecovery(event)`/`Player#dispatchPacing(event, now)` are now the **only** call sites that write `transportState`/`pacingState` from a reducer result (the two "fresh saga/cycle" resets in `reattach()`/`beginNextCycle` are a deliberate exception — those replace the state outright rather than reducing the current one, and log their own `[action] attachStarted (new cycle) → transport …` line instead); each logs `[action] <event> → phase X→Y[, transport A→B]` / `[action] pacing/<event> → cycleAttempt N→M` at `info`, but **only when the state actually changed** (a no-op dispatch, e.g. `'stopped'` fired twice, logs nothing) — this is what made this exact bug class (something dispatching far more often than the visible symptom suggested) diagnosable from the console alone next time.
- **`App`'s `LeafletWarmup.schedule()` call threw `NG0203` on every single route, confirmed in the browser console.** `afterNextRender(() => inject(LeafletWarmup).schedule())` called `inject()` *inside* the `afterNextRender` callback, which runs after render completes and is no longer an injection context — Angular throws `RuntimeError: NG0203` there, uncaught, on every page load (verified live via CDP: the very first console message on any route). Harmless to the rest of the app (the error is caught by Angular's own top-level handler, not this component's logic, so nothing downstream broke) but noisy enough to obscure a real incident's own logging — **fixed** by calling `inject(LeafletWarmup)` in the constructor's own injection context and capturing the resolved instance in a `const`, only *using* it (never `inject`-ing again) inside the deferred callback. If a future `afterNextRender`/`afterRender` callback anywhere in this app needs a DI'd service, `inject()` it before the callback is registered, not inside it.
- **The `/live` "stop freezes the app" bug (docs/MVP2-PLAN.md §S, S-b) — root cause confirmed by a live network trace, not just reasoned about.** Every request in the trace *succeeded* (`POST {mediamtx}/{streamId}/whep` → `201 Created`, `GET /api/devices`, `GET /api/streams`, `GET /api/streams/{id}/detections`, repeat) — this was never a hung connection or a synchronous JS loop on the frontend; it was `Player` never being told the stream was *deliberately* stopped, so every time WHEP happened to reconnect even fleetingly, `reduceRecovery`'s existing, correct `firstSegment` handling reset the backoff `attempt` counter to `0` (right behavior for a genuine recovery — wrong outcome against a connection that kept failing again moments later, driven by S-a's own concurrently-traced backend stop-path trouble), so the next retry always used the shortest 1s backoff delay — a steady, unthrottled reconnect cycle that read, from the user's chair, as the whole app freezing. Fixed with three changes, all documented in full in the S-b Status entry below: (1) a new absorbing `stopped` `PlayerPhase`/`RecoveryEvent` that stops the player from attaching at all once a host page knows the stream is gone — the actual fix, independent of *why* the backend was flapping; (2) a `reconnectTimer`-leak fix in `shared/player/player.ts` (`scheduleReconnect`/`handleWhepFailure` now clear any pending backoff timer before setting a new one — a second, complementary source of extra reconnect attempts); (3) an in-flight guard on `PollScheduler` so a slow/hung backend degrades every poller to stale data instead of an unbounded pile of overlapping requests. Don't re-diagnose this as "just a backend issue, nothing to fix here" if it resurfaces — the frontend genuinely amplified it, and the `stopped` state is what makes an explicit Stop hold regardless of backend behavior.
- **Signals compare with `Object.is` — an `effect()` re-firing does not mean anything meaningful actually changed, and re-deriving a plain primitive from a signal every poll tick re-triggers on every tick regardless (docs/REALTIME-PLAN.md Phase R-a item 2, the O(N) telemetry-burst bug).** `FleetStore`/`FlyPage`'s own 5s asset poll (and any other store shaped like it) sets a signal to a **freshly-fetched object** every tick — `Object.is(newAssetDetails, oldAssetDetails)` is always `false` even when every field is byte-for-byte identical, so any `effect()` reading that signal re-runs every single tick, not just when something on screen actually changed. This is easy to miss because it *looks* idempotent at the call site — `if (id) store.track(id) else store.reset()` reads like a no-op on a repeat call — but the store's own `track()` doesn't know "this is the same id as last time", so unless the **caller** checks that (`fly-logic.ts#trackingIdChanged`, mirroring `core/map/map-store.ts#reconcileTrackers`'s reconcile-by-id idiom: compare the *derived primitive id*, never the enclosing object, and no-op the store call when unchanged), every poll tick silently re-runs whatever expensive/stateful thing `track()` does. **The general rule this app now follows**: any `effect()` whose job is "call a store method when some derived id changes" must derive that id as a `string`/primitive and compare it against the last id *it itself* acted on before calling the store — never trust "the signal changed" to mean "the thing I care about changed". `player.ts`'s own `lastAttachKey` guard (e7cdc46, `player-recovery.ts#attachKey`) is the same idiom one level down, inside the component that receives the (already-primitive) inputs rather than the page deriving them.
- **Not every pre-R-c poller has a matching `GET /api/live` topic — verified by reading the actual DTOs, not assumed from the plan's own naming** (docs/REALTIME-PLAN.md §4, Phase R-c — see the dedicated R-c Status entry for the full writeup). The plan's own item 5 names four stores to convert into `LiveStore` projections (`FleetStore`, `TelemetryStore`, `DetectionsStore`, `EventsStore`); only **two** of those four have a topic whose payload actually matches that store's own domain:
  - `telemetry:<assetId>`/`detections:<assetId>` genuinely match `TelemetryStore`/`DetectionsStore` (`TelemetrySample[]`/`DetectionResult`) — these two convert cleanly, see their own doc comments and the R-c Status entry.
  - `fleet`'s payload is `List<AssetSummaryResponse>` (→ this app's `AssetSummary`) — **not** `core/fleet/fleet-store.ts#FleetStore`'s own domain at all, which is `Device`/`ActiveStream` from `GET /api/devices`+`GET /api/streams`, a completely different pair of REST resources. There is no existing store class polling `AssetSummary` as its own single responsibility either (it's fetched ad hoc by `fly.ts`'s own picker refresh, `core/map/map-store.ts`, `features/asset-detail/asset-detail.ts`, `features/devices/devices.ts`) — `FleetStore` is **not** converted, and nothing else was rewired to `LiveStore.fleet` this cycle either, to keep this task's scope to the four *named* stores rather than an open-ended page audit. `LiveStore.fleet` is still exposed (arrives on every connection for free, being always-on) for a future cycle to point one of those ad hoc call sites at.
  - `event`'s payload is `EventResponse` (→ this app's new `LiveEvent`) — the domain's **generic** `Event` (`STREAM_STARTED`/`DEVICE_ONLINE`/`DEVICE_OFFLINE`/`PIPELINE_ERROR`/`DETECTION`/`TRAINING`), not `DetectionEvent` (the debounced, `OPEN`/`CLOSED`, `peakConfidence`-carrying kind `GET /api/events` and `core/events/events-store.ts#EventsStore` serve — see that interface's own doc comment in `models.ts`, which already documented, *before* R-c, that the generic `Event` has no read side at all). **There is still no SSE topic — or REST endpoint — carrying `DetectionEvent`s.** `EventsStore` is **not** converted; it keeps polling `GET /api/events` completely unchanged. `LiveStore.liveEvents` is exposed anyway (same "arrives for free" reasoning) but nothing in this app reads it yet.
  
  **The practical consequence**: this cycle's actual req/min reduction is telemetry + detections only (per docs/REALTIME-PLAN.md §0's own baseline, the two largest contributors — the `(N+2)`-request telemetry-resolution burst and the 2s detections poll) — the 5s `FleetStore` device/stream poll and the 5s `EventsStore` events poll are unaffected, both **not** named as R-a/R-b's own explicit non-goals either, so this is a genuinely new scope note for whoever picks up a future fleet/events SSE cycle, not a repeat of an already-documented exclusion.
- **`ng test` ≠ `vitest run`.** This project's Vitest specs depend on Angular's zoneless `TestBed` bootstrap, which `@angular/build:unit-test` wires up; invoking `vitest` directly skips that and every spec fails with `TestBed.initTestEnvironment()`/`PlatformLocation` JIT errors. Always use `npm run test:ci` / `npm test` / the Maven `npm-test` execution.
- **WHEP fails for a LAN viewer against the dockerized stack only when `VISION_WEBRTC_HOST` is left unset — this is expected, not a bug, and it's now the operator's choice, not a hard limit** (docs/MVP2-PLAN.md §L, and §V's V-e, which closed this gap after this Gotcha was first written). mediamtx in Docker advertises `127.0.0.1` ICE candidates by default (`MTX_WEBRTCADDITIONALHOSTS: "${VISION_WEBRTC_HOST:-127.0.0.1}"` in `docker-compose.yml`), so with `VISION_WEBRTC_HOST` unset, a browser on a *different* machine than the Docker host still always fails the WHEP exchange and silently lands on HLS via `Player`'s fallback (`shared/player/player-recovery.ts#reduceTransportRecovery`) — this is the entire reason that fallback exists and is treated as load-bearing, not an edge case. Setting `VISION_WEBRTC_HOST` in `.env` to the Docker host's own real LAN IP (see `vision-app/MODULE.md`'s "WHEP viewing" section for how an operator finds it and how to list more than one) makes WHEP work for LAN viewers pointed at that IP too, not just same-host ones. Don't "fix" a LAN demo showing `HLS` in the transport chip without first checking whether `VISION_WEBRTC_HOST` is actually set for that stack — an unset value is still expected same-host-only behavior, not a bug; once it *is* set, only a viewer entirely off that configured LAN/host (a different network, cellular data, a remote friend) should still show `HLS`. This app (`vision-web`) needed no change for V-e — the player's existing try-WHEP-then-HLS fallback already covers both the unset and the off-LAN cases identically; V-e is purely a compose/`.env` configuration lever.
- **`shared/player/player.ts`'s WHEP path gathers ICE non-trickle** (`waitForIceGatheringComplete`, bounded to `ICE_GATHERING_TIMEOUT_MS`=3s) rather than implementing WHEP's optional trickle-ICE PATCH exchange — simpler, and irrelevant to the LAN caveat above (a hung/incomplete gather just means a candidate-poor offer, not a different failure mode). If a future cycle needs faster/more-complete ICE negotiation, trickle is the documented gap, not a bug.
- **`shared/map/flight-plan-dialog.ts` is a modal (`position: fixed`, `z-index: 150`)**, not a page section — deliberately mounted only via `@if` in each host page's template (not always-in-DOM-but-hidden) so its own `afterNextRender` (and therefore Leaflet's `import('leaflet')`) only fires once the dialog is actually opened, matching every other map component's "chunk fetched on first use" convention. `150` sits above the toast host (`z-index: 100`) and the app header (`20`) on purpose — a toast fired while the dialog is open would otherwise render behind it; the dialog itself never toasts, so this is mostly theoretical, not exercised today.
- **`DiscoveredDevice.suggestedCategory` stays a bare `string`.** Even though `Category` is now mirrored (docs/CYCLES-PLAN.md §2), discovery's suggestion is still typed as a plain slug, not `Category`/an enum — there is still no page that lists `GET /api/categories` to validate against, so don't tighten this without adding that consumer first.
- **Leaflet's own CSS is loaded at runtime, not imported as a component stylesheet.** `public/leaflet/leaflet.css` is a manual copy of `node_modules/leaflet/dist/leaflet.css` (kept in sync by hand when the `leaflet` version bumps — there is no build step that does this automatically); `angular.json`'s `assets` glob copies it to `dist/leaflet/leaflet.css` like any other static asset. `shared/map/leaflet-loader.ts#ensureLeafletStylesheet()` (shared by both `LiveMap` and `FleetMap` since docs/CYCLES-PLAN.md §6) injects a `<link>` pointing at it the first time a map is created; idempotent, so either component can call it regardless of which one's map is created first. This is deliberate: importing the real file (14.8 kB raw) as a component `styleUrl` would trip the `anyComponentStyle` budget (10 kB error) and, if imported globally instead, would grow the initial bundle — a runtime-injected `<link>` to a plain static asset is invisible to both.
- **`::ng-deep` in `live-map.css` and `fleet-map.css`** targets DOM Leaflet creates itself (tiles, custom marker/popup/flag divIcons) — those elements never get Angular's `_ngcontent-*` attribute because Angular didn't render them, so emulated view encapsulation would otherwise silently no-op those rules. Still the standard, if deprecated, workaround for third-party-owned DOM inside an Angular component; scoped under `:host` to limit its reach. Each component still carries its **own** copy of the small rules that give Leaflet-owned elements their look (e.g. `.drone-arrow`'s geometry/color) — `shared/map/leaflet-loader.ts` shares the *JS* that builds/configures Leaflet objects, but per-component scoped CSS can't be shared the same way, so this one bit of duplication is intentional. `fleet-map.css`'s popup rules also lean on global classes (`.chip`, `.btn`) from `src/styles.css` for the parts that match existing chip/button styling — those aren't `::ng-deep`'d because a *global* stylesheet already reaches Leaflet-owned DOM without needing to. **The `vision-tiles`/`DARK_TILE_CLASS` CSS-invert dark tint is gone** (docs/CYCLES-PLAN.md §9, CU-b item 6) — "dark" is now the real CARTO Dark Matter tile layer (`Night`, the default), not a filter over OSM; don't reintroduce the filter if OSM ever needs re-tinting for some other reason without checking whether a `Night`-style real layer is the better fix first.
- **`allowedCommonJsDependencies: ["leaflet"]`** in `angular.json` — Leaflet ships UMD only (no `module`/`exports` field), so without this the build emits (non-fatal) "not ESM, can cause optimization bailouts" warnings.
- **`InMemoryTelemetryRepository#findByUsage` (vision-app, not this module) returns the *first* `limit` samples of an ever-growing list, not the most recent.** At the sim's 1 Hz, a usage older than ~200 s will make `GET /api/usages/{id}/telemetry?limit=200` keep returning the same frozen earliest window forever, so `TelemetryStore.latest()`/`sampleAgeSeconds()` will appear to go stale permanently past that point in a long-running demo. This is a backend devsupport-adapter limitation (out of `vision-web/**`'s scope to fix), not a frontend bug — worth knowing before blaming the OSD's staleness indicator. `features/asset-detail/asset-detail-logic.ts#groupTelemetryByDevice`/`freshestSample` (docs/CYCLES-PLAN.md §11) read from the exact same `usageTelemetry` response, so the asset detail page's per-device panels/"source: …" caption inherit this identical frozen-window ceiling — not a new bug, the same one wearing a new UI.
- **CDK virtual scroll (`features/devices/devices.ts`, docs/CYCLES-PLAN.md §11) is this app's first actual use of `@angular/cdk`** beyond it sitting in `package.json` unused (it was pinned ahead of time for exactly this — see WEB-PLAN W6). `*cdkVirtualFor` is a classic structural directive (not the newer `@for` control-flow block — Angular has no virtualized `@for` equivalent), so it coexists in the same template as `@if`/`@for`/`@switch` elsewhere on the page without conflict; the one real constraint is that `<cdk-virtual-scroll-viewport>` needs a **fixed height** (`.asset-viewport`, `height: min(64vh, 26rem)`) and becomes its own self-scrolling region — a deliberate, necessary break from this page's otherwise full-document scroll, not an oversight.
- **`shared/map/live-map.ts` used to live at `features/live/live-map.ts`.** Moved in docs/CYCLES-PLAN.md §11 (CD-b) when `features/asset-detail/asset-detail.ts` needed the identical single-asset map inset — see that file's own MODULE.md entry and `core/fleet/device-logic.ts`'s doc comment for the "no page imports another page's module" precedent this follows. If a search for `live-map.ts` under `features/` comes up empty, this is why — it's under `shared/map/`, not any one feature folder (and `pages/` no longer exists at all, per the folder-structure migration note above).
- **`@angular/cdk/scrolling` alone costs ~17 kB raw / ~4 kB transfer of *eager* bundle**, confirmed by isolating it (temporarily stripping the import and its one call site, rebuilding, restoring) — this is what pushes the initial bundle from 284.67 kB (before this cycle) to 301.91 kB, past the 300 kB *warning* threshold (not the 360 kB error one, which stays comfortably out of reach). None of it is `@angular/cdk/scrolling`'s own code appearing in an eager chunk *by name* (grep for `CdkVirtualScroll`/`ScrollDispatcher`/etc. in the initial chunks finds nothing — post-minification identifiers don't preserve those names), so this is very likely the module's own base dependencies (`Platform`, bidi/RTL support) that Angular's build resolves into a shared vendor chunk regardless of how few lazy chunks reference them. This is a deliberate trade-off, not an oversight: docs/CYCLES-PLAN.md §11 item 4 explicitly asks for "CDK virtual scroll on the asset list", and this is the first time this app has used any `@angular/cdk` submodule beyond what shipped inert in `package.json` — the cost is real but the warning margin (1.91 kB over 300, vs. a 60 kB cushion to the 360 kB error) leaves comfortable room.
- **`PollScheduler`'s heartbeat is 1 s (`TICK_MS`, `core/poll-scheduler.ts`).** Every cadence registered via `schedule(periodMs, callback)` should be a multiple of 1,000 ms (every current caller's is: 1s/2s/5s) — a period that isn't lands on the next tick at or after its due time, not exactly on it, which is a few-hundred-ms slip once, not a growing drift (each task's own `nextDueAt` is recomputed from the tick that actually ran it, not from a fixed schedule).
- **`M` toggles the Live page's map inset** (docs/CYCLES-PLAN.md §9, CU-b item 4) via a page-scoped `document` `keydown` listener in `LivePage`'s constructor — ignored while an `INPUT`/`TEXTAREA`/`SELECT` has focus or a modifier key is held, and removed on `DestroyRef.onDestroy`, so it can never fire from another route. Keep that ignore-list in sync if a future page adds more form-field-like custom elements. **`FlyPage` (docs/MVP3-PLAN.md §C-b) has its own, separate copy of this exact idiom**, extended to `M`/`B`/`F`/`Esc`/`?` — not shared with `LivePage`'s, since the two pages' shortcut sets and guard conditions (Fly's is picker-vs-cockpit-gated too) already diverge and a shared "keyboard shortcut registry" abstraction wasn't judged worth building for two five-line listeners.
- **The map-tile cache's `fetch()` reads depend on the tile host answering with a CORS header — verified against all four current providers, not assumed** (`shared/map/leaflet-loader.ts`'s tile-cache section above has the `curl -I` results). A bare `<img src>` (what every map used before this cache existed) never needed CORS at all; reading the response bytes into a `Blob` to store them does. If a future map layer is added pointing at a tile host that doesn't send `Access-Control-Allow-Origin`, that layer still renders correctly (`resolveTileSrc`'s `catch` falls back to a plain `<img src>`) — it just silently never gets cached. Don't add a fifth `MapLayerDef` without checking its own CORS headers first if caching that layer matters.
- **`FlyPage`'s cockpit CSS reaches into `<vision-player>`'s own DOM via `:host ::ng-deep .stage-video vision-player .frame`** (`fly.css`) to drop the player's normally-fixed 16:9 box so the video can genuinely fill the viewport — the first time this app's `::ng-deep` technique (previously only used for Leaflet-owned DOM, see the entry above) has been pointed at another Angular component's own template instead. Safe because the override only changes sizing (`height: 100%; aspect-ratio: unset`), never `object-fit` — the inner `<video>` keeps its own `contain` letterboxing regardless of the frame's shape, so no distortion. If `shared/player/player.ts`'s `.frame` class is ever renamed, this rule silently stops matching (the cockpit would quietly revert to a 16:9-boxed player, not throw) — grep for `.stage-video vision-player` if `player.ts`'s own CSS is ever restructured.
- **`navigator.sendBeacon` cannot send a WHEP `DELETE`** (docs/REALTIME-PLAN.md Phase R-b item 2) — `sendBeacon` can only ever issue an HTTP `POST`; mediamtx's session resource only accepts a real `DELETE` verb (`internal/servers/webrtc/http_server.go`'s router: `case http.MethodDelete: s.onWHIPDelete(...)`, no fallback for a POST-as-delete convention). This is why `shared/player/player.ts`'s `pagehide` handler uses `fetch(sessionUrl, {method: 'DELETE', keepalive: true})` instead — don't "simplify" this to `sendBeacon` without first confirming mediamtx (or whatever WHEP server is in front) grew a POST-based teardown convention; as of `v1.19.2` it hasn't.
- **The WHEP ICE-restart-via-PATCH flow (`shared/player/webrtc-ice-restart.ts`, docs/REALTIME-PLAN.md Phase R-b item 1) was derived directly from mediamtx's own Go source, not the WHIP/WHEP IETF drafts alone** — `internal/servers/webrtc/session.go`/`http_server.go` at tag `v1.19.2` were fetched (`curl` against `raw.githubusercontent.com`) and read in full before writing a single line of the client-side fragment logic; every function there cites the exact server-side counterpart it mirrors. mediamtx's **own bundled reference WHEP client** (`internal/servers/webrtc/reader.js`, served at `<path>/whep` in a browser) does **not** implement this flow at all — it only ever trickles candidates under the connection's *original* ICE ufrag and falls back to a full teardown+re-POST (a fixed 2s `RETRY_PAUSE`, no grace period) on any `connectionState` failure. If mediamtx's own source or bundled client is ever re-checked against a newer tag than `v1.19.2`, re-verify this flow still matches — there is no upstream reference implementation of ICE-restart-via-PATCH to diff against, only the server's own request-handling logic.
- **`await`ing a `setRemoteDescription`/`setLocalDescription` call is not a safe place to assume nothing else has happened yet — a live soak test caught `ontrack` racing ahead of the very next line 100% of the time (docs/REALTIME-PLAN.md Phase R-b follow-up).** `beginWhepAttach` used to call `scheduleWhepNoTrackTimeout()` only *after* `await pc.setRemoteDescription(...)` resolved, on the (reasonable-looking, wrong) assumption that "the timer starts, then either `ontrack` fires and clears it, or it doesn't and fires" — but on a fast/loopback connection, `pc.ontrack` fires as part of the browser *applying* the remote description itself, which can complete (and dispatch the event) before the `await`'s own JS continuation gets a turn to run. The result: a no-track timeout armed *after* the track had already arrived, with nothing left to ever clear it, firing unconditionally 6s later against a perfectly healthy stream — **every single attach**, confirmed at ~90 reproductions with near-zero jitter, tearing WHEP down and re-POSTing every ~7.15s (worse than the pre-plan baseline this entire plan exists to fix). **Fixed** with a per-attach `Player#whepTrackArrived` flag, checked by `player-recovery.ts#isWhepStillWaitingForTrack` at *both* the "should I arm this timer" call site and the timer's own fired callback — not by reordering alone (the call site *was* also moved earlier, but that only narrows the window; nothing stops the same event from firing even earlier still, so the flag check is what actually closes it). **The general lesson, worth remembering for any future WHEP/negotiation code in this file**: never assume an `await`ed WebRTC promise's continuation runs before a *related* event the browser might dispatch as a side effect of that same operation — always re-check actual observable state (a flag, `pc.getReceivers()`, `pc.connectionState`, etc.) immediately before acting on a timer/callback that was armed speculatively.
- `frontend-maven-plugin` provisions a **pinned** Node/npm into `target/node` per the `pom.xml` properties (`node.version`, `npm.version`) — doesn't use whatever Node is on `PATH`.
- `dist/` (Angular build output) is copied verbatim into `target/classes/META-INF/resources` by `maven-resources-plugin` in `prepare-package`; nothing here compiles Java (`No sources to compile` in `mvn` output is expected, not a failure).
- **`AssetDetailPage`'s telemetry-tracking effect re-entered `TelemetryStore.track()` 50–90×/sec — a self-sustaining loop, not paced by the 5s asset poll — confirmed live via CDP against the running `ng serve` (docs/REALTIME-PLAN.md §4 Phase R-c follow-up).** `features/asset-detail/asset-detail.ts`'s telemetry/detections constructor effects called `track()` unconditionally on every run (unlike `fly.ts`'s already-guarded twin — see the "Signals compare with `Object.is`" Gotcha above), so every ~5s `asset()` reference-churn tick re-entered it with the *same* `deviceId`/`assetId`.
