That alone would only have reproduced the slower R-a-class burst — the actual amplification was worse: `TelemetryStore.track()` → `startTracking()`'s synchronous prologue (`teardownTracking()`) reads the store's own private `currentAssetIdSignal` *while the caller's effect is still the active reactive consumer*, so that read gets attributed to the *caller's* effect, not the store's internal one. Once `startTracking()`'s async continuation later writes a real (non-`undefined`) value to that same signal, it re-notifies the caller's effect — with `asset()`/`assetTelemetryDevices()` unchanged (confirmed via a throwaway diagnostic instrumentation: ~130 consecutive re-runs in 3s, `assetRefChanged`/`devicesArrRefChanged` both `false` on all but the first) — which re-enters `track()`, repeating the read+write and closing a loop paced only by how fast the store's two lookups resolve (~30–45ms locally). `/live/:deviceId`/Wall are unaffected only because those callers never pass an `assetId` at all, so that same signal is written `undefined→undefined` every time — a no-op `Object.is` comparison that never re-notifies anything; this is incidental immunity, not a guard. **Reproduced live, not just reasoned about**: headless Chrome via CDP against a real, already-running `/api/live` SSE backend in this environment (`vision.live.enabled` is on here — the R-c Status entry's own "404-fallback" assumption is stale) captured 721 `[live] track/untrack telemetry:<assetId>` console lines *and 721 real PATCH `/api/live/{connectionId}/topics` requests* in 11.5s (62.6/sec) — **this was already a live PATCH storm against a real backend in this environment, not merely a future risk once one exists.** **Fixed, defense in depth at both layers**: (1) caller-side — `AssetDetailPage`'s telemetry/detections effects now guard with `core/telemetry/telemetry-logic.ts#trackingIdChanged` exactly like `fly.ts`'s (`lastTelemetryDeviceId`/`lastDetectionsStreamId` fields), the primary fix, since it stops `track()` from ever being re-entered with an unchanged id at all; (2) ref-counting layer — `TelemetryStore`/`DetectionsStore.track()` themselves now no-op on an unchanged `(deviceId/streamId, assetId)` key (`lastTrackKey`, keyed via the new `core/live/live-fallback-logic.ts#trackSessionKey`), so an unguarded call site (`LivePage`/`WallTile` today; anything added later) can't reopen this class of loop even without its own guard. `trackingIdChanged` moved from `features/fly/fly-logic.ts` to `core/telemetry/telemetry-logic.ts` (re-exported from `fly-logic.ts` for its own existing import site, tests moved with it) — the established "second consumer needs it, move it to core/" precedent (see `groupTelemetryByDevice`/`batterySeverity` above). New tests: `telemetry-store.spec.ts`/`detections-store.spec.ts` each gained an "N successive `track()` calls with the same id — even with no caller-side guard — subscribe live exactly once and never untrack" case (the exact churn class this bug was); `live-fallback-logic.spec.ts` gained `trackSessionKey` coverage. **Verified fixed live**: re-ran the identical CDP repro against `ng serve` after the fix — zero `[live] track/untrack telemetry:...` lines and zero PATCH requests over the same window for an unchanged asset. `ng serve` on `:4200` was left running throughout, untouched (same PID before/after, verified via `ss -ltnp`).
- **`shared/ui/events-rail.css`'s `.event-meta`/`.event-time` flex row let a long, unbreakable device/asset name (e.g. `X2Twitter.com_hzTcxvzE2-narxmk_720p`, a real simulated-source filename) overflow past its own box and visually overlap `.event-time`/`.event-affordance` at Wall's 300px sidebar (`wall.css`).** Root cause: neither the name nor its containing elements had `min-width: 0` (a flex item's default `min-width: auto` is its content's own minimum size — for an unbreakable token, that's its full rendered width) or any `overflow`/`text-overflow` handling, so the text simply rendered past its box rather than shrinking or wrapping. **Fixed**: the row is now two lines — an unshrinkable top line (state chip, label, time, action; all `flex: none` except `.event-label`, which truncates with ellipsis rather than overflowing if a long relative-time string squeezes it) and a full-width `.event-meta` line below for the source name + confidence, so the name gets the *entire* row's width to truncate against instead of whatever `.event-row-top` has left over (confirmed live: at Wall's 300px sidebar the name previously had ~5px available — invisible — vs. ~225px now, comfortably legible before needing to ellipsize). `.event-source` truncates with `overflow: hidden; text-overflow: ellipsis; white-space: nowrap` and carries the full name in a `title` attribute; `.event-confidence` stays `flex: none` so the `%` is never the part that gives way. Verified via CDP screenshots at both Wall's 300px sidebar (name truncates, e.g. `X2Twitter.com_hzTcxvzE2-narxmk…`) and Command's full-width embed (name renders in full) — no overlap at either.
- **Pre-existing, out-of-scope: `shared/map/fleet-map.ts`'s own layer switcher (`.controls.layers`, `top:0.5rem;left:0.5rem`) overlaps Leaflet's own default zoom control (also `topleft` — `L.map(...)` is constructed with no `zoomControl: false` option, `fleet-map.ts`'s own `initMap()`), confirmed live via a docs/UX-REWORK-PLAN.md §U-c verification screenshot of the new full-bleed Command map** (the "Standard" layer button's own left portion renders behind the zoom control's opaque `+`/`−` box). **Confirmed pre-existing, not a regression from this cycle**: `git diff` against `shared/map/**` for this task is empty — this component was read, not modified (out of this task's own file scope: "don't rework fleet-map/live-map themselves") — so this exact collision already existed on the old `/map` page and the old Command dashboard's own embedded map section, unchanged by anything here. Flagged for whoever next touches `shared/map/fleet-map.ts`: either add `zoomControl: false` and a custom-positioned zoom control that respects `.controls.layers`' own corner, or move one of the two controls to a different corner (`L.control.zoom({position: 'topright'})` collides with the Recenter control's own `top:0.5rem;right:0.5rem` instead — whichever corner is chosen needs auditing against *all three* corner occupants, not just two).
- **Doc-comment staleness flagged by an earlier cycle — fixed by the infra+cleanup batch, 2026-07-24.** `shared/map/live-dock.ts` (the file whose own doc comment carried the worst of this staleness — "each host page (`MapPage`, and now `CommandPage`, …) guarantees at most one is ever rendered") is now **deleted outright** (see the `/map` fleet overview section above), so that particular comment is simply gone rather than fixed in place. `shared/map/fleet-map.ts`'s own doc comment ("`MapPage` resolves a clicked marker's `watch` output…", "activated/released here — `MapPage` owns that lifecycle") and `core/map/map-store.ts`'s own doc comment (naming `MapPage` as a co-consumer) were rewritten to name `CommandPage` as the sole current host and, for the events lifecycle, `shared/ui/notification-bell.ts` as the actual owner. `core/events/events-store.ts`'s own doc comment — the most substantively stale of the four, since it described "O(visible) discipline" as this store's defining trait when `shared/ui/notification-bell.ts`'s permanent `activate()` had already superseded it — was rewritten to describe the current always-on-in-practice reality directly (see the Detection events section's own "Update, docs/UX-REWORK-PLAN.md §U-c" paragraph above). Left here as a worked example of the failure mode, not because it recurs: a doc comment describing *why* a design choice was made ages fine; a doc comment asserting *who else does this today* silently rots the moment that "who" changes, with no compiler to catch it.

**Status: C-c done — MVP3 complete.** Every UI cycle docs/MVP2-PLAN.md's own cycle-order table names (U1/U2/U3/R-b/V-b/E-b) has landed, plus the post-MVP2 stream-stability follow-up (S-b/S-c), plus both of docs/MVP3-PLAN.md's own UI cycles (C-b Fly, C-c Command) on top of C-a's backend work. `DeviceType` was removed to match the server-side drop of that enum (categories are now asset-level data); `models.ts` and every consuming page were realigned in that pass. C2 landed the asset-side DTOs (`AssetSummary`/`AssetDetails`/`AssetUsage`/`TelemetrySample`/`GeoPosition`/`Category`) that earlier phases deferred, plus `TelemetryStore` (`core/telemetry/telemetry-store.ts`) and its `TelemetryOsd`/`LiveMap` consumers. C4 added the `StartSimulationRequest`/`SimulationResponse` DTOs, `VisionApi.startSimulation`/`stopSimulation`, `FleetStore.simulate`/`stopSimulation`, an optional `ToastAction` on `ToastService.ok`, and the Devices page's wizard/chip/stop-action (`features/devices/simulate-logic.ts` + its 2 new `vision-api.spec.ts` cases + 17 `simulate-logic.spec.ts` cases, 19 total added that cycle).

**C6** added the `/map` fleet overview tab (route + tab landed right after Wall, `data: { preload: false }`): `features/map/map-logic.ts` (pure bucketing/trail-windowing/marker-building/auto-fit-reducer/fingerprint logic, 27 new spec cases), `features/map/map-store.ts` (`FleetMapStore`, page-provided like `TelemetryStore` — 5s asset poll + a concurrency-capped 2s telemetry poller per *streaming* asset only, 7 new spec cases), and `features/map/fleet-map.ts`/`map.ts` (the Leaflet rendering child + the page composing it with a "no position yet" rail and an empty state pointing at `/devices`). Two things were **lifted out to shared homes** rather than duplicated, both noted in the plan as an explicit choice point:
- `findVideoDevice` (the Watch-target device resolution) moved from `features/devices/simulate-logic.ts` to `core/fleet/device-logic.ts` (3 spec cases, moved as-is) — this codebase has no precedent for one page importing another page's module, so a shared `core/` home was used instead of a cross-page import.
- The Leaflet bootstrap `LiveMap` already had (`import('leaflet')` + UMD unwrap, runtime stylesheet injection, dark OSM tile layer, rotated drone divIcon) was extracted into `shared/map/leaflet-loader.ts` so `FleetMap` could reuse it without copy-paste — intra-app DRY, unlike the cross-adapter rule on the Java side. `live-map.ts` was refactored to call the same shared functions; its behavior (chunking, styling, features) is unchanged — verified by the unchanged `leaflet-src`/near-identical `live` chunk sizes below.

Per-asset telemetry polling is capped exactly as the plan called out as a risk: only assets `FleetMapStore` currently buckets `streaming` ever get a 2s telemetry poller (`reconcileTrackers` tears one down the instant its asset leaves that bucket); `offline`/`noPosition` assets never poll at all, however many of them there are.

- **Deviation from the task's literal wording**: the CV status dot only ever renders two states, `on`/`off` (green/grey), not a third "degraded" state some phrasing of this feature elsewhere in the docs suggests — the task text handed to this cycle explicitly specifies "green when a detection result arrived in the last 5s, grey 'off' when the endpoint consistently returns empty/errors", which is exactly two states derived from one recency check; no third state was invented.
- **Deviations from the task's literal wording**: `Device.state`/`AssetSummary.lifecycle` stayed **required**/**optional** respectively as already shaped in the codebase (not "optional" for `Device.state` as the task phrasing suggested) — nothing consumes `.state`/`.lifecycle` today, so widening `LifecycleState` in place, without touching `Device.state`'s existing required-ness, was the smaller, equally-correct change. Asset cards' "rename" edits both `displayName` and `category` in one form (the plan's own §8 prose says "rename/category edit"). The device row's `DeviceEdit`/`updateDevice` only ever sends `name` today (the UI has no protocol/uri/options editor); the type mirrors the full pinned PATCH shape for API-layer completeness regardless.

**CU-b** (docs/CYCLES-PLAN.md §9 — "fast & simple + map upgrades") landed all 7 items:

1. **One "+ Add source" entry point.** `features/devices/devices.ts`/`.html`/`.css` — the old stacked Register/Discover/Simulate cards collapsed into one progressive flow behind a page-head "+ Add source" button; a `.method-grid` of three tiles (not numbered steps — they're alternative doors, not a sequence) narrows to whichever method's exact old fields. The warehouse table (+ Assets section) is now the tab's only other content. No capability lost — see the `features/devices/**` section above for the full mapping of old card → new flow step.
2. **Background loading.** `app.routes.ts` dropped `data: { preload: false }` from `/map` and `/debug` — every tab is now idle-preloaded via the existing `IdlePreload` (`core/idle-preload.ts`, unchanged itself). New `core/leaflet-warmup.ts#LeafletWarmup` additionally warms the Leaflet chunk on its own idle callback (`App`'s constructor, `afterNextRender`), since Leaflet isn't a route — it straddles the `live`/`map` chunks. Both are plain dynamic imports (`load()`/`import('leaflet')`); neither changes what ships in the initial bundle, only when the already-lazy chunks are fetched. Skeleton-over-spinner was already this app's convention for empty/loading states going into this cycle (`.empty` blocks with a headline + action, no bare spinners on the Devices/Map/Wall lists) — verified, not re-done.
3. **Poll scheduler consolidation** — see the dedicated section below.
4. **Live layout, video-first** — see `features/live/**` above: player alone in `.stage`, everything else (OSD, detections, Source, Detection settings, Map, PTZ) in a collapsible `.side` rail, map inset independently toggleable with the `M` keyboard shortcut.
5. **Map × streams: docked live preview** — see `features/map/**` above (`LiveDock`, `FleetMap`'s `preview` output, `MapPage`'s `dockedAssetId`/`dockedDeviceId`).
6. **Map layers** — see `shared/map/leaflet-loader.ts#MAP_LAYERS` and `SettingsStore.mapLayer` above: Standard/Night/Relief/Satellite, per-layer attribution, persisted, shared by both maps.
7. **Synthetic test drone** — `core/fleet/simulation-logic.ts#buildTestDroneRequest` (the Simulate step's `testDrone` mode) and `MapPage#addTestDrone` (the Map tab's empty-state "Add a test drone" button) both call `fleet.simulate()` with `videoPath` omitted — CU-a's fully synthetic, moving VIDEO+TELEMETRY device, one click, no file.

**Poll scheduler consolidation (item 3) — what merged and why:**
- **Merged into `PollScheduler` (`core/poll-scheduler.ts`, new):** `FleetStore`'s 5s device/stream poll; `TelemetryStore`'s 2s telemetry poll *and* its 1s age-readout clock; `FleetMapStore`'s 5s asset poll, its 1s age-readout clock, *and* every per-streaming-asset 2s telemetry tracker (a dynamic, N-instance case — each tracker registers/unregisters its own `schedule()` call as assets start/stop streaming); `DetectionsStore`'s 2s detections poll and its 1s CV-status clock. That is up to `1 + (1+1) + (1+1+N) + (1+1)` independent `setInterval`s on a busy `/map` tab, collapsed to **one** shared `setInterval` (`PollScheduler`'s own `TICK_MS = 1_000` heartbeat) — every consumer just registers `(periodMs, callback)` and gets called back on its own cadence. `DetectionsStore` wasn't named in the plan's literal item-3 list (`FleetStore`/`TelemetryStore`/`FleetMapStore`) but duplicates the exact same poll+clock idiom `TelemetryStore` already had (it was built to mirror it, per its own MODULE.md history) — merging it too was the obviously-intended reading of "extract the poll-while-visible idiom … into a single shared helper", not a scope add.
- **Minor behavior harmonization, called out explicitly:** the four 1s "age readout" clocks (`TelemetryStore`, `FleetMapStore`, `DetectionsStore` ×1 each) previously ran unconditionally, even while the tab was hidden; routing them through `PollScheduler` means they now pause with everything else while hidden. Harmless — nothing renders a "seconds ago" label while the tab is hidden anyway — and it's the more consistent behavior, not a regression.
- **Deliberately left alone, with reasons:** `ToastService`'s per-toast auto-dismiss `setTimeout` (one-shot, not recurring — nothing to "poll"); `shared/player/player.ts`'s `latencyTimer` (1s, samples HLS latency) and `retryTimer` (one-shot backoff for a missing first segment) — a *playback* measurement/retry concern tied to the video element's own lifecycle, not a "re-fetch from the backend while the tab is visible" poll, and it deliberately does **not** pause while hidden (a backgrounded tab's video should keep buffering); `features/live/live-map.ts`'s `setTimeout` after the expand/collapse CSS transition (one-shot, not recurring); `features/devices/devices.ts`'s `setTimeout` that clears the "highlighted" outline after using a discovery candidate (one-shot UI timing, not a poll); `core/idle-preload.ts`/`core/leaflet-warmup.ts`'s idle callbacks (browser idle scheduling, a different primitive from a recurring visible-tab poll, and each only ever fires once).
- **8 new spec cases** (`core/poll-scheduler.spec.ts`: 2 for the re-exported `shouldPoll` + 6 for `PollScheduler` itself, using `vi.useFakeTimers()` — the first use of fake timers in this suite; every other spec file relies on real short `setTimeout(…, 0)` flushes instead, but a scheduler literally about timing needs to control time to test cadence/pause/unsubscribe behavior without a multi-second real-time test): registration/cadence, multiple consumers on different cadences off one timer, pause-while-hidden without dropping the eventual call, unsubscribe stops calls, one consumer unsubscribing doesn't affect a sibling.

**Deviations from the task's literal wording:**
- Item 1 names the three methods "Register manually · Discover on network · Simulate — file / over RTSP / synthetic test drone" — read as the Simulate *step's* four mode options (`direct`/`rtsp`/`testDrone`/`synthetic`), not a fourth top-level tile; the method picker stayed three tiles, matching "single progressive Add source flow" more literally than a four-tile grid would.
- Item 4's "keyboard shortcut" for the map inset was left unspecified in the plan; `M` was chosen (mnemonic, not already bound to anything in this app — the shortcut is scoped to `LivePage` only, via a listener added/removed with the route).
- Item 6 lists CARTO Dark Matter, OpenTopoMap, and Esri World Imagery by name; all three (plus Standard/OSM) are wired exactly as named, no substitution.
- Item 7's "one click" for the test drone is literal — `buildTestDroneRequest('')` (blank name) needs no form fields filled at all in the Map tab's empty-state button; the Devices flow's `testDrone` mode still surfaces optional name/home-point/autostart fields (consistent with `direct`/`rtsp`'s existing fields), since that entry point is reached via the same form the other simulate modes use.

**QF-2** (docs/UX-QUICKWINS-PLAN.md, one of four parallel quick-fix agents — scope `features/devices/**` + `core/api/vision-api.ts`'s `createAsset` only): Register-manually's protocol field is now a `<select>` of exactly what this build's adapters consume — new `features/devices/protocols.ts#REGISTERABLE_PROTOCOLS` (`rtsp`/`file`/`mjpeg`/`v4l2`/`sim`/`mavlink`, each with a one-line hint + a realistic URI placeholder, verified against each adapter's own RX-side `supports()`, TX-only `FeedTransmitter` halves excluded), plus a `Custom…` sentinel revealing the old free-text input for a future adapter (`protocolSelectionFor` also re-derives select/custom state from a discovery candidate's protocol). The duplicated "Add the simulated source" empty-state button (Assets vs. Advanced) now renders once — Advanced's empty state just points back at "+ Add source". Orphaned-device dead end: a "Create asset from this device" action (toast action after a successful register, and a row action on any Advanced row with `owner` unassigned) opens an inline panel — name + a category `<select>` (`devices-page-logic.ts#deriveCategoryOptions`, derived from categories already in use among loaded assets, falling back to a small hardcoded set mirroring `InMemoryCategoryRepository`'s dev seed only when none exist yet; **not** wired to `GET /api/categories` — out of this cycle's one-method `vision-api.ts` scope) — that calls the new `VisionApi.createAsset` (`POST /api/assets`, mirrors `AssetController#create`/`CreateAssetRequest` exactly; new `CreateAssetRequest`/`CreateAssetDeviceSpec` in `models.ts`). **Documented, deliberate side effect**: `CreateAssetRequest` has no "existing device id" field (`AssetSpec` always registers a *new* `DeviceRegistration`, verified by reading `AssetController.java`/`CreateAssetRequest.java`/`AssetSpec.java`), so `buildCreateAssetRequestForDevice` wraps the orphan's own protocol/uri/capabilities as a new device, and `devices.ts#confirmCreateAsset` archives the original standalone entry right after — no duplicate left behind, told to the user up front in the panel's own copy. `/devices?category=<slug>` pre-filters the asset-first list (`filterAssetListRowsByCategory`, the contract QF-3's readiness tiles drill into), with a "Filtered by category: …" banner + clear-filter action. **22 new spec cases** (12 `protocols.spec.ts` + 10 `devices-page-logic.spec.ts`), devices+protocols suite **62/62 passing**; `tsc --noEmit` clean for every file this scope touched (`shared/player/**` showed transient errors from QF-4's concurrent in-flight edit at verification time — confirmed unrelated by re-diffing against this scope's own files only).

## U2 / CD-b — asset-first Devices + asset detail page + self-recovering player + vector detection overlay (docs/CYCLES-PLAN.md §11)

Landed all six items: (1) asset-first Devices list (Watch · Open · Archive only, raw devices demoted to a collapsed Advanced section); (2) the new `/assets/:id` asset detail page (characteristics, live video, map, per-source telemetry, usage history, Hardware); (3) multi-telemetry grouped by `deviceId` (CD-a) with a freshest-source map marker; (4) CDK virtual scroll on the asset list, plus an explicit written accounting of where this page's own posture is still `O(total)`, not `O(visible)`; (5) `shared/player/player.ts`'s indefinite self-recovery (pure state machine in `shared/player/player-recovery.ts`); (6) the client-side vector detection overlay (`shared/player/detection-overlay-logic.ts`) with a per-tile overlay/burned/off toggle. Also folded in docs/MVP2-PLAN.md **§U-info** (mid-task scope addendum — the plumbing-first "Source" card became `shared/player/stream-info-panel.ts`) since it touched the exact same Live-rail/asset-detail info surfaces this cycle was already rebuilding.

**New/moved files**: `core/fleet/warehouse-logic.ts` (+`.spec.ts`, generic lifecycle logic lifted from `features/devices/devices-page-logic.ts`), `core/stream-info-logic.ts` (+`.spec.ts`), `shared/player/player-recovery.ts` (+`.spec.ts`), `shared/player/detection-overlay-logic.ts` (+`.spec.ts`), `shared/player/stream-info-panel.ts`, `shared/map/live-map.ts`/`.html`/`.css` (moved from `features/live/`), `features/asset-detail/asset-detail.ts`/`.html`/`.css`, `features/asset-detail/asset-detail-logic.ts` (+`.spec.ts`). **Rewritten**: `shared/player/player.ts` (self-recovery + overlay canvas). **Reshaped**: `features/devices/devices.ts`/`.html`/`.css` (asset-first list + Advanced collapse), `features/devices/devices-page-logic.ts` (+`.spec.ts`, kept page-specific view models, added `AssetListRow`), `features/live/live.ts`/`.html`/`.css` (boxes toggle, `StreamInfoPanel`), `features/wall/wall-tile.ts` (`DetectionsStore` + boxes-cycle button), `features/map/map.ts`/`.html` (rail lists all assets + Open), `app.routes.ts` (`/assets/:id`), `core/api/models.ts` (`TelemetrySample.deviceId`).

**Player recovery, concretely**: `reduceRecovery(state, event)` is a pure reducer over `PlayerPhase = 'idle'|'connecting'|'waiting'|'playing'|'reconnecting'|'error'`. Before ever playing, a missing playlist (`playlistNotReady`) stays `waiting`, retried every 1.5s indefinitely (no cap — the old `FIRST_SEGMENT_RETRY_LIMIT=40` gave-up-after-40-tries behavior is gone, replaced by "poll until ready" per the plan's literal wording). Once attached, a stall watchdog (`isStalled`: no `FRAG_BUFFERED`/native `progress` event for 8s) or a genuine fatal error moves to `reconnecting`, which schedules a reattach after `reconnectDelayMs(attempt)` — capped exponential, 1s→2s→4s→…→30s, held at 30s indefinitely. `error` is now reached only for "this browser can play neither native HLS nor hls.js" — never for a transient network/backend hiccup, which is the whole point: the player never gives up on its own while `src` stays set, verified conceptually against "must survive a backend restart" (an outage of any length is just a longer run of 30s-spaced retries, not a terminal state).

**Detection overlay sync, concretely**: `selectDetectionResult(results, nowMs, latencySeconds, slackBatches=1)` estimates the on-screen frame's capture instant as `nowMs - latencySeconds*1000` (`latencySeconds` is `shared/player/player.ts`'s own measured `behindLive`, piped in as an input — never re-measured), then picks the newest of the newest-first `results` whose `capturedAt` doesn't run ahead of that instant, with one observed inter-batch interval of slack for scheduling jitter; falls back to the oldest result if every one looks "future" (the brief window right after attach before latency has settled). Boxes are drawn on a `<canvas>` sized to the video element's own box, mapped through `object-fit: contain`'s letterbox rect (`Player#letterboxRect`) so they land pixel-correct regardless of aspect ratio; hover hit-tests the last-drawn rects for a label+confidence tooltip.

**Multi-telemetry, concretely**: `TelemetrySampleResponse.deviceId` (CD-a, already server-side) means one usage's `GET /api/usages/{id}/telemetry` response can legitimately mix samples from more than one TELEMETRY-capable device. `groupTelemetryByDevice` regroups them by source for the asset detail page's per-device panels; the map, by contrast, deliberately keeps using `TelemetryStore.latest()`/`trail()` (the whole mixed stream, unfiltered) for its marker/trail — since the backend returns samples chronologically regardless of source, the *last* sample in that flat list already **is** the freshest across every source, which is exactly what item 3 asks the marker to show, with zero new store code needed. `freshestSample` (a separate, explicit "max by timestamp across per-device groups" computation, `O(devices)`) backs only the page's "source: <device name>" caption — a transparency nicety, not the map's actual position math.

**Test count: 189 → 247** (58 new — verified: 189 + 58 = 247, matches the delta exactly):
- `shared/player/player-recovery.spec.ts` — 18 cases (every `reduceRecovery` transition including the "never reaches `error` from `fatalError`/`stalled`" invariant over 50 iterations; `reconnectDelayMs`'s cap and non-positive-attempt handling; `isStalled`'s threshold/default).
- `shared/player/detection-overlay-logic.spec.ts` — 9 cases (`selectDetectionResult`'s slack-window matching, null-latency handling, all-results-in-the-future fallback, single-result edge case; `shouldDrawOverlay`'s three modes).
- `core/stream-info-logic.spec.ts` — 16 cases (`describeSource` per protocol including the credential-stripping/default-port-omission cases; `sessionDurationSeconds`/`formatDuration`/`formatLatency`).
- `features/asset-detail/asset-detail-logic.spec.ts` — 6 cases (`groupTelemetryByDevice`, `telemetryDevices`, `freshestSample`).
- `core/fleet/warehouse-logic.spec.ts` — 17 cases, **relocated as-is** from `features/devices/devices-page-logic.spec.ts` (not new tests — the functions moved, the coverage came with them).
- `features/devices/devices-page-logic.spec.ts` — 9 new cases for `buildAssetListRows`/`filterAssetListRowsByArchived` (device count, streaming derivation, watch-target resolution present/absent, lifecycle defaulting), on top of the 15 pre-existing cases that didn't move.
- No page-component-level specs for `devices.ts`/`live.ts`/`features/asset-detail/asset-detail.ts`/`wall-tile.ts`/`map.ts` — consistent with this app's existing precedent (CU-b's own Status entry makes the same call); exercised by the full production build (twice, from clean) and manual verification against this doc.

**Bundle sizes** (`ng build --configuration production`, before → after; before = the working state this cycle started from, i.e. right after CU-b, per that section's own recorded numbers above):
- **Initial total: 284.67 kB raw / 79.78 kB transfer → 301.91 kB raw / 83.94 kB transfer** (+17.24 kB raw / +4.16 kB transfer). **Isolated and verified**: this is essentially all `@angular/cdk/scrolling` — see its own Gotchas entry above. Rebuilding with that one import (and its one call site in `devices.html`) stripped out lands at 284.80 kB / 79.81 kB, i.e. back within a rounding error of the pre-cycle number; restoring it reproduces 301.91/83.94 exactly. This pushes *past* the 300 kB **warning** threshold (was comfortably under it) but stays far short of the 360 kB **error** one — a deliberate trade-off for item 4's explicit "CDK virtual scroll on the asset list" ask, not an oversight. None of this cycle's other new modules (`core/fleet/warehouse-logic.ts`, `core/stream-info-logic.ts`, `shared/player/player-recovery.ts`, `shared/player/detection-overlay-logic.ts`, `shared/player/stream-info-panel.ts`) are `providedIn: 'root'` or otherwise eager — grep-verified absent from every initial chunk.
- **`devices`: 39.04 kB / 9.24 kB → 65.59 kB / 16.00 kB** (+26.55 kB / +6.76 kB — the asset-first list + virtual scroll markup/logic, the Advanced collapse, and `AssetListRow`'s view-model wiring; the Add-source flow itself is untouched).
- **New `asset-detail` chunk: 24.53 kB / 6.47 kB** — the whole new page (characteristics, video, telemetry panels, usage history, Hardware), reusing `Player`/`StreamInfoPanel`/`LiveMap` rather than rebuilding them, which keeps this smaller than `devices`' own growth despite being an entirely new surface.
- **`live`: 23.27 kB / 6.59 kB → 17.37 kB / 4.84 kB** (**smaller**, despite gaining the boxes toggle and `StreamInfoPanel` — `LiveMap`'s own code moved out of this chunk entirely when it relocated to `shared/map/live-map.ts`, now shared with `asset-detail` via one of the small unnamed shared chunks below rather than duplicated into both).
- **`map`: 20.32 kB / 6.07 kB → 20.82 kB / 6.19 kB** (+0.50 kB / +0.12 kB — the rail's all-assets listing + the new Open button; small, since it reuses the same `FleetMarker`/bucket data the rail already had).
- **`wall`: 6.02 kB / 2.26 kB → 7.00 kB / 2.55 kB** (+0.98 kB / +0.29 kB — `WallTile`'s new `DetectionsStore` provider + boxes-cycle button).
- **`leaflet-src`: unchanged at 149.55 kB / 37.58 kB** — `shared/map/live-map.ts`'s move didn't touch Leaflet itself, only where its *host* component's own code lives.
- **`hls`: unchanged at 517.70 kB / 132.37 kB** — `shared/player/player.ts` grew (self-recovery + overlay canvas), but that growth lands in the *host* chunks that import `Player` (`devices`→no, `wall`/`live`/`asset-detail`/`map`'s shared bits), not in the further-lazy `hls` chunk itself, which is hls.js's own code, untouched.
- **`debug`/`settings`: unchanged** (13.96/4.34, 9.77/2.99) — no source in either changed.
- A handful of small unnamed shared chunks (34.83/7.42, 13.18/4.42, 10.95/3.79 kB and several sub-3kB ones) now carry code common to two or more of `wall`/`live`/`asset-detail`/`map` — most plausibly `shared/map/live-map.ts` (shared by `live` and `asset-detail`) and `Player`/`player-recovery.ts`/`detection-overlay-logic.ts` (shared by `wall`/`live`/`asset-detail`/the map dock) — esbuild's automatic chunk-splitting extracts code reachable from more than one lazy entry point into its own chunk rather than duplicating it, exactly the intended behavior; not independently named per route by the build, so attribution here is by elimination rather than exact.

**Deviations from the task's literal wording, with reasons**:
- **Item 6's "burned"/"off" boxes modes render identically** (both simply suppress the client-side canvas) — disabling the *server's* burn-in itself has no API today and is explicitly out of this cycle's scope ("no server change"), so there is no way to make the delivered video pixels show zero boxes; the two states differ only in what they claim about intent (trust the baked-in boxes vs. want no detection UI/hover at all), not in what's on screen. Documented, not silently swept under a single state.
- **Per-tile toggle on the Wall is a single cycling icon button** (`▢`/`▢·`/`▢×`), not a three-button group like Live/asset-detail's — a compact tile has no room for a labeled `Overlay/Burned/Off` button row; the cycling button still reaches all three states, just via repeated clicks with a `title` tooltip naming the current one.
- **The Devices page's asset list gets no new automatic poll.** Item 4 asks for `O(visible)` polling; this cycle interpreted that as "don't make the existing `O(total assets)` fetch (one `getAsset()` per asset, needed for `deviceCount`/`watchDeviceId`) recur automatically on a timer" rather than as "add a poll, but keep its per-tick cost bounded" — an unconditional 5s poll of an O(total) fetch would be a worse scalability posture for "thousands of assets" than today's load-once/mutate/manual-Refresh cadence, given the backend's in-memory repositories (not this cycle's job — P-a/P-b, MVP2-PLAN) are the actual ceiling. The list still refreshes on every mutation and the existing Refresh button, unchanged from before this cycle.
- **"Battery when live" is omitted from the asset-first list's row decoration** (item 1 lists it as a nice-to-have alongside streaming status/device count). Showing it would need a `FleetMapStore`-style concurrency-capped per-streaming-asset telemetry poller at *list* scope — a meaningful new poller class for a decorative list column, when the same information is one click away (Watch, or the asset detail page's own per-device telemetry panels, which is the page whose entire job is telemetry). Streaming status and device count — the two facts genuinely useful for triage from the list itself — are both present.
- **docs/MVP2-PLAN.md §U-info's "dock panels" was interpreted narrowly**: `LiveDock` (the map's docked preview) did not gain the full `<vision-stream-info>` panel — that would need its own `DetectionsStore` instance for a panel that's explicitly a compact, bandwidth-conscious preview (`MapPage` already caps docked previews at one at a time). It keeps its existing compact OSD-style summary; the full user-meaning-first info panel is the Live rail's and the asset detail page's job, both of which this cycle did rework.
- **Resolution/FPS, called out by §U-info as a "video fact" to show**, are not shown anywhere — verified against every current DTO (`ActiveStreamResponse`, `DetectionResultResponse`, `BoundingBoxResponse`) that no endpoint exposes frame dimensions or a video FPS figure; per §U-info's own "implements what current APIs already serve" scoping, this is a documented gap for a future cycle that adds the field server-side, not a guess.
- **§U-info's "usage link"** (alongside the live-ticking duration) is not a clickable link anywhere yet — there is no usage-detail/replay page to point it at (that's R-b, MVP2-PLAN, a later cycle); the duration itself is shown, the link is deferred until somewhere exists for it to go.

## U3 — flight-plan map editor + WHEP-first playback (docs/CYCLES-PLAN.md §7 CT-b; docs/MVP2-PLAN.md §L/§U3)

Two independent features sharing one cycle: (A) a click-to-drop-waypoints map editor for the Simulate wizard's flight plans, replacing raw home-point fields (CT-b); (B) `shared/player/player.ts` tries WebRTC(WHEP) first when a stream's `whepUrl` is present, falling back to HLS automatically on any failure before a track arrives — integrated into the existing `player-recovery.ts` state machine, not built beside it.

**New files**: `shared/map/flight-plan-logic.ts` (+`.spec.ts`), `shared/map/flight-plan-dialog.ts`/`.html`/`.css`. **Reshaped**: `shared/player/player.ts` (WHEP attach path + transport-aware chip), `shared/player/player-recovery.ts` (+`.spec.ts` — `Transport`/`TransportRecoveryState`/`initialTransportState`/`reduceTransportRecovery`, additive only), `shared/player/stream-info-panel.ts` (new `transport` input), `core/stream-info-logic.ts` (+`.spec.ts` — `transportLabel`), `core/api/models.ts` (`whepUrl?` on `ActiveStream`/`StartStreamResult`/`SimulationResponse`; `RouteMode`/`WaypointRequest`/`TelemetryPlanRequest`; `StartSimulationRequest.telemetry?`), `features/devices/simulate-logic.ts` (+`.spec.ts` cases — `telemetry?` on `FileSimulateForm`) and `core/fleet/simulation-logic.ts` (+`.spec.ts` cases — `telemetry?` on `TestDroneForm`), `features/devices/devices.ts`/`.html`/`.css` (Flight plan block + home-point fallback disclosure), `features/map/map.ts`/`.html` ("Draw a flight plan…" button), `features/wall/wall-tile.ts`, `features/map/live-dock.html`, `features/live/live.ts`/`.html`, `features/asset-detail/asset-detail.ts`/`.html` (all four thread `whepUrl` into `<vision-player>`; `live`/`asset-detail` also thread `transportChanged` into `<vision-stream-info>`).

**Flight-plan editor, concretely**: `FlightPlanDialog` is a modal (not embedded inline), reused verbatim from two unrelated pages exactly like `LiveMap`/`core/fleet/device-logic.ts` before it. Opening it seeds `seedTriangle(home)` — offsets derived once from `scripts/demo.sh`'s own demo triangle's shape relative to its centroid, reapplied near whichever home point the caller has (or San Francisco, as a generic fallback) — so hitting Save immediately produces a flyable 3-waypoint loop. Click the map to append a waypoint (numbered marker + dashed polyline, via the shared `shared/map/leaflet-loader.ts`); drag a marker (only `dragend` commits, so mid-drag doesn't fight the gesture by rebuilding markers on every pixel); per-waypoint altitude + Remove live in the list below the map; a collapsed manual `lat,lon[,altM]`-per-line text fallback covers "I'd rather type/paste than click" (CT-b's own spec text). Speed defaults to `12` m/s (the adapter's own default); route mode is the actual three-value `RouteMode` contract — **loop/bounce/once** — not the task brief's "loop/once" paraphrase (see Deviations). `buildTelemetryRequest` serializes the draft to the wire shape only at Save; `Devices`/`Map` thread it into `StartSimulationRequest.telemetry` via the existing `buildSimulationRequest`/`buildTestDroneRequest` builders' new optional `telemetry` form field — no new HTTP call, the existing `/api/simulations` POST just carries more.

**Transport state machine, concretely**: `player-recovery.ts`'s pre-existing `RecoveryState`/`reduceRecovery` (phase/attempt bookkeeping, capped backoff) are **completely unchanged** — every one of the 18 pre-U3 specs still passes verbatim. `reduceTransportRecovery(state: TransportRecoveryState, event)` wraps them with exactly one added rule: a `webrtc` transport failing (`fatalError`/`stalled`/`unsupported`) while its nested `recovery.phase` is `connecting`/`waiting` (i.e. it has never reached `playing`) falls over to `hls` permanently for the attach lifetime, restarting `hls`'s own recovery from a clean `connecting`; the same failure while `recovery.phase` is `playing`/`reconnecting` (i.e. it *has* played before) instead retries `webrtc` itself via `reduceRecovery`'s own capped backoff — "reconnect cycles per transport". This mirrors `reduceRecovery`'s own `playlistNotReady` precedent (`connecting`/`waiting` = "never played, be gentle" vs. everything else = "played before, this is a real reconnect") applied one level up, at the transport-choice layer instead of the phase layer. `shared/player/player.ts` drives this with a parallel WHEP attach path (`beginWhepAttach`/`handleWhepFailure`/`teardownWhep`) alongside the pre-existing HLS one (`beginAttach`, untouched in its own logic, only its recovery calls now go through `reduceTransportRecovery` instead of `reduceRecovery` directly): `RTCPeerConnection`, one recvonly video transceiver, non-trickle ICE (bounded 3s gather), POST the offer's SDP to `whepUrl` (`Content-Type: application/sdp`), apply the answer, wait for `ontrack` (bounded 6s — "no track within a timeout"). `behindLive` is pinned to `0` the instant a track arrives (a real number the detection-overlay matcher needs, not `null`) though the visible badge reads `'live · WebRTC'` rather than a falsely-precise `'0.0s behind'`. A WHEP-specific watchdog (`startWhepWatchdog`, same `isStalled`/`STALL_WATCHDOG_MS`=8s check as HLS's own) covers a later silent stall. `transportChanged = output<Transport>()` lets a host echo the live transport (`StreamInfoPanel`'s new "Transport" fact, `core/stream-info-logic.ts#transportLabel`, no longer hardcoded `"HLS"`).

**Test count: 247 → 288** (41 new — verified: 247 + 41 = 288, matches exactly):
- `shared/map/flight-plan-logic.spec.ts` — 22 cases (`seedTriangle`'s determinism/fallback, add/move/updateAltitude/remove waypoint ops, manual-text parse/format round-trip including lenient skip-bad-lines behavior, `canSavePlan`'s ≥2 minimum, `buildTelemetryRequest`'s omit-when-null/undefined-below-minimum/all-three-`routeMode`-values serialization).
- `shared/player/player-recovery.spec.ts` — 13 new cases on top of the 18 pre-existing, unmodified ones (`initialTransportState`'s webrtc/hls starting choice; `reduceTransportRecovery`'s fallback-before-playing for `fatalError`/`stalled`/`unsupported`, retry-on-transport-after-playing, no-late-fallback-while-already-reconnecting, hls-unchanged-behavior, terminal `error` reached correctly when hls itself is unsupported, and one full WHEP-attempt→fallback→HLS-plays→HLS-reconnects integration case).
- `features/devices/simulate-logic.spec.ts`/`core/fleet/simulation-logic.spec.ts` — 4 new cases (`buildSimulationRequest`/`buildTestDroneRequest` each: omits `telemetry` when absent, includes it verbatim — and, for the file-modes builder, confirms `latitude`/`longitude` still ride alongside a route since the backend ignores them in that case, not this function's job to suppress).
- `core/stream-info-logic.spec.ts` — 2 new cases (`transportLabel` for both transport values).
- No page-component-level specs for `devices.ts`/`map.ts`/`shared/map/flight-plan-dialog.ts`/`shared/player/player.ts`'s WHEP path — consistent with this app's existing precedent (every prior UI cycle's own Status entry makes the same call); exercised by the full production build (twice, from clean) and manual verification against this doc. `RTCPeerConnection`/`fetch`/Leaflet inside a modal dialog would need a much heavier component-test harness (fake media streams, fake ICE) than this app has ever invested in for `Player`'s existing hls.js path either.

**Bundle sizes** (`ng build --configuration production`, before → after; before = U2's own recorded "after" numbers above, i.e. the working state this cycle started from):
- **Initial total: 301.91 kB raw / 83.94 kB transfer → 301.91 kB raw / 83.94 kB transfer — unchanged, to the byte.** Every new module this cycle (`shared/map/flight-plan-logic.ts`, `shared/map/flight-plan-dialog.ts`, the `player-recovery.ts`/`player.ts` WHEP additions, `core/stream-info-logic.ts#transportLabel`) is reachable only from a lazy route chunk or a component those chunks import — none are `providedIn: 'root'` or otherwise eager. `models.ts`'s new fields/types cost nothing at runtime (types erase; the new optional wire fields are just more properties on interfaces `FleetStore`/`VisionApi` already ship).
- **`devices`: 65.59 kB / 16.00 kB → 68.03 kB / 16.52 kB** (+2.44 kB / +0.52 kB — the Simulate step's Flight-plan block/home-point-fallback markup and `flightPlan`/`buildTelemetryRequest` wiring; `FlightPlanDialog`'s own bulk, being shared with `map`, lands in the unnamed shared chunks below instead).
- **`map`: 20.82 kB / 6.19 kB → 21.89 kB / 6.42 kB** (+1.07 kB / +0.23 kB — the "Draw a flight plan…" button + `onFlightPlanSaved`/`onFlightPlanCancelled`).
- **`live`: 17.37 kB / 4.84 kB → 17.63 kB / 4.91 kB** (+0.26 kB / +0.07 kB — the `whepUrl`/`transportChanged` bindings + `transport` signal).
- **`asset-detail`: 24.53 kB / 6.47 kB → 24.80 kB / 6.52 kB** (+0.27 kB / +0.05 kB — same shape as `live`'s delta).
- **`wall`: 7.00 kB / 2.55 kB → 7.04 kB / 2.56 kB** (+0.04 kB — one new `[whepUrl]` binding, no new signal).
- **`leaflet-src`: unchanged at 149.55 kB / 37.58 kB** — `FlightPlanDialog` reuses `shared/map/leaflet-loader.ts#importLeaflet()` exactly like every other map, no new Leaflet APIs.
- **`hls`: unchanged at 517.70 kB / 132.37 kB** — hls.js's own code; the WHEP path needs no library (`RTCPeerConnection`/`fetch` are browser built-ins) and lives in `Player`'s own host chunks, not this further-lazy one.
- **`debug`/`settings`: unchanged** (13.96/4.32, 9.77/2.99).
- The unnamed shared chunks grew from U2's own recorded ~59 kB (34.83+13.18+10.95 plus several sub-3 kB ones) to six shown chunks totaling ~84.55 kB (35.60/17.55/14.44/11.11/2.97/2.88, plus "4 more" the build output truncates) — most plausibly `FlightPlanDialog`/`flight-plan-logic.ts` (reachable from both `devices` and `map`, so esbuild extracts it rather than duplicating) joining the pre-existing `Player`/`player-recovery.ts`/`LiveMap` shared code; attribution here is by elimination, same caveat U2's own entry already carries.

**Deviations from the task's literal wording, with reasons**:
- **Route mode is loop/bounce/once, not the task brief's "loop/once".** The brief's own parenthetical said "match the actual TelemetryPlan contract", and the actual contract (`vision-application.RouteMode`, CT-a) has three values; implementing only two would have been a narrower, incorrect mirror of a contract this cycle was explicitly told to match.
- **The Map tab's "Add a test drone" button keeps its original one-click, no-dialog behavior**; a *second*, new "Draw a flight plan…" button opens `FlightPlanDialog`. The task text said the empty-state action "can open the same dialog" (permissive, not "must replace") — replacing the existing one-click path would have regressed CU-b item 7's own explicit "one click, no hardware" requirement for the common case (most test-drone placements don't need a custom route); the dialog is additive for the case that does.
- **No `<vision-stream-info>` wiring for `features/map/live-dock.ts`'s compact preview** — unchanged from CD-b's own documented deviation (that panel needs its own `DetectionsStore`, judged not worth it for a bandwidth-conscious docked preview capped at one at a time); `LiveDock` does still bind `<vision-player>`'s new `whepUrl` input, so a docked preview benefits from WHEP-first playback even without the info panel showing which transport is live.
- **The manual waypoint-entry fallback lives *inside* the map dialog** (a collapsed `<details>`), not as a wizard-level alternative to opening the dialog at all — this matches CT-b's own spec text ("an embedded mini-map … with a manual `lat,lon` text fallback", i.e. the fallback is part of the map editor, not a way to skip it), while the wizard's separately-demoted bare home-lat/lon fields are the actual "skip the map entirely" path for a route-free simulation.
- **No dedicated unit tests for `FlightPlanDialog`'s Leaflet-driven interactions** (click-to-add, drag-to-move) — the pure logic behind every one of those actions (`addWaypoint`/`moveWaypoint`/`removeWaypoint`/`updateWaypointAltitude`) is fully unit-tested in `flight-plan-logic.spec.ts`; the imperative Leaflet wiring itself follows `LiveMap`/`FleetMap`'s own precedent of no dedicated component spec, verified by the production build + manual review instead.

## R-b — flight replay cockpit (docs/MVP2-PLAN.md §R)

The asset detail page's usage history gains a **Replay** action on every finished usage: a new `/assets/:assetId/replay/:usageId` cockpit — map trail drawn up to a scrub position, a heading-rotated marker there, a play/pause/1×/4×/16× scrub bar with a detection-density strip, and telemetry/detections panels reading their values *at* the scrub time. Built against R-a/R-a2's already-shipped `GET /api/usages/{usageId}/timeline` (verified against `vision-api/MODULE.md`'s own §Replay entry and the controller/DTO source directly, not assumed).

**New files**: `features/replay/replay-logic.ts` (+`.spec.ts`), `features/replay/replay-map.ts`/`.html`/`.css`, `features/replay/replay.ts`/`.html`/`.css`. **Reshaped**: `app.routes.ts` (new route), `core/api/models.ts` (`UsageTimeline`), `core/api/vision-api.ts` (`usageTimeline`, +2 `vision-api.spec.ts` cases), `core/telemetry/telemetry-logic.ts` (+`groupTelemetryByDevice`/`telemetryDevices`, moved from `features/asset-detail/asset-detail-logic.ts` — see below), `features/asset-detail/asset-detail-logic.ts` (now re-exports those two), `features/asset-detail/asset-detail.html` (usage history's new Replay/Watch-live column).

**A second page needing `groupTelemetryByDevice`/`telemetryDevices` triggered the same "lift to `core/`" move this codebase has made three times before** (`core/fleet/device-logic.ts#findVideoDevice`, `core/fleet/warehouse-logic.ts`'s generic lifecycle logic, `shared/map/live-map.ts`'s move out of `features/live/`) — the replay cockpit's own per-device telemetry panels need the identical per-device split `asset-detail.ts` already had, and this codebase has no precedent for one page importing another page's module. Both functions moved into `core/telemetry/telemetry-logic.ts` (a natural home — it already held `deriveTrail`/`ageSeconds`/`isStale`, the same "sample-shape derivation" family); `asset-detail-logic.ts` re-exports them so its own import site (`asset-detail.ts`) needed zero changes, and its own spec file (`asset-detail-logic.spec.ts`) was trimmed to just `freshestSample`'s coverage (unchanged behavior) while the two relocated `describe` blocks landed, as-is, in `core/telemetry/telemetry-logic.spec.ts` — the exact "relocated as-is, not new tests" idiom `core/fleet/warehouse-logic.spec.ts`'s own history entry already documents.

**Scrub/playback, concretely**: `ReplayPage.load()` fetches `getAsset(assetId)` and `usageTimeline(usageId, {maxPoints: 2000})` (the server's own clamp ceiling — R-a's `UsageTimelineController` docs) exactly once per route activation, no poller. Every panel is a `computed()` over that cached `timeline` signal and an `atMs` scrub signal: `trailPrefix`/`nearestSample` (map trail + marker, `O(log n)` binary search per call) and `bucketDetections`/`nearestDetectionResult`/`isDetectionNear` (the density strip + "what's showing now" panel) — dragging the `<input type="range">` scrub bar only calls `atMs.set(...)`, never HTTP, so it's exactly as smooth as any other signal-driven re-render regardless of flight length. Playback advances `atMs` via `requestAnimationFrame` (not `PollScheduler` — mirrors `shared/player/player.ts`'s own precedent of a raw timer for a single view's playback clock, see Gotchas), calling the pure `advancePlaybackClock(atMs, deltaRealMs, speed, fromMs, toMs)` each frame; reaching `toMs` stops playback rather than looping (a referee reviewing a specific moment wants playback to land and stay, not silently restart), and the Play button relabels **Replay** once at the end. A speed change (1×/4×/16×) needs no special-cased transition — `speed` is read fresh every tick, so the very next frame just advances by a different multiplier.

**Open usages are out of scope by design**: the history row shows Replay only for `endedAt`-carrying usages (an open one shows **Watch live** instead, `/live/:deviceId`); a direct link to an open usage's replay URL still resolves (the endpoint itself works fine — `to` just defaults to "now"), but `ReplayPage` shows a redirect notice rather than the cockpit, since scrubbing a window that's still growing underneath the bar isn't the reviewed-flight experience this feature is for.

**Detections' three honest empty states**, never collapsed into one generic dash: **"No detections recorded for this flight"** (`timeline.detections` is empty outright — a legacy usage from before R-a2's `AssetUsage.streamId` link, or a streamless asset, per R-a's own documented gap), **"No detection data near this moment"** (detections exist elsewhere in the flight, but the nearest one is further than `DETECTION_MATCH_TOLERANCE_MS` = 2s from the scrub position — e.g. CV only ran intermittently), and **"Nothing detected at this instant"** (the nearest inference frame ran, right near the scrub time, and simply saw nothing). The density strip only marks *positive* frames (`detections.length > 0`) — an analyzed-but-empty frame is not "where a detection occurred".

**Test count: 288 → 320** (32 new — verified: 288 + 32 = 320, matches exactly):
- `features/replay/replay-logic.spec.ts` — 30 cases: `nearestSample` (7, incl. exact match, tie-breaking toward the earlier sample, before/after-range clamping), `trailPrefix` (4), `nearestDetectionResult` (2, incl. resolving to a zero-detection result as the honest nearest answer), `isDetectionNear` (3, incl. the exact tolerance boundary), `bucketDetections` (6, incl. merging close results into one bucket and clamping an out-of-range `capturedAt` into the first/last bucket rather than dropping it), `clampToRange` (2), `advancePlaybackClock` (6, incl. a same-`deltaRealMs`-different-`speed` comparison proving the "no special case for speed changes" claim, end-of-range stop, and starting already-at-`toMs`).
- `core/api/vision-api.spec.ts` — 2 new cases (`usageTimeline`: no query params by default; `fromMs`/`toMs`/`maxPoints` passed through when given).
- `core/telemetry/telemetry-logic.spec.ts` — 4 cases for `groupTelemetryByDevice`/`telemetryDevices`, relocated as-is from `asset-detail-logic.spec.ts` (not new — the functions moved, the coverage came with them; net test count is therefore unaffected by the move itself, only by the 32 genuinely new cases above).
- No page-component-level specs for `replay.ts`/`replay-map.ts` — consistent with this app's existing precedent (every prior UI cycle's own Status entry makes the same call, most recently U3's); exercised by the full production build (twice, from clean, AOT `strictTemplates: true` — catches template-binding type errors) and close pattern-matching against `shared/map/live-map.ts`'s already-shipped Leaflet lifecycle code, which `replay-map.ts` mirrors near-verbatim. **Deviation, honestly noted**: no live-stack visual screenshot was taken (the frontend-design skill's own "screenshot and critique" guidance) — doing so needs a booted backend with a finished, telemetry-and-detection-bearing usage seeded, which was judged out of proportion to this cycle's time budget; the CSS reuses `asset-detail.css`'s own already-shipped, visually-verified classes (`.facts`/`.telemetry-grid`/`.detail-grid`/skeleton) nearly verbatim rather than inventing new layout, which is the mitigation for that gap.

**Bundle sizes** (`ng build --configuration production`, before → after; before = U3's own recorded "after" numbers above, i.e. the working state this cycle started from):
- **Initial total: 301.91 kB raw / 83.94 kB transfer → 302.58 kB raw / 84.41 kB transfer (+0.67 kB raw / +0.47 kB transfer)** — **a correction to the task brief's "don't grow it", found while measuring, not aimed for.** Every one of replay's own new modules (`features/replay/**`, `ReplayMap`) is reachable only from the new lazy `replay` chunk — confirmed zero-eager the same way U3's own entry did (grep for the new symbols in the initial chunks finds nothing). The entire delta is `VisionApi.usageTimeline()` — one new method's own minified body — landing in the initial bundle because `VisionApi` is `providedIn: 'root'` and already eagerly loaded by the shell; this is the identical, unavoidable cost every past cycle that added a `VisionApi`/`FleetStore` method also paid (CW-b's 8 new methods cost ~2.9 kB, C4's 2 new methods cost ~0.9 kB) — `models.ts`'s new `UsageTimeline` type itself costs nothing (types erase). Avoiding it would mean either not adding a typed `VisionApi` method (breaking this file's own "no component calls `fetch` directly" convention, carved out today only for the Debug console) or leaving `usageTimeline` unused by a real page — neither was judged an acceptable trade for ~0.67 kB. The bundle stays 2.58 kB over the pre-existing 300 kB **warning** threshold (was already 1.91 kB over it after U2's CDK addition; nowhere close to the 360 kB **error** one) — the same "flagged, not hidden" posture U2's own Status entry set for its own threshold crossing.
- **New `replay` chunk: 19.52 kB raw / 5.94 kB transfer** — the whole cockpit (page + map sub-component + all the scrub/playback/detection logic), comparable to `map`'s own 21.95/6.43 and smaller than `asset-detail`'s 25.20/6.63 despite being an entirely new surface, since it reuses `shared/map/leaflet-loader.ts` rather than rebuilding any map bootstrap.
- **`asset-detail`: 24.80 kB / 6.52 kB → 25.20 kB / 6.63 kB** (+0.40 kB / +0.11 kB — the usage history table's new Replay/Watch-live column + `RouterLink` bindings; `groupTelemetryByDevice`/`telemetryDevices` moving to `core/telemetry/telemetry-logic.ts` costs this chunk nothing net, since it already imported them from `asset-detail-logic.ts`'s now-thinner re-export).
- **`leaflet-src`: unchanged at 149.55 kB / 37.58 kB** — `ReplayMap` reuses `shared/map/leaflet-loader.ts#importLeaflet()` exactly like every other map; no new Leaflet APIs, confirming "leaflet stays shared" held.
- **`devices`/`map`/`live`/`wall`/`debug`/`settings`/`hls`: unchanged (± ~40 bytes of esbuild minification noise on `devices`, not attributable to any changed source in this cycle)** — none of their source changed; verified by diffing against U3's own recorded numbers.

**Deviations from the task brief, with reasons**:
- **Initial bundle grew by 0.67 kB, not "stayed at 301.91 kB" as the brief asked** — see the Bundle sizes entry above for the full accounting; the growth is `VisionApi.usageTimeline()`'s own code, not anything replay-specific leaking eager, and is proportionate to every past cycle's own "new `VisionApi` method costs a few hundred bytes to a few kB" experience.
- **`groupTelemetryByDevice`/`telemetryDevices` moved to `core/telemetry/telemetry-logic.ts`** — not called out in the task brief's own literal text, but a direct, previously-established consequence of "reuse/extend existing telemetry formatting logic" once a second page needed the identical per-device split; see the dedicated writeup above.
- **No dedicated `ReplayMap`/`ReplayPage` component-level tests** — consistent with, not a departure from, this app's own established precedent (every map/page component in this codebase ships without one; only the pure logic underneath is unit-tested) — see the Test count entry above for the honest gap this leaves (no live-stack screenshot) and its mitigation.
- **`AssetDetailPage.assetId`'s route-param mismatch, found but not fixed** — out of R-b's own scope (it's `asset-detail.ts`, not `replay.ts`); flagged in Gotchas instead, with the replay route's own params deliberately named to not repeat it.

## V-b — player live-edge tuning (docs/MVP2-PLAN.md §V)

The user directive behind §V ("the user should see the latest available shot, not a buffered 10 seconds ago") splits into a backend half (V-a: encoder/mediamtx/proxy, `adapters/adapter-publish-hls`/compose, concurrent with this task and not depended on) and this UI half: make `shared/player/player.ts`'s HLS path actually try to stay near the live edge — hls.js ships with almost none of that tuning enabled by default — and stop it silently drifting stale after a stall recovery or a backgrounded tab, instead of just watching a `behindLive` number climb. Also folds in a pre-existing, previously-flagged-but-unfixed bug (below) that R-b found while wiring the replay route but didn't chase to its actual runtime effect.

**New files**: `shared/player/live-edge-logic.ts` (+`.spec.ts`, 16 cases). **Reshaped**: `shared/player/player.ts` (HLS live-edge config constants, snap-to-live wiring, behind-live chip hysteresis — no rewrite of the WHEP/recovery machinery itself), `app.routes.ts` (`assets/:id` → `assets/:assetId`).

**hls.js config, concretely** — every value lives as a named constant with its own doc-comment block directly above `beginAttach` in `shared/player/player.ts` (not restated in full here; see that file):
- `lowLatencyMode: true` — unchanged from before this cycle.
- `liveSyncDurationCount: 2` / `liveMaxLatencyDurationCount: 4` — **segment-count**, not the duration-based `liveSyncDuration`/`liveMaxLatencyDuration` (hls.js throws a config error if you mix the two families). Chosen over duration because V-a shrinks segment/part duration server-side as part of this very same latency push (legacy ~6s segments → LL-HLS parts ~200ms) — a fixed-seconds target would need re-tuning every time that number changes on a given deployment; a count-based one scales automatically on either side of that landing, and behaves identically against a stock playlist that never advertises `PART-HOLD-BACK` at all (confirmed by reading hls.js's own `LatencyController` source directly, not assumed: `synchronizeToLiveEdge` computes its own `maxLatency` from `liveMaxLatencyDurationCount * targetduration` whenever the duration variant is unset). The load-bearing finding here: hls.js's own default for `liveMaxLatencyDurationCount` is **`Infinity`** — meaning without this, hls.js never forcibly reseeks a drifted playhead at all, regardless of how far behind it gets. This player had `lowLatencyMode: true` before V-b but nothing else, so that forced-catch-up mechanism has been silently off since it was first added (U3).
- `maxLiveSyncPlaybackRate: 1.5` — hls.js's own gentle rate-based catch-up (nudges `video.playbackRate` above `1`, capped at `1.5`, while behind-but-within-range, closing the gap without a visible seek jump); gated only by `lowLatencyMode` + a live playlist (verified in hls.js source — not further gated by actual LL-HLS tags), so this activates today, before V-a lands anywhere. Default is `1` (inert) — another mechanism that was silently off.
- `maxBufferLength: 10` (down from hls.js's own `30`s default, sized for VOD scrubbing headroom this player never uses) / `backBufferLength: 30` (unchanged, just promoted to a named constant).

**Snap-to-live, concretely** (`shared/player/live-edge-logic.ts#shouldSnapToLive`/`SnapToLiveReason`, wired from `shared/player/player.ts`): two reasons, both read off state the recovery reducer already owns rather than inventing parallel component flags —
- `'recovered'`: fired the instant `player-recovery.ts`'s own `transportState().recovery.phase === 'reconnecting'` is observed (read *before* the `firstSegment` event is applied, at the exact hls.js `FRAG_BUFFERED` / native `playing` callback that would otherwise transition to `playing`) — a stall or fatal error already tore the previous attachment down and rebuilt it from nothing (`teardownMedia`), so there's no actual stale buffer to correct, but also no cost to landing exactly on `hls.liveSyncPosition` instead of trusting wherever the fresh attach's own start-position logic lands it. Always snaps, deliberately ignoring the (almost always still-`null` at that instant) measured latency.
- `'visibilityRestored'`: fired when `document.hidden` flips `true`→`false` between two ticks of the *existing* 1s latency-sampling timer (`startLatencySampling`) — no new `visibilitychange` listener; diffing `document.hidden` off an already-running heartbeat mirrors `core/poll-scheduler.ts`'s own precedent for checking it, just on `shared/player/player.ts`'s own per-second timer instead of `PollScheduler`'s (this file already deliberately does not pause that timer while hidden — see the pre-existing Status/Gotchas note on why). Only snaps once the fresh measurement clears `SNAP_TO_LIVE_THRESHOLD_SECONDS` (3s) — a tab glanced away from for under a second shouldn't yank the video out from under the viewer.
- Both funnel into `snapToLiveEdge()`: `video.currentTime = hls.liveSyncPosition` when hls.js is the active engine, falling back to the end of the native `seekable` range for Safari's native-HLS path (mirrors the pre-existing `measureBehindLive`'s own fallback shape). Never called for a `webrtc` transport — WHEP has no seekable buffer to fall behind (`behindLive` is pinned to `0`), and in practice `maybeSnapToLive` is only ever invoked from HLS/native code paths in the first place.

**Behind-live chip, concretely** (`shared/player/live-edge-logic.ts#shouldShowBehindLive`/`behindLiveChipLabel`): the chip's quantified tail (`'live · HLS · 4.2s behind'`) only appears once `behindLive` clears `BEHIND_LIVE_SHOW_THRESHOLD_SECONDS` (4s), and only disappears again once it drops below the *lower* `BEHIND_LIVE_HIDE_THRESHOLD_SECONDS` (2s) — the two-line dead band (a `showBehindLive` signal, not recomputed from a single comparison on every render) is what stops the label flickering between `'live · HLS'` and the quantified form as the once-a-second measurement jitters near a single cutoff. Below the show threshold the chip reads a plain `'live · HLS'` — the old unconditional-whenever-known behavior (`'{behind}s behind · HLS'`, shown any time latency was measured at all, even at `0.3s`) is gone; sub-few-second HLS latency isn't worth quantifying at a glance, and `'live · WebRTC'` already sets the "just say live" precedent this now matches. Word order also changed to put the transport right after "live" in both cases (`'live · WebRTC'` / `'live · HLS'` / `'live · HLS · Xs behind'`), per the task brief's own example format — previously HLS's behind form read `'{X}s behind · HLS'`, transport last.

**Route-param bug, concretely** — see the Gotchas entry (rewritten in place, not duplicated here) for the full writeup: `app.routes.ts`'s `assets/:id` didn't match `AssetDetailPage.assetId`'s input name, and — confirmed empirically with a throwaway `RouterTestingHarness` spec, not just re-reasoned from the router source — this was not inert: every real navigation to the asset detail page landed on the "Unknown asset" empty state, unconditionally. Fixed by renaming the path segment to `assets/:assetId`; no call site needed touching (every `router.navigate([...])` to this route is positional, not name-based).

**Test count: 320 → 336** (16 new, all in `shared/player/live-edge-logic.spec.ts`): `shouldSnapToLive` (5 cases — `'recovered'` always true across null/0/small/large latencies, `'visibilityRestored'` never snaps on `null`, threshold boundary, small-drift no-snap, custom threshold); `shouldShowBehindLive` (7 cases — the hysteresis band's both directions, boundary values on each threshold, `null` never shows regardless of current state, a same-value-opposite-starting-state pair proving the hysteresis rather than a single comparison, custom thresholds); `behindLiveChipLabel` (4 cases — webrtc ignores latency/flag entirely, hls plain form while the flag is false or latency unmeasured, hls quantified form with `toFixed(1)` rounding). No new events were added to `player-recovery.ts`'s reducer, so its own 31 pre-existing specs (18 base + 13 from U3) are untouched, exactly as the task scoped ("player recovery reducer specs extended only if you add events to it") — V-b's logic only *reads* `TransportRecoveryState`, it never feeds anything back into `reduceRecovery`/`reduceTransportRecovery`. No new component-level spec for `shared/player/player.ts`'s own wiring (the timer diffing, the `Hls.Events.FRAG_BUFFERED`/native-`'playing'` call sites, the `document.hidden` check) — consistent with this app's standing precedent (every prior cycle's own Status entry makes the same call for `Player`'s WHEP path, `FlightPlanDialog`'s Leaflet wiring, every map component, etc.); exercised by the full production build (twice, from clean) and manual reasoning against hls.js's own read source (`node_modules/hls.js/dist/hls.mjs`) for the exact default values and gating conditions cited above, not assumed from the public docs alone.

**Bundle sizes** (`ng build --configuration production`, before → after; before = R-b's own recorded "after" numbers above, i.e. the working state this cycle started from):
- **Initial total: 302.58 kB raw / 84.41 kB transfer → 302.59 kB raw / 84.41 kB transfer — flat within rounding.** `shared/player/live-edge-logic.ts` is reachable only through `Player`, which is never `providedIn: 'root'` and only ever lands in lazy chunks — grep-verified absent from the initial bundle, same check every past cycle here has made.
- **`live`: 17.63 kB / 4.91 kB → 17.71 kB / 4.92 kB** (+0.08 kB — the `shared/player/player.ts` changes this cycle lands in every chunk that renders `<vision-player>`).
- **`map`: 21.89 kB / 6.42 kB → 21.95 kB / 6.43 kB** (+0.06 kB — same reason, via `LiveDock`'s player).
- **`wall`: 7.04 kB / 2.56 kB → 7.10 kB / 2.58 kB** (+0.06 kB — same reason, via `WallTile`'s player).
- **`asset-detail`: 25.20 kB / 6.63 kB → 25.20 kB / 6.63 kB — unchanged to the byte**, despite also rendering `<vision-player>`; the delta the other three chunks show landed in one of the unnamed shared chunks instead for this host (esbuild's own chunk-splitting choice, not something this cycle controls — same "attribution by elimination" caveat U2's/U3's own Status entries already carry for these unnamed chunks).
- **`replay`: unchanged at 19.52 kB / 5.94 kB** — `ReplayPage`/`ReplayMap` never render `<vision-player>`.
- **`devices`: 68.03 kB / 16.52 kB → 68.07 kB / 16.54 kB** (+0.04 kB — `DevicesPage` doesn't render `<vision-player>` either; this is the same few-tens-of-bytes esbuild minification noise R-b's own entry already called out as "not attributable to any changed source in this cycle").
- **`leaflet-src`/`hls`/`debug`/`settings`: unchanged** (149.55/37.58, 517.70/132.37, 13.96/4.33, 9.80/2.99) — `hls.js`'s own code is untouched by this cycle (only the *config object* `shared/player/player.ts` passes to it changed, which lives in `player.ts`'s own host chunks, not the further-lazy `hls` chunk); Leaflet/Debug/Settings have no player.
- **Unnamed shared chunks**: still ~10–11 of them, net total essentially unchanged from U3's own last recorded figure (~84.55 kB) — the two largest (35.60 kB, 14.44 kB) match U3's own recorded values exactly; the rest shifted by small amounts either way (e.g. one moved 17.55→18.30 kB, another 11.11→10.26 kB) rather than uniformly growing, consistent with `shared/player/player.ts`'s changes landing in whichever shared chunk esbuild currently puts code reachable from more than one lazy entry point into — same "attribution by elimination" caveat U2's/U3's own Status entries already carry for this bucket, not re-litigated precisely here.

**Deviations from the task brief, with reasons**:
- **The task's own example chip format ("live · HLS · 4s behind") uses whole seconds; this keeps one decimal place** (`'4.2s behind'`), matching the pre-existing chip's own precision and `core/stream-info-logic.ts#formatLatency`'s identical `toFixed(1)` convention — the task's example was read as illustrative shorthand, not a literal formatting spec, and one decimal is more honest for a number whose whole point is "how bad is it" without being falsely precise to the millisecond.
- **`maxLiveSyncPlaybackRate`/the rate-based catch-up mechanism isn't named anywhere in the task brief's own literal bullet list** ("tighten liveSyncDuration/liveMaxLatencyDuration … or their Count variants") — read as the concrete implementation of the same bullet's own "live-edge catch-up" phrase, which is hls.js's own name for exactly this mechanism (confirmed by reading `LatencyController` source, not assumed); omitting it would have left `lowLatencyMode: true` (already present before this cycle) continuing to gate a feature nothing ever turned on.
- **No dedicated `shared/player/player.ts` component-level spec added for the new wiring** — see the Test count entry above; consistent with, not a departure from, this app's own standing precedent for `Player`.
- **The route-param fix folds R-b's flagged-not-fixed Gotcha into this cycle** rather than leaving it for a future one — the task brief explicitly asked for it, and it happened to already be flagged in this exact doc, which is also where V-b's own player changes are documented.
