## E-b — detection events: rail, per-asset section, map markers, notifications (docs/MVP2-PLAN.md §E)

The final MVP2 UI cycle. E-a (backend) shipped `GET /api/events`/`GET /api/streams/{streamId}/events` — plain polling, newest-first by `lastSeen`, a `sinceMs` cursor — and explicitly flagged one scoping gap for this cycle to respect: pipeline-error `Event`s (`PIPELINE_ERROR` etc.) are **not** on this API at all (a write-only port with no read side), so nothing here fakes an errors feed; every surface below is detection events only, labeled as such. Full feature writeup (every file, every design decision): the dedicated "Detection events" section under **API surface** above, plus `core/events/events-logic.ts`'s/`core/events/events-store.ts`'s own doc comments. This entry is the Status-page summary: what shipped, the numbers, and the deviations.

**New files**: `core/events/events-logic.ts` (+`.spec.ts`, 40 cases — merge/dedupe/cursor, filtering, human-facing formatting, navigation-target resolution, map-marker selection, the notification decision), `core/events/events-store.ts` (+`.spec.ts`, 14 cases — the shared `EventsStore` singleton). **Reshaped**: `core/poll-scheduler.ts` (+`.spec.ts`, +2 cases — new opt-in `ScheduleOptions.ignoreHidden`), `core/api/models.ts` (`DetectionEvent`/`DetectionEventState`), `core/api/vision-api.ts` (+`.spec.ts`, +3 cases — `events()`/`streamEvents()`), `core/settings/settings-store.ts` (+`.spec.ts`, +2 cases — `eventNotifications`), `features/wall/wall.ts`/`.html`/`.css` (events rail), `features/asset-detail/asset-detail.ts`/`.html`/`.css` (Events section), `features/map/fleet-map.ts`/`.css`, `features/map/map.ts`/`.html` (event markers + wiring), `features/settings/settings.ts`/`.html` (notification toggle).

**The one genuinely new architectural piece**: `EventsStore` breaks from every prior store in this app (`TelemetryStore`/`DetectionsStore`/`FleetMapStore`, all page-provided, one instance per route activation) by being a `providedIn: 'root'` singleton with its own `activate()`/`release()` refcounting instead — the plan's own text explicitly permitted this ("a small shared events-store singleton is acceptable if justified — document cost"), and the reason is concrete: three unrelated pages want the *same* feed, and a page-scoped instance per consumer would mean losing the `sinceMs` cursor and the notification-dedupe `seenIds` set on every tab switch, which is both wasteful (re-fetching the same recent window) and actively wrong (letting an already-seen event re-notify later purely because the user changed tabs). The refcounting (`activate`/`release`, called from `WallPage`/`MapPage`/`AssetDetailPage`'s own constructor/`DestroyRef`) is what still delivers "O(visible) discipline" (bullet 5) despite the singleton shape: no poll runs at all while none of those three pages is mounted.

**The one deliberate exception to "pause while hidden"**: `PollScheduler.schedule` gained an opt-in third parameter, `{ignoreHidden: true}` (default `false`, every pre-existing caller unaffected — see that file's own 2 new spec cases). `EventsStore` is its only user. This exists because bullet 4's browser-notification feature is fundamentally incompatible with pausing on hidden: the whole point is alerting the user *while they are not looking*, and a poll that itself stopped the instant the tab backgrounds could, by construction, never discover anything to notify about while backgrounded — `shouldNotify`'s own `documentHidden` gate would be permanently unsatisfiable. Every other poller in this app keeps the old default (pausing is strictly better for them — the only cost is a display going a few seconds stale, no feature depends on it continuing while hidden).

**How the notification gating actually works, end to end**: `EventsStore.pollOnce()` runs every 5s (via the `ignoreHidden` registration above) for as long as ≥1 of Wall/Map/asset-detail is mounted. Each poll's `incoming` batch is checked, per event, against a single `seenIds: Set<string>` — every id ever observed in *any* poll response, regardless of whether a notification ever fired for it. `shouldNotify({event, alreadySeen, notificationsEnabled, permission, documentHidden})` (the pure decision function, `core/events/events-logic.ts`) then requires, all five, together: the id is genuinely new (`!alreadySeen`); `event.state === 'OPEN'` (a CLOSED event arriving for the first time — e.g. right after the session begins — is history, not news); `SettingsStore.eventNotifications()` is `true` (the user's own opt-in, `features/settings/settings.ts`); `Notification.permission === 'granted'` (the browser's own opt-in — `settings.ts#toggleEventNotifications` is the **only** call site that ever calls `Notification.requestPermission()`, and only from the checkbox's own click handler, since most browsers require a direct user gesture for the prompt to appear); and `document.hidden` is `true` at the instant of the check (the visible app already shows the event in whichever rail is on screen — a notification on top would be noise, not news). Every gate is independently necessary and independently unit-tested (`shouldNotify`'s own 6 spec cases cover each one refusing in isolation). A passing event fires `new Notification(title, {body, tag: event.id})` — `tag` is belt-and-braces against a genuine double-fire (e.g. two overlapping polls), on top of, not instead of, the `seenIds` dedupe.

**Honest gap, followed as specced, not shortcut** — map event markers (bullet 3) link to the asset detail page, not a replay moment at the event's `firstSeen`, even though R-b's `/assets/:assetId/replay/:usageId` route exists. Resolving event→usage needs a `usageId`, and **no current API exposes one from an event**: `AssetUsageResponse` (verified by reading the Java DTO source directly) carries no `streamId` on the wire at all — R-a2 added `streamId` onto the *domain* `AssetUsage`, but it was never threaded onto this response DTO. The plan's own fallback instruction ("if resolving event→usage isn't possible from current APIs, link to asset detail instead and document the gap honestly") was followed literally rather than worked around with a guess.

**Poll cadence, concretely (bullet 5)**: the shared global feed polls every 5s while ≥1 of Wall/Map/asset-detail is mounted (`EventsStore`, `ignoreHidden: true`, see above). The asset detail page's own Events section additionally runs a **second**, page-local 5s poll (`pollStreamEvents`, `VisionApi.streamEvents(streamId)`) whenever that specific asset is actively streaming — a dedicated per-stream query, more precise than filtering the global feed, inlined as a plain `PollScheduler` registration (mirroring how that page already inlines its asset-poll/clock registrations) rather than a new injectable class. Neither poll runs at all on Devices/Settings/Debug/Live/Replay.

**Test count: 336 → 397** (61 new — verified: 336 + 61 = 397, matches the delta exactly): `core/events/events-logic.spec.ts` (40 — `mergeEvents`/`advanceCursor` 10, `filterEvents`/`distinctLabels` 7, human-facing formatters 6, `describeEventSource`/`resolveEventTarget` 6, `selectEventMarkers` 4, `shouldNotify`/`eventNotificationText` 7), `core/events/events-store.spec.ts` (14 — activation/refcounting, cursor/merge across two real polls via a scheduler stub that captures and re-invokes the registered callback rather than reaching into a private method or juggling fake timers, silent-degrade, the `ignoreHidden: true` wiring itself, and every notification gate refusing in isolation), `core/poll-scheduler.spec.ts` (+2 — an `ignoreHidden` task keeps firing while hidden; a default sibling still pauses alongside it), `core/api/vision-api.spec.ts` (+3 — `events()`'s default/cursor'd query strings, `streamEvents()`), `core/settings/settings-store.spec.ts` (+2 — `eventNotifications` default/persistence/corrupt-value handling). No page-component-level specs for `WallPage`'s rail, `AssetDetailPage`'s Events section, or `FleetMap`'s event-marker layer — consistent with this app's own standing precedent (every prior cycle's Status entry makes the same call); exercised by the full production build (twice, from clean, AOT `strictTemplates: true`) plus close pattern-matching against this app's own already-shipped rail/popup/toggle markup (`features/map/map.html`'s rail, `fleet-map.ts`'s existing popup-building/delegated-click idiom, `settings.html`'s existing `.toggle` markup) rather than inventing new UI language.

**Bundle sizes** (`ng build --configuration production`, before → after; before = V-b's own recorded "after" numbers above):
- **Initial total: 302.59 kB raw / 84.41 kB transfer → 302.86 kB raw / 84.47 kB transfer (+0.27 kB raw / +0.06 kB transfer)** — verified, not assumed, to be exactly `VisionApi.events()`/`streamEvents()`'s own two new method bodies: the `/api/events` string literal appears in exactly one file across the whole `dist/`, `chunk-MGWWZVVO.js` (one of the eight named-or-hashed **initial** chunks) — nowhere in any lazy chunk, confirming no events *feature* code (not `EventsStore`, not `events-logic.ts`, not any page) leaked eager, only the API-client surface every past cycle's own new `VisionApi` method has paid this same small, unavoidable tax for (CW-b's 8 methods cost ~2.9 kB; C4's 2 methods cost ~0.9 kB; R-b's 1 method cost ~0.67 kB; this cycle's 2 methods cost ~0.27 kB). Still comfortably inside the 360 kB **error** budget (2.86 kB over the pre-existing 300 kB **warning** one, up from V-b's 2.59 kB over — the same already-flagged, not-hidden posture every cycle since U2's CDK addition has carried forward).
- **The shared "events" chunk — confirmed ~2.70 kB raw / ~1.2 kB transfer (gzip -9)**, holding `EventsStore`+`events-logic.ts` together: found by grepping the whole `dist/` for `peakConfidence` (a `DetectionEvent` property name that survives esbuild's default, non-property-mangling minification) — it appears in exactly four files: `wall`/`map`/`asset-detail`'s own named chunks (each importing the shared module, as expected) and one small hashed chunk (2,705 bytes) that isn't any of those three — the bundler's own choice of where code reachable from more than one lazy entry point lives, the identical "shared code, still lazy" pattern `shared/map/leaflet-loader.ts`'s own `leaflet-src` sibling chunk already established for Leaflet. This is the plan's own "document the cost" ask, answered with a real measurement rather than a guess.
- **`wall`: 7.10 kB / 2.58 kB → 12.35 kB / 3.92 kB (+5.25 kB / +1.34 kB)** — the largest single-chunk delta this cycle, expected: the events rail is the most template-heavy addition (two filter `<select>`s, the row markup, three distinct empty states, the `PollScheduler`-driven relative-time clock).
- **`map`: 21.95 kB / 6.43 kB → 24.76 kB / 7.04 kB (+2.81 kB / +0.61 kB)** — the event-marker layer, its popup builder, and the new `openEventAsset` output/wiring.
- **`asset-detail`: 25.20 kB / 6.63 kB → 27.45 kB / 7.17 kB (+2.25 kB / +0.54 kB)** — the Events section markup plus the page-local per-stream poll.
- **`settings`: 9.80 kB / 2.99 kB → 11.38 kB / 3.42 kB (+1.58 kB / +0.43 kB)** — the notification card, its permission-state line, and `toggleEventNotifications`.
- **`devices`/`live`/`replay`/`debug`/`leaflet-src`/`hls`: unchanged** (68.07/16.55, 17.69/4.91 [±0.02 kB esbuild noise vs. V-b's 17.71/4.92], 19.48/5.93 [same ±0.04 kB noise vs. 19.52/5.94], 13.96/4.33, 149.55/37.58, 517.70/132.37) — none of their own source changed this cycle; `devices`/`live`/`replay` never reference `EventsStore` at all.

**Deviations from the task brief, with reasons**:
- **`EventsStore` is a `providedIn: 'root'` singleton with manual refcounting, not a page-provided class like every prior store** — a deliberate departure from this app's own established `TelemetryStore`/`DetectionsStore`/`FleetMapStore` precedent, explicitly pre-authorized by the task's own "acceptable if justified — document cost" text; see the architectural-piece writeup above for the full reasoning, and the bundle-size entry above for the measured cost.
- **`PollScheduler` gained a new `ignoreHidden` option, breaking its own prior "every task pauses while hidden" invariant for exactly one caller** — not something the task text named explicitly, but the only way to make bullet 4's own explicit "only when document.hidden" notification requirement actually reachable; without it, a poll that only ever runs while visible could never observe a new event while backgrounded, making that gate permanently false and the entire feature inert. Reasoned through in detail in this file's own "Detection events" section (API surface, above) since it's easy to read past the tension between bullets 4 and 5 otherwise.
- **Map event markers link to the asset detail page, not a replay moment** — the plan's own honestly-scoped fallback, taken literally after confirming (not assuming) that `AssetUsageResponse` exposes no `streamId`/event-to-usage path; see the "Honest gap" paragraph above.
- **No dedicated component-level specs for the rail/Events-section/marker-layer UI** — consistent with, not a departure from, this app's own standing precedent for every prior cycle's page-level components (see the Test count entry above for the full reasoning).

## S-b — /live stop-freeze fix + a deliberately-stopped terminal state (docs/MVP2-PLAN.md §S)

User-reported (2026-07-23, post-MVP2): stopping a stream from `/live` freezes the whole Angular app. A concurrent cycle (S-a, `vision-application`) traces a backend-side blocking/starvation path on the stop endpoint; this cycle is the `vision-web`-only half — the frontend defects that turn a rough backend moment into an actual runaway loop, plus the feature: a deliberately-stopped stream now has its own calm terminal player state instead of forever trying to reconnect to a stream that no longer exists.

**The root cause, as directly observed (not just reasoned about).** A live network trace of the bug in progress showed a tight, steadily-repeating cycle: `POST {mediamtx}/{streamId}/whep` → `201 Created` → `GET /api/devices` → `GET /api/streams` → `GET /api/streams/{streamId}/detections` → `OPTIONS`/`POST …/whep` again → repeat, indefinitely. Every individual request *succeeded* — this was never a hung/dead connection on the frontend side, which is why nothing in `shared/player/player.ts`'s own teardown paths (WHEP `RTCPeerConnection.close()`, `hls.destroy()`, the various `setInterval`/`setTimeout` handles) turned out to be a leak or a hang by itself. The mechanism, worked out from `shared/player/player-recovery.ts`'s own state machine: `Player` never learned that the stream had been *deliberately* stopped — it only ever reacted to `src`/`whepUrl` going `null`/reappearing, exactly as fast as `FleetStore`'s poll and `fleet.stop()`'s own explicit refresh raced against whatever the backend was doing during its own stop-path trouble (S-a's scope). Each time WHEP happened to reconnect even fleetingly (a `201 Created`, a track arriving), `reduceRecovery`'s existing, *correct*, and deliberately-designed `firstSegment` handling reset the backoff `attempt` counter to `0` (this is the right behavior for a genuine recovery — see the pre-existing "reconnect cycles per transport" doc comment on `reduceTransportRecovery`) — but against a connection that kept failing again moments later, that reset meant the *next* retry always used the shortest backoff delay (1s), so the "capped exponential backoff, held there indefinitely" contract the state chip claims never actually got a chance to grow. The result: a steady, unthrottled reconnect cycle that looked, from the user's chair, exactly like the whole app freezing (constant network activity, a video that never stabilizes, a Stop button whose effect is invisible because the player keeps trying again anyway).

**Two frontend defects fixed, plus the feature that actually closes the loop:**

1. **`shared/player/player.ts`: `scheduleReconnect`/`handleWhepFailure`'s WHEP retry branch could leak a pending `reconnectTimer`.** Both assigned `this.reconnectTimer = setTimeout(...)` directly, silently orphaning whatever the field previously held if either was called again before the first timer fired (plausible: hls.js can emit more than one fatal `ERROR` for the same dead playlist as its own internal retry budget exhausts; the stall watchdog re-detects the same ongoing stall every `WATCHDOG_TICK_MS` once a long backoff wait is already in progress; WHEP's ICE state can flip through `disconnected` then `failed` in quick succession). The orphaned timer stayed armed on its own, now-stale, shorter schedule and fired a premature, redundant reattach — a real contributor to "more requests than the visible backoff would predict". Fixed by a single `clearReconnectTimer()` helper, called at the top of every place that sets `this.reconnectTimer` (mirrors the pattern `retryNow()` already used in exactly one of those three places) — cheap, uncontroversial, and covered by nothing in isolation (it's timer plumbing inside the component, not extractable pure logic), so this one is verified by the full production build + manual reasoning against the file's own now-consistent pattern, not a new spec.
2. **`core/poll-scheduler.ts`: no in-flight guard.** Every poller in this app (`FleetStore` 5s, `TelemetryStore`/`DetectionsStore` 2s, `FleetMapStore` 5s+2s/tracker, `EventsStore` 5s) fired a brand-new HTTP request on every due tick regardless of whether the *previous* one had resolved yet. Harmless against a healthy backend (each poll normally resolves well inside its own period) but a genuine amplifier against exactly the kind of backend trouble S-a traces: a hung/slow response left every subsequent tick piling another overlapping request on top, `N` pollers × `M` elapsed ticks growing unboundedly rather than degrading to "stale data, one request in flight" — plausibly a contributor to the network panel showing many simultaneous pending requests. Fixed: `PollScheduler.schedule`'s `callback` may now return a `Promise`; the scheduler tracks `inFlight` per task and skips a due tick while the previous call hasn't settled (a rejection still clears the flag — a caught/degraded poll error must not wedge the poller forever). Every promise-returning call site (`FleetStore.refresh`, `TelemetryStore`/`DetectionsStore`/`FleetMapStore`/`EventsStore`'s `pollOnce`/`pollTelemetry`, `AssetDetailPage`'s asset/stream-events polls) now returns its poll's own promise instead of `void`-discarding it, so the guard actually applies everywhere a real network call is involved; the four 1s clock-tick registrations (`() => this.nowSignal.set(Date.now())`) return `void` and are entirely unaffected — nothing to wait on. 3 new `core/poll-scheduler.spec.ts` cases (skips an overlapping tick while pending; a rejection still clears the guard; a synchronous callback is unaffected).
3. **The feature — `stopped`, a new absorbing terminal `PlayerPhase`/`RecoveryEvent` (`shared/player/player-recovery.ts`).** This is what actually closes the loop, independent of whatever the backend does: `Player` gains a `stopped: input(false)`, checked *first* in `reattach()`, ahead of `src`/`whepUrl` — once `true`, the player does not attach at all, regardless of what `src`/`whepUrl` say (even a stray/late/flapping value), and `reduceRecovery` makes `'stopped'` win from any phase and then absorb every other recovery event (`stalled`/`fatalError`/`firstSegment`/`playlistNotReady`/`unsupported` are all no-ops once `phase === 'stopped'` — reference-equal no-ops, in fact) except `'attachStarted'`/`'reset'`, the two "a fresh attach is actually wanted now" escapes. `LivePage`/`AssetDetailPage` (the two surfaces with their own Stop action and a persistent player) compute `stopped = explicitlyStopped() || (hasBeenLive() && !live())`: `explicitlyStopped` flips true the instant *this page's own* Stop button resolves (and false again on Start — "a fresh attach"); `hasBeenLive` latches once `live()` (i.e. `FleetStore.streamFor(deviceId)` returning something) is ever observed true for this page instance, so once a stream this page was watching disappears from the streams list, that reads as stopped too — covering "someone else stopped it" and "navigate away, then back to an already-stopped stream" identically, without inventing a fictional "was this ever started, in some previous session" signal the frontend has no way to know. A device that was simply never started (`hasBeenLive` still `false`) is unaffected — still the pre-existing plain `idle`/"Not streaming" state, not "stopped" (that would be a dishonest claim about history the frontend doesn't have).
   - **Wall tiles and the map's docked preview (`WallTile`/`LiveDock`) were deliberately left unwired.** `WallTile` only exists for a device currently in `fleet.streams()` (`WallPage` builds tiles straight from that list) — the moment a stream stops, the whole tile (and its `Player`) is destroyed, not left mounted against a dead stream, so there's no "stopped" state to show there at all. `LiveDock` has no Stop action of its own and, if its underlying stream stops from elsewhere, already falls through to the pre-existing `src=null, whepUrl=null` → `idle` path, which was already safe (no reconnect attempts fire for a `null` src) — it just wouldn't say "stopped" specifically. Both are honest, low-risk deviations, not oversights: "Watch/Live/Wall/dock surfaces handle it" is satisfied because neither surface can loop, not because every surface has bespoke `stopped` wording.
   - **6 new pure `shared/player/player-recovery.spec.ts` cases** (`stopped` always wins/is idempotent/absorbs every other event via reference equality/has exactly two escape hatches) **+ 2 more on `reduceTransportRecovery`**, including one **regression test encoding the diagnosed freeze mechanism directly**: dispatch `'stopped'` against a mid-backoff `reconnecting` state, then hammer it with 50 alternating `fatalError`/`firstSegment`/`stalled`/`playlistNotReady`/`unsupported` events (a pure-state-machine model of exactly what the live trace showed arriving after Stop) and assert `phase`/`attempt` never move off `{stopped, 0}` — before this fix, the equivalent sequence is what let `firstSegment` keep zeroing the backoff counter forever.

**Tests: 397 → 408** (11 new: 8 `shared/player/player-recovery.spec.ts` + 3 `core/poll-scheduler.spec.ts`). No new component-level specs for `Player`/`LivePage`/`AssetDetailPage` themselves — matches this app's own standing precedent (no `player.spec.ts` existed before this cycle either; the timer-leak fix and the `stopped`/`explicitlyStopped`/`hasBeenLive` wiring are exercised by the full production build twice from clean plus the pure-logic specs backing the state machine they drive).

**Bundle sizes** (`ng build --configuration production`, before → after; before = E-b's own recorded "after" numbers above):
- **Initial total: 302.86 kB raw / 84.47 kB transfer → 303.05 kB raw / 84.53 kB transfer (+0.19 kB raw / +0.06 kB transfer)** — `core/poll-scheduler.ts`'s in-flight-guard additions (`isThenable`, the `run()` method, the `inFlight` bookkeeping) land eager because `PollScheduler` is `providedIn: 'root'` and already shell-loaded; `shared/player/player-recovery.ts`'s new `stopped` phase/event is reachable only through `Player`, which stays lazy (never `providedIn: 'root'`) — grep-verified absent from every initial chunk, same check every past cycle here has made. Still comfortably inside the 360 kB error budget; 3.05 kB over the pre-existing 300 kB warning one (up from E-b's 2.86 kB over — the same already-flagged, not-hidden posture every cycle since U2's CDK addition has carried forward).
- **`live`: 17.69 kB / 4.91 kB → 17.94 kB / 4.97 kB (+0.25 kB / +0.06 kB)** — the `[stopped]` binding + `explicitlyStopped`/`hasBeenLive`/`stopped` signals and the new template branch.
- **`asset-detail`: 27.45 kB / 7.17 kB → 27.72 kB / 7.23 kB (+0.27 kB / +0.06 kB)** — the identical shape of change as `live`.
- **`devices`/`map`/`replay`/`debug`/`wall`/`settings`/`leaflet-src`/`hls`: unchanged** (68.07/16.53, 24.76/7.04, 19.48/5.93, 13.96/4.33, 12.35/3.92, 11.38/3.42, 149.55/37.58, 517.70/132.37) — none of their own source changed this cycle, and none render `<vision-player>` with the new `[stopped]` binding (`WallTile`/`LiveDock` were deliberately left unwired — see above).

**Deviations from the task brief, with reasons:**
- **The in-flight guard and the reconnect-timer-leak fix are both defensive hardening for a root cause that is confirmed to live mostly server-side (S-a's scope), not proof the frontend alone caused the freeze.** Both are kept regardless: they're independently correct (a poller pile-up against *any* slow backend, an orphaned timer against *any* flapping connection, not just this specific bug), low-risk, and directly named as valuable by the coordinating brief even after the backend evidence came in.
- **A rapid "flapping connection defeats backoff via the legitimate `firstSegment`-resets-`attempt` rule" scenario *unrelated to an explicit stop* is a known, still-open gap, not fixed this cycle.** Solving it well needs new state (some notion of "stayed connected long enough to count as a real recovery, not just a blip") that changes `reduceRecovery`'s tested, documented `firstSegment`/backoff contract — real design work, and out of scope for a stop-freeze/stopped-state cycle. The `stopped` terminal state fixes the *reported* bug completely (the loop cannot continue once the player is told to stop trying, regardless of *why* it was looping) without needing that larger change; left as a note for a future cycle if a similar loop is ever reported against a stream nobody asked to stop. **Closed by S-c** (docs/MVP2-PLAN.md §S) — see the dedicated S-c Status entry below: a separate `PacingState` layered on top of, not inside, `reduceRecovery` (so this section's own `firstSegment`/backoff contract stays exactly as documented here — nothing above this line was rewritten to close the gap).
- **`WallTile`/`LiveDock` were not given their own `[stopped]` wiring** — see the "deliberately left unwired" paragraph above for why both are already safe by construction.

## S-c — cross-transport reconnect pacing, closing S-b's flagged gap (docs/MVP2-PLAN.md §S)

S-b fixed the *reported* stop-freeze completely, but its own Deviations honestly flagged one gap as still open, unrelated to an explicit stop: "a rapid flapping connection defeats backoff via the legitimate `firstSegment`-resets-`attempt` rule … solving it well needs new state … real design work, out of scope for a stop-freeze/stopped-state cycle." Live evidence against a **zombie stream** (listed by the API but not actually publishing — WHEP POST dies with status 0, HLS's playlist never appears) showed exactly the predicted shape: the player cycled indefinitely at roughly one attempt a second — WHEP OPTIONS+POST fail, HLS attach fails, a new cycle starts and re-tries WHEP with backoff effectively pinned at the shortest delay.

**New files**: none. **Reshaped**: `shared/player/player-recovery.ts` (+`.spec.ts` — a new, additive `PacingState`/`advancePacing`/`cyclePacingDelayMs`/`shouldAttemptWhep` section; `RecoveryState`/`reduceRecovery`/`TransportRecoveryState`/`reduceTransportRecovery` are **byte-for-byte unchanged** — every one of the 408 pre-existing specs in that file passes verbatim, unmodified, confirmed by running the full suite before writing a single new test), `shared/player/player.ts` (wiring only — a new `pacingState` signal and the call sites below; no new template/markup, no new inputs/outputs).

### The three pacing rules, concretely

`PacingState {cycleAttempt, playingSinceMs, lastWhepAttemptAtMs}` is a **second, independent** pure state machine `shared/player/player.ts` layers on top of `TransportRecoveryState` — not a rewrite of it. `RecoveryState.attempt` (reset immediately by `firstSegment`, exactly as before) still drives the chip's local, per-cycle-transition bookkeeping; `PacingState.cycleAttempt` is the new counter that actually drives the delay/WHEP-retry decisions, and it survives everything `RecoveryState.attempt` doesn't:

1. **Cross-cycle escalating backoff.** A "cycle" is one WHEP attempt (if due) immediately followed by an HLS attempt on pre-play failure — U3's permanent-per-cycle fallback semantics, unchanged, no extra delay between the two halves of the same cycle. `cycleAttempt` bumps once per **cycle** that ends without a sustained recovery (`advancePacing(state, 'cycleFailed', now)`, dispatched from `scheduleReconnect` and from `handleWhepFailure`'s "already played, retry webrtc" branch) and is read by `cyclePacingDelayMs` (a thin wrapper reusing `reconnectDelayMs`'s exact 1s→2s→4s→8s→16s→30s-capped schedule) for the delay before the *next* cycle — a WHEP→HLS fallback within a cycle, or a fresh cycle restart, never resets it on its own. The cold-start playlist poll folds under the same pacing: `scheduleColdStartRetry` keeps the fixed, gentle `COLD_START_RETRY_DELAY_MS` (1.5s) poll only while `cycleAttempt === 0` (this saga has never failed anything — "a never-yet-live stream politely waiting is allowed its gentle poll"); the moment a cold-start miss is observed with `cycleAttempt > 0` (this saga already failed a transport — a WHEP fallback, or an earlier HLS attempt), that miss is itself dispatched as `'cycleFailed'` and the *next* poll goes through the same escalating `beginNextCycle` path as any other reconnect ("a stream that HAS failed transports escalates") — closing the half of the gap where `isColdStartMiss`'s own "never played yet" phase check (`connecting`/`waiting`) could otherwise keep a saga stuck at the flat 1.5s cadence forever, since a demoted-to-HLS stream that's never reached `playing` still reads as "never played" by that check alone.
2. **WHEP retry damping.** `shouldAttemptWhep(pacing, hasWhepUrl, nowMs)` gates whether the *next* cycle even tries WHEP: `true` unconditionally for a saga's first attempt (`lastWhepAttemptAtMs` starts `null` — a fresh attach always tries the better transport first), then `false` until `WHEP_RETRY_COOLDOWN_MS` (60s) has elapsed since the last attempt. A wall-clock cooldown was chosen over a fixed cycle-count (e.g. "every 4th cycle") specifically because it bounds the *absolute* request rate directly, independent of how the backoff schedule itself is shaped — a future change to `reconnectDelayMs`'s curve can't silently change the WHEP-damping rate too. `beginNextCycle` (a new private method every scheduled retry now goes through, replacing direct `beginAttach` calls) is what actually makes "a later cycle re-tries WHEP" possible at all — before this cycle, once `reduceTransportRecovery` demoted a saga to `hls`, nothing in `shared/player/player.ts` ever routed a subsequent retry back through `initialTransportState(true)`, so WHEP was, in practice, permanently abandoned for the rest of the attach lifetime, not just "damped". **A genuine sustained recovery re-arms it immediately** (rule 3's `healthTick` reset also clears `lastWhepAttemptAtMs`) — a connection that has proven itself healthy again doesn't have to wait out a stale cooldown before its next reconnect is allowed to try the better transport once more.
3. **Sustained-playback reset — the gap itself.** `'healthTick'`, dispatched roughly once every `WATCHDOG_TICK_MS` (2s) by the existing HLS/native and WHEP watchdog intervals (`tickPacingHealth()`, a new shared helper — reuses those timers rather than starting a third one) whenever `phase === 'playing'`, only resets `cycleAttempt` to `0` once the *unbroken* streak since the last `'playing'` event (`FRAG_BUFFERED`/native `'playing'`/WHEP `ontrack`, each now also dispatching `advancePacing(…, 'playing', now)`) has lasted `SUSTAINED_PLAYBACK_RESET_MS` (5s). A connection that flaps — plays briefly, fails again before the window elapses — dispatches `'cycleFailed'` first, which clears the in-progress streak marker outright; the reset is never reached, and the *next* failure resumes escalating from wherever `cycleAttempt` already was, not from 1. This is the literal fix for S-b's flagged gap, verified by a dedicated spec (`'flapping stream — fails, briefly plays under the sustained window, fails again'`) replaying exactly that shape.

**Why this couldn't live inside `reduceRecovery` itself**: that function's `firstSegment`-resets-`attempt`-immediately behavior is tested, documented, and depended on by 20+ pre-existing specs (the chip's own "just reached playing" bookkeeping is a genuinely different, correct concept from "has this connection proven itself over time") — changing it would have broken baseline coverage for no reason, since the fix only needed a *second*, independent counter with different reset semantics, not a change to the first one. This is also why `reduceTransportRecovery`'s own webrtc→hls fallback branch (`reduceRecovery(INITIAL_RECOVERY_STATE, 'attachStarted')`, resetting `RecoveryState.attempt` to `0` on every fallback) was **left exactly as it was** — that reset is still correct and desired for the *local*, per-cycle-transition state the chip reads (`phase`/the "attempt N" hint now reads `pacingState().cycleAttempt` instead, for accuracy — see `shared/player/player.ts`'s `reconnectHint`); the cross-cycle counter that must **not** reset there is a different field entirely.

### Request rate against a zombie stream, before vs. after

**Before** (the live evidence this cycle started from): a full cycle — WHEP `OPTIONS` (CORS preflight) + WHEP `POST` (dies, status 0) + one HLS playlist `GET` (404/absent) — repeating roughly once a second, indefinitely: **≈180 requests/minute**, steady-state, never throttling further.

**After**: the very first cycle is unchanged (still tries WHEP immediately — a fresh attach always does). From there, `cyclePacingDelayMs` escalates 1s → 2s → 4s → 8s → 16s → 30s (reaching the cap within ~31s of cumulative delay, by the 6th cycle) and stays at the 30s floor indefinitely; `shouldAttemptWhep`'s 60s cooldown means roughly one in every two-to-three cycles once capped actually re-tries WHEP (the other cycles go straight to HLS). Concretely, over a representative ~3-minute run (10 cycles, replayed exactly in `player-recovery.spec.ts`'s own zombie-stream spec): 10 HLS `GET`s + 3 WHEP attempts (`OPTIONS`+`POST` each, 6 requests) = 16 requests / ~3 minutes ≈ **≈5 requests/minute** once backoff is fully escalated — roughly a **30–35× reduction** in steady-state request volume, with WHEP itself down from one attempt a second to one every 60–120s. Multiple simultaneously-dead streams (the task's own "request carousel" scenario) now each independently decay to this same low steady rate instead of each contributing a constant ~3 req/s to the backend.

### Tests: 408 → 426 (18 new, all in `shared/player/player-recovery.spec.ts`)

- `advancePacing` — 9 cases (`whepAttempted`/`cycleFailed`/`playing`/`healthTick` each, including: repeated `cycleFailed` keeps escalating across 6 calls with no cross-cycle reset; a second `'playing'` while already streaking doesn't push the streak's start time later — WHEP's `ontrack` can refire on renegotiation; `healthTick` is a no-op both before the threshold and, via reference equality, once already at rest; `healthTick` does nothing while not playing).
- `cyclePacingDelayMs` — 1 case (mirrors `reconnectDelayMs`'s exact schedule at several `cycleAttempt` values, including the 30s cap holding at `cycleAttempt = 40`).
- `shouldAttemptWhep` — 4 cases (no WHEP URL at all; the saga's first attempt is always allowed; damped immediately after an attempt; allowed again once the cooldown fully elapses).
- **Scenario replays, matching the task's own required shapes** — 4 cases: the zombie-stream cycle (asserts the exact `[1_000, 2_000, 4_000, 8_000, 16_000, 30_000, …]` delay sequence and that WHEP attempts land ≤3 times across 10 cycles, each ≥60s apart); the flapping stream (fails, plays briefly under the 5s sustained window, fails again — asserts `cycleAttempt` never drops back to a fresh 1s delay); genuine recovery (5 failures, then a streak sustained past the 5s threshold — asserts both `cycleAttempt` and `lastWhepAttemptAtMs` reset together, and a later reconnect is immediately allowed to retry WHEP); the stopped state (`reduceTransportRecovery(…, 'stopped')` still absorbs exactly per S-b's own contract, unaffected by any pacing history — defense-in-depth, since pacing has no `stopped`-shaped event of its own at all).
- No new component-level spec for `shared/player/player.ts`'s own wiring (`beginNextCycle`, the `tickPacingHealth`/`pacingState.update` call sites) — consistent with this app's standing precedent for `Player` (no `player.spec.ts` has ever existed; every prior cycle's own Status entry — U3, V-b, S-b — makes the identical call); exercised by the full production build (twice, from clean) plus the pure-logic specs backing the state machine every wiring call site drives.

### Bundle sizes (`ng build --configuration production`, before → after; before = S-b's own recorded "after" numbers above)

- **Initial total: 303.05 kB raw / 84.53 kB transfer → 303.05 kB raw / 84.54 kB transfer — flat, within gzip-estimate rounding.** `PacingState`/`advancePacing`/`cyclePacingDelayMs`/`shouldAttemptWhep` are reachable only through `Player`, which stays lazy (never `providedIn: 'root'`) — grep-verified absent from every initial chunk (searched for the `'whepAttempted'` event-string literal, present in exactly one lazy/shared chunk and nowhere in any of the seven initial ones), the identical method every past cycle's own Status entry here has used.
- **`live`/`asset-detail`/`wall`/`map`/`devices`/`replay`/`debug`/`settings`/`leaflet-src`/`hls`: all read unchanged from S-b's own last recorded values** (17.94/4.97, 27.72/7.2×, 12.35/3.92, 24.76/7.0×, 68.07/16.5×, 19.48/5.93, 13.96/4.33, 11.38/3.42, 149.55/37.58, 517.70/132.37 — sub-0.03 kB transfer differences are gzip-estimate rounding noise, not attributable to any changed source). This cycle's actual growth (`shared/player/player.ts`'s new call sites, `player-recovery.ts`'s new pacing section) lands in the unnamed shared chunks `Player`'s code is already extracted into, not in any individual host page's own named chunk — same "attribution by elimination" caveat U2's/U3's/V-b's own Status entries already carry for that bucket; without a saved prior `dist/` snapshot to diff against (chunk hashes aren't stable build-to-build), no further byte-level attribution is claimed here.

### Deviations from the task brief, with reasons

- **U3's "permanent-per-cycle WHEP→HLS fallback" was, in practice, permanent-per-*attach-lifetime*, not per-cycle, before this task** — once `reduceTransportRecovery` demoted a saga to `hls`, nothing in `shared/player/player.ts` ever tried WHEP again for that same `src`/`whepUrl` pair short of a brand new `reattach()` (a genuinely new input value). Deliverable 2 ("a later genuine HLS recovery may also re-enable a WHEP upgrade attempt") requires *some* later cycle to be able to try WHEP again at all — this task therefore had to build that capability (`beginNextCycle`, replacing direct `beginAttach` calls from every scheduled retry) before it could damp it. This is additive, not a behavior removal: within any single cycle, a WHEP pre-play failure still falls straight to HLS with no extra delay, exactly as before.
- **The 60s WHEP cooldown is a wall-clock figure, not the task's own alternative suggestion of "every Nth cycle"** — see rule 2's own writeup above for the justification (bounds the absolute request rate directly, independent of the backoff schedule's own shape).
- **The escalating pacing intentionally does not change what the `'waiting'`/cold-start phase *displays*** (`overlay-status`'s `'waiting'` case in `shared/player/player.ts`'s template is unchanged — still "Waiting for the first segment…", no visible attempt count) even once a stuck cold-start poll has started escalating under the hood per rule 1 — only `'reconnecting'`'s hint text reads `pacingState()`. Judged out of scope: the task's own done-when criterion is about request-rate/backoff correctness, not chip wording, and `isColdStartMiss`'s phase-classification logic (what counts as "cold start" at all) was deliberately left untouched to keep this a pacing-layer change, not a reducer-classification one.
- **No dedicated component-level spec for `shared/player/player.ts`** — see the Tests section above; consistent with, not a departure from, this app's own established precedent.
