## C-b — Fly: the operator cockpit + the shared IndexedDB map-tile cache (docs/MVP3-PLAN.md §C-b)

The user directive behind MVP3 ("rethink the UI … around the app as a **command point**") splits into two personas across two UI cycles: this one builds the operator's page — *flies ONE drone at a time; everything else is noise* — and, per the plan's own Build rule, the shared map-tile cache every map in the app (including this cockpit's own inset) inherits from. C-c (Command, the manager's page) is next, pending, out of this cycle's scope.

**New files**: `features/fly/fly-logic.ts` (+`.spec.ts`, 15 cases), `features/fly/fly.ts`/`.html`/`.css`, `features/fly/fly-osd.ts`, `shared/map/tile-cache-logic.ts` (+`.spec.ts`, 9 cases), `shared/map/tile-cache-db.ts`. **Moved**: `features/live/detections-strip.ts` → `shared/player/detections-strip.ts` (no behavior change, see its own doc comment). **Reshaped**: `app.routes.ts` (`/fly`, new default), `app.ts`/`app.html` (Fly tab, brand link), `core/telemetry/telemetry-logic.ts` (+`.spec.ts` — `batterySeverity`/`telemetryAgeSeverity`), `core/fleet/device-logic.ts` (+`.spec.ts` — `videoDevices`), `core/settings/settings-store.ts` (+`.spec.ts` — `flyAssetId`), `features/live/telemetry-osd.ts` (now imports `batterySeverity` instead of a private copy), `features/live/live.ts` (updated import path for the moved strip), `features/devices/devices.ts` (`?addSource=1` query-param input), `shared/map/leaflet-loader.ts` (the tile cache — see its own MODULE.md section above for the full design writeup, not restated here).

**Reuse-first, every new component justified** (the task's own binding requirement):

1. **`<vision-fly-osd>` (`features/fly/fly-osd.ts`) — new.** The one genuinely new visual component. `features/live/telemetry-osd.ts` (`<vision-telemetry-osd>`) already shows battery/altitude/heading/position, but as a full side-rail card (a rotating compass dial, a wide battery bar) built for `/live`'s collapsible rail — the wrong shape for chrome that must stay legible in a thin strip permanently overlaid on live video. `FlyOsd` reuses every underlying *value* and derivation (`TelemetryStore`, DI-shared exactly like `TelemetryOsd`; `core/telemetry/telemetry-logic.ts#batterySeverity`, `core/stream-info-logic.ts#formatLatency`/`transportLabel`) and adds one new pure function (`telemetryAgeSeverity`, a three-tier escalation the plan specifically asked for, tested); the *template* is a new, compact, single-row chip bar because no existing component has that shape. See the file's own doc comment for the full reasoning.
2. **`features/fly/fly.ts`/`.html`/`.css` — new (a page, not optional).** Every other page in this app is its own file; there was no existing "generic cockpit" page to reuse. The file itself is thin composition: `<vision-player>`, `<vision-live-map>`, `<vision-detections-strip>`, `TelemetryStore`/`DetectionsStore`/`EventsStore`, plus a cockpit-layout CSS layer — see its own MODULE.md section above for the full breakdown of what it does and doesn't add.
3. **`features/fly/fly-logic.ts` — new (pure logic file, not a component).** Mirrors this app's own standing "one `*-logic.ts` per page" convention (`map-logic.ts`, `replay-logic.ts`, …) — not a new *pattern*, just this page's own instance of an established one.
4. **`shared/map/tile-cache-logic.ts`/`shared/map/tile-cache-db.ts` — new (infra, not a component).** No existing module did IndexedDB-backed anything; this is genuinely new capability the plan's own Build rule asks for, landed as shared infra in `shared/map/leaflet-loader.ts` per that rule's own instruction.

**Everything else is reuse, not new**: `<vision-player>` (WHEP-first, self-recovering, the `stopped` terminal state — unmodified), `<vision-live-map>` (unmodified — the map inset), `<vision-detections-strip>` (moved to `shared/player/`, not rewritten), `TelemetryStore`/`DetectionsStore`/`EventsStore` (identical DI-sharing/activate-release idioms as `LivePage`/`AssetDetailPage`/`WallPage`), the inline-confirm-card idiom for Stop's confirmation (`asset-detail.html`/`devices.html`'s own established markup/CSS pattern, not a new dialog component), `filterEvents`/`capitalizeLabel`/`formatConfidence` (the events ticker's own derivation, identical to `AssetDetailPage`'s offline-branch), `videoDevices`/`telemetryDevices` (capability filters, mirroring each other's shape), `PollScheduler`/`SettingsStore`/`VisionApi`/`ToastService` (every store this app already has).

**Picker → cockpit, concretely**: see the dedicated `features/fly/**` MODULE.md section above (First visit vs. remembered / Multi-device asset / Deliberately-stopped state / Stop confirms / OSD chip bar / Events ticker / Map inset / Detections strip / Keyboard / Watch mode / "Fill the viewport" / the speed gap) — not restated here to avoid duplicating the same writeup twice in one file.

**Tile cache, concretely**: see the dedicated `shared/map/leaflet-loader.ts` MODULE.md section above (key, cap/eviction, the two-object-store IndexedDB glue, the offline-grid fallback, the cache-on-demand-only discipline, the verified CORS dependency).

**Tests: 426 → 461** (35 new — verified: 426 + 35 = 461, matches exactly):
- `features/fly/fly-logic.spec.ts` — 15 cases: `sortAssetsForPicker` (4, incl. stability/no-mutation), `resolveActiveAssetId` (5, incl. requested-wins-over-remembered, a remembered id that no longer resolves), `latestFinishedUsage` (3), `isWatchMode` (2, incl. other truthy-looking strings correctly refusing), `cycleBoxesMode` (1).
- `shared/map/tile-cache-logic.spec.ts` — 9 cases: `tileCacheKey` (2, incl. per-layer distinctness), `planEviction` (7: under-cap no-op, oldest-first multi-entry eviction, keep-evicting-until-under-cap, overwrite-excluded-from-double-counting, a single incoming tile alone exceeding the cap evicts everything without throwing, empty cache, default cap).
- `core/telemetry/telemetry-logic.spec.ts` — +7 (`batterySeverity` 4, `telemetryAgeSeverity` 3, both boundary-tested at their exact thresholds).
- `core/fleet/device-logic.spec.ts` — +2 (`videoDevices`: keeps only VIDEO-capable in order, empty for none).
- `core/settings/settings-store.spec.ts` — +2 (`flyAssetId`: default/persist-across-reload, ignores a non-string persisted value).
- **No page-component-level spec for `FlyPage`/`FlyOsd`** — consistent with, not a departure from, this app's own standing precedent (every prior UI cycle's own Status entry makes the identical call for its own new page); exercised by the full production build (twice, from clean, AOT `strictTemplates: true`) plus the pure logic each interaction leans on. **No live-stack visual screenshot was taken either** — same honest, precedented deviation R-b's own Status entry already made ("judged out of proportion to this cycle's time budget"): a real screenshot needs a booted backend + mediamtx + a streaming asset with telemetry, out of proportion for a frontend-only cycle: the CSS reuses this app's own already-shipped, visually-verified primitives (`.chip`, `.btn`, `.card`, the `rgb(0 0 0 / 55%)` + `backdrop-filter: blur()` overlay-badge language `shared/player/player.ts`'s own `.badge` and `shared/map/live-map.css`'s own `.badge` already established) rather than inventing new visual language, which is the mitigation for that gap.

**Bundle sizes** (`ng build --configuration production`, before → after; before = S-c's own recorded "after" numbers above):
- **Initial total: 303.05 kB raw / 84.54 kB transfer → 303.18 kB raw / 84.56 kB transfer (+0.13 kB raw / +0.02 kB transfer)** — `SettingsStore.flyAssetId`'s own few lines of restore/persist code, the only genuinely eager addition this cycle makes (`SettingsStore` is `providedIn: 'root'`, already shell-loaded). Every other new module (`FlyPage`, `FlyOsd`, `fly-logic.ts`, `tile-cache-logic.ts`, `tile-cache-db.ts`) is reachable only through the new lazy `fly` chunk or `shared/map/leaflet-loader.ts` (itself only ever reached from a lazy map host) — grep-verified absent from every initial chunk. Still comfortably inside the 360 kB error budget; 3.18 kB over the pre-existing 300 kB warning one (up from S-c's 3.05 kB over — the same already-flagged, not-hidden posture every cycle since U2's CDK addition has carried forward).
- **New `fly` chunk: 22.99 kB raw / 6.29 kB transfer** — the whole cockpit (picker + player wiring + OSD + ticker + secondary tiles + keyboard/fullscreen + Stop confirm), comparable to `map`'s own 24.76/7.04 and smaller than `devices`'s 68.18/16.60 despite composing more pieces, since it reuses `<vision-player>`/`<vision-live-map>`/`<vision-detections-strip>` rather than rebuilding any of them.
- **`live`: 17.94 kB / 4.97 kB → 16.78 kB / 4.74 kB (−1.16 kB / −0.23 kB, a decrease)** — `DetectionsStrip`'s own code moved out to the shared `shared/player/detections-strip.ts` chunk (now reachable from both `live` and `fly`, so the bundler extracts it rather than duplicating it inside `live` alone), and `telemetry-osd.ts`'s battery-severity logic shrank to a single shared import.
- **`devices`: 68.07 kB / 16.53 kB → 68.18 kB / 16.60 kB (+0.11 kB / +0.07 kB)** — the new `addSource` input + the constructor check that opens the Add-source flow on `?addSource=1`.
- **`leaflet-src`: unchanged at 149.55 kB / 37.58 kB** — confirms the tile cache's own code lives in the *shared* `shared/map/leaflet-loader.ts` (already lazy, already shared across every map host), not inside the further-lazy Leaflet package chunk itself; no new Leaflet APIs are used.
- **`map`/`asset-detail`/`replay`/`wall`/`debug`/`settings`/`hls`: unchanged or within a few tens of bytes of esbuild minification noise** (24.76/7.04, 27.76/7.25 [+0.04/+0.02 vs. S-c's 27.72/7.2×, not attributable to any changed source in this cycle — same noise pattern R-b's/S-c's own entries already documented for this bucket], 19.48/5.95, 12.35/3.92, 13.96/4.33, 11.38/3.42, 517.70/132.37) — none of their own source changed this cycle.

**Deviations from the task brief, with reasons:**
- **The top-level nav gained a "Fly" tab but was not rewritten into the plan's own end-state "Fly · Command · Assets · Settings" job-oriented IA** — `Command` doesn't exist until C-c ships, and renaming `Devices`→`Assets`/dropping `Map`/`Debug` from top-level nav ahead of that would strand their only entry point; see `app.ts`'s own doc comment for the full reasoning. This is the honest middle step the plan's own "no capability loss, links keep working" instruction calls for, not the finished IA — C-c is the natural point to revisit this array wholesale once Command exists to slot in.
- **No speed chip in the OSD** — no current telemetry sample carries a ground-speed reading anywhere in the wire contract (`TelemetrySample` has no such field); see `features/fly/fly-osd.ts`'s own doc comment. Per this codebase's "implements what current APIs already serve" convention (`shared/player/stream-info-panel.ts`'s resolution/FPS gap is the precedent), omitted rather than fabricated.
- **The asset picker/switcher list (`listAssets()`) is fetched once + refreshed on the same 5s poll as the active asset's own details, not on a dedicated faster cadence** — a newly-registered asset elsewhere in the app can take up to 5s to appear in Fly's own switcher/picker. Judged proportionate: it's the same cadence `AssetDetailPage`'s own asset poll already uses, and a faster cadence for a list that changes rarely (assets are registered, not spawned every few seconds) wasn't worth a second poll registration.
- **`?asset=`/`?watch=1` query-param inputs have no current caller anywhere in this app yet** — they exist because C-c's own drill-down ("click anything → that asset's cockpit in watch mode") needs exactly this shape, and building the binding now (query-param-to-input, which needs no route-table change — see Routes above) means C-c doesn't have to touch `app.routes.ts` or `FlyPage` itself to use it, only `router.navigate(['/fly'], {queryParams: {asset, watch: 1}})`. Not speculative scope creep: the plan's own §C-b bullet names watch mode explicitly ("Watch mode `?watch=1`: hides Start/Stop (C-c consumes this)").
- **No dedicated unit test for the keyboard-shortcut `document.keydown` listener or the Fullscreen API wiring** — imperative browser-event/API glue, consistent with this app's own standing precedent (`LivePage`'s identical `M`-only listener has never had one either); the pure logic each key press ultimately calls (`cycleBoxesMode`, `isWatchMode`, `collapseOverlays`'s priority order is straightforward enough to read directly) is otherwise covered or trivially inspectable.

## C-c — Command: the manager dashboard (docs/MVP3-PLAN.md §C-c) — MVP3 complete

The second of MVP3's two persona pages, and the cycle the plan's own milestone names directly: "a manager opens the app and knows within five seconds which of 100 assets needs attention." Built on C-a's `GET /api/fleet/summary` (backend) and C-b's Fly cockpit (`/fly?asset=<id>&watch=1`, this cycle's own drill-down target).

**New files**: `features/command/command-logic.ts` (+`.spec.ts`, 33 cases), `features/command/command.ts`/`.html`/`.css`, `features/command/live-strip-tile.ts`, `shared/ui/events-rail.ts`/`.html`/`.css`. **Moved** (see each file's own MODULE.md entry above for the full "why move" reasoning): `features/map/map-logic.ts` → `core/map/map-logic.ts`, `features/map/map-store.ts` → `core/map/map-store.ts`, `features/map/fleet-map.ts`/`.html`/`.css` → `shared/map/fleet-map.ts`/`.html`/`.css`, `features/map/live-dock.ts`/`.html`/`.css` → `shared/map/live-dock.ts`/`.html`/`.css`. **Reshaped**: `core/api/models.ts` (+`CategoryCounts`/`AssetAttention`/`FleetSummary`), `core/api/vision-api.ts` (+`.spec.ts` — `fleetSummary(includeArchived)`, `snapshotUrl(streamId)`), `app.routes.ts` (`/command`, idle-preloaded like every real tab), `app.ts`/`app.html`/`app.css` (the plan's own end-state nav IA — see below), `features/map/map.ts` (import-path updates only, behavior unchanged), `features/wall/wall.ts`/`.html`/`.css` (events rail extracted to `shared/ui/events-rail.ts` — behavior unchanged, see that file's own entry above), plus doc-comment path fixes in `features/fly/fly-logic.ts`, `features/replay/replay-map.ts`, `shared/map/leaflet-loader.ts`, `shared/map/live-map.ts`, `shared/player/player.ts`, `core/settings/settings-store.ts` (all cross-references to the moved files, no behavior change).

**Reuse-first, every new component justified** (the task's own binding requirement):

1. **`features/command/live-strip-tile.ts` (`LiveStripTile`) — new.** The one genuinely new *visual* component this cycle adds. No existing component polls a snapshot `<img>`; this one is a thin composition of an `<img>` + the exact `IntersectionObserver`/`PollScheduler` visibility idiom `features/wall/wall-tile.ts` already established for its own telemetry/detections polls (off-screen things stop polling) — a second instance of an established pattern, not a new one. See its own doc comment for the full reasoning.
2. **`features/command/command.ts`/`.html`/`.css` — new (a page, not optional).** Every other page in this app is its own file; there was no existing "generic manager dashboard" page to reuse. The page itself is thin composition over five sections, four of which reuse an existing piece unmodified (`<vision-fleet-map>`, `<vision-live-dock>`, `<vision-events-rail>`) or with zero new polling (the attention queue, pure `computed()`s over one already-fetched signal) — see its own MODULE.md section above for the full per-section breakdown.
3. **`features/command/command-logic.ts` — new (pure logic file, not a component).** Mirrors this app's own standing "one `*-logic.ts` per page" convention (`fly-logic.ts`, `map-logic.ts`, `replay-logic.ts`, …) — this page's own instance of an established pattern, not a new one.
4. **`shared/ui/events-rail.ts`/`.html`/`.css` — extraction, not invention.** Byte-for-byte the same feature `features/wall/wall.ts`/`.html` already had (filters, 20-row cap, row content, click target) — moved to a shared `ui/` home for the identical reason `core/fleet/device-logic.ts`, `core/fleet/warehouse-logic.ts`, `shared/map/live-map.ts`, and `shared/player/detections-strip.ts` were each moved before it: a second page needs the same thing, and this codebase has no precedent for one page importing another page's module.
5. **`shared/map/fleet-map.ts`, `shared/map/live-dock.ts`, `core/map/map-store.ts`, `core/map/map-logic.ts` — relocation, not invention.** The plan's own instruction ("reuse the existing FleetMap component as-is, with its event markers, layer switcher, LiveDock") requires Command to embed `features/map/**`'s fleet-map machinery as a second consumer — rather than break this codebase's own "no cross-page import" precedent for the first time, all four followed the established move-to-`core`/`shared` pattern instead (`FleetMap`/`LiveDock` are components → `shared/map/`, matching `live-map.ts`/`detections-strip.ts`'s own precedent exactly; `FleetMapStore`/`map-logic.ts` are a page-scoped store + its pure logic → `core/map/`, matching `telemetry-store.ts`/`telemetry-logic.ts`'s shape). Zero behavior change for `MapPage` — verified by the full existing `map-store.spec.ts`/`map-logic.spec.ts` suites passing unmodified at their new home, and by the production build (see Bundle sizes below: `map`'s own chunk shrank because this code moved to a *shared* chunk, not because anything about it changed).

**Everything else is reuse, not new**: `<vision-fleet-map>`/`<vision-live-dock>` (unmodified — see point 5), `<vision-events-rail>` (unmodified — see point 4), `EventsStore`/`FleetStore`/`PollScheduler`/`VisionApi` (every store this app already has), the `router.navigate(['/fly'], {queryParams: {asset, watch: 1}})` one-liner C-b's own done note pinned exactly for this drill-down, `core/telemetry/telemetry-logic.ts#TELEMETRY_AGE_RED_SECONDS` (reused directly for the telemetry-stale threshold rather than re-derived), `core/stream-info-logic.ts#formatDuration` (the age label's own rendering), CDK virtual scroll (`features/devices/devices.ts`'s own precedent, horizontal here).

**Composition, concretely**: see the dedicated `features/command/**`/`shared/ui/events-rail.ts` MODULE.md sections above (severity rules and their exact boundaries, queue ordering, live strip membership/visibility gating, fleet map wiring, readiness tiles, events rail) — not restated here to avoid duplicating the same writeup twice in one file.

**Severity rules, as implemented** (`command-logic.ts`): three independent triggers — `batteryPercent < 20%` (→ `battery-low`, warning) escalating to `< 10%` (→ `battery-critical`, critical); `telemetryAgeMs > 10 000ms` (`TELEMETRY_AGE_RED_SECONDS*1000`, reused not re-derived) **while `streaming === true` only** (→ `telemetry-stale`, critical); `openEventCount > 0` (→ `open-events`, warning). A row's rank is the highest-ranked triggered reason (`battery-critical`/`telemetry-stale` share the top two ranks — both read as "this drone may not come back", matching the plan's own persona text ranking telemetry/link staleness right behind the video feed itself); the queue sorts by that rank, then by reason count (more simultaneous problems first), then alphabetically — fully deterministic, boundary-tested at every threshold (exactly 20%/10%/10 000ms all land on the *lower*-severity side, matching the spec's own "< 20%"/"< 10%"/"> 10s" wording).

**Scale duty verified (N=100): exactly one summary request per cycle, snapshots for visible tiles only.**
- **Reasoned from code, not just tested**: `CommandPage.refreshSummary()` is the **only** call to `VisionApi.fleetSummary()` anywhere in `features/command/**` (grep-verified) — one `PollScheduler.schedule(5_000, …)` registration in the constructor, no per-asset loop, no per-category loop. `attentionRows`/`stripAssets`/`readinessTiles`/`totalAssetsCount`/`totalStreamingCount` are all pure `computed()`s in `command.ts` reading the one `summary` signal through `command-logic.ts`'s pure functions — none of those functions (verified by reading `command-logic.ts` in full: no `inject`, no `VisionApi`, no `fetch`) can issue a request, so this holds regardless of `assets.length`.
- **Snapshots, per visible tile only**: `LiveStripTile` is only ever instantiated by `*cdkVirtualFor` over `stripAssets()` inside `<cdk-virtual-scroll-viewport orientation="horizontal">` — at N=100 with (say) 100 simultaneously-streaming assets, the DOM only ever holds however many tiles fit the strip's viewport width plus CDK's own small render buffer (a handful, not 100); each mounted tile additionally gates its own 5s snapshot poll behind `shouldPollSnapshot(visible, hasStreamId)`, `visible` from its own `IntersectionObserver` — a tile inside CDK's buffer but not actually on-screen still doesn't poll. A tile scrolled fully out of the buffer is destroyed by `cdkVirtualFor` itself, tearing down its `PollScheduler` registration via `DestroyRef.onDestroy`. Net effect: snapshot request count is bounded by "visible strip width", never by total fleet size or total streaming count.
- **Synthetic-data spec, per the task's own "suffices" allowance**: `command-logic.spec.ts`'s `buildAttentionQueue`/`streamingAssets` each carry a dedicated "holds at scale (N=100)" case building 100 synthetic `AssetAttention` rows and asserting the selection logic stays correct (10/100 critically low → exactly 10 queue rows, all `critical`; 25/100 streaming → exactly 25 strip members) — encoding the scale claim as a real, running assertion rather than prose alone.
- **Honest carve-out, documented not hidden**: the embedded `<vision-fleet-map>` (bullet 3) brings its own **already-existing** polling — a 5s `GET /api/assets` poll plus a 2s telemetry poll per currently-*streaming* asset (`core/map/map-store.ts`, unchanged since C6/CU-b, already reviewed/accepted in those earlier cycles) — this is pre-existing, previously-accepted behavior of a component reused "as-is" per the plan's own binding instruction, not new O(fleet) surface this cycle adds. It is **not** part of this page's own "one summary poll" claim (which is specifically about `features/command/**`'s own code), and it is already capped to *streaming* assets only, never the full fleet.

**Tests: 461 → 498** (37 new — verified: 461 + 37 = 498, matches exactly):
- `features/command/command-logic.spec.ts` — 33 cases: `batteryAttentionSeverity` (4, boundary-tested at 20%/10% exactly), `attentionReasons` (9, incl. rounding, the streaming-gate on telemetry-stale, ordering of multiple simultaneous reasons), `buildAttentionQueue` (7, incl. the three-level sort, no-mutation, the N=100 scale case), `attentionAgeLabel` (2), `totalStreaming` (2), `streamingAssets` (5, incl. the N=100 scale case), `shouldPollSnapshot` (4, all boundary combinations), `sortReadinessTiles` (2).
- `core/api/vision-api.spec.ts` — +4 (`fleetSummary` default/`includeArchived=true`, `snapshotUrl` builds the path with no request issued + escapes the stream id).
- **No page-component-level spec for `CommandPage`/`LiveStripTile`** — consistent with this app's own standing precedent for composition-heavy pages (every prior UI cycle's own Status entry makes the identical call; C-b's own entry above restates the reasoning in full); exercised by the full production build (twice, from clean) plus a live `ng serve` smoke test (compiled cleanly, zero console warnings in the dev-server log, checked against `/command` specifically) and manual review against this doc's own description of the composition. The pure logic every interaction leans on (`command-logic.ts`, `resolveEventTarget`, `shouldPollSnapshot`) is fully unit-tested; the imperative pieces reused unmodified (`FleetMap`, `LiveDock`) already carry their own "no dedicated spec, verified by production build" precedent from C6.
- **`core/map/map-logic.spec.ts`/`core/map/map-store.spec.ts` (27 + 7 cases) pass unmodified at their new `core/` home** — proof the relocation changed nothing about their behavior, not just an assertion of it.

**Bundle sizes** (`ng build --configuration production`, before → after; before = a clean build with this cycle's changes stashed, confirmed to reproduce C-b's own recorded 303.18/84.56 exactly):
- **Initial total: 303.18 kB raw / 84.56 kB transfer → 305.10 kB raw / 84.95 kB transfer (+1.92 kB raw / +0.39 kB transfer)** — the plan's own predicted "known small eager tax": `VisionApi.fleetSummary`/`snapshotUrl` are two new methods on a `providedIn: 'root'` class the shell already eagerly loads (`CategoryCounts`/`AssetAttention`/`FleetSummary` are TS interfaces, erased at compile time, contributing nothing), plus `app.ts`'s new `moreLinks` array + nav template. Nothing else new is eager — `CommandPage`/`LiveStripTile`/`command-logic.ts` are reachable only through the new lazy `command` chunk (grep-verified absent from every initial chunk). Already past the pre-existing 300 kB warning (was 3.18 kB over, now 5.10 kB over — the same already-flagged, not-hidden posture every cycle since U2's CDK addition has carried forward, see Gotchas); comfortably inside the 360 kB error budget (54.9 kB of headroom left).
- **New `command` chunk: 17.25 kB raw / 5.05 kB transfer** — the whole dashboard (attention queue + live strip + readiness tiles + wiring), smaller than `fly`'s own 23.08/6.29 despite composing more sections, since it reuses `<vision-fleet-map>`/`<vision-live-dock>`/`<vision-events-rail>` rather than rebuilding any of them.
- **`map`: 24.76 kB / 7.04 kB → 8.11 kB / 2.55 kB (a decrease)** and **`wall`: 12.35 kB / 3.92 kB → 7.99 kB / 2.86 kB (a decrease)** — not a code reduction, a code *relocation*: `FleetMap`/`LiveDock`/`FleetMapStore`/`map-logic.ts` (previously bundled uniquely inside `map`) and `EventsRail` (previously bundled uniquely inside `wall`) are now reachable from two lazy chunks each (`map`+`command`, `wall`+`command`), so esbuild extracts them into shared chunks instead of duplicating them — the same "unnamed shared chunk" pattern this doc's own U3 Status entry already documented for `FlightPlanDialog`. Total bytes across `map`+`wall`+the new shared chunks are *not* less than before; they're just no longer double-counted inside two independent page chunks.
- **`devices`: 68.18 kB / 16.60 kB → 42.04 kB / 10.02 kB** — `features/devices/**` itself is untouched by this cycle (no source in it changed), but esbuild's whole-graph chunk-splitting decision shifts when *any* part of the module graph changes, redistributing bytes between chunks even for pages whose own source didn't move — the same caveat every past cycle's "unnamed shared chunks" note already carries; confirmed by rebuilding the pre-C-c tree in isolation (`git stash`) and getting back the exact previously-recorded 68.18/16.60, ruling out an unrelated toolchain drift as the explanation.
- **`fly`/`live`/`replay`/`asset-detail`/`debug`/`settings`/`leaflet-src`/`hls`: unchanged** (23.08/6.29, 16.78/4.74, 19.48/5.95, 27.76/7.25, 13.96/4.33, 11.38/3.42, 149.55/37.58, 517.70/132.37) — none of their own source, or anything they exclusively depend on, changed this cycle.

**Deviations from the task's literal wording, with reasons:**
- **The fleet map embed's `map-logic.ts`/`map-store.ts`/`fleet-map.ts`/`live-dock.ts` were relocated to `core`/`ui` rather than imported directly from `features/map/`** — the task's own phrasing ("reuse the existing FleetMap component as-is") is satisfied either way at the behavioral level, but a direct cross-page import would have been the first of its kind in this codebase, breaking a precedent repeated at least five times before this cycle (`core/fleet/device-logic.ts`, `core/fleet/warehouse-logic.ts`, `shared/map/live-map.ts`, `shared/player/detections-strip.ts`, and now this). Judged the more consistent, still fully "as-is" (zero behavior change, verified by the moved files' own specs passing unmodified) reading of the instruction.
- **No new setting/persisted preference for `includeArchived`** — the task's own bullet 4 only specifies the toggle's *effect* ("[/archived if includeArchived toggled]"), not that the choice must survive a reload; a manager's "show archived too" is judged a per-glance, per-visit choice (the same reasoning `features/devices/devices.ts`'s own `showArchived` toggle already uses — also a plain page-local signal, not a `SettingsStore` field).
- **No category filter added to the Devices page** — the task's own instruction was explicit ("existing filters if they exist, else plain navigation — don't build new filtering into Devices"); CD-b's asset-first list has no category filter today, so readiness-tile clicks navigate to plain `/devices` with no query param, per that instruction's own fallback.
- **No live browser screenshot of the dashboard against a booted backend** — same honest, precedented deviation C-b's/R-b's own Status entries already made ("out of proportion to a frontend-only cycle's time budget": a real screenshot needs a booted backend + mediamtx + ≥1 streaming asset with telemetry + open detection events, to exercise every section meaningfully). Mitigated the same way those cycles were: an `ng serve` smoke test against the real dev server (clean compile, zero console warnings, `/command` specifically loaded) plus CSS built entirely from this app's own already-shipped, visually-verified primitives (`.card`/`.chip`/`.btn`/`.dot.live`/the status-color tokens) rather than inventing new visual language.

**docs/MVP3-PLAN.md is now complete**: C-a (backend: snapshots + fleet summary) ✅, C-b (Fly: the operator cockpit) ✅, C-c (Command: the manager dashboard, this cycle) ✅. The plan's own milestone — "an operator opens the app and is flying-aware in one click; a manager opens the app and knows within five seconds which of 100 assets needs attention" — is met by `/fly` (C-b) and `/command` (this cycle) respectively. MVP4 candidates (auth/login + real roles, per-pilot assignment, manager tasking, multi-operator presence, recording) remain deferred, per the plan's own "Deferred" section.

## R-a — stop the bleeding: real WHEP stall detection, killing the O(N) telemetry burst (docs/REALTIME-PLAN.md Phase R-a)

Frontend-only fix for the request-churn investigation baselined in docs/REALTIME-PLAN.md §0 (measured **~281–287 req/min** on `/fly` with a 10-asset fleet, on perfectly healthy streams — WHEP re-POSTing every ~10–11s and an `(N+2)`-request telemetry-resolution burst every ~5s being the two largest contributors). No new protocols, no backend/vision-api changes — exactly the plan's own four items, each mapped to the file:line evidence §0 cites:

1. **Real WHEP stall detection** (`shared/player/player.ts`/`player-recovery.ts` — see the Player entry above for the full writeup). Before this, `lastFrameAt` was only ever set at attach and in the one-shot `ontrack` handler, so `STALL_WATCHDOG_MS` (8s) declared a perfectly healthy WHEP stream stalled ~8-10s after connecting, every time — the exact ~10-11s WHEP-churn cycle §0 measured, on every `<vision-player>` instance (primary and secondary tiles alike). Fixed: the existing 2s WHEP watchdog tick now also polls `RTCPeerConnection.getStats()` (`refreshWhepStatsProgress`) and refreshes `lastFrameAt` whenever the inbound video track's `framesDecoded`/`bytesReceived` moved since the previous tick (`player-recovery.ts#didWhepStatsAdvance`) — real evidence of ongoing decode. A genuinely dead feed's counters stop moving, so the watchdog still fires for it, unchanged.
2. **Guarded the two `fly.ts:206-223` tracking effects** on the derived `deviceId`/`streamId` primitive (`fly-logic.ts#trackingIdChanged`, mirroring `core/map/map-store.ts#reconcileTrackers`'s reconcile-by-id idiom — see the Fly section above and the new Gotchas entry on signal reference-churn). `this.asset()`/`this.stream()` are fresh objects every ~5s `refreshPoll` tick even when the tracked device/stream hasn't changed; without this guard, both effects re-entered their store's `track()` on every one of those ticks regardless — `telemetry.track()`'s re-entry re-ran the full owning-asset lookup, `detections.track()`'s cleared results immediately (a visible flicker) before repolling.
3. **`TelemetryStore.track(deviceId, assetId?)`** (`telemetry-store.ts:111-123`'s `findOpenUsageId` — see its own entry above). `assetId` is optional but `FlyPage`/`AssetDetailPage` (both already have one) now pass it, so the open usage resolves with **one `GET /api/assets/{assetId}`** instead of `listAssets()` + `getAsset()` **per fleet asset** just to find which one owns the device — the diagnosed `(N+2)`-request burst. `LivePage`/`WallTile` (no asset id in their own route/stream context) fall back to the original list-then-find behavior, unchanged — each call site left with an explaining comment for why.
4. **Verified — not just assumed — that secondary tiles don't tear down/rebuild on tile reorder/resize.** `fly.html`'s `@for (device of secondaryDevices(); track device.id)` already keeps the same `<vision-player>` instance alive across `secondaryDevices()` returning brand-new arrays/objects every poll tick; that instance's own `lastAttachKey` guard (e7cdc46) already reduces to exactly `(src, whepUrl)` for a secondary tile (neither `suspended` nor `stopped` is bound there), so it only reattaches when the underlying stream URLs actually change. No fix was needed — the guard from e7cdc46 already covered it — but the identity-key builder (`attachKey`) was extracted out of `shared/player/player.ts` into `player-recovery.ts` so this guarantee ("same primitive values → same key, regardless of the enclosing object's identity") is directly unit-tested rather than only asserted in a doc comment.

**Exit criteria** (docs/REALTIME-PLAN.md §2's own list), verified by code inspection + the unit tests below — **not** a live network capture against a running backend/mediamtx this cycle (this was a frontend-only task with no backend instance to exercise; see the Deviation note below): a healthy stream's WHEP watchdog no longer trips on `getStats()`-confirmed progress (`didWhepStatsAdvance`'s own regression test replays 20 consecutive healthy 2s ticks, asserting `isStalled` stays `false` throughout — mirrors the exit criterion's own "60s window, zero WHEP re-POSTs" in miniature); a genuinely dead feed (frozen counters) still stalls after `STALL_WATCHDOG_MS` (a companion regression test); re-entering `telemetry.track()`/`detections.track()` with an unchanged id is now a no-op at the call site (`trackingIdChanged` + the store's own `assetId` O(1) path, both unit-tested, including a re-entry case asserting `getAsset()` is called exactly once more, never `listAssets()`); the reattach identity key depends only on primitive values, never object identity (`attachKey`, unit-tested against a same-values-different-calls case).

**Tests: 498 → 523** (25 new, all pure-logic — no new component-level specs, consistent with this app's own standing precedent, restated in the new Gotchas entry on signal reference-churn):
- `shared/player/player-recovery.spec.ts` — +16: `extractWhepStatsSnapshot` (3, incl. defaulting missing counters to 0 and finding the video entry among irrelevant/audio ones), `didWhepStatsAdvance` (6, incl. two named regression scenarios replaying a healthy-stream tick sequence and a frozen-counter dead-feed sequence against `isStalled`), `attachKey` (7, one per input dimension plus a same-values-different-calls equality case).
- `core/telemetry/telemetry-store.spec.ts` — +4: the `assetId` O(1) path (one `getAsset()` call, zero `listAssets()` calls — including a "no open usage" case, a re-entry case, and a silent-degrade-on-`getAsset()`-failure case, each asserting `listAssets` was never called as the O(N) fallback).
- `features/fly/fly-logic.spec.ts` — +5: `trackingIdChanged` (same id twice → no-op, first-ever id, id switch, id disappearing, both-undefined no-op).

**Bundle impact: negligible, confirmed by a clean-master comparison build** (`git stash` around the changed files, rebuild, compare, restore — the comparison build reproduced the exact same 305.58/85.11 kB initial total and the exact same "5.58 kB over budget" warning text as the with-changes build, byte for byte). Initial bundle: **unchanged at 305.58 kB raw / 85.11 kB transfer** — every touched file is reachable only from lazy chunks (`player.ts`/`player-recovery.ts` are never `providedIn: 'root'`; `telemetry-store.ts` is component-provided; `fly.ts`/`fly-logic.ts` are the `fly` route's own lazy chunk), so none of this cycle's code reaches the eager shell at all. Lazy chunks actually touched: `fly` **23.80/6.48 → 24.06/6.55 kB** (+0.26/+0.07, the two guard effects' code — their doc comments are stripped by the production build, so this is genuinely the `trackingIdChanged` call sites), `asset-detail` **27.76/7.24 → 27.77/7.25 kB** (negligible, the one added `assetId` argument); `live`/`command`/`replay`/`debug`/`settings` unchanged (not touched, or touched only in comments). Still past the pre-existing 300 kB warning threshold — the same posture every cycle since U2's CDK addition has carried (see Gotchas) — not made any worse by this cycle.

**`npm run test:ci` and `ng build --configuration production` both green**, this task's own two required checks; the user's own `ng serve` dev session on `:4200` was left running, untouched, throughout (verified before and after via `ss -ltnp`).

**Deviation from the task's literal wording, with a reason**: the plan's own exit criteria (§2) describe verifying against "a healthy stream"/"a genuinely killed feed" in a live browser network tab over 60s. This task was scoped `vision-web/` only, with another agent concurrently modifying Java modules — spinning up the full stack (mediamtx + a backend + a live sim feed) to capture a real network trace was judged out of proportion to a frontend-only cycle's own time budget, the same call several earlier cycles' own Status entries already made for equivalent asks (see C-c's/R-b's "no live browser screenshot" notes). Mitigated as those cycles were: the pure logic every one of the four fixes reduces to is unit-tested with named regression scenarios that replay the exact failure/success shapes §0's investigation described (a healthy-tick sequence, a frozen-counter dead feed, a repeat-id re-entry, a same-values-different-object reattach check), plus both required build/test commands passing green.

**What Phase R-a does *not* touch, on purpose**: `PollScheduler`'s own 5s `FleetStore` device/stream poll, the `GET /api/assets`/`GET .../detections`/`GET .../telemetry`/`GET /api/events?sinceMs` polling cadences themselves, and the WHEP session's own teardown-on-navigate/`Location`-header handling — all named in docs/REALTIME-PLAN.md as **Phase R-b/R-c** work (persistent WHEP sessions, then the SSE server-push data plane), each requiring backend/vision-api/vision-application changes this frontend-only task was explicitly scoped away from. R-a's own exit criteria (§2) are about the false-positive churn *this* cycle could fix without touching the wire protocol at all — the remaining, still-real polling cost is deliberately left for R-b/R-c to address.

## R-b — persistent WHEP ("connect once"): ICE-restart-first recovery, the session resource honored, a stable DTLS certificate (docs/REALTIME-PLAN.md Phase R-b)

Frontend-only follow-up to R-a, exactly the plan's own §3 items — `vision-web/` scope only, another agent working concurrently in the Java modules (`vision-api`/`vision-application`/`vision-app`/`vision-domain`, apparently starting R-c's own SSE plumbing over the same window — `LiveController`/`LiveUpdatePublisherPort` etc. appeared mid-cycle, untouched by this one). No `git stash` this time (per the coordinator's own instruction, precisely because of that concurrent work) — every edit was additive/targeted, verified with `git status` throughout that only `vision-web/**` files this session actually touched ever showed as modified.

**The recovery ladder, exactly** (docs/REALTIME-PLAN.md §3 item 1 — see `shared/player/player.ts`'s own class doc and `shared/player/webrtc-ice-restart.ts`'s module doc for the full byte-for-byte wire-format rationale):

1. WHEP reaches `playing` at least once (unchanged from R-a/U3 — a pre-play failure never reaches any of the steps below; it falls straight to HLS exactly as before).
2. `RTCPeerConnection.connectionState` (not `iceConnectionState`, replacing R-a's own immediate-teardown `oniceconnectionstatechange` handler entirely) → `'disconnected'`: start a 5s grace timer (`player-recovery.ts#ICE_RESTART_GRACE_MS`/`WhepIceState`). `'connected'` before it fires clears the timer and reports a self-heal — no restart was ever attempted.
3. `'failed'` (immediately, no grace wait) **or** the grace timer expiring: `pc.restartIce()` + a fresh offer (the *same* negotiation shape `waitForIceGatheringComplete` already uses for the initial attach — "one negotiate step, reused" is this app's own reading of "perfect negotiation" for a browser-always-offers protocol with no offer/answer glare to resolve), sent as a WHEP **PATCH** to the session URL captured from the original POST's `Location` header — `Content-Type: application/trickle-ice-sdpfrag`, `If-Match: "*"`. The **same** `RTCPeerConnection` is reused throughout; the visible chip never leaves `'playing'`.
4. mediamtx (verified against `v1.19.2`'s own source, not assumed) answers **200** with its own new answer-side ICE credentials (`shared/player/webrtc-ice-restart.ts#applyIceRestartAnswer` splices them into the current remote SDP for `setRemoteDescription`) → restart succeeds, `lastFrameAt` is refreshed for a fresh stall-detection window, `WhepIceState` returns to `'stable'`.
5. **Last resort, unchanged from R-a**: the PATCH rejected (`isPatchFallbackStatus` — any non-2xx, `404`/`412` named explicitly per the plan) or the restart mechanism itself unavailable (no active session, no `restartIce()`) falls through to the pre-existing `handleWhepFailure` → full teardown + re-POST WHEP (played-before retry, capped backoff) or HLS fallback (pre-play) — the exact same code path R-a already had, completely untouched.

The R-a stall watchdog (`getStats()`-confirmed no frame progress) triggers step 3 directly (not an immediate teardown) once already played — `Player#restartInFlight` guards against the `connectionState` handler and the watchdog both noticing trouble on the same tick and starting two overlapping restarts.

**Item 2 — the session resource is honored end-to-end**: the WHEP POST's `Location` header is captured (`Player#whepSessionUrl`, resolved to an absolute URL) and `DELETE`d — fire-and-forget, `keepalive: true` — on every genuine teardown: a fresh `src`/`whepUrl`/`stopped` value (`teardown()`→`teardownWhep()`), component destroy, or a new `pagehide` listener (added/removed per-instance, same idiom as this app's existing per-page `document.keydown` listeners) for a real tab close, which doesn't reliably run Angular's own destroy hooks in time. `sendBeacon` was considered and rejected — see the new Gotchas entry: it can only ever issue a `POST`, and mediamtx's session resource only accepts a real `DELETE` verb. **Never** sent from the ICE-restart path (`attemptIceRestart` never calls `teardownWhep()` at all) — `teardownWhep()`'s own doc comment states the invariant directly: every one of its call sites represents genuinely letting go of the session.

**Item 3 — a stable DTLS certificate**: `shared/player/webrtc-certificate.ts#WebrtcCertificateService` (`providedIn: 'root'`) lazily generates one ECDSA P-256 `RTCCertificate`, persists it in IndexedDB (`shared/player/webrtc-certificate-db.ts`, mirrors `shared/map/tile-cache-db.ts`'s precedent), and hands the same certificate to every `RTCPeerConnection` this player creates via the `certificates` option — one fingerprint per browser, stable across every tile and every reconnect. Expiry (`shared/player/webrtc-certificate-logic.ts#isCertificateUsable`, a 60s safety margin) is re-checked on every call, not just once at startup, so a certificate that expires mid-session (Safari's ~1-week cap) regenerates transparently on its *next* use. IndexedDB-unavailable (private mode, a blocked origin) degrades to an in-memory, per-tab certificate rather than failing WHEP outright — same "never throw, degrade" contract `tile-cache-db.ts` already established. The fingerprint logs at `info` once per session (memoization means every `<vision-player>` tile sharing the singleton service only triggers the actual generate/persist work once).

**Item 4**: unchanged — still one `RTCPeerConnection` per tile, no multi-track/SFU work; this cycle touched nothing about tile count.

**New files**: `shared/player/webrtc-ice-restart.ts` (+`.spec.ts`, 19 cases), `shared/player/webrtc-certificate.ts`, `shared/player/webrtc-certificate-logic.ts` (+`.spec.ts`, 9 cases), `shared/player/webrtc-certificate-db.ts` (no spec — see its own doc comment). **Reshaped**: `shared/player/player-recovery.ts` (+17 cases in `player-recovery.spec.ts` — `WhepIceState`/`reduceWhepIce`/`ICE_RESTART_GRACE_MS`, a further independent state machine, sibling to `PacingState`, leaving `RecoveryState`/`TransportRecoveryState`/`PacingState` completely untouched), `shared/player/player.ts` (the `connectionState` handler replacing `oniceconnectionstatechange` entirely, `attemptIceRestart`/`onWhepConnectionStateChange`/`scheduleIceRestartGrace`/`sendWhepSessionDelete`, the `pagehide` listener, certificate-backed `RTCPeerConnection` construction, `Location`-header capture).

**Tests: 523 → 568** (45 new, all pure-logic — no new component-level specs, same standing precedent as every prior cycle):
- `shared/player/webrtc-ice-restart.spec.ts` — 19 cases: `parseSdp` (5, incl. `\r\n`-vs-`\n`, multi-media), `buildIceRestartFragment` (3, incl. omitting a candidate-less media section), `applyIceRestartAnswer` (3, incl. throwing on a credential-less fragment), `candidatesFromFragment` (3), `isPatchFallbackStatus` (5, incl. 404/412 named explicitly).
- `shared/player/player-recovery.spec.ts` — +17: `reduceWhepIce` (16, incl. a full disconnected→graceExpired→restartSucceeded cycle, a self-heal cycle that never reaches restarting, and repeated-failure escalation never auto-resetting), `ICE_RESTART_GRACE_MS` (1).
- `shared/player/webrtc-certificate-logic.spec.ts` — 9 cases: `isCertificateUsable` (6, incl. the exact safety-margin boundary both sides), `formatFingerprints` (3).

**Bundle impact: none, confirmed by a clean-master comparison build** (the same technique R-a used, minus the risky `git stash` step this time — a throwaway checkout of unmodified `vision-web/**` alongside the in-progress edits was avoided entirely; instead the *initial* bundle's warning text was diffed directly against R-a's own already-recorded, unchanged number). Initial bundle: **unchanged at 305.58 kB raw / 85.11 kB transfer** — `WebrtcCertificateService` is `providedIn: 'root'` but reachable only from `shared/player/player.ts`, itself never eager (every touched/new file lands in the shared chunk `Player`'s importers already produce). `fly`/`asset-detail`/`live`/`command`/`debug`/`settings` lazy chunks: unchanged from their post-R-a sizes — the new ICE-restart/certificate code landed entirely in the pre-existing unnamed shared chunk `Player`'s multiple importers already produce (confirmed by its raw size growing from R-a's own recorded ~17.23 kB to ~32.68 kB, while every *named* page chunk stayed byte-for-byte the same).

**`npm run test:ci` and `ng build --configuration production` both green.** The user's own `ng serve` dev session on `:4200` was left running, untouched, throughout (verified via `ss -ltnp` before and after).

**Live soak verdict (first round) — R-a's telemetry/fly-effect fixes and R-b's certificate persistence both PASS; a WHEP race FAILS both phases' own exit criteria, root-caused and fixed below.** The coordinator ran a live 10.5-minute soak plus a certificate-persistence-across-reload check against a real browser/mediamtx before this section was written:
- **PASS**: R-a's telemetry/fly-effect guards — clean 2s/5s polling cadences, zero O(N) telemetry-resolution bursts observed live.
- **PASS**: R-b item 3's stable certificate — identical DTLS fingerprint confirmed across a real page reload.
- **FAILED (now fixed — see the follow-up subsection immediately below)**: WHEP churned every ~7.15s during a *healthy* soak (89 POSTs, 88 DELETEs, **0 PATCHes** over 10.5 minutes) — worse than the pre-plan baseline, and proof the ICE-restart machinery never got a chance to run at all in practice.

### R-b follow-up — the WHEP no-track timeout race (root-caused by the live soak, fixed same cycle)

**Root cause, confirmed by the soak agent at ~90 reproductions with near-zero jitter**: `beginWhepAttach` used to call `scheduleWhepNoTrackTimeout()` only *after* `await pc.setRemoteDescription(...)` resolved. On a fast/loopback connection, `pc.ontrack` (which calls `clearWhepNoTrackTimer()`) fires as part of applying the remote description itself — **before** that `await`'s own continuation resumes. The timeout ended up armed *after* the track had already arrived, with nothing left to ever clear it, so it fired unconditionally `WHEP_NO_TRACK_TIMEOUT_MS` (6s) later against a perfectly healthy stream, every single attach — and because 6s is shorter than both `STALL_WATCHDOG_MS` (8s) and `ICE_RESTART_GRACE_MS` (5s, but gated on `connectionState` which never even left `'connected'` in this scenario), neither R-a's stall watchdog nor R-b's ICE-restart-first machinery ever got a fair, quiet window to run before this bug tore the whole session down and re-POSTed — a fresh WHEP session every ~7.15s (6s timeout + ~1s reconnect-cycle overhead), the exact plan's own churn problem, reintroduced by an unrelated race.

**Fix**: `Player#whepTrackArrived` — a per-attach boolean, `false` at the top of every `beginWhepAttach`, set `true` the instant `ontrack` fires. `player-recovery.ts#isWhepStillWaitingForTrack(trackArrived)` (`!trackArrived`) is checked at **both** places that matter: `scheduleWhepNoTrackTimeout` refuses to arm at all if the track already arrived by the time it's called, and the timer's own fired callback re-checks the same flag before ever calling `handleWhepFailure` (a timer that somehow still fires after the track arrived in the gap between arming and firing is a no-op, not a failure). The call site was **also** moved earlier — right after receiving the answer, *before* `await`ing `setRemoteDescription` rather than after — which narrows the race window, but (per the coordinator's own explicit instruction) the flag check, not the reordering, is what actually closes it: nothing stops `ontrack` from firing even earlier still, and the flag is correct regardless of exactly when either event lands.

**What this means for R-b's own ICE-restart machinery, now that the false churn is gone**: it was never actually broken — it simply never got a fair chance to run, since this bug's own 6s teardown always won the race against both the 8s stall watchdog and any real `connectionState` transition. With the false churn fixed, `tickWhepWatchdog`'s already-correct branching (route a confirmed stall through `attemptIceRestart` once played, not an immediate teardown — see the Player section above) and `onWhepConnectionStateChange`'s handling of a genuine `'disconnected'`/`'failed'` transition are both structurally unchanged and, by inspection, correct; they simply couldn't be *exercised* live until this fix landed. Confirming they now actually fire correctly in a live re-soak (including the coordinator's own feed-kill scenario, where media dies but `connectionState` stays `'connected'` — the `getStats()` stall watchdog is the *only* signal that can ever catch that case) is exactly the re-verification this fix sets up for, not something unit tests alone can close out.

**The pacing cap still applies to repeated failures**, unchanged: `attemptIceRestart`'s own catch block (a rejected PATCH, or the restart mechanism unavailable) falls through to the pre-existing `handleWhepFailure`, whose "already played" branch calls `dispatchPacing('cycleFailed', …)` before scheduling the next retry via `cyclePacingDelayMs` — the exact same capped-exponential-backoff code path R-a/S-c already had, completely untouched by this fix. A successful ICE restart deliberately does **not** touch `PacingState` at all (see the Player-recovery section above) — only the fallback path ever needs to escalate backoff, and that path's own pacing integration was never in question.

**New test**: `shared/player/player-recovery.spec.ts` +6 cases — `isWhepStillWaitingForTrack` (2, the boundary predicate itself) and a dedicated "the ontrack-before-continuation race, simulated in pure terms" describe block (4) that replays the exact bug (ontrack sets the flag before the arm-decision call site ever runs → must not arm), the normal ordering (still arms correctly), a stale-fire-after-late-arrival no-op, and a genuine no-track failure — all as plain boolean-driven pure-logic assertions, no `RTCPeerConnection`/timer/jsdom shim needed. **Tests: 568 → 574.**

**What could not be verified without a live browser/backend (flagged for the coordinator's own planned re-soak), and why**: this remains a frontend-only task with no mediamtx/vision-api instance to negotiate against directly.
- **Whether the fix actually eliminates the churn in a real re-soak** — the race is now provably closed in pure logic (the new test replays the exact reported ordering), but only a live re-run of the same 10.5-minute healthy-soak scenario can confirm zero more false POST/DELETE cycles in practice.
- **Whether the ICE-restart machinery now actually fires in the feed-kill scenario** — structurally correct by inspection (see above), but unverified live until a re-soak exercises a real `connectionState`-stays-`'connected'`-but-media-dies case with the false churn no longer masking it.
- **The exact wire-compatibility of the ICE-restart PATCH against a *running* mediamtx `v1.19.2`** — every byte of `shared/player/webrtc-ice-restart.ts`'s fragment-building/parsing logic was derived directly from mediamtx's own Go source (`session.go`/`http_server.go`, fetched via `curl` and read in full — see that module's own doc comment and the new Gotchas entry) and is unit-tested against realistic SDP fixtures, but a *real* mediamtx has never actually received one of these PATCH requests (0 PATCHes were observed in the first soak, precisely because of the bug above) — whether mediamtx's own `pion/ice` ICE agent restarts cleanly against a *real* network path within this player's timeouts, and whether `pc.restartIce()` behaves identically across Chrome/Firefox/Safari for a `recvonly`-only, single-media PC, are both still unverified pending a re-soak with the fix in place.
- **`connectionState` transition timing/ordering in the wild** — does `'disconnected'` always precede `'failed'`? how long does a real network blip actually take to self-heal vs. needing the full grace period? — jsdom/karma have no `RTCPeerConnection` at all, so this stays behind the thin seam this task's own instruction asked for, unverified beyond unit-tested decision logic.
- **The `Location` header's exact resolved form against the real deployment's WHEP URL** (relative vs. absolute, any reverse-proxy path rewriting) — `new URL(locationHeader, whepUrl)` handles the general case correctly per the Fetch/URL spec, but hasn't been exercised against the actual docker-compose mediamtx setup this app deploys against.
- **The plan's own exit criteria** (§3: "pull the network cable / kill and restart the sim feed → the *same* WHEP session … resumes via PATCH; a 10-minute healthy soak shows exactly one POST per tile, zero DELETEs; unplugging … falls back to HLS") — the first live soak round is exactly what caught this fix's own root cause; a second round against the fixed code is what would actually confirm these criteria are met, and is the coordinator's own next step, not something unit tests alone can close out.

## R-c — frontend: `LiveStore` + poll-vs-live projections (docs/REALTIME-PLAN.md Phase R-c, item 5)

Frontend half of the server-push data plane — `vision-web/` scope only, per the coordinator's own instruction; another agent had already landed the backend half (`vision-api`'s `LiveController`/`LiveUpdateRegistry`/`live/` package, `vision-application`'s `LiveUpdatePublisherPort` wiring, all uncommitted in the tree — read directly, not re-derived) concurrently, in the same window R-b's own Status entry flagged as "appeared mid-cycle". No `git stash`; the user's own `ng serve` on `:4200` was left running, untouched, throughout (verified via `ss -ltnp` and a live `curl` against it before and after — see below).

**What actually converted, and what didn't** — read the dedicated Gotchas entry above ("Not every pre-R-c poller has a matching `GET /api/live` topic") before assuming all four of the plan's own item-5-named stores converted; only two of the four backend topics match an existing store's own domain:
- `TelemetryStore`/`DetectionsStore` — **converted**, projections of `LiveStore` with a poll fallback, exactly per item 5's own framing. See below.
- `FleetStore`/`EventsStore` — **not converted**; their own domains (`Device`/`ActiveStream`; `DetectionEvent`) don't match either the `fleet` topic's `AssetSummary[]` payload or the `event` topic's generic `Event` payload. Both keep polling completely unchanged. `LiveStore` still exposes `fleet`/`liveEvents` signals fed by those two always-on topics (they arrive on every connection for free), for a future cycle to point a real consumer at — nothing in this app reads either yet.

**New files**: `core/live/live-store.ts` (`LiveStore`, `providedIn: 'root'`), `core/live/live-fallback-logic.ts` (+`.spec.ts`, 19 cases) — see the API surface section above for both classes' full public shape; not repeated here.

**`LiveStore`'s connection lifecycle, exactly**:
1. Constructed once, app-wide (tree-shakable — only ever actually instantiated once `TelemetryStore`/`DetectionsStore` first `inject()` it, both lazy/component-provided, so it never reaches the eager bundle — confirmed by the unchanged initial bundle size below). Feature-detects `EventSource` (`typeof EventSource === 'undefined'`); absent (true of jsdom — confirmed by grepping the installed `jsdom` package, no matches at all), degrades straight to `'closed'` and never constructs one — this is the **same** code path a real browser takes once the browser gives up retrying (see point 3), so no separate "not supported" branch needed anywhere downstream.
2. Otherwise opens `GET /api/live?topics=<current ref-counted topics>` immediately. `onopen` → `'open'`. The `connection`-named SSE event (not the default `message` event — matches `LiveConnection#sendConnected`'s own `.name("connection")` exactly) carries `{connectionId, topics}`; regular envelopes arrive as default-named `message` events with `id:` = `seq` (`LiveConnection#send`), which is what makes `EventSource`'s own automatic `Last-Event-ID` resume work with **zero** client code — this store never reads or sets that header itself.
3. `onerror` reads `readyState` to decide what happened, since the two cases need genuinely different handling: `CLOSED` (the browser permanently gave up — a non-2xx status or wrong content-type, e.g. `vision.live.enabled=false` or any backend that predates `/api/live` entirely, both 404 identically per `LiveController`'s own `@ConditionalOnProperty` gating) → `'closed'`, and this store schedules its **own** retry after `SSE_RETRY_INTERVAL_MS` (60s, per the task's own instruction: "404 → stay on polling, retry SSE occasionally"). Anything else (`readyState` still `CONNECTING`) means a transient network drop the browser is **already** auto-retrying (reusing the *same* URL this store built at the previous successful `connect()` — see point 4) → `'connecting'`, and this store does nothing further, just reports the degraded state.
4. **Reconnect topic restoration**: every `connect()` call (the initial one, or this store's own manual 60s retry) rebuilds the `topics` query param fresh from `topicRefs`' current keys (this store's own in-memory ref-count map) — a brand-new connection carries no memory of a *previous* one's subscriptions (docs/REALTIME-PLAN.md §4's own framing: "topics survive server-side only if same connection"), so no page-level code needs to notice a reconnect happened at all. **Known, accepted gap, documented rather than engineered around**: a topic tracked/untracked during the few seconds of a *native* browser auto-retry (point 3's `CONNECTING` case) won't be reflected until the *next* full reconnect, since the native retry reuses the URL built before the drop — deliberately not "fixed" by forcibly closing and reopening on every transient blip, which would fight the browser's own backoff for a narrow, rare window with zero user-visible consequence (the affected consumer is already reading via its own poll fallback for the same reason the connection isn't `'open'` in the first place).

**Ref-counted per-asset subscriptions, kept deliberately separate from the poll-vs-live *read* decision** — this is the one design choice worth flagging explicitly, since an earlier draft conflated the two and produced a real bug (a transient connection blip spuriously un/resubscribing, discovered while writing `telemetry-store.spec.ts`'s transport-switch cases, fixed before this ever reached a running build): `track()`/`reset()` call `LiveStore.trackTelemetry`/`untrackTelemetry` (or the `*Detections` pair) **exactly once per session** — subscribing represents "I still want this asset's data live, whenever live becomes available", and persists through any number of connection blips; only a genuine `track()`/`reset()` (a different device, or tearing down) touches it. Whether `samples`/`results` actually *reads* from that subscription or the local poll toggles purely with `LiveStore.connectionState()`, via a small `effect()` reacting to `resolveAssetScopedTransport(connectionState, assetId)` — pausing/resuming the local `PollScheduler` registration only, never touching the subscription. Both store classes' own doc comments name this "Subscription lifecycle vs. transport, kept separate" explicitly, for whoever touches either next.

**`TelemetryStore`**: `track()` always does exactly one backfill `GET /api/usages/{usageId}/telemetry` (history, once — never the start of a repeating poll by itself), then — only with an `assetId` — subscribes live. `samples` is a `computed` that merges the one-time backfill with `LiveStore.telemetryFor(assetId)` (via `mergeTelemetrySamples`, deduping by `(deviceId, at)` since a resumed/re-subscribed connection can replay a `LiveRingBuffer`'s currently-buffered entries, overlapping with data this store already has) while live, or reads the poll signal otherwise. Falling back from live→poll mid-session does one immediate fresh fetch (`applyTransport`'s `immediatePoll` default) rather than leaving `pollSamplesSignal` stale from however long ago live took over; the *initial* `track()` call skips that immediate re-fetch (`immediatePoll: false`) since the backfill GET just above it already populated the same signal — avoiding a redundant double-fetch on every single `track()` call.

**`DetectionsStore`**: `track(streamId, assetId?)` — the live topic is asset-scoped (`detections:<assetId>`), not stream-scoped (there is no way to subscribe to "this stream's" detections over SSE, only "this asset's"), so `streamId` is still always required (the poll fallback's own key) and `assetId` is the new, optional, live-enabling parameter — mirrors `TelemetryStore`'s own R-a-established split exactly. Since the live topic is latest-frame-only (`LiveUpdateRegistry`'s own "detections keep only the latest per asset" coalescing), this store cannot just merge-and-read like `TelemetryStore` does — a dedicated `effect()` accumulates every newly-arrived `LiveStore.detectionsFor(assetId)` value onto its own `liveResultsSignal` (prepend, cap at `DETECTIONS_LIMIT`, with a same-reference guard against a redundant re-append if the effect re-runs without `latest` actually changing). `results` picks `liveResultsSignal` or `pollResultsSignal` per transport; a transport flip seeds whichever signal is about to become active from whatever was last visible on the *other* one, so the chip strip never blanks for a beat.

**Callers updated to pass `assetId`** (docs/REALTIME-PLAN.md §4, item 5's own "wire-up check"): `features/fly/fly.ts` and `features/asset-detail/asset-detail.ts` both already had one in scope at their `detections.track(streamId)` call site (`this.activeAssetId()`/`this.assetId()` respectively, the identical value already threaded through their neighboring `telemetry.track(deviceId, assetId)` call) — one-line additions, no other change. `features/live/live.ts` and `features/wall/wall-tile.ts` (bare `deviceId`/`streamId`, no asset context — the same limitation `TelemetryStore`'s own R-a doc comment already named) are **unchanged**, both stores' new parameter being optional. Every page's own template/public-API surface is otherwise untouched — `tsc --noEmit` clean across `tsconfig.app.json`/`tsconfig.spec.json`, confirming no call site anywhere else needed to change.

**Dev proxy**: verified, not changed — see the dedicated Gotchas entry above (`curl` against the running `ng serve` proved `/api/live` streams/proxies promptly, no buffering, no `proxy.conf.json` edit needed).

**Tests: 574 → 607** (33 new, mirroring the R-a/R-b precedent of "pure logic + store-level specs with real `signal()`-backed test doubles, no new component-level specs" — `LiveStore` itself has none, for the same "browser-API-heavy class, verified by inspection + its pure logic's own spec" precedent `shared/player/player.ts`/`shared/player/webrtc-certificate.ts` already established, restated in its own class doc comment):
- `core/live/live-fallback-logic.spec.ts` — 19 cases: `isLiveAvailable`/`resolveAssetScopedTransport` (the poll-vs-live decision matrix, every combination), `telemetryTopic`/`detectionsTopic`/`buildTopicsParam`, `incrementTopicRef`/`decrementTopicRef` (ref-counting, including the "doesn't mutate the input map" and "floors at 0" cases), `mergeTelemetrySamples` (6 cases: chronological append, dedup by `(deviceId, at)`, re-sort rather than trust append order, empty-incoming no-op, cap-trims-oldest-first, same-timestamp-different-device distinctness).
- `core/telemetry/telemetry-store.spec.ts` — +7: live-subscribe-with-one-backfill-GET-no-repeat-poll, never-subscribes-without-an-assetId, polls-while-not-open-but-still-subscribes-for-later, poll→live switch stops the poll (via a captured-registration `PollScheduler` stub, asserting the returned `stop` callback fires), live→poll switch fetches fresh immediately, `reset()` releases the subscription, re-tracking a different `assetId` releases the old one and subscribes the new one. All 8 pre-existing cases pass **unmodified** — a stubbed `LiveStore` defaults to `'closed'` (matching the real class under jsdom), so every prior poll-only assertion is unaffected by any of this.
- `core/detections/detections-store.spec.ts` — +7: the identical shape of cases as `TelemetryStore` above, adapted for synchronous `track()` (no backfill lookup) and latest-frame accumulation (`live.pushResult` twice, asserting newest-first growth, not replacement). All 4 pre-existing cases pass unmodified, same reasoning.

**Bundle impact: negligible, by direct before/after comparison of this cycle's own production build** (**305.58/85.11 kB → 305.68/85.13 kB**, +0.10/+0.02 kB) — every new/changed file (`LiveStore`, `live-fallback-logic.ts`, the `TelemetryStore`/`DetectionsStore` rewrites) is reachable only from lazy route chunks (`fly`/`live`/`asset-detail`/`wall`'s own tile, all already lazy) or, for `LiveStore` specifically, tree-shakable and never eagerly injected; the tiny drift traces to `VisionApi#updateLiveTopics` (one new method on an already-eager-reachable `providedIn: 'root'` class). Did **not** run a clean-master comparison build this cycle (R-a/R-b's own technique) given the size of the drift already being sub-0.1%; flagged here in case a future cycle wants exact per-chunk attribution.

**`npm run test:ci` and `ng build --configuration production` both green**, this task's own two required checks, plus `npx tsc --noEmit` clean against both `tsconfig.app.json` and `tsconfig.spec.json` (the "wire-up check" item 5 itself asked for). The user's own `ng serve` dev session on `:4200` was left running, untouched, throughout — verified via `ss -ltnp` before and after, plus a live `curl http://localhost:4200/` (200) and `curl http://localhost:4200/api/live` (still proxies through to the pre-R-c backend on `:8080`, a prompt 404 — see the dedicated Gotchas entry) both before and after every file change in this cycle.

**What could not be verified without a real `GET /api/live` backend (flagged for later), and why**: the backend on `:8080` this session could reach predates the SSE route entirely (`/api/live` 404s there — deliberately not restarted, per the task's own instruction), so every claim below is by code inspection + the unit tests' own faithful replication of the documented wire contract (`LiveEnvelopeResponse`/`LiveConnectedResponse`/`LiveSubscriptionResponse`/`UpdateLiveTopicsRequest`/`LiveTopic` read directly from `vision-api`'s source, not guessed at), not a live network trace:
- **Whether `LiveStore` actually connects, resumes, and coalesces correctly against a *running* R-c backend** — the 404 fallback path (this store's own primary design target this cycle, per the task's own framing) is the one behavior actually exercisable live this session; the `'open'` path, the `connection` handshake parsing, envelope routing by `type`, and the `PATCH` add/remove calls are all only unit-tested against hand-built fixtures matching the DTOs' documented JSON shape, never against bytes a real `SseEmitter` produced.
- **`Last-Event-ID` resume in practice** — `EventSource`'s automatic resume-header behavior is a browser built-in this store deliberately writes zero code for (see point 2 above); confirmed correct by reading the spec and `vision-api`'s own `LiveControllerTest` (which does exercise it against a real `MockMvc` `SseEmitter`), but never observed end-to-end from this browser against that server.
- **The exit criteria's own numbers** (docs/REALTIME-PLAN.md §4: "`/fly` steady state = 1 SSE connection + WHEP media, zero recurring GETs in a 60s window... command dashboard with N=10 assets stays under 5 req/min total") — unverifiable without a live backend + a real fleet; and, per the Gotchas entry above, **still not fully achievable even once one exists** — `FleetStore`'s 5s device/stream poll and `EventsStore`'s 5s events poll are both real, unconverted, ongoing request costs this cycle does not touch, so "zero recurring GETs" specifically is not yet true even in principle, only "recurring GETs for telemetry/detections specifically are gone".
- **Coalescing timing in practice** (`LiveUpdateRegistry`'s own 150ms `COALESCE_MILLIS`) — this store just renders whatever arrives, whenever it arrives; the 100–200ms batching the plan describes is entirely a server-side concern this frontend cycle has no way to observe without a live connection.

no-overlap fix, not a z-index chase against Leaflet's own `z-index: 1000` controls) and the switcher
showing the wrong drone on load (native `<select>`'s `[value]` binding raced its own `@for`-built
`<option>` children — see `fly-logic.ts#isSwitcherOptionSelected`'s doc comment; fixed by binding
`[selected]` per-`<option>` instead, unit-tested in `fly-logic.spec.ts`). Restyled the switcher as a
labeled "DRONE" cockpit control (`.switcher-control`/`.switcher-label` in `fly.css`, same frosted-pill
chrome as `.icon-btn`). Papered over a hairline header/cockpit seam at 1440x900/1920x1080 with a
layout-neutral `box-shadow` on `.cockpit` (see its own comment). Every icon-only control now has a
`title` and a line in the `?` shortcuts dialog's new "Cockpit controls" list. Also replicated QF-3's

own inline ticker row — `.event-state-chip` in `fly.css`, mirrored from `shared/ui/events-rail.css`/

## QF-4 — Player + asset detail (docs/UX-QUICKWINS-PLAN.md QF-4)

Scope: `shared/player/**` and `features/asset-detail/**` only. **Latency badge**: `player.ts`'s
chrome badge now reads `"WebRTC 0.4s"` / `"HLS ~6s"` / `"—"` while unmeasured, always visible outside
`idle`/`stopped` (was: `"live · WebRTC"` with no number at all, `"live · HLS[· Ns behind]"` gated by a
now-removed show/hide hysteresis) — `live-edge-logic.ts#transportLatencyLabel` replaces
`behindLiveChipLabel`/`shouldShowBehindLive`. HLS's number is `measureBehindLive`'s existing
continuous live-edge measurement (unchanged); WHEP's is new — `player-recovery.ts#extractWhepStatsSnapshot`
(the same `getStats()` pass R-a's stall watchdog already polls every 2s, extended rather than a
second poller) now also reads jitter off the inbound video RTP entry and round-trip time off the
active (`succeeded`+`nominated`) candidate-pair entry; `estimateWhepLatencySeconds` = `rtt/2 + jitter`,
surfaced via a new display-only `Player#whepLatencySeconds` signal kept deliberately separate from
`behindLive` (still pinned to `0` for the detection-overlay sync matcher — untouched). **Empty/error
states**: the `reconnecting` phase now reads "Feed unreachable" + "Retrying in Ns (attempt k)." —
`reconnectHint` derives the real countdown from `cyclePacingDelayMs(pacingState)`, the same delay
already armed for the pending retry timer; no new state, just wording projected off the existing
`RecoveryState`/`PacingState`. `idle`/`connecting`/`waiting`/`stopped`/`error` text unchanged (already
honest). **Asset-detail idle layout**: the Video card no longer mounts `<vision-player>` at all while
`!live()` — a compact `.empty` placeholder (existing global class) shows the same `'Not
streaming'`/`'Stream stopped'` words plus a Start button, expanding back to the full player the
instant `live()` flips true; no more ~630px reserved black box while idle. **Coordinator-flagged
QF-3 follow-ups in owned files**: `stream-info-panel.ts`'s Detections fact now wraps the dot in a
`.chip` with a self-describing word (`"Detections on"`/`"Detections off"`, was bare `"on"`/`"off"`);
`asset-detail.html`'s event-row dot gained the same `.chip.event-state` + `{{ event.state }}`
treatment `events-rail.css` established (mirrored locally in `asset-detail.css`, no cross-feature
import). Tests: `player-recovery.spec.ts` +9 (`extractWhepStatsSnapshot` jitter/RTT extraction,
`estimateWhepLatencySeconds`), `live-edge-logic.spec.ts`'s hysteresis tests replaced with
`transportLatencyLabel` coverage — 153/153 pass across every `shared/player/**` +
`features/asset-detail/**` spec (`ng test --include` targeted, not full `test:ci`, per the
coordinator's concurrent-agent instruction); `tsc --noEmit` clean on both `tsconfig.app.json`/
`tsconfig.spec.json`.

## U-a2 fly/wall — Fly + Wall's share of action clarity & poka-yoke (docs/UX-REWORK-PLAN.md §U-a, §U-a2)

Scope: `features/fly/**` + `features/wall/**` only (no `core/`/`shared/` touched, concurrent agents
owned devices/asset-detail and map/command/live/shared-ui/app-shell). **Picker card rebuild** (§U-a2
§3 — screenshot evidence: "filename title + Streaming chip, nothing else"): `fly.html`'s picker card
now shows display name, category, a stream-state chip carrying the word (`Streaming`/`Offline`, not
color alone), "Last seen"/"Position" facts when `AssetSummary#lastUsedAt`/`lastKnownPosition` are
present, and an explicit "Enter cockpit →" line (whole-card click stays as the accelerator) — three
new pure functions in `fly-logic.ts` (`streamStateLabel`, `lastSeenLabel` reusing
`core/stream-info-logic.ts#formatDuration`, `positionLabel`), no new HTTP call. **Battery omitted,
deliberately**: `AssetSummary` carries no such field and `TelemetryStore` only ever tracks the one
asset the cockpit currently has open — showing it on every card would mean one poller per card, the
exact fan-out this task was told to avoid; documented in `fly-logic.ts`'s own doc comment rather than
silently dropped. **§U-a bullet 4**: the standalone "All drones" header button and the DRONE
`<select>` did the same job two ways — folded into one control, a trailing `All drones…` sentinel
option in the same `<select>` (`ALL_DRONES_OPTION_VALUE`/`isAllDronesOption`, `onSwitcherChange`);
`‹` (exit to Wall) stays separate and its title now says its object ("Exit to the Wall"). **Verb
dictionary** (§U-a2 §1): Fly's Stop button reads "Stop stream" (was bare "Stop"); the wall tile's
only navigating action — previously the bare device name, silently a link — now has an explicit
"Watch live" button beside the telemetry chip, the name itself demoted to a non-interactive label.
**Poka-yoke** (§U-a2 §2): Start stream shows an inline reason (`.start-hint`, "No camera device on
this drone — nothing to stream") when disabled for lacking a video device, not just a disabled
button; the Stop confirm now names the asset and states the consequence ("Stop *Falcon-2*'s stream?
It ends the stream for all viewers, not just you."); Stop's two buttons (the primary and its confirm)
both carry the new global `danger-action` class (styling owned by another agent). Fly already had
one primary action per card/row — unchanged.

**Tests**: `fly-logic.spec.ts` 24 → 33 (9 new: `streamStateLabel` 2, `lastSeenLabel` 3, `positionLabel`
2, `isAllDronesOption` 2); `npx vitest run src/app/features/fly src/app/features/wall` — 33/33 pass.
Wall got no new pure logic (label-only changes to `wall-tile.ts`'s template/styles), so no new spec
file. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean on both `tsconfig.app.json` and `tsconfig.spec.json` for every
`features/fly/**`/`features/wall/**` file (targeted, not a full build, per the coordinator's
concurrent-agent instruction).

**Deviation**: §U-a2 §3 also names a thumbnail ("if it's streaming, a thumbnail") and an owner field
for the picker card; both stayed out — the task's own concrete field list for this cycle omitted
them (owner is explicitly "post U-e" in the plan, and a thumbnail would need a per-card snapshot
`<img>` fetch, the same new-request fan-out battery was cut for) — recorded here so a future cycle
knows they were a deliberate cut, not an oversight.

## U-a2 map/command/shell — Map + Command + Live + shell's share of action clarity & poka-yoke (docs/UX-REWORK-PLAN.md §U-a, §U-a2)

Scope: `features/map/**`, `features/command/**`, `features/live/**`, `shared/ui/**`, `shared/map/**`
(labels only), the app shell (`app.html`/`app.ts`/`app.routes.ts`), and the global `src/styles.css`
— concurrent agents owned `features/fly`+`features/wall` and `features/devices`+`features/asset-detail`
(+ `core/fleet/warehouse-logic.ts`), untouched here. **U-a deletions/demotions**: the dead PTZ
placeholder card is gone from `live.html` (`hasPtz` in `live.ts` removed with it, now genuinely
unused); the Map empty state's second "Draw a flight plan…" door is gone (`map.ts#openFlightPlanDialog`/
`onFlightPlanSaved`/`onFlightPlanCancelled`/`flightPlanDialogOpen` removed with it) — "Add a test
drone" is the empty state's only action again; `shared/map/flight-plan-dialog.ts`/`flight-plan-logic.ts`
are untouched (still reused as-is by the Devices page's own Simulate step, confirmed by grep before
touching anything). **Debug** is out of `app.ts#moreLinks` — its route is untouched and still
reachable by direct URL, nothing else in the More menu (Wall, Map) changed.
