**Verb dictionary (§U-a2 item 1) — every "Watch"/"Preview"/"Open" instance in this scope, old →
new**: map rail row — "Watch" → "Watch live" (`onWatch`, full-page `/live/:deviceId`), "Preview"
→ "Watch live" (`onPreview`, docks `<vision-live-dock>` inline — shown only while streaming), "Open"
→ "Details" (`openAsset`, `/assets/:id`); `shared/map/fleet-map.ts` marker popup — "Preview" →
"Watch live" (inline dock) and "Open full cockpit" → "Watch live" (full page; both targets are "see
the live video, no controls" per the dictionary, the full-page one via `/live/:deviceId` on Map or
`/fly?asset=…&watch=1` on Command depending on host — never the Fly *piloting* verb, `watch=1`
suppresses controls) — both buttons now read identically, a deliberate consequence of the plan's own
"inline vs. page is a presentation detail, not a new verb" instruction; each keeps a distinguishing
`title` tooltip (`"…inline beside the map"` / `"…on its own page"`) since two adjacent same-labeled
buttons otherwise have no way to tell apart which is which before clicking. Event popup "Open asset"
→ "Details" (same `/assets/:id` target). `command.html` attention row — "Watch" → "Watch live"
(`watchAsset`, `/fly?asset=…&watch=1`), "Open" → "Details" (`openAsset`). `shared/map/live-dock.html`
— "Open full cockpit" → "Watch live" (same full-page-vs-inline reasoning as the fleet-map popup).
`shared/ui/events-rail.ts` gained a new `eventActionLabel(event)` (pure, reuses `resolveEventTarget`)
so its own row shows "Watch live ›" or "Details ›" rather than a bare hover cue — see next paragraph.

**Cards state their action (§U-a2 §2.6)**: `command.html`'s readiness tile (a `<button>` styled as a
card, previously only a `title` tooltip) gained a visible "View in Devices →" line;
`features/command/live-strip-tile.ts` (the whole tile is the click target) gained a visible
"Watch live →" line; `shared/ui/events-rail.ts`'s event row (a full-width clickable row relying only
on `:hover` before this) gained the `eventActionLabel` chevron described above, absent entirely for a
row `eventClickable` already reports as unresolvable — never promises a click that goes nowhere.

**Poka-yoke global styling (§5)** — `src/styles.css` gained three pieces other agents' kebabs already
consume (confirmed by reading their in-flight diffs before finalizing the CSS, not guessed at):
`.danger-action` (red-tinted text/icon only — no auto-inferred divider from sibling position, which
would have broken Fly's own non-menu uses of this same class in a horizontal HUD row and a
Cancel/Confirm dialog row; verified against `fly.html`'s actual two call sites before settling on
this shape) plus a higher-specificity `.kebab-menu .danger-action:hover` pairing so the red hover
tint wins over the menu's own generic grey row-hover; `.disabled-reason` (small muted text under a
disabled control); and — not originally scoped to this task, added after `features/devices/devices.html`'s
in-flight diff showed `.kebab`/`.kebab-trigger`/`.kebab-menu`/`.kebab-item`/`.kebab-divider`/
`.kebab-reason` already in use with no CSS anywhere to back them — a full reusable kebab (⋯) menu
primitive mirroring `app.css`'s own `.tab-more`/`.tab-more-menu` `<details>` idiom (see the stylesheet's
own doc comment for the expected HTML shape). `.kebab-reason` gets the same visual treatment as
`.disabled-reason` (the Devices agent named it separately; not renamed here, out of this scope's
files).

**Tests**: targeted `ng test --include` over `features/command/**`, `core/map/**`, `shared/map/**`
— 98/98 passing (5 spec files: `command-logic`, `core/map/map-store`, `core/map/map-logic`,
`shared/map/flight-plan-logic`, `shared/map/tile-cache-logic` — none of their own logic changed,
confirms no regression). `npx tsc --noEmit` clean on both `tsconfig.app.json`/`tsconfig.spec.json`.
No new spec file needed (every change here is template/global-CSS/doc-comment, no new pure logic —
`events-rail.ts#eventActionLabel` is a thin two-branch wrapper over the already-tested
`resolveEventTarget`, consistent with this app's own "no page-component spec" precedent elsewhere).

## U-a2 devices/asset-detail — Devices + asset-detail's share of action clarity & poka-yoke (docs/UX-REWORK-PLAN.md §U-a item 7, §U-a2)

Scope: `features/devices/**`, `features/asset-detail/**`, and `core/fleet/warehouse-logic.ts`'s
action-matrix only (concurrent agents owned `features/fly`+`features/wall` and
`features/map`+`features/command`+`features/live`+`shared/ui`+the app shell). **Item 7 — kebab
collapse**: the Devices page's Advanced-table row actions and the asset detail page's Hardware
section's device-row actions both collapsed from a flat button wall into one per-row `<details>`
kebab (⋯) menu — a plain, feature-local `<details>`/`<summary>` popover (mirrors `app.css`'s own
`.tab-more` idiom), not a new shared component; **mid-task, the concurrent map/command/shell agent
found this page's own in-flight `.kebab*` markup with no CSS behind it yet and promoted the exact
same shape into a shared `src/styles.css` primitive** — this page's own local CSS copy was removed
once that landed, rather than kept as a shadowing duplicate (see `devices.css`/`asset-detail.css`'s
own doc comments). Every menu entry now reads verb+object via two new label maps on the shared
lifecycle module, `core/fleet/warehouse-logic.ts#DEVICE_ACTION_LABELS`/`ASSET_ACTION_LABELS`
("Rename device…", "Assign to asset…", "Archive device" — ellipsis only for the two that open a
form first) — a pure, additive extension of that module's existing API, nothing there was renamed
or removed. "Create asset from this device" is now **"Promote to asset…"** (button, toast action,
and dialog header alike), its dialog keeping the exact QF-2 explainer paragraph unchanged.

**Item 2 — verb dictionary + lifecycle simplification**: the asset-first list's row actions are now
exactly **Watch live** (the one visible primary button, poka-yoke rule 5) **/ Details / Archive
asset**, the latter two moved into the kebab. Both operator-facing asset surfaces this scope owns —
the list and the asset detail header — now show only **Archive + Restore** (`core/fleet/warehouse-logic.ts#operatorAssetActions`,
new): activate/deactivate no longer render on either, since this codebase has no separate "Advanced"
table for *assets* (only for devices) to retreat those two states into — a deliberate, documented
loss of UI surface for them in this cycle (still reachable via the API/debug console), not a backend
change. Device-level actions stay exactly where item 3 of the plan puts them: Warehouse → Advanced
and the asset detail page's own Hardware section, never on an asset row/card.

**Item 3a — poka-yoke prevention, reasoned**: `warehouse-logic.ts` gained `reasonedDeviceActions(state,
owned, ownerDeviceCount?)` / `reasonedAssetActions(state)` / `operatorAssetActions(state)` — each
action returned as `{action, available, reason?}`, letting a kebab render a disabled entry with its
reason inline (`.kebab-reason`) instead of enabled-then-error. The reasons come from
`availableDeviceActions`/`availableAssetActions`'s own existing state matrix — no second rule set
invented — **plus one real backend precondition, verified by reading `DefaultAssetService`/
`DefaultDeviceService` (vision-application) rather than assumed**: `setState` on either always stops
a running stream itself as part of the very same transition, so Archive is **never** gated on
streaming (the plan's own worked example, "Archive disabled with 'Stop the stream first' while
streaming", does not hold in this domain and was deliberately not implemented). The one precondition
that *is* real — `DefaultAssetService#unassignDevice` refuses (409) to unassign a device that is its
owning asset's only one — now disables Unassign with a reason *before* the click:
`devices-page-logic.ts#DeviceOwner` gained `deviceCount` (threaded through `mapDeviceOwners`), read
by both pages' `deviceActionsFor`.

**Item 3b — Undo over confirm**: Archive (both asset and device, in every surface this scope owns)
now fires immediately with no confirm dialog — the asset list's inline confirm panel, the Advanced
table's inline archive row, the asset header's confirm card, and the Hardware section's inline
archive row are all gone. Each success toast carries an **Undo** action (`ToastService.ok`'s existing
optional `ToastAction`, unmodified) that calls the same lifecycle-restore path the explicit "Restore"
kebab entry uses. Archive bypasses `FleetStore.deleteAsset`/`deleteDevice` — calling `VisionApi`
directly plus `fleet.refresh({quiet:true})` — because that store's own `run()`-wrapped toast has no
Undo slot and adding one needs `core/fleet/fleet-store.ts`, out of this batch's scope; mirrors
`FleetStore#assignDevice`/`#unassignDevice`'s own precedent of a bespoke try/catch when the generic
wrapper's toast isn't the one a caller needs. **Two known, documented gaps, both scope-bounded, not
bugs**: (1) the toast's own auto-dismiss is `ToastService`'s existing fixed 4s `'ok'` duration, not
the 10s the plan names — changing that timing needs the same out-of-scope `core/toast.service.ts`;
(2) an asset archive's Undo restores the asset's own lifecycle only, not the individual devices
Archive cascaded onto (`AssetDeletionResponse#devicesDeleted`) — those stay archived until
independently restored from the Advanced table, since Undo's own primary use case ("wrong row,
seconds ago") doesn't need full cascade reconstruction. **Item 3c**: one primary action per row is
already satisfied by the above (Watch live on the list; Start/Stop/Watch stay the Advanced table's
and Hardware section's own visible actions, only the lifecycle wall moved into the kebab).

**Tests**: `core/fleet/warehouse-logic.spec.ts` 17 → 33 (+16: the full reasons matrix for
`reasonedDeviceActions`/`reasonedAssetActions`/`operatorAssetActions` across every lifecycle
state × ownership × `ownerDeviceCount` combination, plus the two label maps' verb+object wording and
a compile-time totality check); `features/devices/devices-page-logic.spec.ts` 34 → 35 (+1:
`mapDeviceOwners`'s new `deviceCount`, three existing assertions updated for the same field).
**Targeted `ng test --include` over the three touched spec files — 70/70 passing**; a full
reactor-wide `ng test --watch=false` also run for cross-agent regression confidence — **661/661**
passing. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean on both `tsconfig.app.json`/`tsconfig.spec.json`.

## U-b — Visual restyle: engineered, not generated (docs/UX-REWORK-PLAN.md §U-b)

Scope: the full `vision-web/` CSS surface (global tokens, component stylesheets, a handful of
template class/structure hooks) — no other agent held any part of this module concurrently. No
behavior change beyond the one item the plan itself asked for (§7, panel-state memory). Read via
the `frontend-design` skill first; then applied the plan's own 7 items literally rather than a
free restyle, since the plan had already done the design research.

**1. Panels** (`src/styles.css` `:root`): `--radius` 10px → **5px**, `--radius-sm` 6px → **3px**
(same ~0.6 ratio preserved; `--radius-pill` untouched — a pill is a different geometry, not the
"rounded card" tell). The 3-step elevation model (`--bg` L0 → `--panel` L1 → `--panel-raised` L2)
was already consistent across every consumer (audited every `var(--panel*)` call site) — rather
than rename tokens (would touch ~20 files for no visual change), it's now spelled out as an
explicit doc comment on the token block itself, and `--panel-hover` is documented as *not* a fourth
elevation step: every one of its call sites pairs it with `:hover`, confirmed by grep, so it's the
interaction-tint a panel/row goes to on hover, at any elevation, never a resting state. Shadow
audit: `.card` and every panel/tile stylesheet already carried no `box-shadow` (a prior cycle's own
discipline); the 3 non-`.card` `box-shadow` uses that did exist were reviewed individually and kept
— `features/fly/fly.css`'s `.cockpit` hairline-seam fix (flat, zero-blur, not an elevation cue),
`shared/map/fleet-map.css`'s event-marker glow ring (an icon treatment, not a panel), and (after
converting a fourth one, see below) nothing else. Two changes: `features/devices/devices.css`'s
`#add-source-card.highlighted` "just added" ring was the one real violation (a `.card` gaining a
`box-shadow` on a non-overlay state) — converted to `outline`/`outline-offset: -1px` transitioning
`outline-color`, same visual ring, no layout effect, not a shadow. `features/fly/fly.css`'s
`.hud-confirm .card`/`.hud-shortcuts .card` (a full-screen-scrim confirm dialog and the keyboard-
shortcuts help panel) were missing a shadow despite being genuine floating overlays — given one
(`box-shadow: var(--shadow)`), completing the "shadows only on true overlays" rule in both
directions rather than only the removal direction.

**2. Palette**: swept every component stylesheet for a second saturated hue — none found (`--accent`
#4f8cff is the only one; `--ok`/`--warn`/`--danger` are mutually distinct and away from the
accent's blue; no gradients anywhere, confirmed by `grep -rn gradient`). The one real finding:
`--live` (`#ff4d6a`) and `--danger` (`#ff5d5d`) were ~10° apart in hue — visually "the same red"
despite meaning different things (`--live` = a temporal "happening right now" state: the streaming
tally dot, a still-open detection event; `--danger` = something failed). Moved `--live` to
**`#ff3d78`** (~340° rose/magenta) rather than off the red family entirely — a green/amber "live"
light would be the stranger convention for a camera system, so the fix is separation *within* the
warm family, documented at the token itself. No hardcoded companion of the old `--live` hex existed
anywhere (verified — everything referenced `var(--live)` directly) except one: `features/fly/fly.css`'s
`.ticker-row.open` used a translucent `rgb(255 77 106 / 45%)` border (a var() can't carry alpha as a
separate channel), updated in lockstep to `rgb(255 61 120 / 45%)` with a comment tying it back to
the token.

**3. Typography**: `npm install @fontsource/b612-mono` (pinned `5.2.7`), but the two needed woff2
files (400/700, upright, latin-only) are copied into `public/fonts/` and served via a hand-written
`@font-face` pair in `src/styles.css` rather than importing the package's own CSS — mirrors this
module's existing `public/leaflet/leaflet.css` convention (Gotchas: "kept in sync by hand", not a
build step) and gives exact control over "woff2 latin subset only": no woff fallback, no italic,
`unicode-range` copied verbatim from the package's own `400.css`/`700.css`. `--mono` now leads with
`"B612 Mono"`, existing stack demoted to fallback — every existing `.mono`/`var(--mono)` consumer
(ids, timestamps, the OSD's "Position" line, the player's latency badge, Fly's cockpit HUD) picked
this up with zero template changes. `font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums` (inert on non-digit
characters, confirmed safe to apply broadly) was added to: the global `.mono` and `.chip` classes;
`features/live/telemetry-osd.ts`'s `.value` (also switched to the mono font — every OSD numeric
readout, not just Position); `features/fly/fly-osd.ts`'s `.v`/`.chip.dim` (already mono, just
missing the numeric-width guarantee); `shared/player/player.ts`'s latency badge (already mono);
`features/command/command.css`'s `.readiness-total` stat-tile number (switched to mono too); the
three duplicated `.fact dd` facts-grid rules (`shared/player/stream-info-panel.ts`,
`features/asset-detail/asset-detail.css`, `features/replay/replay.css`) got tabular-nums **only**,
deliberately not the mono font — that grid mixes numeric readouts with text facts (category,
transport) the mono font shouldn't touch; and `features/wall/wall-tile.ts`'s `.telemetry-chip`.
Chrome/label scale systematized (`src/styles.css`'s own new doc comment above `h1`–`h3`): two
registers, not one — *display* text (headers, `.brand`) stays mixed-case/600-weight/`-0.01em`
tracking; *structural/label* text (`.label`, `th`, and now `app.css`'s `.tab`) goes uppercase/loosened
`+0.04–0.05em` tracking. The header's nav tabs moved from the display register (0.88rem/500) into
the label register (0.78rem uppercase/600/`+0.04em`) — the one piece of top-level chrome that didn't
already have it.

**4. Header**: confirmed zero action buttons in `app.html` (brand link, nav tabs, the `More`
overflow, two status chips — nothing else) — no removal needed. "Tighten … flat, bordered bottom,
no shadow" was mostly already true; the one real change was `.app-header`'s background:
`color-mix(in srgb, var(--bg) 88%, transparent)` + `backdrop-filter: blur(8px)` (a frosted-glass
affordance, the same soft-depth family as a shadow) → a flat, fully opaque `var(--bg)`. Documented
bonus: this also structurally closes the hairline hand the header's own translucency played in the
`.cockpit` seam gotcha (Gotchas, "a hairline seam between the sticky app header … and this
full-bleed view") — the `fly.css` defensive `box-shadow: 0 -2px 0 #000` fix is left in place as
harmless belt-and-suspenders, not removed.

**5. Segmented controls** (new `.segmented` in `src/styles.css`, joined `.btn`s via
`margin-left: -1px` + per-first/last-child radius rather than `overflow: hidden`, since the map
layer group must still `flex-wrap` at narrow widths): applied to the "boxes: Overlay/Burned/Off"
group on `features/live/live.html` and `features/asset-detail/asset-detail.html` (template class
`row boxes-toggle` → `row segmented`; both files' own local `.boxes-toggle`/`.btn.active` CSS —
duplicated verbatim between the two — removed in favor of the shared global rule, same
promotion-to-global precedent as the kebab menu) and to the map layer picker in
`shared/map/fleet-map.html`/`shared/map/live-map.html` (`class="controls layers segmented"`; each
file's own `.controls.layers` rule gained an explicit `gap: 0` since a component-scoped rule
outranks the global one on specificity). **Deliberately not converted**: `features/wall/wall-tile.ts`'s
boxes toggle is a single cycling icon button, not three parallel buttons — its own doc comment
already explains why ("there is no room for a labeled toggle group at wall-tile scale" on a
30-tile wall); forcing a 3-button segmented control back in would reintroduce the exact space
problem that choice was made to avoid, so it stays as-is.

**6. Panel state memory** (new `core/panel-state.ts`: `readPersistedFlag`/`writePersistedFlag`, two
tiny pure functions over `localStorage`, not a service/store class — see its own doc comment for
why this isn't folded into `core/settings/settings-store.ts`). Wired into exactly the four named,
already-existing toggles: `features/live/live.ts`'s `railOpen`/`mapInsetVisible` and
`features/fly/fly.ts`'s `mapVisible`/`detectionsStripOpen` — each gained a persisted-key constant, an
initial `signal(readPersistedFlag(KEY, <existing default>))`, and one `effect(() => writePersistedFlag(KEY, …))`
in the constructor; every existing toggle method (`toggleRail`, `M`/`Esc` handlers, the detections-
strip button) needed no change at all. `SettingsStore.wallDensity`/`mapLayer` already persisted
before this task (confirmed by reading the store) and were left untouched, per the task's own "skip
any that already persist" instruction; `features/devices/devices.ts`'s `advancedDevicesOpen` was
**not** touched either — real, but not one of the four toggles this item named, so out of scope
for this pass. 3 new spec cases (`core/panel-state.spec.ts`).

**7. Contrast**: `--text-faint` bumped `#5f6b7c` → **`#788496`** — WCAG contrast ratio (used as an
accessible proxy for the APCA check this item asked for, since this codebase has no APCA
calculator) measured ~3.3:1 against `--panel` / ~3.6:1 against `--bg` before the change, both below
the ~4.5:1 a small caption should clear; the new value measures ~4.3:1 / ~4.7:1 against the same
two surfaces while staying visibly the dimmest tier below `--text-muted` (~6:1 against both,
unchanged — already solid, not touched).

**Verify**: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean on both `tsconfig.app.json`/`tsconfig.spec.json`; `npm run
test:ci` **664/664** (661 baseline + 3 new `core/panel-state.spec.ts` cases, nothing else changed
count-wise — every other edit this cycle was CSS/template/token, no new pure logic besides
`panel-state.ts`); `ng build --configuration production` succeeds, initial bundle **309.26 kB raw /
85.88 kB transfer** (baseline immediately before this task: 308.06 kB / 85.64 kB — **+1.20 kB raw /
+0.24 kB transfer**, almost entirely `styles.css` itself, 7.00 kB → 8.26 kB raw; the two B612 Mono
woff2 files, 19.05 kB + 18.98 kB, are `public/` static assets fetched on demand via `@font-face`,
like Leaflet's tiles — they do **not** count against the JS/CSS bundle budget at all, confirmed by
listing `dist/fonts/`). The initial-bundle warning (budget 300 kB) pre-dates this task (baseline was
already 8.06 kB over); this task's own contribution to that overage is +1.20 kB.

**One build hazard worth remembering for any future inline-`styles` edit**: a CSS `/* comment */`
written *inside* a component's inline `styles: \`…\`` template literal must never contain a literal
backtick — even inside a comment, it terminates the outer JS template literal early and produces
`FatalDiagnosticError: Failed to resolve @Component.styles to a string`, a confusing error that
doesn't name the offending file. Hit once this cycle (a `` `.mono` `` reference inside
`features/live/telemetry-osd.ts`'s inline styles, fixed by quoting it `".mono"` instead) — plain
external `.css` files and this module's global `src/styles.css` have no such restriction, since
they're never parsed as TypeScript.

## Two-bug fix cycle — `AssetDetailPage`'s telemetry-churn loop + the events-rail name/time overlap

Two independent bugs; see their own dedicated Gotchas entries above for the full write-up (root
cause, live repro evidence, fix) — this entry is just the verification record.

- **Bug 1 (serious)**: `AssetDetailPage`'s telemetry/detections tracking effects now guard with
  `trackingIdChanged` exactly like `fly.ts`; `TelemetryStore`/`DetectionsStore.track()` themselves
  also no-op on an unchanged `(id, assetId)` key (defense in depth). Confirmed live via CDP against
  the running `ng serve` (untouched throughout — same PID before/after): 62.6 track/untrack lines/sec
  and a matching real PATCH storm before the fix, zero of either after, for the same asset/duration.
- **Bug 2 (cosmetic)**: `shared/ui/events-rail.css`/`.html` — the event row is now two lines (an
  unshrinkable top line, a full-width name+confidence line below); the name truncates with ellipsis
  + `title` instead of overflowing into the time/action text. Verified via CDP screenshots at both
  Wall's 300px sidebar and Command's full-width embed.

**Verify**: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean on both `tsconfig.app.json`/`tsconfig.spec.json`; `npm run
test:ci` **670/670** (664 baseline + 6 net new — `telemetry-store.spec.ts`/`detections-store.spec.ts`
each +1 churn-simulation case, `live-fallback-logic.spec.ts` +4 `trackSessionKey` cases,
`trackingIdChanged`'s own 5-case suite moved from `fly-logic.spec.ts` to
`core/telemetry/telemetry-logic.spec.ts` alongside the function itself — net 0 from the move, +6
overall); `ng build --configuration production` succeeds, initial bundle **309.26 kB raw / 85.89 kB
transfer** — byte-for-byte the same raw size as the immediately-preceding baseline above (85.89 vs.
85.88 kB transfer is rounding noise), confirming every changed file this cycle (`TelemetryStore`/
`DetectionsStore`/`live-fallback-logic.ts`/`asset-detail.ts`/`fly-logic.ts`/`EventsRail`) is reachable
only from already-lazy chunks, same as R-c's own precedent. `ng serve` on `:4200` was left running,
untouched, throughout (verified via `ss -ltnp` before/after, plus `curl` against it before/after
every file change) — no commits, no stash, per this task's own scope.

## U-c — Screen consolidation: map-first Command (docs/UX-REWORK-PLAN.md §U-c, read together with its own user-amendments blockquote — authoritative where the two differ)

Scope: `features/command/**`, `features/map/**`, `shared/ui/**`, the app shell (`app.html`/`app.ts`/
`app.css`/`app.routes.ts`) — concurrent agents owned `cv-service/**` (Python) and the Java modules,
untouched here. One small, deliberate exception to the file-scope list, both justified below:
`core/toast.service.ts`/`core/toast.service.spec.ts` (the task's own instruction to extend
`ToastService` "properly, with tests" if it needed a duration option or a `'notification'` kind — it
did) and one doc-comment fix in `core/api/vision-api.ts` (a stale file reference in a comment, no
behavior change, see its own entry below).

### 1. Layout — map-first, three real panels, not overlays

`CommandPage` is now a full-bleed page (`.command-shell`, `height: calc(100vh - var(--header-height))`,
mirrors `features/fly/fly.css`'s `.cockpit` — the app's other full-bleed page): a slim topbar (title,
asset/streaming counts, "include archived"), then `.command-body`, a **CSS Grid** row of up to five
tracks — `[rail?] [rail-toggle] [map] [panel-toggle?] [panel?]` — computed by
`command-logic.ts#commandGridColumns(railOpen, panelState)`, unit-tested across all 6 combinations
(2 rail states × 3 panel states) rather than assembled from template `[class.x]` toggles.

**Deliberately real grid-track siblings, not `position: absolute` overlays on top of the map** — this
is the one specific instruction ("learn from Fly's switcher-overlap incident: geometric separation
over z-index") this task took most literally, and for a concrete, checked reason:
`shared/map/fleet-map.ts`'s own zoom/layer controls are fixed at `top: 0.5rem` in *its own* corners
(unmodifiable — the plan explicitly rules out reworking `fleet-map`/`live-map` themselves). An
absolutely-positioned rail sharing that corner would occlude them, reproducing the exact
"switcher unreachable under the map inset" bug (docs/UX-QUICKWINS-PLAN.md QF-1) byte-for-byte, just
with a different pair of controls. Docking rail/panel as real layout siblings instead means the
map's own corner controls and this page's own chrome never share a pixel — by construction, not by
z-index arbitration. **Verified live, not just reasoned about**: a `ng serve`+backend screenshot at
1600×900 (`/command` and `/command?asset=<id>`) confirms the rail/panel never occlude the map's own
zoom/layer/recenter controls at any panel-open combination — see the Gotchas entry below for the one
pre-existing, out-of-scope overlap this same screenshot *did* surface (Leaflet's own zoom control vs.
`fleet-map.ts`'s own layer switcher — unrelated to this task, `shared/map/**` untouched, confirmed via
`git diff`).

- **Entity rail (left, collapsible, `vision.command.railOpen`, `core/panel-state.ts`)** — replaces
  *both* the old separate "attention queue" card *and* the old `/map` page's "every asset" rail with
  one slim, always-available list: `command-logic.ts#buildEntityRows(assets)` sorts **every** asset
  (not just flagged ones) attention-first — highest triggered reason rank, then more simultaneous
  reasons, then alphabetical — so a quiet fleet still shows a complete, ordered list, not an empty
  card. Each row: a live/offline dot, name, category, and a severity chip (CRIT/WARN) only when
  `reasons.length > 0`. Clicking a row calls `selectAsset(assetId)`.
- **Selection → right detail panel (`vision.command.panelOpen`)** — a rail row click **or**
  `<vision-fleet-map>`'s own `(preview)` output (a direct marker click — **unchanged component
  behavior**, per the task's own "keep the existing marker/popup behavior" instruction) both call
  `CommandPage.selectAsset(assetId)`: sets `selectedAssetId`, force-reopens the panel (a fresh
  selection always shows it, even if previously collapsed — collapsing means "get out of my way for
  now", not "never show me a panel again"), and resolves the video device via a **one-shot**
  `FleetMapStore.resolveWatchDevice(assetId)` call — the exact reuse the old docked-preview's own
  `onPreview` already established, just retargeted (see item 2 below for what changed and why).
  `panelState` (`'hidden' | 'open' | 'collapsed'`) is tied to `selectedAsset()` — a `computed()` that
  re-derives from the current summary, not the raw id — so a selection that stops existing (archived,
  or the "include archived" toggle flipped) self-heals the layout back to no panel.
- **`AssetPanel` (`<vision-asset-panel>`, `features/command/asset-panel.ts`/`.html`/`.css`)** — the
  one genuinely new component this rework adds (every other section reuses something that already
  existed, per the plan's own binding reuse-first rule). ~380px, `.segmented` tabs (docs/UX-REWORK-PLAN.md
  §U-b item 5's own primitive, reused verbatim): **Status** (stream/lifecycle/open-events/telemetry-age
  chips + the same `attentionReasons` "why" sentence the rail's own severity chip summarizes),
  **Telemetry** (a `dl.facts`/`.fact` grid — position/altitude/heading/battery/sample-age — the same
  idiom independently duplicated three times already by `features/asset-detail/**`/
  `shared/player/stream-info-panel.ts`/`features/replay/**`; a fourth consistent copy, not a new
  pattern), **Video** (`<vision-player>`, unmodified, `[compact]="true"`, mirrors
  `shared/map/live-dock.ts`'s own usage — "reuse existing components (vision-player, telemetry facts
  patterns) rather than new ones", read literally). Actions: **Watch live**/**Details** (emits
  `watchLive`/`openDetails`; `CommandPage` navigates — the panel itself touches neither `Router` nor
  `VisionApi`). **Zero HTTP, zero stores** — every input (`asset: AssetAttention`, `marker:
  FleetMarker | undefined`, `videoDeviceId`/`stream`) is data `CommandPage` already has from its own
  existing pollers (the one summary poll + the embedded map's own `FleetMapStore`), so selecting an
  asset adds **no new recurring request** — only the one-shot device lookup above.

### 2. Removed (per the plan's own user-amendments blockquote)

- **Warehouse-readiness tiles** — gone from Command entirely ("drill-down lives in Warehouse itself").
  `command-logic.ts#sortReadinessTiles`/`totalStreaming` deleted (not moved — grep-verified no other
  consumer first); `CategoryCounts` import dropped.
- **Live strip** — gone. `live-strip-tile.ts` (`LiveStripTile`) **deleted outright** (grep-verified
  `command.ts`/`.html` were its only consumers); `command-logic.ts#streamingAssets`/`shouldPollSnapshot`
  deleted with it. `core/api/vision-api.ts#snapshotUrl` (its one API-client method) is **left in
  place, currently unused** — a small, self-contained, still-correct method; its own doc comment now
  says so plainly rather than pointing at a deleted file (the one justified touch to a file outside
  this task's own scope list).
- **Events rail** — gone from Command (moved into the header bell's dropdown, see item 3).
- **`<vision-live-dock>`** — no longer used by Command (its role is now `AssetPanel`'s own Video tab,
  a superset: facts + actions, not just a bare player). Left untouched in `shared/map/**` (out of this
  task's file scope) — see the Gotchas entry: it now has **zero consumers at all**, `MapPage` (its
  other host) having been deleted the same cycle.

### 3. Events → notifications (the header bell)

New: `shared/ui/notification-bell.ts`/`.html`/`.css` (`<vision-notification-bell>`), mounted in
`app.html`'s header next to the existing live-count/online status chips — a deliberate, named
exception to docs/UX-REWORK-PLAN.md §U-b item 4's "zero action buttons in persistent chrome", carved
out explicitly by the plan's own §U-c user-amendments blockquote.

- **Unread badge**: `shared/ui/notification-logic.ts#unreadEvents(events, readIds)` — `readIds` is a
  signal, replaced wholesale with every currently-listed event id whenever the dropdown opens
  (`(toggle)` on the native `<details>`, this app's third consumer of the `.tab-more`/`.kebab-menu`
  disclosure idiom after those two). Independent of `EventsStore`'s own private `seenIds` (a
  poll-arrival dedupe with a different job).
- **Toasts**: `shared/ui/notification-logic.ts#newlyOpenedEvents(events, knownIds)` — genuinely new,
  currently-`OPEN` arrivals only (mirrors `core/events/events-logic.ts#shouldNotify`'s own
  `!alreadySeen && state==='OPEN'` pair, but with **no** permission/hidden gating: a toast is this
  app's own chrome, inert unless the tab is open, so there's no "user isn't looking" case to gate on
  the way the opt-in native `Notification` path has). The very first read after mount seeds
  `toastedIds` (a plain mutable `Set`, mirrors `EventsStore`'s own `seenIds` precedent) with whatever
  is already in the feed **without** toasting it — history, not news; only a *later* poll's genuinely
  new arrivals toast. Each toast: `ToastService.notify(text, action?)` — **new method + `'notification'`
  kind** (`core/toast.service.ts`, own `DISMISS_AFTER_MS` entry, 6s between `ok`'s 4s and `error`'s
  9s; own `.toast.notification` style in `toast-host.ts`, the app's one accent color, no new hue) —
  with a `{label: 'Watch live' | 'Details', onClick}` action built from the same `resolveEventTarget`
  every other events consumer uses.
- **Dropdown = `<vision-events-rail>`, reused wholly unmodified, third host** — "reuse events-rail's
  row rendering by moving it into the dropdown", read literally: the whole component moves in
  (`class="bell-dropdown"`), not just its template. Its own filters/cap/row-content/click-target
  needed zero changes. Row click closes the dropdown then resolves/navigates exactly like
  `WallPage.openEvent`.
- **`EventsStore` stays the data source, unchanged public API** — but its own cost model changes:
  `NotificationBell` is a **fourth consumer that never releases** (`App` is the root component, only
  destroyed on a full page unload), so the store's 5s poll is now **effectively always-on**, matching
  `FleetStore`'s "starts at boot, never stops" posture rather than its own former "O(visible)
  discipline" one. **Intended, not a regression** — an unread badge/toasts that only worked while the
  operator happened to be on Wall/Command/an asset page would defeat the point. `FleetMap`'s own event
  markers (unchanged) simply read a feed the bell now keeps warm everywhere.

### 4. Routes

`features/map/map.routes.ts` is now a bare `{path: 'map', redirectTo: 'command', pathMatch: 'full'}`
— `MapPage`/`map.ts`/`.html`/`.css` **deleted**, not just unrouted (the plan's own "components fold
in"): every job that page did (fleet map, "every asset" rail, docked preview, empty-state "Add a test
drone") now lives in `features/command/**`. `CommandPage` gained `requestedAssetId`
(`input<string|undefined>(undefined, {alias:'asset'})`, mirrors `fly.ts`'s identical `?asset=`
input) so a redirected `/map?asset=<id>` (or any future `?asset=` link) resolves into a selection on
arrival — `/map` itself never actually supported `?asset=` (grep-verified before adding this), so
there was nothing concrete to preserve *today*, but old bare `/map` bookmarks keep working, which is
the redirect's actual job. `app.ts`'s `moreLinks` loses `Map` (`Wall` stays — the plan's own
user-amendments blockquote: "the Wall stays as its own route … Command does NOT absorb Wall").

### 5. Styles/colors

Followed the U-b system strictly — no new tokens, no new colors. `.segmented` (tabs), `.chip`/`.dot`
(severity/streaming indicators), `.card`-adjacent flat panels (`var(--panel)`/`var(--panel-raised)`,
borders not shadows), `var(--shadow)` only on the bell's own popover (a true overlay). The one new
color-*adjacent* decision — the notification toast's own accent tint — reuses `var(--accent)`/
`var(--accent-soft)` verbatim, audited against U-b item 2's "one saturated accent" rule before
writing it (see `toast-host.ts`'s own comment).

### Tests

`ng test --watch=false` (full suite, run twice — once targeted mid-task, once full at the end, per
the task's own instruction): **670 → 677** (+7 net: `command-logic.spec.ts` 33 → 27 tests — old
`buildAttentionQueue`/`totalStreaming`/`streamingAssets`/`shouldPollSnapshot` suites removed with the
functions they tested, `buildEntityRows`/`commandGridColumns` suites added, `-6` net — plus two new
files, `shared/ui/notification-logic.spec.ts` (+7) and `core/toast.service.spec.ts` (+6, fake-timers-
based, covers every `ToastKind`'s own dismiss duration/action wiring/`dismiss(id)`)). `npx tsc --noEmit`
clean on both `tsconfig.app.json`/`tsconfig.spec.json`. `ng build --configuration production`
succeeds: initial bundle **309.26 kB → 325.88 kB raw / 85.89 kB → 91.99 kB transfer** (+16.62 kB raw
/ +6.10 kB transfer) — almost entirely `EventsRail`/`EventsStore`/`core/events/events-logic.ts` moving
from a shared **lazy** chunk into the **initial** bundle now that `App`'s own header eagerly imports
`NotificationBell` → `EventsRail` (an unavoidable consequence of "a bell in the header" — a header is
eager by definition); pushes the pre-existing 300 kB *warning*-only budget overage from 9.26 kB to
25.88 kB over (still nowhere near the 360 kB *error* threshold). `command`'s own lazy chunk grew
20.32 kB → 34.15 kB (the new `AssetPanel` + the rewritten `command-logic.ts`/`command.ts`); the old
`map` lazy chunk is **gone** (no `loadComponent` left in `features/map/map.routes.ts`).

**Manual verification**: two `google-chrome --headless=new --screenshot` captures at 1600×900 against
`ng serve`+the real backend (both left running/untouched throughout, per this task's own instruction)
— `/command` (rail + full-bleed map + header bell showing a real unread count from already-open
detection events) and `/command?asset=<realAssetId>` (confirms the query-param deep link opens the
panel, the Status tab renders the correct severity chip/why-text for a real 0%-battery asset, and the
rail/toggle/map/panel grid never overlaps the map's own corner controls at any panel state).

**Known, documented gaps, not silently dropped**: (1) the rail is not CDK-virtualized — the original
plan's own ">200 assets" scale note is aspirational, not restated as a requirement by this task's own
five numbered instructions, and this app's demo-scale fleets don't need it yet; a future cycle can add
`*cdkVirtualFor` to `.rail-list` exactly as `features/devices/devices.ts`'s own asset list already
does. (2) The pre-existing Leaflet-zoom-vs-layer-switcher overlap inside `fleet-map.ts` (Gotchas,
above) — out of this task's scope to fix, flagged for the next agent who touches `shared/map/**`.
(3) `shared/map/live-dock.ts` is now a zero-consumer orphan (Gotchas, above) — left in place, not
deleted, since `shared/map/**` was never in this task's own file scope.

## Detection model picker (docs/CV-MODELS-PLAN.md item 4)

Scope: `core/settings/settings-store.ts` (+ its spec), `features/settings/**`, `features/live/**`;
`features/asset-detail/**` was checked and excluded — it calls `fleet.start(device.id,
settings.effective())` but hosts no confidence/fps card of its own to extend, so there was nothing
to add the picker to there.

**Store**: `PipelineProfile`/`PipelineSettings` gained a required `model: DetectionModelId` field
(`'yolo11n.pt' | 'orion12l.pt' | 'yolo11n.pt,orion12l.pt'` — ids are exactly cv-service's registry
checkpoint filenames, the composite one comma-joined per docs/CV-MODELS-PLAN.md item 2 — exported
alongside `DETECTION_MODEL_OPTIONS` (id/label/one-line hint for General/Military/Both) and
`DEFAULT_DETECTION_MODEL = 'yolo11n.pt'`) — the same profile/draft/custom-save/revert semantics
confidence and fps already had; `effective()`, `adjust()`, `saveDraftAs()` all extended, no new
methods added. Migration-safe restore: a `customProfiles` entry or `draft` persisted before this
field existed (missing entirely, not merely invalid) backfills to `DEFAULT_DETECTION_MODEL` via a
new `withValidModel()` helper — the same "a corrupt/missing field never sinks the rest of the
object" pattern `isMapLayerId`/`eventNotifications` already used, generalized to the two call sites
(`customProfiles`, `draft`) that needed it instead of one flat field.

**UI**: `features/settings/settings.html`'s "Detection profile" card gained a 3-card picker
(General/Military/Both), reusing the existing `.profiles`/`.profile` card CSS verbatim — the same
selectable-card look as the preset picker directly above it, so both hints and the "Both" cost are
always visible rather than hidden behind a hover/click (poka-yoke: see the cost before picking it,
not after). `features/live/live.html`'s rail "Detection settings" card instead reuses that page's
own existing `.row.segmented` 3-button idiom (already established there for the Detection-boxes
overlay/burned/off toggle) plus a `title` tooltip per button and one dynamic hint line underneath
showing the selected option's own hint — a narrower-rail-appropriate compact form of the same
disclosure, not a new widget. Both pages' profile summary lines (`conf … · … fps`) now append the
model's label too (`settings.ts#modelLabel`), so a saved custom profile's model choice is visible at
a glance, not just its confidence/fps.

**The honesty-required "Both" cost**: surfaced verbatim as measured — "Runs both models on every
frame — boxes update ~3×/s, far slower than either model alone (measured: ~346ms/frame vs. ~58ms for
General alone)." No number is invented for Military-alone (not measured); its hint states only what
it detects, not a speed claim — `orion12l.pt` is a much larger checkpoint (53 MB vs. 5.6 MB) than
`yolo11n.pt`, so assuming "same speed as General" would likely have been false.

**Backend gap — found missing, reported, then closed mid-task by a concurrent agent, verified
here**: at the time this task started, `StartStreamRequest`/`StartAssetStreamRequest`
(`vision-api/src/main/java/com/drones/vision/api/dto/`, read-only from this task's own original file
scope) had no `model` field — only `confidenceThreshold`/`inferenceFps`/`overlayBurnIn` — so
`PipelineConfig.mergeOntoDefaults()` always kept `defaults().model()` regardless of what a
stream-start request carried; this was reported rather than hacked around (the task's own explicit
instruction), since `core/api/**` mirrors the Java DTOs and was out of the originally assigned scope.
**While this task was still in progress, a separate concurrent agent added exactly that field**
(`StartStreamRequest.java`/`StartAssetStreamRequest.java` gained a `String model` component;
`mergeOntoDefaults()` now builds `new ModelRef(model, defaults.model().version())` when `model` is
present and non-blank, else keeps `defaults.model()` unchanged — same field name, same "raw
possibly-composite string, verbatim, never split here" contract this task's own frontend already
assumed) — confirmed by reading their diff and their three new `StreamControllerTest` cases
(override, absent, blank-treated-as-absent). Given the direct request mid-task to make the picker
actually reach the backend, this task then (a) added the matching `model?: string` field to
`core/api/models.ts#StartStreamRequest` (the one `core/api/**` edit made here, now that the DTO it
mirrors genuinely has the field) and (b) re-verified the whole chain: `GrpcDetectionPort` already
sends `config.model().id()` as the wire `model_id` (`adapter-cv-grpc/src/main/java/.../GrpcDetectionPort.java:263`,
untouched, no change needed there), and `live.ts`/`settings.ts`/`asset-detail.ts`/`devices.ts`/
`fly.ts` all pass `settings.effective()` straight into `fleet.start()` → `VisionApi.startStream()` →
`HttpClient.post(url, request)` with no destructuring anywhere in that chain
(`core/fleet/fleet-store.ts:166`, `core/api/vision-api.ts:83`). Net result: the picker is wired
end to end, verified two ways — `./mvnw -B -pl vision-domain,vision-application,vision-api -am test
-Dtest=StreamControllerTest` (scoped, not reactor-wide, per this file's own build-safety rule) — 30/30
pass, including the concurrent agent's new model-override cases; and this task's own frontend tests
(below) confirm the field survives the profile/draft/persistence path unchanged. Not independently
re-verified here: `adapter-cv-grpc`/`cv-service` themselves (untouched by either agent this cycle,
already covered by the plan's own cv-service pytest suite per docs/CV-MODELS-PLAN.md) and the live
dev backend process (left running/untouched, per this task's own instruction not to disturb it —
its `target/classes` predate this change and would need a rebuild+restart neither task performed).

**Tests**: `core/settings/settings-store.spec.ts` 13 → 19 (6 new: every built-in profile defaults to
`DEFAULT_DETECTION_MODEL`; adjusting `model` on an active preset turns it into a revertible custom
draft exactly like adjusting confidence/fps does; saving a draft's model choice — including the
composite id — as a reusable profile; that choice's round-trip across a simulated reload; a corrupt
persisted draft `model` is ignored without losing the draft's other fields; a `customProfiles` entry
persisted with no `model` field at all — i.e. saved before this task — migrates to the default
without dropping its own confidence/fps). `npx ng test --watch=false --include=src/app/core/settings
--include=src/app/features/live --include=src/app/features/settings` — 19/19 pass (the only spec
file inside this scope is `settings-store.spec.ts`; `features/live`/`features/settings` have none of
their own). `npx tsc --noEmit` clean on both `tsconfig.app.json` and `tsconfig.spec.json`. Full `npm
run test:ci` (run once, per the concurrent-agent instruction to report failures outside this task's
own files as environmental rather than fixing them) — 683/683 tests passing across 37/37 spec files,
zero failures anywhere.

**Follow-up once `core/api/models.ts` gained `model?: string`** (see the Backend-gap paragraph
above): re-ran `ng test --watch=false --include=src/app/core/settings --include=src/app/features/live
--include=src/app/features/settings --include=src/app/core/api` — 50/50 pass (adds
`core/api/vision-api.spec.ts`'s 31 cases, unaffected — a new optional field on a request interface
changes nothing existing constructs there). `tsc --noEmit` re-checked clean on both configs.

## `FleetStore`/`EventsStore` become `LiveStore` projections; Promote-to-asset via `deviceIds`; archive-undo device cascade (docs/REALTIME-PLAN.md §4's backend follow-up batch)

Scope: `core/fleet/**`, `core/events/**`, `core/live/**`, `features/devices/**` only, per the
coordinator's own instruction — a concurrent agent had `core/settings`+`features/settings`+
`features/live` (+`features/asset-detail`), and (per `git status`) several others were touching
`features/command`/`features/map`/`shared/ui` besides. Backend half (`vision-api`'s
`LiveTopicKind`/`LiveTopic`/`LiveUpdateRegistry` two new topics, `DevicesSnapshotResponse`,
`CreateAssetRequest#deviceIds`) landed uncommitted, in the tree, ahead of this task — read directly
from source (see vision-api/MODULE.md's own "Backend follow-up batch" entry), not re-derived. No
`git stash`; `ng serve` on `:4200` left running, untouched, throughout; no commits.

**Public API of both stores is unchanged — no consuming page needed to change** (`FleetStore.devices`/
`.streams`/`.reachable`/`.loading`/`.liveDeviceIds`; `EventsStore.events`/`.activate`/`.release`),
exactly like R-c's own `TelemetryStore`/`DetectionsStore` precedent.

**What now arrives via SSE vs poll**:
- **`FleetStore`**: `devices`/`streams` are now set atomically from the always-on `devices` topic's
  `DevicesSnapshotResponse` (both lists from the *same* envelope — never independently stale
  relative to each other) whenever `LiveStore` is `'open'`; the 5s `GET /api/devices`+`GET
  /api/streams` poll is the documented fallback, paused entirely while live and resumed (with an
  immediate re-fetch) the moment live drops. The constructor's own one-time `refresh()` stays
  unconditional — mirrors `TelemetryStore`'s "always one backfill fetch" precedent, painting
  something before the SSE handshake completes. No ref-counted subscribe/unsubscribe — `devices` is
  always-on, unlike `telemetry:<assetId>`/`detections:<assetId>`.
- **`EventsStore`**: `events()` is now also fed by the always-on `detection-events` topic (the exact
  `DetectionEventResponse` shape `GET /api/events` already served, FIFO, snapshot-on-connect
  oldest-first); the 5s poll (still `ignoreHidden: true`) is the fallback, paused while live. **One
  retained signal, not two** — a deliberate departure from `TelemetryStore`/`DetectionsStore`'s own
  "poll-fed signal + live-fed signal + a `computed` selecting between them" shape: both transports
  here feed the *same* `mergeEvents` upsert-by-id merge (its own "incoming always wins" contract
  already reconciles overlap/replay from either source), so there is nothing to select between.
  Notification semantics (`seenIds` dedupe, `OPEN`-only, permission+`documentHidden`-gated) are
  identical regardless of which transport actually delivered an event.
- **`LiveStore`**: two new always-on envelope types routed (confirmed the exact wire strings —
  `'devices'`/`'detection-events'` — by reading `LiveTopicKind.java` directly, not assumed from
  naming). `'devices'` → `DevicesSnapshot`, a plain wholesale replace like `'fleet'`. `'detection-events'`
  → `DetectionEvent`, accumulated **chronologically (oldest-first, true FIFO append, trimmed from the
  front on overflow)** — deliberately the *opposite* order from `liveEvents`' own newest-first
  prepend, because the same `DetectionEvent` id recurs across its `OPEN`→`CLOSED` lifecycle and
  `EventsStore`'s own `mergeEvents` needs same-id repeats processed in true chronological order (a
  later array entry must win) to resolve which state is actually current.

**Promote-to-asset, the actual request-shape change** (docs/CYCLES-PLAN.md QF-2 → docs/UX-REWORK-PLAN.md
§U-a2 §3 → this task): `CreateAssetRequest` (`models.ts`) gains `deviceIds?: readonly string[]`
(`devices` made optional too — either/both may supply the required ≥1 device, mirroring the Java
DTO's own new shape). `buildCreateAssetRequestForDevice` (`features/devices/devices-page-logic.ts`)
now builds `{displayName, category, deviceIds: [device.id]}` — no `devices` array at all.
`devices.ts#confirmCreateAsset`'s own re-register-then-archive workaround (register a brand-new
device wrapping the original's connection details, then `fleet.deleteDevice(device.id)` to archive
the now-superseded original) is **deleted outright**, not merely deprecated — the dialog's own
explanatory paragraph (`devices.html`) rewritten to match ("Assigns this device … to a new asset —
its own id, history, and connection details are kept exactly as they are"). Kept
`buildCreateAssetRequestForDevice`'s own tests (`devices-page-logic.spec.ts`), rewritten for the new
shape (same case count, new assertions).

**Archive-Undo cascade (devices flow only, docs/UX-REWORK-PLAN.md §U-a2 item 3b's own documented
gap, now closed)**: `archiveAssetNow`'s Undo toast action now calls a new `undoArchiveAsset(assetId,
displayName, devicesArchived)` instead of the plain `restoreAssetNow` — restores the asset first
(sequencing matters: a device list/assign call against a still-archived asset could 404 otherwise),
then, since `devicesArchived` (`AssetDeletionResponse#devicesDeleted`, captured at the exact moment
of *this* archive, never re-derived or guessed) says how many devices to expect, fetches the asset's
current device list and restores every device Archive's own cascade left `DELETED`
(`Promise.allSettled` — one stuck/failed device restore never blocks the rest). The toast names
exactly what happened: `"Restored X and N device(s)."` on full success, a specific partial-failure
sentence (succeeded/failed counts, pointing at the Advanced table) when any device restore fails,
without rolling back the asset restore itself. **The explicit "Restore asset" kebab entry
(`restoreAssetNow`, for an asset surfaced via "Show archived") is deliberately left untouched** — it
has no trustworthy `devicesArchived` figure of its own to act on (the asset may have been archived
in an earlier session, or had devices independently archived/restored since), so it stays
asset-lifecycle-only, exactly as its own doc comment already said before this task.

**A genuine, reproduced infinite-loop bug found and fixed while wiring `EventsStore`'s live
accumulation — flagged here as material for this file's own Gotchas section next time it's
touched, since the general lesson generalizes well beyond this one call site.** The first cut of
`applyIncoming` wrote `this.eventsSignal.set(mergeEvents(this.eventsSignal(), incoming, …))` from
inside the live-arrival `effect()`: reading `eventsSignal()` through its tracked public getter
registers `eventsSignal` as a dependency of *whichever effect is currently executing*, and the very
next line's `.set()` — `mergeEvents` builds a brand-new array every call, never the same reference
twice — immediately re-dirtied that same signal, re-notifying the very effect that had just read it,
which read-and-wrote again, forever. **Confirmed live, not just reasoned about**: `ng test` hung
indefinitely and could not be stopped by any test-level timeout (a synchronous JS loop cannot be
preempted by an async mechanism at all — only an external process kill ended it); bisected to the
exact test/line via `console.error` instrumentation plus binary-search test isolation (`--filter`),
confirming zero iterations of the *other*, unrelated transport effect ran either, since the whole
worker thread was permanently pegged inside the one loop. **Fixed** by switching to
`eventsSignal.update((existing) => mergeEvents(existing, incoming, …))` — `.update()`'s callback
receives the current value directly, with no tracked read at all — exactly the idiom
`DetectionsStore`'s own identical-shaped accumulation effect (`liveResultsSignal.update(...)`)
already used, which is precisely what kept *that* class safe from this exact bug shape.
**The general lesson**: inside an `effect()`, never read a signal via `.set(fn(signal()))` when that
signal might also be written by the same effect (directly, or through whatever call chain the
effect triggers) — `.update(fn)` is the safe idiom whenever an effect accumulates onto signal state
it also owns. `FleetStore.applyDevicesSnapshot` was re-checked against this same class of bug once
found here (it never reads `devicesSignal`/`streamsSignal`/`reachableSignal` before writing them —
every write derives purely from the incoming `snapshot` parameter) and confirmed safe by inspection
and by its own spec run never hanging.

**New file**: `core/fleet/fleet-store.spec.ts` (5 cases) — this class had no dedicated spec before
this task (its pre-existing surface was a thin `run()`-wrapped pass-through over `VisionApi`,
exercised only indirectly); the new poll-vs-live transport switch is exactly the kind of decision
logic this codebase always covers directly, mirroring `telemetry-store.spec.ts`/
`detections-store.spec.ts`'s own "LiveStore projection" section — stubs `LiveStore` with real
`signal()`s, same technique.

**Tests**: `events-store.spec.ts` 14 → 19 (+5: no-poll-while-activated-live,
live-arrival-folds-in-with-no-poll, poll→live stops the poll, live→poll refetches immediately, a
same-id `OPEN`→`CLOSED` pair arriving in one live batch resolves to `CLOSED` and notifies exactly
once for the genuine `OPEN`); `fleet-store.spec.ts` new, 5 cases;
`devices-page-logic.spec.ts`'s `buildCreateAssetRequestForDevice` suite rewritten in place (4 cases,
same count). Targeted `ng test --watch=false --include=core/fleet --include=core/events
--include=core/live --include=core/telemetry --include=core/detections --include=features/devices`
— **266/266 passing**. Full `npm run test:ci` — **693/693 passing across 38 files** (per the
concurrent-agent instruction, a failure outside this task's own files would be reported as
environmental rather than fixed; none observed). `npx tsc --noEmit` clean on both
`tsconfig.app.json`/`tsconfig.spec.json`.

**Bundle impact, measured** (`ng build --configuration production`): initial bundle **325.88 kB →
332.70 kB raw / 91.99 kB → 93.94 kB transfer (+6.82 kB raw / +1.95 kB transfer)** — `LiveStore`
(+`live-fallback-logic.ts`) was previously tree-shakable into whichever lazy chunk first injected it
(`fly`/`live`/`asset-detail`/a `WallTile`); now that **both** `FleetStore` (eager since `app.ts`'s
own constructor injects it) and `EventsStore` (eager since `shared/ui/notification-bell.ts`, per
docs/UX-REWORK-PLAN.md §U-c) inject it directly, it moves into the initial bundle for real — an
expected, unavoidable consequence of "the two remaining named R-c stores becoming projections," not
a regression to chase.

**What could not be verified without a real `GET /api/live` backend actually delivering
`devices`/`detection-events` traffic** (the same caveat R-c's own Status entry already named for the
original four topics, unchanged in kind): envelope routing by `type`, both stores' transport-switch
effects, and the archive-undo cascade's own device-restore sequencing are all exercised via
`LiveStore`/`VisionApi` test doubles reproducing the documented wire/DTO shapes (read directly from
`LiveTopicKind.java`/`DevicesSnapshotResponse.java`/`CreateAssetRequest.java`, never guessed at), not
against bytes a running backend actually produced this session.

## Infra + cleanup — mediamtx 1.19.2 → 1.19.3 bump + live WHEP re-soak, `live-dock.ts` deletion, map zoom-control fix, stale MapPage/O(visible) doc-comment fixes (2026-07-24)

Scope: `docker-compose.yml`, `vision-web/src/app/shared/map/**`, `core/map/**`/`core/events/events-store.ts` doc comments, and the live WHEP soak this bump's own compose comment obligates. Two other agents worked concurrently in the Java modules and `vision-web` features/onboarding+devices+asset-detail — untouched by this batch.

**mediamtx bump, rationale (see `docker-compose.yml`'s own rewritten comment for the full text):** 1.19.3 fixes #5975 (WebRTC/HLS CORS-credentials: mediamtx used to echo the request's own `Origin` back in `Access-Control-Allow-Origin` instead of `"*"` when `AllowOrigins` is a wildcard, letting any third-party site piggyback on a browser that had ever entered credentials for this mediamtx) — directly relevant to this stack's HLS+WHEP viewing path. The prior comment's own #4022 citation (WebRTC freeze-on-interruption) was retired to a "historical, not the actual rationale" footnote: that bug was fixed in v1.11.1, years before this pin was ever taken. `docker compose pull mediamtx && docker compose up -d mediamtx` recreated only the `vision-mediamtx-1` container — the backend JVM and `ng serve` dev session were never touched, confirmed by `docker compose ps`/`ss -ltnp` before and after.

**Live re-soak against 1.19.3, docs/REALTIME-PLAN.md's own R-b/R-d exit criteria** (a fresh file-protocol simulated asset via `POST /api/simulations`, watched on `/fly?asset=…&watch=1` through headless Chrome + CDP, network/console fully captured):

| Phase | Duration | Result |
|---|---|---|
| Healthy soak | 4 min (task-specified, shorter than R-b's own 10-min exit criterion) | **PASS** — exactly 1 WHEP `OPTIONS`+`POST` (→204/201) for the whole run, **zero** re-POSTs, **zero** DELETEs, zero console exceptions. `RTCPeerConnection.getStats()` confirmed continuous decode the entire time (`framesDecoded` climbing from 19 → 6162+ at a steady ~24–25 fps, `framesDropped: 0`, `connectionState: 'connected'` throughout) |
| Feed-kill (source file renamed mid-stream) | ~90s down + ~60s recovery | **PASS** — see the wire-level detail below; full ladder (ICE-restart → teardown/re-POST with capped exponential backoff → recovery) exercised and correct |

**mediamtx 1.19.3's WHEP PATCH behavior, recorded precisely (this plan's own "unverified pending a re-soak" item from the R-b Status entry above — now verified, against a real mediamtx, for the first time):**
1. Stall detected after 8000ms of no frame progress (`STALL_WATCHDOG_MS`, unchanged) → first ICE-restart PATCH → **mediamtx 1.19.3 returns `200`**, identical to 1.19.2's documented contract (`session.go`'s shared WHIP/WHEP ICE-restart branch) — media briefly reported "advancing" again at the signaling layer even though the underlying source was genuinely gone (nothing to actually decode).
2. `connectionState` → `'disconnected'` ~7s later → 5s grace timer → a second stall detection → second ICE-restart PATCH to the *same* session URL → **mediamtx 1.19.3 returns `404`** this time (the session resource had already been invalidated server-side, consistent with the underlying RTSP path having no publisher at all — not a wire-format regression, mediamtx correctly reports a session it no longer tracks). The frontend's `isPatchFallbackStatus` (already named `404`/`412` explicitly per the R-b Status entry above) correctly treated this as the fallback trigger it was designed for.
3. Full teardown → `DELETE` (`404`, idempotent-safe) → fresh `POST` retries against the still-dead path (`404` each time, no publisher) with a clean, capped exponential backoff observed exactly: **1000ms → 2000ms → 4000ms → 8000ms → 16000ms → 30000ms (cap)** — matching S-c's pacing design byte-for-byte, never a tight retry loop.
4. Once the video file was restored and the underlying RTSP republish came back online (confirmed via `docker logs vision-mediamtx-1`: `stream is available and online` for the same path), the next scheduled (30s-capped) retry succeeded with a fresh `POST` → `201`, and playback fully recovered (`framesDecoded` climbing steadily again, `connectionState: 'connected'`).

**Environment caveats, disclosed rather than let quietly undermine the above:** (a) the `<video>` element's own `currentTime`/`paused` properties never advanced in this headless-Chrome session even while `getStats()` proved continuous healthy decode throughout — a rendering-pipeline quirk of this specific headless environment, not a mediamtx or app regression (the transport-level signal is the one the exit criteria actually care about, and it was unambiguously green); a prior soak session's differently-configured Chrome profile did show `currentTime` advancing, so this is flagged as an environment difference worth a look, not dismissed. (b) One incidental full page reload landed mid-feed-kill-test (Vite/Angular dev-server HMR reload, `[vite] connecting…`/`[vite] connected.` confirms this tab was HMR-attached) — almost certainly the concurrent Java-side agent's own work finally letting a blocked frontend rebuild (`features/devices/**`, outside this batch's scope) land; it manifested as one extra, cleanly-successful WHEP reattach (`POST`→`201`) and is unrelated to mediamtx. **Verdict: mediamtx 1.19.3 introduces no regression against any exit criterion; the pin stays at 1.19.3, not reverted.**

**`shared/map/live-dock.ts`/`.html`/`.css` deleted** — grep-verified zero remaining references anywhere in `src/` before removal (see the `/map` fleet overview section's own updated blockquote above for the full "why it was already dead" history, and this file's Gotchas entry for the doc-comment staleness it also carried).

**Leaflet zoom-control vs. layer-switcher overlap fixed** in both `shared/map/fleet-map.ts` and `shared/map/live-map.ts` — see the `/map` fleet overview section's own `FleetMap` bullet above for the exact mechanism (`zoomControl: false` + `L.control.zoom({position: 'bottomright'})`). Visually verified via headless-Chrome screenshots of `/command` (fleet map) and `/fly`'s cockpit inset (`LiveMap`) before/after.

**Four stale MapPage/"O(visible) discipline" doc-comment references fixed** (flagged by an earlier cycle's own Gotchas entry, now resolved) — see that Gotchas entry above for the full before/after per file (`shared/map/fleet-map.ts`, `core/map/map-store.ts`, `core/map/map-logic.ts`'s adjacent `features/map/map.ts` falsehood, `core/events/events-store.ts`).

**Frontend gate**: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean on `tsconfig.app.json` (a transient, unrelated `features/devices/**` compile error surfaced mid-session from the concurrent Java-side agent's own in-flight work — confirmed via `git status`/`git diff --stat` to be entirely their own uncommitted files, not this batch's; gone by the time this batch finished). Targeted `ng test --watch=false --include=shared/map --include=core/map --include=core/events` — **124/124 passing**. Full `npm run test:ci` — **707/707 passing across 39 files**. `/command` and `/fly` (picker + cockpit) both screenshot-verified rendering correctly, zoom control no longer overlapping the layer switcher on either.

## U-d — Asset & warehouse rework: frontend half (docs/UX-REWORK-PLAN.md §U-d)

A backend agent built the pinned contract this batch (`POST /api/devices/probe`; `PUT/GET/DELETE
/api/assets/{id}/image`; `PATCH /api/assets/{id}` attributes) concurrently — coded against verbatim
per that task's own "code defensively" instruction; this section's own "Live-verification" list at
the end names exactly what a live restart against the finished backend should confirm.

### New feature: `features/onboarding/**` — the wizard

**Route**: `/add-source` (`onboarding.routes.ts`, own lazy chunk — 52.73 kB raw / 13.62 kB transfer,
see Bundle impact below), replacing the inline "+ Add source" card the Devices page used to open on
itself. Reached from Warehouse's own "+ Add source" button (`DevicesPage#goToAddSource`) and from
Fly's pre-existing `/devices?addSource=1` empty-state link, which now redirects straight through
(`DevicesPage`'s constructor still reads the `addSource` query-param input, but navigates to
`/add-source` instead of opening a card — Fly's own link, out of this task's scope, needed no edit).

**Architecture** — four pieces, each with one job, mirroring how every other page/store pair in this
app is split:

- `onboarding.ts`/`.html`/`.css` — `OnboardingPage`, a thin `@switch (store.step())` shell: one
  section per step, a Back/Next footer, nothing else. `providers: [OnboardingStore]` scopes a fresh
  store instance to this route, the same DI-sharing idiom `AssetDetailPage` already uses for
  `TelemetryStore`/`DetectionsStore` — a second pass through the wizard never starts warm with a
  previous attempt's half-filled form.
- `onboarding-store.ts` — `OnboardingStore`, the wizard's own "component store" the plan's own
  wording asked for: every signal across all four steps, plus the actual HTTP orchestration
  (`probe()`, `scan()`, `createAsset()`, …). Deliberately thin glue — every yes/no decision and every
  request shape is delegated to `onboarding-logic.ts`, exactly like `features/devices/devices.ts`'s
  own pre-existing relationship with `devices-page-logic.ts`.
- `onboarding-logic.ts` — pure, Angular-free: the step-machine (`nextStep`/`prevStep`, `WizardStep`/
  `ConnectMethod` types), the poka-yoke advance gates (`canAdvanceFromProfile`/`canAdvanceFromConnect`/
  `canAdvanceFromTest`), and the request builders (`buildProbeRequest`/`buildCreateAssetRequest`/
  `buildPostSimulationAssetEdit`). Fully unit-tested (`onboarding-logic.spec.ts`) with no HTTP, no
  router, no DOM at all.
- `image-downscale.ts` — the Profile step's photo pipeline, split pure/DOM per its own doc comment:
  `computeTargetDimensions`/`isAcceptableImageType`/`JPEG_QUALITY_STEPS` are pure and tested
  (`image-downscale.spec.ts`); `downscaleImageToJpeg` draws into a real `<canvas>` (`createImageBitmap`
  when available, an `HTMLImageElement`+`Image.onload` fallback otherwise) and tries
  `JPEG_QUALITY_STEPS` (0.92 → 0.35) until the encoded `Blob` clears `MAX_IMAGE_BYTES` (2MB, the
  pinned `PUT /api/assets/{id}/image` limit) or the ladder runs out (best effort at the lowest step —
  the `PUT` itself is the final, authoritative check). jsdom (this app's test env) has no canvas, so
  this half is exercised only by hand — see Live-verification below.
- `protocols.ts` (+ its own spec) moved here **verbatim, unchanged**, from `features/devices/` — the
  Register method's protocol `<select>` (`REGISTERABLE_PROTOCOLS`/`CUSTOM_PROTOCOL_OPTION`/
  `placeholderForProtocol`/`protocolSelectionFor`) is now onboarding-only; the Warehouse page never
  rendered this select at all, only `Device.protocol` as a plain chip.

### The 4 steps

1. **Profile** — display name, registration/tail number (optional, the `registrationNumber`
   attributes-key convention — see `core/fleet/asset-attributes.ts` below), photo upload with preview
   (canvas downscale, see above), category picker (`core/fleet/category-logic.ts`, moved from
   `features/devices/devices-page-logic.ts` — see below).
2. **Connect** — the pre-existing register/discover/simulate 3-choice component, **moved wholesale**
   out of `features/devices/devices.ts` into `OnboardingStore`, preserving every field and behavior
   byte-for-byte: the protocol `<select>` + `Custom…` escape hatch + advanced-mode stream options,
   the discovery scan + "Use" candidate-fill, and all four simulate modes (`direct`/`rtsp`/
   `testDrone`/`synthetic`) including the flight-plan dialog integration (`shared/map/flight-plan-dialog.ts`,
   imported read-only — that module's own doc comment is now one line stale, "the Devices page's
   Add-source Simulate step"; not fixed here, `shared/map/**` is a different concurrent agent's scope
   this batch, flagged below). One behavioral simplification: the old page's `scrollIntoView`+
   outline-highlight hack for "Use" (needed because the register form and the discovery table shared
   one crowded page) is gone — the wizard's own step transition (discover → register sub-view) *is*
   the affordance now, so nothing was lost, just no longer needed.
3. **Test** — `POST /api/devices/probe` (UX-DESIGN §5.1 "test-before-save"): the decoded frame, WxH/
   codec/fps/telemetry-detected facts, warnings, a Retry button, and the specific error text the
   backend's own `ApiErrorBody.message` supplies verbatim (`describeHttpError`'s existing default
   branch already surfaces any non-500 server message, 422 included — no new case needed there).
   **Poka-yoke**: `OnboardingStore#canAdvanceTest` requires the *last successful probe* to still match
   the *current* protocol/URI/options (`probeStillCurrent`, a `computed` diffing the last-probed
   request against the live one) — editing the connection after a successful probe silently
   re-disables Next until it's re-tested, not just "the first probe ever succeeded". **Skipped
   entirely for Simulate** (`nextStep('connect', 'simulate')` → `'create'` directly) — starting the
   simulation *is* the proof it works; there is no separate device to probe ahead of it.
4. **Create** — a summary, then the actual persistence call:
   - **Register/Discover**: **one** `POST /api/assets` call, `CreateAssetRequest.devices[0]`
     embedding the connection as a `CreateAssetDeviceSpec` (name = the asset's own display name — this
     wizard has no separate device-naming field anywhere in it, on purpose: it thinks in assets, not
     devices). This is what closes docs/UX-REWORK-PLAN.md §U-d item 2's "orphaned-device dead end" —
     no device is ever registered on its own, unowned.
   - **Simulate, `direct`/`rtsp`/`testDrone`**: `POST /api/simulations` "as today"
     (`fleet.simulate()`, so the device/stream lists refresh and a failure is toasted exactly like
     every other simulate call site), then a follow-up `PATCH /api/assets/{id}` applies
     name/`registrationNumber` if either was given (`buildPostSimulationAssetEdit`) — the simulation
     endpoint has no `attributes`/custom-name-at-create-time shape of its own.
   - **Simulate, `synthetic`** — **a deliberate deviation from "as today", flagged for review**: the
     pre-wizard "Add the simulated source" quick-add (`buildSyntheticRegisterRequest`) registered a
     bare `sim`/`sim://demo` **device**, with no owning asset at all — itself an orphaned-device dead
     end, just a zero-hardware one. Perpetuating that *inside the very wizard whose stated job is
     closing that gap* was judged inconsistent, so `synthetic` is instead routed through the **same**
     `POST /api/assets` call as Register/Discover, using `buildSyntheticRegisterRequest`'s own
     `protocol`/`uri` output as the embedded device's connection. `features/devices/devices.ts`'s own
     empty-state "Add the simulated source" one-click quick-add is **unchanged** — still the old
     orphaning quick-add, deliberately, since it predates this wizard, isn't part of the 3-choice
     component, and is out of this specific plan item's stated scope; flagged in case a future cycle
     wants it closed too.
   - **Photo**, if one was chosen, is applied via `PUT /api/assets/{id}/image` after the asset exists,
     on every path above — a soft failure here (`applyPhotoIfAny`/`finishCreate`'s own try/catch)
     still lets the asset creation itself succeed, with its own toast naming the photo failure
     separately, never silently swallowed.
   - Every path ends the same way: `fleet.refresh({quiet:true})` (Warehouse sees the new asset/device
     immediately on the next visit), a success toast, then `router.navigate(['/assets', assetId])`.

### Shared logic promoted to `core/` (the established "second consumer → `core/`" rule)

Three pieces moved out of `features/devices/**`'s own private modules once the wizard became a
second consumer — the exact same rule `core/fleet/device-logic.ts`/`core/fleet/warehouse-logic.ts`
themselves document as precedent, applied again:

- **`core/fleet/simulation-logic.ts`** absorbed `SimulateMode`/`FileSimulateForm`/
  `buildSimulationRequest`/`buildSyntheticRegisterRequest` (previously `features/devices/simulate-logic.ts`)
  alongside the `testDrone`-only `buildTestDroneRequest`/`TestDroneForm` it already held — every
  zero-hardware `POST /api/simulations`/`POST /api/devices` request-builder now lives at one shared
  home, since both the wizard's Connect step (every mode) and Warehouse's own one-click "Add the
  simulated source" quick-add (`buildSyntheticRegisterRequest` only) need pieces of it.
  `features/devices/simulate-logic.ts` keeps only the Warehouse-only display concern that was never
  shared: `isSimulatedAsset`/`mapSimulatedDevices`/`SimulatedDeviceInfo` (mapping *already-created*
  simulated assets back to their devices, for the Advanced table's "Simulated" chip/stop action).
- **`core/fleet/category-logic.ts`** (new file) absorbed `CategoryOption`/`DEFAULT_CATEGORY_OPTIONS`/
  `deriveCategoryOptions` from `features/devices/devices-page-logic.ts` — both the wizard's Profile
  step and Warehouse's own "Promote to asset…" panel need the identical "existing categories +
  fallback list" picker. `devices-page-logic.ts` re-exports everything from here (mirrors its own
  pre-existing `warehouse-logic.ts` re-export block) so its own import site needed no changes.
- **`core/fleet/asset-attributes.ts`** (new file) — the `registrationNumber` attributes-key
  convention: `REGISTRATION_NUMBER_ATTRIBUTE_KEY`, `registrationNumberOf`, `withRegistrationNumber`
  (create/set), `withoutRegistrationNumber` (explicit clear — see its own doc comment for why a
  *create* flow and an *edit* flow need different blank-input behavior). Shared by the wizard's
  Create step and the asset detail page's inline edit — the one convention both need to agree on.

### Warehouse rename (docs/UX-REWORK-PLAN.md §U-d item 4)

`features/devices/**` is now "the Warehouse" everywhere a person reads it, while staying `/devices`
everywhere a machine reads it:

- **Page**: `<h1>Warehouse</h1>`, updated intro copy; nav tab label `app.ts`'s `tabs` array: `'Assets'`
  → `'Warehouse'` (C-c's own prior rename, itself now superseded).
- **Route**: `devices.routes.ts` keeps `path: 'devices'` (link-compatibility — every existing
  `routerLink="/devices"`/`router.navigate(['/devices', …])` call site across this app, and any
  bookmark, keeps working verbatim) and adds `path: 'warehouse'` as an alias to the same lazy
  component, `data: { preload: false }` so `IdlePreload` doesn't fetch the identical chunk twice.
- **Scope narrowed**: the add-source card/method-grid/register-discover-simulate forms are gone from
  this page entirely (moved to the wizard, see above) — `DevicesPage` lost ~280 lines and every one of
  `protocols.ts`'s imports; "+ Add source" is now one line, `goToAddSource()` →
  `router.navigate(['/add-source'])`. **Unchanged, on purpose**: the asset-first list, the collapsed
  Advanced (raw devices) table, every kebab menu, and the Advanced table's own "Promote to asset…"
  surgery flow (`buildCreateAssetRequestForDevice`, for an *already-existing* orphaned device) — the
  plan's own exit condition is explicit that Advanced "keeps working for surgery but is no longer the
  only outcome", not that it goes away.

### `features/asset-detail/**` additions (docs/UX-REWORK-PLAN.md §U-d item 3)

- **Photo**: `assetImageSrc` (a `computed`) binds `<img [src]>` to `api.assetImageUrl(assetId)` only
  when `asset()?.hasImage` is true and the fetch hasn't already 404'd this page-view
  (`imageLoadFailed`, an `(error)` handler on the `<img>` itself — reset only when `assetId()` changes
  via the routing effect, deliberately *not* on every 5s poll tick, or a genuine 404 would flicker
  retry-then-fail every poll cycle forever). Rendered next to the header's name/chips.
- **Registration/tail number**: `registrationNumber` (a `computed` off `registrationNumberOf`) in the
  Characteristics card, with an inline edit (`editingRegistrationNumber`/`registrationNumberDraft`) —
  `confirmEditRegistrationNumber` always starts from the asset's **complete current** `attributes`
  map before adding/removing the one key (see the "full replacement, not a merge" note below), and
  blanking the field to empty explicitly *removes* the key (`withoutRegistrationNumber`), not "leave
  the old value alone" the way the wizard's own create-time `withRegistrationNumber` behaves.
- **Attributes editor**: gated behind `settings.advancedMode()` — raw key/value rows
  (`attributeRows`/`addAttributeRow`/`removeAttributeRow`/`updateAttributeKey`/`updateAttributeValue`),
  seeded from `attributesToRows(asset.attributes)` on open, submitted as
  `attributeRowsToRecord(attributeRows())` on Save (both pure, `asset-detail-logic.ts`, tested). Shows
  every attribute raw, `registrationNumber` included — it's the escape hatch, not a second,
  inconsistent view of the same data.
- **Verified against the actual backend source, not assumed**: `PATCH /api/assets/{id}`'s
  `attributes` field is a **full replacement map, never a merge**
  (`vision-application/.../AssetEdit.java`'s own Javadoc: "replacement attributes, or `null` to keep
  the current map" — read directly before writing either editor). Both editors above are careful to
  always build their submitted map from the asset's **complete** current `attributes`, never a
  partial delta — sending just `{registrationNumber: "…"}` would silently wipe out every other
  attribute the asset had.
